S^{\prime}:G^{\prime}\times_{G}V\to G^{\prime}\times_{G}E
is the section naturally induced from
S
. By abuse of language, the K-chart morphism

K\to G^{\prime}\times_{G}K

induced from the natural maps
V\to G^{\prime}\times_{G}V
and
E\to G^{\prime}\times_{G}E
is also called a group enlargement.

Finally, the morphism which will be used most frequently in this paper is a combination of the above more elementary kinds.

Definition 3.7

(Morphisms of Kuranishi spaces)
.

A
strict morphism
or
strict embedding
of Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
is a strict map
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
such that there exists a commutative diagram of strict maps

\textstyle{K_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{b}}
\textstyle{K_{4}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{c}}
\textstyle{K_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{21}}
\textstyle{K_{2}}

(3.1)

where
\iota_{a}
is a stabilization by a disk bundle,
\iota_{b}
is a group enlargement, and
\iota_{c}
is an open embedding. It implies in particular the group morphism
\iota_{21}^{G}
, the base map
\iota_{21}^{V}:V_{1}\to V_{2}
, and the bundle map
\iota_{21}^{E}:E_{1}\to E_{2}
, are all embeddings.
A conjugacy class of strict morphisms from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
is called a
morphism
.

Remark 3.8
.

The above definition basically says that a morphism
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
is an embedding if (up to group enlargement) the domain map is an embedding and the restriction of
K_{2}
to a neighborhood of the embedding image is isomorphic to a stabilization. One can also define a weaker “infinitesimal version” of K-chart embeddings. In the smooth category one can prove that the infinitesimal version is equivalent to the above definition. However, in the topological category it may not be so.

One can check that compositions of embeddings are still embeddings, which ultimately follows from the fact that every
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundle over
G^{\prime}/G
for a group embedding
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
is induced from a
G
-representation. Also notice that stabilization maps, open embeddings, and group enlargements are all special cases of embeddings.

Definition 3.9
.

The categories of regularly stratified topological and smooth Kuranishi spaces, denoted by
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
and
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
, are the categories whose objects are stratified Kuranishi charts in the topological and smooth categories and whose morphisms are embeddings.

Proposition 3.10
.

\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
and
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
are regular stratification categories.

Proof.

Disjoint union and direct product make the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
distributive monoidal, with monoidal unit being the singleton
K_{0}=(\{0\},{\rm pt},0,0)
. On the other hand, one can obviously take pullbacks of any
A
-stratified Kuranishi space with respect to homogeneous poset maps
f:B\to A
.
∎

3.1.2.
Free quotients

There is another relation among Kuranishi spaces which will appear in the construction. This notion first appeared as
group enlargement
in
[
3
, Section 4]
and then showed up as
free quotient
in
[
4
, Lemma 3.5]
.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
K^{\sim}=(G^{\sim},V^{\sim},E^{\sim},S^{\sim})
be a (topological or smooth) Kuranishi space where
G^{\sim}=G\times G^{\prime}
is the product of two compact Lie groups. Suppose the
G^{\prime}
-action on
V^{\sim}
is free. Then the
free quotient
of
K^{\sim}
by
G^{\prime}
is the Kuranishi space

K:=K/G^{\prime}:=(G,V^{\sim}/G^{\prime},E^{\sim}/G^{\prime},S^{\sim}/G^{\prime}).

Notice that there is a natural strict morphism (which is not an embedding) from
K^{\sim}
to
K
. We also call the morphism a free quotient by
G^{\prime}
.

3.2.
Rigidified embeddings

In our geometric construction we can obtain objects and morphisms living in a more rigid category of Kuranishi charts. In short, we only allow the stabilization to be a product bundle coming from a
G
-representation.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
be a strict embedding.

(1)

A
rigidification
(which may not exist) of
\iota_{21}
consists of a
G_{1}
-representation
W_{21}
(viewed as a
G_{1}
-vector bundle
\pi_{21}:\underline{\smash{W}}_{21}\to V_{1}
), a
G_{1}
-invariant open neighborhood
D_{21}\subseteq W_{21}
of the origin (viewed as a disk bundle over
V_{1}
), and an open embedding
\theta_{21}:G_{2}\times_{G_{1}}{\rm Stab}_{D_{21}}(K_{1})\to K_{2}
such that the diagram (
3.1
) is realized by

We denote the rigidification by
(D_{21}\subseteq W_{21},\theta_{21})
.

(2)

Two rigidifications
(D_{21}\subseteq W_{21},\theta_{21})
and
(D_{21}^{\prime}\subseteq W_{21}^{\prime},\theta_{21}^{\prime})
are called
equivalent
if there exists an isomorphism
\tau:W_{21}\cong W_{21}^{\prime}
of representations, which induces an isomorphism of K-charts

\tau:{\rm Stab}_{D_{21}}K_{1}\cong{\rm Stab}_{D_{21}^{\prime}}K_{1}

such that after shrinking
D_{21}
and
D_{21}^{\prime}
properly, as K-chart morphisms one has

\theta_{21}=\theta_{21}^{\prime}\circ\tau.

(3)

A
rigidified embedding
from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
is an embedding together with an equivalence class of rigidifications.

Note that not all embeddings can be rigidified. On the other hand, rigidified embeddings are well-behaved. We prove that the composition of rigidified embeddings is naturally rigidified.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
be an embedding with a rigidification
(D_{21}\subseteq W_{21},\theta_{21})
and
\iota_{32}:K_{2}\to K_{3}
be an embedding with a rigidification
(D_{32}\subseteq W_{32},\theta_{32})
. Viewing
W_{32}
as a
G_{1}
-representation via the group embedding
G_{1}\to G_{2}
, define

W_{31}:=W_{21}\oplus W_{32},\ D_{31}:=D_{21}\oplus D_{32}.

Define
\theta_{31}:{\rm Stab}_{D_{31}}(K_{1})\to K_{3}
as the composition

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Then
(D_{31}\subseteq W_{31},\theta_{31})
is a rigidification of
\iota_{32}\circ\iota_{21}
, whose equivalence class only depends on the equivalence classes of the rigidifications of
\iota_{32}
and
\iota_{21}
.

Proof.

This lemma holds essentially because a trivial bundle over the total space of a trivial bundle is canonically trivial.
∎

Remark 3.14
.

If in Definition
3.12
we do not require the bundle
W_{21}
to be trivial, then there is no well-defined composition for rigidified embeddings.

Remark 3.15
.

One could criticize that rigidified embeddings are not anything natural. On the other hand, our geometric construction (the AMS construction) of Kuranishi flow categories allows us to obtain a topological Kuranishi flow category with rigidified embeddings as morphisms. This is very helpful in the smoothing procedure: once a stratum is smoothed, the total space of a trivial disk bundle is then canonically smoothed, giving a smooth tubular neighborhood in a higher stratum.

3.3.
Stable complex structures and normal complex structures

Next, we discuss stable and normal complex structures on Kuranishi spaces. Geometrically, such structures arise from index-theoretic considerations of (virtual) tangent bundles of moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic curves. Such structures are also instrumental for incorporating the FOP perturbation scheme to define integral invariants.

3.3.1.
Stable morphisms of vector bundles

We start with a few basic definitions.

Definition 3.16
.

Let
G
be a Lie group,
V
be a
G
-space, and
E\to V
be an equivariant vector bundle.

(1)

Let
F\to V
be another
G
-equivariant vector bundle. A
G
-equivariant
stable (iso)morphism
from
E
to
F
is an equivalence class of triples
(R^{-},R^{+},\tau)
where
R^{\pm}
are a pair of real vector spaces (viewed as trivial representations of
G
) and

\tau:\underline{\smash{R}}^{-}\oplus E\to\underline{\smash{R}}^{+}\oplus F

is a
G
-equivariant (iso)morphism of vector bundles. The equivalence relation is induced by
(R^{-},R^{+},\tau)\sim(R^{-}\oplus W,R^{+}\oplus W,\tau\oplus{\rm Id}_{\underline{\smash{W}}})
where
W
is another real vector space. Denote a stable (iso)morphism by

\tau:E\overset{s}{\to}F.

(2)

If
E,F
are complex vector bundles, then a stable (iso)morphism
\tau:E\overset{s}{\to}F
is called
complex
if it is represented by a triple
(R^{-},R^{+},\tau)
where
R^{\pm}
are complex vector spaces and
\tau
is a complex vector bundle map.

One can verify that stable morphisms can be composed and stable isomorphisms admit inverses as stable isomorphisms.

3.3.2.
Stable complex structures

Definition 3.17
.

Let
G
be a Lie group,
V
be a
G
-space, and
E\to V
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle.

(1)

A
G
-invariant stable complex structure
on
E
consists of an equivalence class of pairs
(\tau,F)
where
F\to V
is a
G
-equivariant complex vector bundle and
\tau:E\overset{s}{\to}F
is a
G
-equivariant stable isomorphism. The equivalence relation is generated by the following relation:
(\tau_{1},F_{1})
and
(\tau_{2},F_{2})
are equivalent if there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{E}}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{2}}
\textstyle{F_{2}}

of stable isomorphisms where the right vertical arrow is a complex stable isomorphism.

(2)

Suppose
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are
G
-equivariant stably complex vector bundles over a
G
-space
V
. A
G
-equivariant stable (iso)morphism
f:E_{1}\overset{s}{\to}E_{2}
is said to be
stably complex
if there exist representatives
(\tau_{1},F_{1})
and
(\tau_{2},F_{2})
of their stable complex structures and a commutative diagram of stable (iso)morphisms

\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{2}}
\textstyle{F_{2}}

where the right vertical arrow is a
G
-equivariant complex stable (iso)morphism.

(3)

Let
V
be a smooth
G
-manifold (with boundary and corners). A
G
-equivariant stable complex structure on
V
is a
G
-equivariant stable complex structure on
TV
. Notice that for each stratum
\partial^{\alpha}V\subseteq V
, the stable complex structure on
TV
induces a stable complex structure on
T(\partial^{\alpha}V)
because the normal direction is trivial.

Now we consider smooth Kuranishi space
K=(G,V,E,S)
such that the
G
-action on
V
has only finite stabilizers. In this setting,
TV
contains a trivial subbundle isomorphic to
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
. On the other hand, in our construction, the obstruction bundle
E
can always be made complex. This simplifies the definition of stable complex structures.

Definition 3.18
.

A
stably complex Kuranishi space
is a smooth Kuranishi space
K=(G,V,E,S)
equipped with a
G
-invariant complex structure on
E
and a
G
-invariant stable complex structure on
TV/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
.

An
open embedding
of stably complex Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
is a smooth open embedding
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
such that 1) the vector bundle isomorphism

d\iota_{21}^{V}:TV_{1}/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{1}\to TV_{2}/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{2}

is stably complex and 2) the vector bundle isomorphism
\iota_{21}^{E}:E_{1}\to E_{2}
is complex linear.

We consider stabilizations of a stably complex Kuranishi space
K=(G,V,E,S)
by a disk bundle
\pi_{D}:D\to V
contained in a
G
-equivariant complex vector bundle
N\to V
, which is

{\rm Stab}_{D}(K)=(G,D,\pi_{D}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{D}^{*}N,\pi_{D}^{*}S\oplus\tau_{N})

Then
\pi_{D}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{D}^{*}N
carries a canonical complex structure. On the other hand, the bundle
TD/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
is non-canonically isomorphic to
\pi_{D}^{*}TV/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}\oplus\pi_{D}^{*}N
. An isomorphism can be induced from a
G
-invariant connection on the fiber bundle
D\to V
.

Definition 3.19
.

The
stabilization
of a stably complex Kuranishi space
K=(G,V,E,S)
by a disk bundle
D
contained in a
G
-equivariant complex vector bundle
N\to V
(equipped with a
G
-invariant complex linear connection) is the smooth stabilization
{\rm Stab}_{D}(K)
with the induced stably complex structure on
TD/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
and the complex structure on
\pi_{D}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{D}^{*}N
.

Definition 3.20
.

A
strict embedding
of stably complex Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
consists of a smooth strict embedding
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
such that in the diagram (
3.1
), the stabilization
\iota_{a}:K_{1}\to K_{3}
is provided by a disk bundle in a complex vector bundle and the open embedding
\iota_{c}:K_{4}\to K_{2}
is an open embedding of stably complex Kuranishi spaces.

An
embedding
of stably complex Kuranishi spaces is a conjugacy class of strict embeddings.

Lemma 3.21
.

(Strict) embeddings of stably complex Kuranishi spaces can be composed.

Proof.

Essentially one needs to consider disk bundles over disk bundles to ensure the compatibility of stable complex structures. Given Ehresmann connections on
D_{21}\to V_{1}
and
D_{32}\to V_{2}
, the horizontal distribution of
D_{21}
can then be lifted to
D_{32}
, giving a Ehresmann connection on the iterated bundle.
∎

Remark 3.22
.

We could define the category

\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\mathbb{C}}

whose objects are regularly stratified stably complex (in particular, smooth) Kuranishi spaces and whose morphisms are embeddings. It is easy to see that this is a regular stratification category, and there is a natural forgetful functor

\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\mathbb{C}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}.

One can also consider the rigidified version. However, it is rather complicated to obtain stably complex Kuranishi spaces in our construction (although we could). Below we consider a more flexible one using normal complex structures, which suffices for our purpose.

3.3.3.
Normally complex Kuranishi spaces

Moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic curves admit stable complex structure because of the nature of the Cauchy–Riemann operator. In Section
16
we will follow the strategy of Abouzaid–Blumberg
[
1
,
2
]
to do the explicit construction. However, it is a bit cumbersome to construct stable complex structures compatibly. Instead, we will only construct the normal complex structure which is sufficient for our applications and where the compatibility is easier to achieve.

Let
V
be a
G
-space. Notice that a stable morphism of
G
-equivariant vector bundles
E,F\to V
(Definition
3.16
) induces the following well-defined linear maps. For each
x\in V
, let
G_{x}\subseteq G
be the stabilizer. Then the fibers
E_{x}
and
F_{x}
split as

\displaystyle\ E_{x}=\mathring{E}_{x}\oplus\check{E}_{x},\

\displaystyle\ F_{x}=\mathring{F}_{x}\oplus\check{F}_{x}

where
\mathring{E}_{x}
,
\mathring{F}_{x}
are
G_{x}
-trivial summands and
\check{E}_{x}
,
\check{F}_{x}
are
G_{x}
-nontrivial pieces. Then an equivalence class of stable isomorphisms induces well-defined
G_{x}
-equivariant maps

\check{\tau}_{x}:\check{E}_{x}\to\check{F}_{x}.

(In particular, if
G
is trivial, the maps
\check{\tau}_{x}
are all trivial.)

Definition 3.23
.

Let
G
be a Lie group,
V
be a
G
-space, and
E\to V
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle.

(1)

Two stable complex structures on
E
, represented by
(\tau_{1},F_{1})
and
(\tau_{2},F_{2})
, are
normally equivalent
, if there exists a diagram of stable isomorphisms

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{E}}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{2}}
\textstyle{F_{2}}

where the right vertical arrow is complex, such that for all
x\in V
, the following induced diagram of linear maps commutes.

\textstyle{\check{E}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\check{\tau}_{1,x}}
\textstyle{\check{F}_{1,x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\check{E}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\check{\tau}_{2,x}}
\textstyle{\check{F}_{2,x}}

(2)

A
G
-invariant
normal complex structure
on
E
is a normal equivalence class of stable complex structures. Notice that a normal complex structure on
E
equips each
\check{E}_{x}
with a
G_{x}
-invariant complex structure.

(3)

Suppose
E_{1},E_{2}\to V
are equipped with
G
-equivariant normal complex structures. A
G
-equivariant stable morphism
\tau:E_{1}\overset{s}{\to}E_{2}
is called
normally complex
if for each
x\in V
, the induced linear map
\check{E}_{1,x}\to\check{E}_{2,x}
is complex linear.

Now we define the notion of normal complex Kuranishi spaces.

Definition 3.24
.

A
normally complex Kuranishi space
(NC Kuranishi space for short) is a smooth Kuranishi space
K=(G,V,E,S)
equipped with a
G
-invariant complex structure on
E
and a
G
-invariant normal complex structure on
TV/{\mathfrak{g}}
. An
open embedding
of NC Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}=(G_{1},V_{1},E_{1},S_{1})
to
K_{2}=(G_{2},V_{2},E_{2},S_{2})
is a smooth open embedding
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
such that 1) the vector bundle isomorphism

d\iota_{21}:TV_{1}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}\to TV_{2}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}

is normally complex and 2) the vector bundle isomorphism
\iota_{21}^{E}:E_{1}\to E_{2}
is complex-linear.

One can again discuss stabilizations (by complex vector bundles), strict embeddings, conjugations, and embeddings of normally complex Kuranishi spaces. Then one obtains a regular stratification category called the category of normally complex Kuranishi spaces, denoted by

\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}

and the corresponding rigidified category
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
.

4.
Derived Orbifolds

In this section we describe another regular stratification category called the category of derived orbifolds. We will consider as general as the smooth category, but not the topological category.

4.1.
Stratified orbifolds

All orbifolds, unless declared, are assumed to be smooth and effective. They are spaces which are locally modelled by
U/\Gamma
where
\Gamma
is a finite group and
U
is an effective smooth
\Gamma
-manifold. Orbifolds with boundary and corners are locally modelled on
U/\Gamma
where
U
is a
\Gamma
-manifold with boundary and corners. If
A
is a regular poset (Definition
2.4
), then one can consider the notion of
A
-orbifold. Without giving the detailed definition, we remind the reader that a smooth effective
A
-orbifold contains charts of the form

U_{\alpha}\times[0,\epsilon)^{(\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma})},\ {\rm where\ }\alpha\leq\gamma\in A^{\max},

where
U_{\alpha}
is a
\Gamma
-manifold without boundary. Here we always assume that the isotropy group acts trivially on the normal factors. For example, if
X
is a smooth
A
-manifold acted on by a compact Lie group
G
with finite stabilizers, then the quotient
X/G
is naturally a smooth effective
A
-orbifold.

Definition 4.1

(Derived orbifolds)
.

Fix a regular poset
A
. An
A
-stratified
derived orbifold
is a triple
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
where
{\mathcal{U}}
is an effective
A
-orbifold,
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is a smooth orbifold vector bundle, and
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is a continuous section. A derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}
is called
compact
if the coarse space of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
is a compact topological space.

Similar to the case of Kuranishi spaces, we define embeddings of derived orbifolds as combinations of several more basic morphisms.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}
be an
A
-stratified derived orbifold.

(1)

A
morphism of derived orbifolds
from an
A_{1}
-stratified derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}=({\mathcal{U}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{1})
to an
A_{2}
-stratified derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}=({\mathcal{U}}_{2},{\mathcal{E}}_{2},{\mathcal{S}}_{2})
consists of a morphism
i:A_{1}\to A_{2}
of
\underline{\smash{\bf RegPos}}
, a smooth stratified orbifold map
\phi_{21}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
, and a smooth orbibundle map
\widehat{\phi}_{21}:{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
covering
\phi_{21}
such that the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\phi}_{21}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{21}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}

Such a morphism induces an orbispace map
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-1}(0)\to{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{-1}(0)
.

(2)

An
open embedding of derived orbifolds
from
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
is a morphism such that the poset map
i:A_{1}\to A_{2}
is an isomorphism onto an Alexandrov open subset, the orbifold map
\phi_{21}
is an isomorphism onto an open subset, and the bundle map
\widehat{\phi}_{21}
is an isomorphism over the image of
\phi_{21}
.

(3)

Given a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
, for any open subset
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
with
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, the
shrinking
of
{\mathcal{D}}
to
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, denoted by
{\mathcal{D}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
, is the derived orbifold
({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}})
. It admits a natural open embedding into
{\mathcal{D}}
.

(4)

A disk bundle over an orbifold is an open neighborhood of the zero section of an orbifold vector bundle. A
stabilization
of a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
by a disk bundle
{\mathcal{N}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
contained in a vector bundle
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is the derived orbifold

{\rm Stab}_{\mathcal{N}}{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{N}},\pi_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\pi_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}},\pi_{\mathcal{N}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{\mathcal{F}})

where
\tau_{\mathcal{F}}
is the tautological section. We call the natural morphism

{\mathcal{D}}\to{\rm Stab}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{D}})

the
stabilization morphism
by
{\mathcal{N}}
.

(5)

An
embedding of derived orbifolds
from
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}=({\mathcal{U}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{1})
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}=({\mathcal{U}}_{2},{\mathcal{E}}_{2},{\mathcal{S}}_{2})
is a morphism
{\bm{\phi}}_{21}:{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
such that there exists a disk bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{21}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
and an open embedding
\theta_{21}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{21}}({\mathcal{D}}_{1})\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
extending
\phi_{21}
.

Definition 4.3
.

The category of stratified derived orbifolds, denoted by
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, has objects being stratified derived orbifolds and morphisms being embeddings of derived orbifolds.

The following is readily checked.

Proposition 4.4
.

The category
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
is a regular stratification category (Definition
2.10
). ∎

One can see that there are functors

\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}.

The first arrow sends
K=(G,V,E,S)
to the quotient
{\mathcal{D}}=(V/G,E/G,S/G)
. Notice that a conjugacy class of Kuranishi space embeddings induces a well-defined embedding of derived orbifolds. The second arrow is induced by passing to the zero locus
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
of a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
.

4.1.1.
Rigidified embeddings

We would like to introduce an analogue of rigidified embeddings for orbifolds. We need to relax the triviality requirement. A flat orbifold vector bundle is an orbifold vector bundle together with a flat connection. A key property of flat vector bundles is, if
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is flat and
{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is flat, then
{\mathcal{F}}
is canonically a flat bundle over
{\mathcal{U}}
.

For example, if
G
is a compact Lie group acting on a smooth manifold
V
with only finite stabilizers and
W
is a representation of
G
, then the vector bundle
(V\times W)/G
is flat. Notice that the pullback of rigid vector bundles is still rigid.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\phi_{21}:{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
be an embedding of derived orbifolds.

(1)

A
rigidification
of
\phi_{21}
consists of a disk bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{21}
contained in a flat vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{21}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
and an open embedding

\psi_{21}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{21}}({\mathcal{D}}_{1})\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}

which extends
\phi_{21}
.

(2)

Two rigidifications
({\mathcal{N}}_{21}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{21},\psi_{21})
,
({\mathcal{N}}_{21}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{21}^{\prime},\psi_{21}^{\prime})
are equivalent if there is a flat bundle isomorphism
\tau:{\mathcal{F}}_{21}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{21}^{\prime}
which identifies
{\mathcal{N}}_{21}
with
{\mathcal{N}}_{21}^{\prime}
after an appropriate shrinking such that

\psi_{21}^{\prime}=\psi_{21}\circ\tau.

(3)

A
rigidified embedding
of derived orbifolds is an embedding together with an equivalence class of rigidifications.

One can see that rigid derived orbifold embeddings can be composed. Therefore we can define a category of derived orbifolds with rigid embeddings as morphisms. This category is denoted by

\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}.

Notice that there is a quotient functor

\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}\to\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}.

4.2.
Normal complex structures

Normal complex (NC for short) structures are essential for constructing FOP transverse perturbations. We recall the definitions here.

4.2.1.
Basic notions

Suppose
G
acts on a set
A
. A subgroup
H\subseteq G
is called an
A
-essential subgroup, denoted by

H\subseteq_{A}G

if there exists
a\in A
whose stabilizer is
H
.

Given a real representation
V
of a finite group
\Gamma
, the
basic decomposition
of
V
is the splitting

V=V_{\Gamma}\oplus\check{V}_{\Gamma}

where
V_{\Gamma}
is the direct sum of trivial subrepresentations and
\check{V}_{\Gamma}
is the direct sum of nontrivial irreducible subrepresentations. An
NC structure
on
V
is a
\Gamma
-invariant complex structure on
\check{V}_{\Gamma}
.

4.2.2.
NC structures

Definition 4.6
.

Let
U
be a
\Gamma
-manifold and
E\to U
be a
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundle. An
NC structure
on
E
consists of, for each
U
-essential subgroup
H\subseteq_{U}\Gamma
, a
H
-invariant complex structure
I^{\check{E}_{H}}
on the subbundle

\check{E}_{H}\subseteq E|_{U_{H}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

For each
g\in\Gamma
which conjugates
H
to
H^{\prime}:=gHg^{-1}
, the bundle isomorphism
\check{E}_{H}\to\check{E}_{gHg^{-1}}
sends
I^{\check{E}_{H}}
to
I^{\check{E}_{H^{\prime}}}
.

(2)

For each pair of
U
-essential subgroups
K\subseteq H\subseteq\Gamma
, one has the
K
-equivariant decomposition

\check{E}_{H}\cong\check{E}_{K}\oplus(\check{E}_{H}\cap E_{K}|_{U_{H}}).

We require that the restriction of
I^{\check{E}_{H}}
to
\check{E}_{K}
coincides with
I^{\check{E}_{K}}
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be an effective orbifold and
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be an orbifold vector bundle. An
NC structure
on
{\mathcal{E}}
consists of, for each bundle chart
\hat{C}=(\Gamma,U,E)
of
{\mathcal{E}}
, an NC structure
{\bm{I}}^{E}
on the vector bundle
E
, such that chart embeddings respect those complex structures. An
NC structure
on
{\mathcal{U}}
is by definition an NC structure on
T{\mathcal{U}}
.

A
normally complex derived orbifold
is a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
together with NC structures on
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Definition 4.8

(Embeddings of NC derived orbifolds)
.

(1)

An
open embedding of normally complex derived orbifolds
from
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
is an open embedding of derived orbifolds which respects normal complex structures.

(2)

A stabilization of an NC derived orbifold is a stabilization by a disk bundle which is contained in a complex orbifold vector bundle.

(3)

An embedding of NC derived orbifolds
\iota_{21}:{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
is an embedding such that there exists a stabilization of
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
by a disk bundle
{\mathcal{N}}_{21}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
and an open embedding from
{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{N}}_{21}}({\mathcal{D}}_{1})\to{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
which extends
\iota_{21}
.

(4)

A
rigidified embedding
of NC derived orbifolds from
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
is a rigidified embedding whose rigidification can be represented by a disk bundle in a rigid complex vector bundle. Two rigidifications are equivalent if they (after appropriate shrinking to smaller disk bundles) differ by an isomorphism of rigid complex vector bundles.

Therefore we obtained the category
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}
of normally complex derived orbifolds with morphisms being embeddings and the category
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}_{\rm rig}
with the same objects with morphisms being rigidified embeddings. One can see that they are both regular stratification categories.

Lemma 4.9
.

There are natural functors of regular stratification categories

\displaystyle\ \underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}\to\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}},\

\displaystyle\ \underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}\to\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}_{\rm rig}

Proof.

Let
K=(G,V,E,S)
be a stably complex Kuranishi space. Then by definition,
E
has a
G
-invariant complex structure and the vector bundle

TV/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}

has a
G
-invariant stable complex structure. This means there exists a real vector space
R
and a complex structure on

TV/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}.

Consider the quotient chart

K/G:=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})

where
{\mathcal{U}}=V/G
. Given any
x\in V
with stabilizer
G_{x}\subseteq G
, one has the
G_{x}
-invariant decomposition

T_{x}V\cong T_{x}V_{G_{x}}\oplus N_{x}V_{G_{x}}.

The stable complex structure on
TV/\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
induces a complex structure on
N_{x}V_{G_{x}}
. The collection of these complex structures forms a normal complex structure on the quotient orbifold. One can easily check that the morphisms descend to morphisms in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}
and
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}_{\rm rig}
.
∎

5.
Collars

Various inductive constructions require moduli spaces, which appear as, e.g., morphism spaces of flow categories, to have compatible collars. While one can possibly discuss collars in an abstract manner, we refrain from doing so. Instead, in this section we only consider the categories with geometric meanings. More precisely, in this section, the category
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
belongs to the list
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf Man}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}
,
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
etc. Following an idea of Fukaya, we explain how to equip the geometric objects of interest with collars “on the outside” which facilitates inductive constructions of various sorts.

5.1.
The subcategory of collared objects

For the discussions in this section, let
\epsilon>0
be a positive real number. The model for collars is the space
[0,\epsilon)^{k}
which is stratified by subsets of
\{1,\ldots,k\}
. More generally, for any finite set
S
, we can form the collar
[0,\epsilon)^{S}
stratified by the set
A^{(S)}
(Notation
2.2
). These standard collars are objects of all the relevant regular stratification categories
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
listed above.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be either
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf Man}}
,
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
, or
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
.
For any
\epsilon>0
, an
\epsilon
-collaring
of an
A
-stratified object
M\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
is a collection of open embeddings

\partial^{\alpha}M\times[0,\epsilon)^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}}\to\partial^{\gamma}M,\ \alpha\leq\gamma

(called a collar embedding from
\alpha
to
\gamma
)
such that 1) its restriction to
\partial^{\alpha}M
is the inclusion of
\partial^{\alpha}M
into
M
and 2) whenever
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}M\times[0,\epsilon)^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\gamma}M}
\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}M\times[0,\epsilon)^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}^{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\beta}M\times[0,\epsilon)^{\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}^{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

An
\epsilon
-collaring and an
\epsilon^{\prime}
-collaring are
germ-equivalent
if for a certain
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}
, for all
\alpha\leq\gamma
, the restrictions of the collar embeddings to
\partial^{\alpha}M\times[0,\epsilon^{\prime\prime})^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}}
are identical. A
collared object
in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
is an object together with a germ-equivalence class of
\epsilon
-collarings.

Definition 5.2
.

A morphism between two collared objects
K
and
K^{\prime}
with underlying poset map
i:A\to A^{\prime}
is called
collared
if there exists a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
such that for each
\alpha\leq\gamma
in
A
, the morphism over the strata
\gamma\mapsto i(\gamma)
in the collared neighborhoods of the strata
\alpha\mapsto i(\alpha)
is given by

\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}K\times{\rm Collar}_{\epsilon}^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{i(\alpha)}K^{\prime}\times{\rm Collar}_{\epsilon}^{\mathbb{Face}_{i(\alpha)}^{i(\gamma)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\gamma}K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{i(\gamma)}K^{\prime}}

where the map along the collar direction is the identity map after identifying
\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}\cong\mathbb{Face}_{i(\alpha)}^{i(\gamma)}
.

In this way we obtain a subcategory

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

of collared objects, whose morphisms are collared morphisms. Notice that the empty set and singletons are always collared. Moreover, the collar structures respect products and disjoint unions. Therefore, the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
is a regular stratification category (Definition
2.10
).

5.2.
Outercollaring construction

For manifolds with boundary, it is well-known that one can construct collars. However, it is not easy to find collars for more complicated objects, for example, smooth manifolds with corners. Here we would like to adopt the outercollaring construction from
[
27
, Section 17]
to avoid such discussions. Instead of finding collars in the given geometric object, we add collars outside. In general, for each category
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
listed at the beginning of this section, we would like to construct monoidal functors called the
\epsilon
-outercollaring

\{\cdot\}:\underline{\smash{\bf C}}\to{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}.

Some care must be taken when we are in the smooth category.

5.2.1.
Outercollaring of stratified spaces

We first discuss the outercollaring of objects in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
. Let
A
be a regular poset and
X
be an
A
-space. Upon choosing a nonnegative number
\epsilon\geq 0
, we would like to define a new
A
-space denoted by
{}_{\lfloor}X
called
the outer collaring of
X
of width
\epsilon
such that the association
X\mapsto{}_{\lfloor}X
satisfies certain functorial properties (see Lemma
5.3
).

We first assume that
A
has a unique maximal element
\gamma
, i.e.,
X
has a single top stratum. Abbreviate
\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}^{\gamma}
. Define

{}_{\lfloor}X=\left(\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in A}\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}}\right)/\sim,

where the equivalence relation
\sim
is generated by the following relation: if
\alpha\leq\beta
(which implies
\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}\subseteq\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}
), we identify

\big(x,(t_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}}\big)\in\partial^{\beta}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}}

with

\big(y,(s_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}}\big)\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}}

if
x=y\in\partial^{\alpha}X
,
s_{j}=0
when
j\notin\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}
and
t_{j}=s_{j}
when
j\in\mathbb{Face}_{\beta}
. We call
{}_{\lfloor}X
the
outer collaring
of
X
of width
\epsilon
(see Figure
1
).

X
\partial^{\beta_{2}}X
\partial^{\beta_{1}}X
\partial^{\alpha}X
\partial^{\beta_{2}}X
\partial^{\beta_{1}}X
X
\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{2}
\partial^{\beta_{1}}X\times[-\epsilon,0]
\partial^{\beta_{2}}X\times[-\epsilon,0]

Figure 1.
The local picture of an outer collaring of a manifold with corners with strata
\alpha<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}
. The gray area is in the top stratum.

Now consider the general situation when the regular poset has arbitrarily many maximal elements. For each maximal
\gamma\in A^{\max}
, one has the outer collaring

{}_{\lfloor}(\partial^{\gamma}X).

Notice that if
\alpha\leq\gamma_{1},\alpha\leq\gamma_{2}
, then there is a natural isomorphism

{}_{\lfloor}\big(\partial^{\alpha}(\partial^{\gamma_{1}}X)\big)\cong{}_{\lfloor}\big(\partial^{\alpha}(\partial^{\gamma_{2}}X)\big).

Then define

{}_{\lfloor}X:=\left(\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in A^{\max}}{}_{\lfloor}(\partial^{\gamma}X)\right)/\sim

where
\sim
means we identify points in
{}_{\lfloor}(\partial^{\gamma_{1}}X)
and points in
{}_{\lfloor}(\partial^{\gamma_{2}}X)
with respect to the above natural isomorphisms. Figure
2
describes an example of the outer collaring of a space
X
with two top strata.

The local picture of an outer collaring of a composed manifold with corners with two top strata
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
of dimension 2, three 1-dimensional strata
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}
, and one zero-dimensional stratum
\alpha
.

The following statement is straightforward from the definition.

Lemma 5.3
.

The
\epsilon
-outer collaring is a regular stratification functor

{}_{\lfloor}\{\cdot\}:\underline{\smash{\bf RegTop}}\to{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf RegTop}}.\qed

The outer collar construction is compatible with group actions. If
G
acts on
X
, then for each
g\in G
and
(x,(t_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}}
, define

g\cdot(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})=(gx,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-\epsilon,0]^{\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}}\subseteq{}_{\lfloor}X.

It is easy to check that this gives a
G
-action on
{}_{\lfloor}X
.

5.3.
Category of admissible smooth manifolds and orbifolds

The outercollaring of stratified topological manifolds is completely identical to the case of stratified spaces. However, the case with smooth manifolds is tricky.

We recall the notion of
admissible manifolds with corners
introduced in
[
27
, Chapter 25]
. Let
V
be a smooth manifold without boundary. For
t=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})\in[0,+\infty)^{k}
, denote

T_{i}:=e^{1/t_{i}}.

Consider smooth functions

f:V\times[0,\epsilon)^{k}\to{\mathbb{R}}.

We consider the
C^{m}
-norm stratumwise using the above coordinates
T_{i}
instead of
t_{i}
. Let

|f|_{C^{m}}

be the pointwise
C^{m}
-norm in the above sense.

Definition 5.4
.

(cf.
[
27
, Definition 25.3]
) We say
f:V\times[0,\epsilon)^{k}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is
admissible
if for each compact
K\subseteq V\times[0,\epsilon)^{k}
and
m>0
, there exist
\sigma(m,K)>0
and
C(m,K)>0
such that

\left|\frac{\partial f}{\partial T_{i}}\right|_{C^{m}}\leq C(m,K)e^{-\sigma(m,K)T_{i}},\ i=1,\ldots,k.

Admissible functions are smooth (see
[
27
, Remark 25.5]
). Using the ring of admissible functions (see
[
27
, Lemma 25.13]
), we can consider corner charts on a smooth manifold with corners with admissible coordinate changes. In this way, one obtains a subcategory

\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}_{\rm adm}

of
\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}
. Notice that the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}
of collared smooth manifolds is automatically a subcategory of
\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}_{\rm adm}
.

Similarly, one can consider the admissible versions of the categories
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
and
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
. The key feature of the admissibility is that the outercollaring is well-defined (see
[
27
, Section 17.3]
). Indeed, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exist functors

{}_{\lfloor}\epsilon:\underline{\smash{\bf C}}_{\rm adm}\to{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

where
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
is either
\underline{\smash{\bf SMan}}
, or
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
, or
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
. For each admissible object
M
, let the result be called the
\epsilon
-outercollaring
of
M
.

Finally, also notice that the standard collars are normally complex derived orbifolds. In fact manifolds are automatically normally complex orbifolds. Hence one has the collared versions

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}},\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}_{\rm rig}.

6.
Flow Categories, Multimodules, and Homotopies

6.1.
Flow categories

The concept of flow categories was introduced by Cohen–Jones–Segal
[
22
]
. We need a variant of the original construction similar to
[
52
, Section 7]
and
[
5
, Section 7,8]
. See also the formulations in
[
14
]
.

Definition 6.1

(Flow categories)
.

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a regular stratification category (Definition
2.10
). A
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-enriched
flow category

{\mathbb{F}}
consists of the following elements.

(1)

The set of objects
{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, which is a countable poset (not assumed to be regular).

(2)

For any pair
p\leq q
of objects, a morphism space
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
, which is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
stratified by a normal poset
A_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
(Definition
2.5
).

(3)

For any triple
p\leq r\leq q
of objects, the composition map is defined as follows. It consists of an injective poset map homogeneous of degree
1

A_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times A_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to A_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

with image

A_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq A_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},

together with a morphism in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}:M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}|_{A_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}}.

Moreover, the following conditions must be satisfied.
10
10

10

The first four conditions are usually required for flow categories. The remaining conditions are imposed in order to obtain the correct chain level information.

(1)

M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
p\leq q
.

(2)

M_{pp}^{\mathbb{F}}
is a monoidal unit of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
.

(3)

When
p=r
resp.
r=q
, the composition
M_{pp}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
resp.
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{qq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
is the natural isomorphism from the monoidal structure.

(4)

(Associativity)
For any quadruple
p\leq r\leq s\leq q
of objects, the following diagram commutes, where the arrows are defined using composition maps:

\textstyle{M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rs}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{sq}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{ps}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{sq}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}}

(5)

If
p<r_{1},r_{2}<q
and
A_{pr_{1}q}^{\mathbb{F}}\cap A_{pr_{2}q}^{\mathbb{F}}\neq\emptyset
, then either
r_{1}\leq r_{2}
or
r_{2}\leq r_{1}
.

(6)

Maximal elements of each
A_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}
are true boundary strata of
A_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
(Definition
2.5
).

Example 6.2
.

A
trivial flow category
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{O}}
, is the flow category whose object set has a single element
e
with self-morphism space
M_{ee}^{{\mathbb{O}}}
being a monoidal unit of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
.

6.2.
Flow multimodules and homotopies

Flow bimodules are abstract packages of moduli spaces considered in situations such as continuation maps, PSS and SSP maps, etc. There is a more general notion of flow multimodules allowing multiple flow categories as inputs, which model situations such as the pair-of-pants product.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a regular stratification category. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
and
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be flow categories
11
11

11

One could consider more than one output; however, that will not be used in this paper.
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. A
multimodule
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, denoted by
{\mathbb{X}}
, consists of the following objects.

(1)

For each tuple of objects
p_{1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,p_{m}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
,
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, an object
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
with underlying normal poset
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
.

(2)

For any
i
and a pair of objects
p_{i}\leq q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
, an injective poset map homogeneous of degree
1

A^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}_{p_{i}q_{i}}\times A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

with image

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\subseteq A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},

together with a morphism in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}|_{A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}}.

(6.1)

(3)

Similar to above, for a pair of objects
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, an injective poset map homogeneous of degree
1

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times A_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

with image

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\subseteq A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},

together with a morphism in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}|_{A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}}.

(6.2)

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

When
p_{i}=q_{i}
(recall
M_{pp}^{\mathbb{F}}
is a monoidal unit), the morphism (
6.1
) is the natural isomorphism

M_{p_{i}p_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

Similarly, when
q^{\prime}=p^{\prime}
, the morphism (
6.2
) is the natural isomorphism

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{p^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

(2)

The following associativity properties hold.

(a)

For
p_{i}<r_{i}<q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{r_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}r_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}}

(6.3)

(b)

For
i<j
,
p_{i}<q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
p_{j}<q_{j}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}}

(6.4)

(c)

For
q^{\prime}<r^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\times M_{r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}}

(6.5)

(d)

For
p_{i}<q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}}

(6.6)

(3)

If
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}r_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\neq\emptyset
, then either
r_{i}\leq q_{i}
or
q_{i}\leq r_{i}
. In the former case, one has

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}r_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}r_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}.

In the latter case,

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}r_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i}r_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}.

(4)

If
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\neq\emptyset
, then either
r^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
or
q^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}
. In the former case, one has

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

In the latter case,

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cap A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

(5)

When
p_{i}<q_{i}
, the morphism (
6.1
) has codimension 1 and maximal elements of
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
are true boundary strata. Similarly, when
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, the morphism (
6.2
) has codimension 1 and maximal elements of
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
are true boundary strata.

Definition 6.4
.

A multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is called a
bimodule
, usually denoted by
{\mathbb{B}}
.

6.2.1.
Products of multimodules

In the above we only defined the situation with one output—this is the main scenario that will appear in this paper. On the other hand, there is no difficulty in extending the definition to multimodules with multiple output flow categories.

In general, let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\prime}
be flow categories enriched over
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. A multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{n}^{\prime})
, denoted by
{\mathbb{X}}
, consists of objects

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

together with structural maps

M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

and

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{i-1}^{\prime}q_{i}^{\prime}p_{i+1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{q_{i}^{\prime}p_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}^{\prime}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

which satisfy conditions similar to those in Definition
6.3
. We omit the details.

It is straightforward to define products of multimodules. Suppose
{\mathbb{X}}
is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Then for objects
p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
,
q_{j}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{j}
,
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
,
q^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
, the collection of objects

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m}q_{1}\cdots q_{n};p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}}:=M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{q_{1}\cdots q_{n};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}

define a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
.

6.2.2.
Homotopies of multimodules

For the following definition, denote by
[0,1]
the homogeneous poset with one unique maximal element, thought as the total space of the unit interval, and two codimension
1
element, which are the boundary points
\{0\}
and
\{1\}
.

Definition 6.5
.

Given two multimodules
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, a
homotopy
of multimodules from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{H}}
, consists of the following objects.

(1)

For each tuple
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
of objects of involved flow categories, an object
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
stratified by a regular poset
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times[0,1]
.

(2)

For a tuple
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
, a morphism in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}\to{\mathbb{H}}}\sqcup\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{H}}}:M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\sqcup M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}|_{A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times\{0\}}\sqcup M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}|_{A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times\{1\}}.

(3)

For a tuple
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
and
p_{i}\leq q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
,
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
, injective poset maps homogeneous of degree
1

A_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\to A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

and

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times A_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}},

together with morphisms in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
after pulling back the target to using the above poset maps (which we omit from the notations)

\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}:M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

(6.7)

and

\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}:M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}.

(6.8)

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

When
p_{i}=q_{i}
resp.
q^{\prime}=p^{\prime}
, the morphism (
6.7
) resp. (
6.8
) is the natural isomorphism from the monoidal structure of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
.

(2)

(Associativity H1)
The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}}

Hence we have a well-defined morphism

M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}.

(3)

(Associativity H2)
For
\alpha=0,1
and
i=1,\ldots,m
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}}

(4)

(Associativity H3)
For
p_{i}\leq r_{i}\leq q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
q^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{r_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\times M_{r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}r_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{r^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}}

(6.9)

(5)

(Associativity H4)
For
i<j
,
p_{i}\leq q_{i}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
p_{j}\leq q_{j}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{i}r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}}

6.3.
Lifts of flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies

The key issue of constructing Floer chain complex (and various chain maps, homotopies) is to regularize certain moduli spaces. Using the terminologies we just introduced, the associated flow categories (and multimodules, homotopies), whose morphism spaces are certain moduli spaces, are enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
. The regularization requires a lift to a better category which allows manipulations in differential topological or algebraic topological sense.

Let
\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}
and
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be two regular stratification categories (Definition
2.10
). Let
{\bf F}:\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a covariant functor satisfying the natural compatibility conditions.

(1)

{\bf F}
respects the distributive monoidal structure.

(2)

The diagram
\textstyle{\underline{\hat{\bf C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bf F}
\textstyle{\underline{\bf C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\bf RegPos}}
commutes.

(3)

{\bf F}
respects the pullback. Namely, if
\hat{M}
is an
A
-stratified object of
\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}
and
B\subseteq A
, then one has the natural isomorphism

{\bf F}(\hat{M})|_{B}\cong{\bf F}(\hat{M}|_{B}).

Definition 6.6
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{F}}
is a
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-enriched flow category. A
lift
of
{\mathbb{F}}
with respect to
{\bf F}:\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
is a
\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}
-enriched flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

The set of objects are identical
{\rm Ob}\hat{\mathbb{F}}={\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
.

(2)

{\bf F}(M_{pq}^{\hat{\mathbb{F}}})=M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
and
{\bf F}_{*}\iota_{prq}^{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}=\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}
.

Definition 6.7
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
are
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-enriched flow categories and
{\mathbb{X}}
is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
are lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
in
\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}
respectively.

(1)

A
lift
of
{\mathbb{X}}
which
extends

\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
is a multimodule
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
such that for any tuple of objects
p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime}
of the involved flow categories and
p_{i}\leq q_{i}
,
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
, one has

{\bf F}(M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{X}}})=M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

and

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},\

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

(2)

Suppose
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
are two such lifts of multimodules
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
respectively. Let
{\mathbb{H}}
be a homotopy from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. A lift of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
\underline{\smash{\hat{\bf C}}}
which extends
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
, and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
is a homotopy
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
from
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
such that for any tuple of objects
p_{1},\cdots,p_{m};p^{\prime}
,
q^{\prime},p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, one has

{\bf F}(M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{H}}})=M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}},

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{H}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}},\

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{H}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}},

and

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}\to\hat{\mathbb{H}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}\to{\mathbb{H}}},\

\displaystyle\ {\bf F}_{*}\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}\to\hat{\mathbb{H}}}=\iota_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{H}}}.

6.4.
Outercollaring of flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a regular stratification category which admits the outercollaring construction. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Fix an
\epsilon>0
(the
outercollaring width
). Define the
\epsilon
-outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}
, which is a flow category
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, as follows. The set of objects is the same as
{\mathbb{F}}
. For each pair
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, the morphism space is

M_{pq}^{{}_{\lfloor}{}_{\epsilon}{\mathbb{F}}}:={}_{\lfloor}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}.

For
p\leq r\leq q
, the composition is the outercollaring

\iota_{prq}^{{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}}:={}_{\lfloor}{}_{\epsilon}\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}:{}_{\lfloor}(M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}})\cong{}_{\lfloor}M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times{}_{\lfloor}M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\cong M_{pr}^{{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}}\times M_{rq}^{{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}}\to{}_{\lfloor}M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}\cong M_{pq}^{{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}}.

It is straightforward to check that this set of data satisfies the definition of flow categories.

The case of outercollaring of multimodules and homotopies are similar. We omit the details. We remark that one must choose the same outercollaring width of involved flow categories to obtain a corresponding outercollaring of multimodules and homotopies.

6.5.
Multimodule concatenations

6.5.1.
Bimodule concatenations

Before we discuss the more general multimodule concatenation, we first consider the bimodule case, which was discussed in
[
2
, Section 5]
.

We first describe the intuitive picture. Suppose
{\mathbb{F}},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}
are flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
,
{\mathbb{B}}
is a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
is a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
. For example,
{\mathbb{B}}
and
{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
are bimodules corresponding to continuation maps. We would like to define a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
which characterizes the features of compositions of continuation maps. Then given a pair of objects
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
and
p^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}
, the space
M_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
should be the quotient

\left(\bigsqcup_{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\right)/\sim

(6.10)

where the equivalence relation is generated by viewing the images of points of

M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\times M_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}

in either
M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
or
M_{p;q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
the same point.

However, this construction does not work for any category
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. For example, when
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}=\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
, because of the symmetry groups, there is no obvious way to construct the above quotient in
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
.

To rigorously define the notion of concatenations, one needs some preparations. Let
\mathbb{F},\mathbb{F}^{\prime},\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
be flow categories. Let
\mathbb{B}
be a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
be a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
. For each pair of objects
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
and
p^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}
, define the set

A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\circ{\mathbb{B}}}:=\left(\bigsqcup_{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\right)/\sim

where the equivalence relation
\sim
is defined as follows. For any
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
images of the two maps

\textstyle{A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\times A_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}\times\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{A_{p;q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}}

are identified. The equivalence relation
\sim
is generated by this identification.

Lemma 6.8
.

Two pairs
(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
and
(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
are equivalent if and only if one of the following conditions holds: 1)
p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
and there exists
v\in A_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\ b^{\prime}=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(a^{\prime},v),\

\displaystyle\ a^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,b^{\prime\prime});

2)
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
and there exists
w\in A_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\ a^{\prime}=\iota_{p;q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(b^{\prime},w),\

\displaystyle\ b^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,a^{\prime\prime}).

Proof.

We first check that the relation defined in the statement of this lemma, denoted by
\approx
, is an equivalence relation. The only non-obvious condition is the transitivity. Suppose we have three elements
(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
,
(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, and
(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Suppose
(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\approx(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})
and
(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\approx(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})
. Without loss of generality, we may assume one of the following holds.

(1)

p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}
. In this case, there exists
v\in A_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
and
w\in A_{q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\ b^{\prime}=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(a^{\prime},v),\

\displaystyle\ a^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,b^{\prime\prime})

and

\displaystyle\ c^{\prime}=\iota_{p;q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(b^{\prime},w),\

\displaystyle\ b^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{q^{\prime}r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,c^{\prime\prime}).

Then by the associativity conditions for the bimodules, one has

c^{\prime}=\iota_{p;q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(b^{\prime},w)=\iota_{p;q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(a^{\prime},v),w)=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(a^{\prime},\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(v,w))

and

a^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,b^{\prime\prime})=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,\iota_{q^{\prime}r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,c^{\prime\prime}))=\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(v,w),c^{\prime\prime}).

Hence
(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\approx(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})
.

(2)

p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
and
r^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
. In this case, there exists
v\in A_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
and
w\in A_{r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

b=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(a,v),\ a^{\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,b^{\prime})

and

b=\iota_{p;r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(c,w),\ c^{\prime}=\iota_{r^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,b^{\prime}).

Then we see

b\in\partial^{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}A_{p;q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\cap\partial^{p;r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}A_{p;q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\neq\emptyset.

Then by the condition for bimodule/multimodules (see (3) and (4) of Definition
6.3
), either
r^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
or
q^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}
. Without loss of generality, assume that
p^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}
. Then one has

b\in A_{p;p^{\prime}r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}

As the poset maps underlying the structural maps are all injective (see Definition
6.1
and Definition
6.3
), it follows that there exists
u\in A_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

c=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(a,u),\ v=\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(u,w).

Then by using the associativity of the bimodules, one can conclude that

a^{\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,b^{\prime})=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(u,w),b^{\prime})=\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(u,\iota_{r^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,b^{\prime}))=\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(u,c^{\prime})

which implies
(a,a^{\prime})\approx(c,c^{\prime})
.

Therefore,
\approx
is an equivalence relation.

Then one can see that the two relations
\sim
and
\approx
contain each other. Hence the lemma is proved.
∎

The following corollary is an obvious consequence.

Corollary 6.9
.

The natural map

A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}

is injective.

Denote the image of the above map by

A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}.

More generally, assume
p_{1}^{\prime}<\cdots<p_{l}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, denote

A_{p;(p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{l}^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{l}A_{p;p_{i}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}.

The following corollary is also obvious.

Corollary 6.10
.

If
A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\cap A_{p;q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
, then either
p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
or
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
.

We define a partial order between two elements
x,y\in A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
as follows. We denote
x\leq y
if there exist
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
,
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in A_{p^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
,
x^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime\prime}\in A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
such that
x^{\prime}\leq y^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\leq y^{\prime\prime}
and such that
x
is represented by
(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
and
y
is represented by
(y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})
.

Lemma 6.11
.

This is a partial order. Moreover, the map
A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is a poset map.

Proof.

The only non-obvious condition is the transitivity. Suppose
x\leq y\leq z
, then there exists
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
such that
x
is represented by
(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
,
y
is represented by
(y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
such that
x^{\prime}\leq y^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\leq y^{\prime\prime}
. There also exists
\tilde{p}^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
such that
y
is represented by
