(\tilde{y}^{\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{\tilde{p}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
and
z
is represented by
(\tilde{z}^{\prime},\tilde{z}^{\prime\prime})\in A_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{\tilde{p}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
such that
\tilde{y}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime\prime}
. Since
(y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})\approx(\tilde{y}^{\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime})
, by Lemma
6.8
, either
p^{\prime}\leq\tilde{p}^{\prime}
or
\tilde{p}^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
.

(1)

When
p^{\prime}\leq\tilde{p}^{\prime}
, there exists
v\in A_{p^{\prime}\tilde{p}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\ \tilde{y}^{\prime}=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}\tilde{p}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(y^{\prime},v),\

\displaystyle\ y^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}\tilde{p}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}).

Then define

\tilde{x}^{\prime}=\iota_{p;p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}(x^{\prime},v)

Then as
x^{\prime}\leq y^{\prime}
, one has
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{y}^{\prime}
. Moreover, as
x^{\prime\prime}\leq y^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(v,\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime})
, it follows that
x^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime})
for some
w
and
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}
. Moreover,
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}\leq\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}
. It then follows that
(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\approx(\tilde{x}^{\prime},\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime})
and
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{y}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}\leq\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime\prime}
. Hence
x\leq z
.

(2)

When
\tilde{p}^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
, the argument is similar. There exists
w\in A_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\ y^{\prime}=\iota_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(\tilde{y}^{\prime},w),\

\displaystyle\ \tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,y^{\prime\prime}).

Then define
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,x^{\prime\prime})
. Then since
x^{\prime\prime}\leq y^{\prime\prime}
, one has

\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,x^{\prime\prime})\leq\iota_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}(w,y^{\prime\prime})=\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime\prime}.

Moreover, as
x^{\prime}\leq y^{\prime}=\iota_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(\tilde{y}^{\prime},w)\in A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
, there exists
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\in A_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
and
u\in A_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
such that
x^{\prime}=\iota_{p;\tilde{p}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}(\tilde{x}^{\prime},u)
and
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{y}^{\prime}\leq\tilde{z}^{\prime}
. Hence
x\leq z
.

Lastly, it follows immediately from the definition of the partial order that the map
A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is a poset map.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

The poset
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is a normal poset.

Proof.

We first verify that this poset is homogeneous (see Definition
2.3
). Let
x\in A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
be an arbitrary element. Notice that each
A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\cong A_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is homogeneous. Hence if
x\in A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, the depth of
x
in this subset is well-defined, denoted by
d_{p^{\prime}}(x)
. As maximal elements of
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
are exactly maximal elements of
A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
for all possible
p^{\prime}
, we only need to show that
d_{p^{\prime}}(x)
is independent of
p^{\prime}
. Suppose
x\in A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\cap A_{p;q^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume
p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}
. Then
x\in A_{p;(p^{\prime}q^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. We need to show that
d_{p^{\prime}}(x)=d_{q^{\prime}}(x)
.

Claim.
Maximal elements of
A_{p;(p^{\prime}q^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
have depth
1
.

Proof of the claim.
The depth is clearly at least one. If the depth is bigger than
1
, then there exists a non-maximal element
y
such that
x<y
. Then there exists
r^{\prime}
different from
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}
such that
x\in A_{p;r^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Then by the definition of the equivalence relation, the three elements
p^{\prime},q^{\prime},r^{\prime}
are totally ordered. In either case,
x
cannot be a maximal element of
A_{p;(p^{\prime}q^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
.
End of the proof of the claim.

Therefore, both
d_{p^{\prime}}(x)
and
d_{q^{\prime}}(x)
are 1 higher than the depth of
x
in the subset
A_{p;(p^{\prime}q^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Hence
d_{p^{\prime}}(x)=d_{q^{\prime}}(x)
. Hence
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is a homogeneous poset.

Next we need to show that the poset is regular (see Definition
2.4
). Given any
\gamma\in A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, the three conditions for
\gamma
in Definition
2.4
are about elements below
\gamma
. We may assume that
\gamma\in A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, which is a regular poset. Hence all three conditions for
\gamma
holds. Therefore,
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is regular.

Lastly, we verify that
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is normal (Definition
2.5
), i.e., there are only fake boundaries or true boundaries. Let
\gamma
be an element of depth
1
.

Claim. There exist at most two distinct
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
such that
\gamma\in A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
.

Suppose it is not the case, then there exists three distinct
p_{1}^{\prime},p_{2}^{\prime},p_{3}^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
such that
\gamma
is representable in
A_{p;p_{i}^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times A_{p_{i}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
for
i=1,2,3
. Then the three elements
p_{1}^{\prime},p_{2}^{\prime},p_{3}^{\prime}
are totally ordered; we may assume
p_{1}^{\prime}<p_{2}^{\prime}<p_{3}^{\prime}
. Then it is easy to find an increasing path starting from
\gamma
of length
2
. This contradicts the condition that
\gamma
has depth
1
.

Now we discuss in two cases. First, suppose there exists a unique
p^{\prime}
such that
\gamma\in A_{p;p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, which is a normal poset. Hence
\gamma
is adjacent to at most two maximal elements. Second, suppose
\gamma\in A_{p;p_{i}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
for
p_{1}^{\prime}\neq p_{2}^{\prime}
. Then we may assume
p_{1}^{\prime}<p_{2}^{\prime}
and
\gamma\in A_{p;(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. As
\gamma
has depth 1, it is a maximal element of
A_{p;(p_{1}^{\prime}p_{2}^{\prime});p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Hence by the properties of bimodule/multimodules (see (2) of Definition
6.3
), it is a true boundary in both
A_{p;p_{1}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
and
A_{p;p_{2}^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. Hence
\gamma
is adjacent to exactly two maximal elements in
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
. This finishes the proof that
A_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is normal.
∎

The above technical results allow us to do the following construction.

(1)

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be either
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
or
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. If we define

M_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}:=\left(\bigsqcup_{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\right)/\sim

as topological spaces, then it is naturally stratified by
A_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\circ{\mathbb{B}}}
. Moreover, if
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}=\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
, then
M_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is a stratified pseudomanifold. Moreover, one obtains a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
, denoted by
{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
. If the flow categories and bimodules are enriched in the collared version of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, then
{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
is also collared.

(2)

If
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}=\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, let
{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
be the underlying stratified orbifolds in
M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}
and
M_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
respectively. Then define

{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}:=\left(\bigsqcup_{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\right)/\sim

in the similar way. Then it is a stratified orbifold. Moreover, the obstruction bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{p^{\prime};p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
induce orbifold vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
, with induced Kuranishi sections

{\mathcal{S}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{U}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}.

Then one obtains objects
M_{p;p^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
together with structural maps making a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
. Lastly, if the flow categories and bimodules are enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, so is the concatenation
{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
.

6.5.2.
General concatenations

Now we consider concatenations in a general regular stratification category
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. There is no canonical construction. We characterize the notion of concatenations in an axiomatic way.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
be flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
resp.
{\mathbb{Y}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
resp. over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Then a
concatenation
of
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
at
{\mathbb{G}}_{i}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
, consists of the following objects.

(1)

A multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
.

(2)

For each two tuples
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};r_{i})
and
(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n};r^{\prime})
of objects, a codimension zero morphism

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}\to M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}.

They are required to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For each tuple
(r_{1},\cdots,r_{i-1},p_{1},\cdots,p_{m},r_{i+1},\cdots,r_{n};r^{\prime})
, the object
M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
is stratified by
A_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
.

(2)

The underlying poset maps of the structural maps of
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is the same as the previously discussed special cases.

(3)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{r_{i}s_{i}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{i}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}s_{i}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};s_{i}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}s_{i}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}}

(4)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}}

(5)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};s^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}\times M_{s^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};s^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}\times M_{s^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}}

We can see that the previously discussed canonical construction of concatenations satisfies the above definition. On the other hand, in many situations, the construction of the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is not canonical but involves choices one needs to make.

Remark 6.14
.

We would like to reiterate that in Definition
6.13
,
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is part of the data: we do not construct
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
using
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
. This is in contrast with the gluing construction in
[
2
, Section 5]
, where the concatenation is carried out for bimodules enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
by thickening up the boundary strata. In our applications, all
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
arise from geometric context, and the notations in Definition
6.13
already ensure the desired chain-level statements.

6.6.
Weak flow categories, bimodules, and homotopies

In certain cases it is convenient to use the notion of direct products of flow categories, which satisfy a weaker version of Definition
6.1
. We make the following definitions in order to have a simple formulation of certain structures on multimodules involved in the equivariant pair-of-pants product.

Definition 6.15
.

A
weak flow category
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{F}}
, consists of a countable partially ordered set of objects
{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
and a morphism space
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
as an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
for each pair
p\leq q
, and composition maps
\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}:M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
(whose underlying poset map is not necessarily homogeneous of degree
1
) which satisfy (1)—(5) of Definition
6.1
.

Example 6.16
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
be (weak) flow categories. Define
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
as a weak flow category with

{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m}=\prod_{j=1}^{m}{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{j},

morphism spaces

M_{(p_{1}\cdots p_{m})(q_{1}\cdots q_{m})}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}=\prod_{j=1}^{m}M_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}},

and product structural maps.

We can also define the similar notion of weak multimodules.

Definition 6.17
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be weak flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. A
weak multimodule

{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
consists of the same type of objects
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
and structural maps (whose underlying poset map is not necessarily homogeneous of degree
1
) satisfying conditions (1)–(4) of Definition
6.3
.

The following lemma is readily checked.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Then the objects
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
and structural maps in
{\mathbb{X}}
define a weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
.

The following notion is introduced merely to simplify the formulation of multimodule concatenations. It is not the same as the more geometric bimodule obtained via continuation maps.

Definition 6.19
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a (weak) flow category. The
naive identity bimodule
of
{\mathbb{F}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{I}}^{\mathbb{FF}}
, is the weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}})
with

M_{p;q}^{{\mathbb{I}}^{\mathbb{FF}}}=M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

and structural maps induced from composition maps of
{\mathbb{F}}
.

The case of homotopies is similar. We omit the definition and the corresponding lemma.

6.6.1.
Multimodule concatenations as weak bimodule concatenations

Now we consider the situation of multimodule concatenations. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. The concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
at
{\mathbb{G}}_{i}
is defined previously as a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
(together with other data). As multimodules can be treated as weak bimodules (Lemma
6.18
), we can interpret the concatenation as a concatenation of a pair of weak multimodules.

First, notice that the notion of concatenations can be extended from multimodules to weak multimodules. Then we consider the product of weak bimodules

{\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1}{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1}}\times{\mathbb{X}}\times{\mathbb{I}}^{\mathbb{G}_{i+1}\mathbb{G}_{i+1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{I}^{\mathbb{G}_{n}\mathbb{G}_{n}}

as a weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1}\times{\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{G}}_{n})
. Then the multimodule concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is equivalent to the weak bimodule concatenation

({\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{I}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1}{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1}}\times{\mathbb{X}}\times{\mathbb{I}}^{\mathbb{G}_{i+1}\mathbb{G}_{i+1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{I}^{\mathbb{G}_{n}\mathbb{G}_{n}})\circ{\mathbb{Y}}.

The purpose of introducing this formal notion is to simplify the definition of equivariance of multimodules when the symmetry group permutes the source flow categories.

7.
Algebraic Structures

In this section we describe how to obtain algebraic structures such as chain complexes, chain maps, and chain homotopies from, respectively, flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies. When additional structures exist, these chain-level objects also carry corresponding structures such as equivariance and module structures over Novikov rings.

7.1.
Unobstructedness, local finiteness, grading, and orientations

We start by considering the most basic properties of flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies that lead to chain-level structures. Notice that these structures will only produce chain complex etc. as abelian groups. To obtain objects as modules over various Novikov rings, one needs certain extra symmetries on the flow categories etc.

7.1.1.
Unobstructedness

In geometric applications, the flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies always satisfy an additional property which does not have a general formulation. Here we specify this property.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
. It is said to be
unobstructed
12
12

12

This terminology comes from Lagrangian Floer theory. If we formulate the Lagrangian Floer flow category, then in general the true boundary of moduli spaces contain configurations with disk bubbles, potentially causing obstruction to define a chain complex.
if for any triple
p<r<q
of objects, the structural morphism

\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}:M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

is a homeomorphism onto
\partial^{prq}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. Hence the union of these strata is the true boundary of
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
.

If
\underline{\smash{{\bf C}}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
is a functor of regular stratification categories and
{\mathbb{F}}
is a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
,
{\mathbb{F}}
is called
unobstructed
if the induced flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
is unobstructed.

Similarly, we can define the notion of unobstructedness for multimodules and homotopies enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
. We omit the details. In applications within this paper, all geometric flow categories and their lifts are unobstructed.

7.1.2.
Convergence

We need certain properties guaranteeing the convergence of the chain-level objects in certain nonarchimedean completions. To incorporate the equivariant case, we allow a second functional which is bounded from one side to give a different completion.

Definition 7.2
.

A
filtration
on a (weak) flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
is a monotonic function

{\mathcal{A}}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\ (k\geq 1)

where
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
has the product partial order. The filtration is called
locally finite
if for each
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
and any
c>0
,

\#\Big\{q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\ |\ p<q,\ {\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(q)<c,\ \alpha=1,\ldots,k\Big\}<\infty.

A (weak) flow category equipped with a locally finite filtration is called a
locally finite
(weak) flow category.

Lemma 7.3
.

If
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,m
are locally finite filtered flow categories, then the product
({\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\mathcal{A}}_{m})
is a locally finite weak flow category.

Proof.

Straightforward.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

In a typical Floer theoretic setup, the action
{\mathcal{A}}
is
{\mathbb{R}}
-valued. In the equivariant case considered in Part 4 of this paper, the action will have two components.

Now we consider the local finiteness condition for multimodules. The condition we need is not only to guarantee a well-defined convergent chain map, but also that multimodule concatenations preserve such conditions.

Definition 7.5
.

Let
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
and
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-enriched locally finite (weak) flow categories. Suppose
{\mathcal{A}}
is
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
-valued and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
is
{\mathbb{R}}^{k^{\prime}}
-valued. A (weak) bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is called
locally finite
if the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

For any
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
,
c^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\#\Big\{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\ |\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\neq\emptyset,\ \min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\geq c^{\prime}\Big\}<\infty.

(2)

For all
c\in{\mathbb{R}}

f^{\mathbb{B}}(c):=\sup\Big\{\max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\ |\ \max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq c\ {\rm and}\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\neq\emptyset\Big\}<+\infty,

and

\lim_{\rho\to-\infty}h^{\mathbb{B}}(c,\rho)\\
:=\lim_{\rho\to-\infty}\sup\Big\{\min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\ |\ \max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq c,\ \min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq\rho,\ {\rm and}\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\neq\emptyset\Big\}=-\infty.

The case of multimodules can be obtained as a special case of weak bimodules.

Definition 7.6
.

Let
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,m
and
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be locally finite flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. A
locally finite multimodule
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
which is a locally finite weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
.

We verify that concatenations of locally finite multimodules are locally finite.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be either
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
or
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
. Let
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,m
,
({\mathbb{G}}_{j},{\mathcal{B}}_{j})
,
j=1,\ldots,n
,
({\mathbb{G}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})
, be locally finite flow categories. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a locally finite multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
be a locally finite multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Then the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is a locally finite multimodule.

Proof.

We verify conditions of Definition
7.5
one by one. Suppose (1) of Definition
7.5
does not hold for the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
. Then there exist

p_{1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,p_{m}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{m},r_{1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,r_{i-1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1},\ r_{i+1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1},\ldots,r_{n}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{n},

c^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and infinitely many distinct
r_{\nu}^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\ M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ{\mathbb{Y}}}\neq\emptyset,\

\displaystyle\ \min_{1\leq\beta\leq l^{\prime}}{\mathcal{B}}_{\beta}^{\prime}(r_{\nu}^{\prime})\geq c^{\prime}

(7.1)

It follows that there exists a sequence
r_{i,\nu}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{i}
such that

\displaystyle\ M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i,\nu}}^{\mathbb{X}}\neq\emptyset,\

\displaystyle\ M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}r_{i,\nu}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r_{\nu}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}\neq\emptyset.

By choosing a subsequence, we may assume the terms of the sequence
r_{i,\nu}
are either all identical, or all distinct. If all
r_{i,\nu}
are identical, then it contradicts the local finiteness of
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Hence all
r_{i,\nu}
are distinct. Then by the local finiteness of
{\mathbb{X}}
, for all
\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,l_{i}\}
, the sequence
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,\alpha}(r_{i,\nu})
is bounded from above; moreover, by choosing a subsequence, for some
\alpha
the sequence
{\mathcal{B}}_{i,\alpha}(r_{i,\nu})\to-\infty
. Then by the local finiteness of
{\mathbb{Y}}
, one has

\lim_{\nu\to\infty}\min_{1\leq\beta\leq l^{\prime}}{\mathcal{B}}_{\beta}^{\prime}(r_{\nu}^{\prime})=-\infty.

This contradicts (
7.1
). Therefore, (1) holds for the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
.

Now we verify (2) for the concatenation.
Choose
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
. We need to show that

f^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}(c)<+\infty

where
f^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
is defined in Definition
7.5
. Consider any collection of objects
p_{1},\ldots,p_{m},r_{1},\ldots,r_{i-1},r_{i+1},\ldots,r_{n},r^{\prime}
such that

\max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k_{i}}{\mathcal{A}}_{i,\alpha}(p_{i})\leq c,\ \max_{1\leq\beta\leq l_{j}}{\mathcal{B}}_{j,\beta}(r_{j})\leq c

and

M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}\neq\emptyset.

Then by the definition of concatenation, there exists
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\ M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\neq\emptyset,\

\displaystyle\ M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{Y}}}\neq\emptyset.

Then

\max_{1\leq\beta\leq l_{i}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i,\beta}(r_{i})\leq f^{\mathbb{X}}(c).

Denote
c^{\prime}=\max\{c,f^{\mathbb{X}}(c)\}
. Then by the assumption, one has

\max_{1\leq\beta\leq l^{\prime}}{\mathcal{B}}_{\beta}^{\prime}(r^{\prime})\leq f^{\mathbb{Y}}(c^{\prime}).

This proves that

f^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}(c)\leq f^{{\mathbb{Y}}}(c^{\prime})<+\infty.

Moreover, suppose

\rho=\min\Big\{\min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k_{j}}{\mathcal{A}}_{j,\alpha}(p_{j})\ |\ 1\leq j\leq m\Big\}.

Then

\min_{1\leq\beta\leq l_{i}}{\mathcal{B}}_{i,\beta}(r_{i})\leq h^{{\mathbb{X}}}(c,\rho)

Denote

\rho^{\prime}:=\min\Big\{h^{{\mathbb{X}}}(c,\rho),\min_{1\leq\beta\leq l_{j}}{\mathcal{B}}_{j,\beta}(r_{j})\ |\ j\neq i\Big\}.

Then

\min_{1\leq\beta\leq l^{\prime}}{\mathcal{B}}_{\beta}^{\prime}(r^{\prime})\leq h^{\mathbb{Y}}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})

which goes to
-\infty
as
\rho^{\prime}\to-\infty
. Therefore (2) of Definition
7.5
holds for the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
.
∎

Lastly we state the local finiteness of multimodule homotopies.

Definition 7.8
.

Let
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
and
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be two locally finite weak flow categories. Suppose
{\mathcal{A}}
is
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
-valued and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
is
{\mathbb{R}}^{k^{\prime}}
-valued. Suppose
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
are two locally finite weak bimodules over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
(Definition
7.5
). Then a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
is called a
locally finite homotopy
if the following are satisfied.

(1)

For any
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
and
c^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\#\Big\{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\ |\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\neq\emptyset,\ \min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\geq c^{\prime}\Big\}<\infty.

(2)

For all
c\in{\mathbb{R}}

\sup\Big\{\max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\ |\ \max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq c\ {\rm and}\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\neq\emptyset\Big\}<+\infty,

and

\lim_{\rho\to-\infty}\sup\Big\{\min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\ |\ \max_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq c,\ \min_{1\leq\alpha\leq k}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)\leq\rho,\ {\rm and}\ M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\neq\emptyset\Big\}=-\infty.

Definition 7.9
.

Let
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,m
and
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be locally finite flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
be locally finite multimodules over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. A
locally finite homotopy
from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
is a locally finite weak homotopy between the corresponding weak bimodules.

7.1.3.
Grading

Now we consider gradings on the flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a regular stratification category which is either
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, or
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. Define the notion of
virtual dimension
by

{\rm dim}^{\rm vir}C:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm dim}U-{\rm dim}G-{\rm rank}E,&\ C=(G,U,E,S)\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}},\\
{\rm dim}{\mathcal{U}}-{\rm rank}{\mathcal{E}},&\ C=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}},\\
{\rm dim}C,&\ C\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}\end{array}\right.

We allow different components of
C
to have different virtual dimensions.

Below we define gradings. In most of this paper, we will consider absolute, namely,
{\mathbb{Z}}
-gradings; in certain situations we will reduce to cyclic gradings.

Definition 7.10
.

Let
N
be a nonnegative integer. All flow categories and multimodules are assumed to be enriched in either
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, or
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
.

(1)

A (cohomological)
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading
on a flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
is a function
p\mapsto|p|\in{\mathbb{Z}}
satisfying

{\rm dim}^{\rm vir}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}=|q|-|p|-1,\ \forall p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}.

(2)

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be flow categories equipped with
{\mathbb{Z}}
-gradings. A multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is said to have cohomological degree
r
if for all
p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}

{\rm dim}^{\rm vir}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=|p^{\prime}|-|p_{1}|-\cdots-|p_{m}|-r.

7.1.4.
Orientations

Defining chain complexes in characteristics other than
2
requires orientations. We do not include orientations as part of the structure on objects in the categories
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
,
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
, or
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
as it is cumbersome to discuss orientations on the corners. Instead, we regard it as an extra structure on the flow category, multimodule, or homotopy.

Convention.
Let
M
be an oriented manifold with boundary. The induced boundary orientation is the one such that the

{\rm n}_{\rm out}\wedge{\rm or}_{\partial M}\in\det TM

coincides (up to positive scalar) with the orientation
{\rm or}_{M}
of
M
.

Definition 7.11
.

Let
C=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
be an
A
-stratified derived orbifold with
A
being a normal poset (Definition
2.5
).

(1)

For each element
\alpha\in A
, an orientation on
\partial^{\alpha}C
is an orientation of the virtual vector bundle
T\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}-{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V}
, or equivalently an orientation on the determinant line

\det(\partial^{\alpha}C):=\det(T\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}})\otimes\det({\mathcal{E}}^{*}|_{T\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}}).

Notice that for any face
\beta\in\mathbb{Face}_{\alpha}
, an orientation on
\partial^{\alpha}C
induces an orientation on
\partial^{\beta}C
by regarding the collar coordinate to point “inward.”

(2)

An orientation on
C
consists of orientations on all top strata
\partial^{\gamma}C
such that for each fake boundary stratum
\beta
, the orientation on
\partial^{\beta}C
induced from the (exactly) two adjacent top strata are opposite.

(3)

Let
C^{\prime}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
be another
A^{\prime}
-stratified derived orbifold. An embedding
\iota:C\to C^{\prime}
is said to be
orientation-preserving
if the underlying poset map has degree zero (i.e. maps top strata to top strata) and if for each top stratum
\gamma\in A
(which is mapped to
\gamma^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
), the induced map of determinant lines

\det(\partial^{\gamma}C)\to\det(\partial^{\gamma^{\prime}}C^{\prime})

is orientation-preserving.

On the other hand, an orientation on a stratified pseudomanifold
X
is defined to consist of orientations on all top strata
\gamma\in A^{\rm max}
satisfying the similar compatibility condition on fake boundaries.

Definition 7.12
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
(resp.
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
). A
coherent orientation
on
{\mathbb{F}}
is a collection of orientations on the object
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
such that the singletons
M_{pp}^{\mathbb{F}}
are positively oriented and such that whenever
p<r<q
, the morphism

\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}:M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to\partial^{prq}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

is orientation-preserving up to factor

(-1)^{|p|-|r|}.

Here by the definition of flow categories (Definition
6.1
), the top strata of
\partial^{prq}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
are true boundaries of
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
, hence have canonically induced orientations.

A flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
or
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
equipped with a coherent orientation is called
oriented
.

Similarly we have the notion of orientation for multimodules. Notice the signs appearing in the definition, which are put in order to obtain the correct chain maps.

Definition 7.13
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded oriented flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
(resp.
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
). Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a degree zero multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Suppose all flow categories are equipped with coherent orientations. An
coherent orientation
on
{\mathbb{X}}
consists of orientations on the charts
M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{X}}}
such that

(1)

For each
i=1,\ldots,m
, the morphism

M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\to\partial M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}

is orientation-preserving up to the sign

(-1)^{(\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|)(|p_{i}|-|q_{i}|)}.

In particular, for
i=1
, the boundary orientation coincides with the product orientation.

(2)

The morphism

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to\partial M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}

is orientation-preserving up to the sign

(-1)^{{\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}+1}.

A multimodule over oriented flow categories equipped with coherent orientations is called an
oriented multimodule
.

We consider the orientation for concatenations. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Recall that for each tuple
p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
,
r_{j}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{j}
and
r^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
, the top strata of
M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
is the disjoint union of the top strata of

\bigsqcup_{r_{i}}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}.

We define each of these products to be oriented by the product orientation times the sign

(-1)^{\left(\sum_{j<i}|r_{j}|\right){\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}}

(if the concatenation happens at the
i+1
-th input). The moduli spaces of the concatenation necessarily have fake boundaries and one can check easily that the above defined orientation on the interior induces an orientation on the orientations in the sense of Definition
7.11
. We then need to check that these orientations on individual moduli spaces define a coherent orientation of the multimodule.

Lemma 7.14
.

The orientation induced on
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is coherent.

Proof.

Fix a moduli space

M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}p_{1}\cdots p_{m}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}.

We first need to verify that the orientations on two open strata of this moduli space induce opposite orientations on the shared fake boundary stratum. Choose objects
r_{i}<s_{i}
of
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
. The two open strata are

\displaystyle\ M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}},\

\displaystyle\ M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};s_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}s_{i}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}.

(7.2)

The shared fake boundary stratum is

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{i}s_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{i-1}s_{i}r_{i+1}\cdots r_{m};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}.

Use the product orientation as a reference. The orientation on this fake boundary induced from the first differs from the product orientation by

\left(\sum_{j<i}|r_{j}|\right){\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}+{\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}+\left(\sum_{j<i}|r_{j}|\right)(|r_{i}|-|s_{i}|).

The second orientation on the fake boundary differs by a sign

\left(\sum_{j<i}|r_{j}|\right){\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};s_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}+{\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}+1.

One can see that they are indeed opposite.

Then one has to verify conditions listed in Definition
7.13
for the concatenation. For breakings at some
j
-th input
s_{j}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}_{j}
for
j<i
and breakings at some
i+l-1
-th input
q_{l}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{l}
, the verification is straightforward.

∎

Definition 7.15
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded oriented flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
(resp.
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
),
{\mathbb{X}}_{0},{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
be oriented flow multimodules over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
of degree zero (see Definition
7.10
). Let
{\mathbb{H}}
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded homotopy from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. A
coherent orientation
on
{\mathbb{H}}
consists of orientations on the charts
M_{pp^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
such that

(1)

The embedding

(-M_{pp^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{0}})\sqcup M_{pp^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{1}}\to\partial M_{pp^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

is orientation-preserving.

(2)

The embedding

M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\to\partial M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

is orientation-preserving up to the sign

(-1)^{\left(\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|\right)(|p_{i}|-|q_{i}|)}.

(3)

The embedding

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to\partial M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

is orientation-preserving up to the sign

(-1)^{{\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}+1}.

7.2.
Algebraic structures without symmetry

We define chain complexes, chain maps, and chain homotopies associated to flow categories, bimodules, and homotopies enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. We only impose the Novikov convergence condition but not the symmetries. Hence the resulting objects are only modules over
{\mathbb{Z}}
but not the Novikov rings.

7.2.1.
The chain group

We only state the construction for the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded case. The cyclically graded case can be produced in a similar fashion.

To a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded locally finite weak flow category
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
(see Definition
7.2
), one can associate a chain group

C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}):=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}p_{i}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}a_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}},\ \displaystyle\sup_{i}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p_{i})<+\infty\ \forall\alpha=1,\ldots,m,\\
\forall c>0,\ \#\big\{i\ |\ {\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p_{i})>-c\ \forall\alpha=1,\ldots,k\big\}<\infty\end{array}\right.\right\}.

It is not necessarily finitely generated over
{\mathbb{Z}}
. We call it the
Floer chain group
associated to
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
. It carries a natural valuation

{\mathfrak{v}}=({\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathfrak{v}}_{k}):C_{*}({\mathbb{F}})\setminus\{0\}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\ {\rm where}\ {\mathfrak{v}}_{\alpha}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}p_{i}\right)=\sup_{i}{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p_{i}).

For each
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, denote

C_{k}({\mathbb{F}}):=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}p_{i}\in C_{*}({\mathbb{F}})\ |\ {\rm deg}(p_{i})=k\right\}.

Suppose
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
and
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime})
are two weak Novikov flow categories. The Floer chain group
C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
is a kind of completion of the tensor product chain groups of the factors. Indeed, on the algebraic (finite) tensor product

C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})

there is the valuation
{\mathfrak{v}}
induced by
{\mathfrak{v}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathfrak{v}}^{\prime\prime}
. Then one can form the completion

C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}).

7.2.2.
The differential

Now, we assume that the flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
is enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
and it is oriented. We define a linear map

d:C_{k}({\mathbb{F}})\to C_{k-1}({\mathbb{F}}),\ k\in{\mathbb{Z}}

in the following way. Note that the morphism space
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
is an oriented and compact pseudomanifold of dimension

{\rm dim}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\equiv{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)-1.

When the dimension is zero,
M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
consists of finitely many oriented points, which defines a count
n_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
; in other cases, define
n_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}=0
. Define

d\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}p_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}}a_{i}n_{p_{i}q}^{\mathbb{F}}q.

The local finiteness property stated in Definition
7.2
implies that
d
is well-defined.

Proposition 7.16
.

If
{\mathbb{F}}
is unobstructed, then
d^{2}=0
. Therefore,
(C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}),d)
gives rise to a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded chain complex of free abelian groups, called the
complex
associated to
{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

To show
d^{2}=0
, consider pairs
p,q
with
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)=2
. By Definition
7.12
, the coefficients of
d^{2}(p)
in
q
is the oriented count of the union of products

\bigcup_{p<r<q}M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}.

Consider the 1-dimensional component of the pseudomanifold
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. The boundary points only appear on the true boundary strata of
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. By the definition of unobstructedness (Definition
7.1
), the true boundary strata are all the top strata of the products
M_{pr}\times M_{rq}
. By the coherence of the orientation (see Definition
7.12
), these counts cancel. Hence
d^{2}=0
.
∎

If
C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
and
C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
are complexes associated to flow categories
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}
, then the tensor product complex
C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
is defined. The following can be checked readily.

Lemma 7.17
.

The tensor product differential extends to the completion
\displaystyle C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
.

7.2.3.
Chain maps and homotopies

Now consider an oriented, unobstructed,
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded Novikov multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Let

m_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}

be the count of
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}
when the dimension is zero and
0
otherwise. Define (formally) a linear map

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}:C({\mathbb{F}}_{1}){\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}\cdots{\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})

by linearly extending (over
{\mathbb{Z}}
)

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}(p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m}):=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}m_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}p^{\prime}.

(7.3)

The Novikov property (Definition
7.5
) implies that
\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}
is well-defined and extends to the completion.

Proposition 7.18
.

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}
extends to the completion
\displaystyle C({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})
and is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear chain map of degree
{\rm index}({\mathbb{X}})
.

Proof.

The grading follows from the definition (see (2) of Definition
7.10
). To prove that
\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}
is a chain map, one needs to look at the orientation. By definition, the differential of the tensor product is determined by

d(p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|}p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{i-1}\otimes d^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}(p_{i})\otimes p_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m}.

Look at one-dimensional components of the moduli space
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
(see Definition
7.13
), whose oriented boundary is

\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m}\Big((-1)^{(\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|)(|p_{i}|-|q_{i})|}M_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\times M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\Big)\sqcup\Big((-1)^{{\rm dim}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}+1}M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\Big).

Moreover, each component above is nonempty only if both factors are zero-dimensional. It follows that (also using (
7.3
))

\sum_{q^{\prime}}\langle\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}(p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m}),q^{\prime}\rangle\langle d^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(q^{\prime}),p^{\prime}\rangle\\
=\sum_{i=1}^{m}(-1)^{\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|}\sum_{q_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\langle d^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}(p_{i}),q_{i}\rangle\langle\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}}(p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{i-1}\otimes q_{i}\otimes p_{i+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m}),p^{\prime}\rangle

which means exactly that
\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}}
is a chain map.
∎

Now we consider the situation of multimodule concatenations (see Subsection
6.5
). Let

{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}

be flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
which are oriented, graded, and Novikov. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
which is oriented, graded, and Novikov. Let
{\mathbb{Y}}
be a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
which is oriented, graded, and Novikov. Then one can consider the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
which is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Lemma
7.7
implies that the concatenation is Novikov. Lemma
7.14
says that the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is coherently oriented. Hence one obtains a chain map

\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}:C({\mathbb{G}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{G}}_{i-1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{G}}_{i+1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{G}}_{n})\to C({\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}).

Lemma 7.19
.

There holds

\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}(y_{1},\cdots,y_{i-1},x_{1},\cdots x_{m},y_{i+1},\cdots,y_{n})=\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{Y}}(y_{1},\cdots,y_{i-1},\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}),y_{i+1},\cdots,y_{n}).

Proof.

One only needs to verify this identity for
x_{\alpha}=p_{\alpha}
and
y_{\beta}=r_{\beta}
for generators of the involved chain complexes. Fix
r^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
. The
r^{\prime}
-coefficient of the left is the signed count of the disjoint union

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};r_{i}}^{\mathbb{X}}\times M_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}.

By our orientation conventions (note that the moduli spaces are zero-dimensional), it is equal to

n_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}n_{p^{\prime}r_{1}\cdots r_{n};r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{Y}}.

As only zero dimensional moduli spaces contribute, the sign above is positive; therefore, the induced count produces the right hand side.
∎

Lastly, suppose
{\mathbb{X}}_{0},{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
be two, unobstructed, oriented,
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded Novikov multimodules over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
of the same degree
n
, and
{\mathbb{H}}
is an oriented, unobstructed, Novikov homotopy from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. For each tuple
(p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime})
, the orientation on
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}
induces an integral count

n_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Define a linear map

\tilde{\Psi}^{\mathbb{H}}:C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}\cdots\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}_{m})\to C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})

by
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linearly extending

p_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes p_{m}\mapsto\sum_{p^{\prime}}n_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}p^{\prime}.

Using the conditions stated in Definition
7.15
, the following statement is immediate.

Lemma 7.20
.

\tilde{\Psi}^{\mathbb{H}}
extends to
C({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})
and is a chain homotopy from
\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}
to
\tilde{\Phi}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
.

7.3.
Symmetries on flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies

In the geometric situations considered in this paper, there are three types of symmetries on flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies. The first is the typical kind of Novikov symmetry, which is basically the symmetry related to changing cappings of contractible periodic orbits. The second and the third only appear in the equivariant case. The second type is the symmetry on the Borel model
S^{\infty}\cong E{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, given by the monoid
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, which shifts the coordinates, rather than the group
{\mathbb{Z}}
. The third is symmetry by a finite group
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
.

7.3.1.
Twisted Novikov groups

Definition 7.21

(Novikov groups)
.

(1)

A
Novikov group
consists of a free abelian group
\mathbb{\Pi}
together with morphisms

\displaystyle\ \lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k}):\mathbb{\Pi}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\

\displaystyle\ \mu:\mathbb{\Pi}\to 2{\mathbb{Z}}

A
morphism
of Novikov groups from
(\mathbb{\Pi},\lambda,\mu)
to
(\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
consists of a group homomorphism
\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
and a linear map
\sigma:{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k^{\prime}}
such that the following diagrams commute.

\textstyle{\mathbb{\Pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}^{k^{\prime}}}

\textstyle{\mathbb{\Pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm Id}
\textstyle{\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}}

(2)

The category of Novikov groups admits finite products, denoted by

(\mathbb{\Pi},\lambda,\mu)\oplus(\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})=(\mathbb{\Pi}\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime},\lambda\oplus\lambda^{\prime},\mu+\mu^{\prime}).

(3)

An
twisted Novikov group
, denoted by
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
, consists of a Novikov group
\mathbb{\Pi}
together with a finite group
G
acting on
\mathbb{\Pi}
by isomorphisms of Novikov groups. Notice that the semi-direct product
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
is a not necessarily abelian.

Example 7.22
.

(1)

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold. Then

\Pi=\frac{\pi_{2}(X)}{{\rm ker}\omega\cap{\rm ker}c_{1}}

together with the symplectic area
\lambda(a)=\omega(a)
and twice the Chern number
\mu(a)=2c_{1}(a)
is a Novikov group.

(2)

{\mathbb{Z}}
with the inclusion
\lambda:{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
and the multiplication by two
\mu:{\mathbb{Z}}\to 2{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
is a Novikov group.

(3)

Any finite group is a twisted Novikov group.

7.3.2.
Novikov rings

Let
R
be a commutative ring with unit, typically
{\mathbb{Z}}
or
{\bf F}_{\sf p}
. Let
(\mathbb{\Pi},\lambda,\mu)
be a Novikov group. We define the Novikov ring
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
as follows. Define

\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}:=\left\{f:\mathbb{\Pi}\to R\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}\forall\alpha=1,\ldots,k,\ \displaystyle\inf\{\lambda_{\alpha}(a)\ |\ f(a)\neq 0\}>-\infty,\\
\forall c>0,\ \#\big\{a\in\mathbb{\Pi}\ |\ f(a)\neq 0,\ \lambda_{\alpha}(a)<c,\ \alpha=1,\ldots,k\big\}<\infty\end{array}\right.\right\}.

(7.4)

Lemma 7.23
.

\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
is a graded unital commutative ring.

Proof.

We can grade
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
by dualizing the grading on
{\mathbb{\Pi}}
induced by the morphism
\mu:{\mathbb{\Pi}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
. The product structure is defined as follows. Choose
f^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
. Define formally
f^{\prime}f^{\prime\prime}:\mathbb{\Pi}\to R
by

f^{\prime}f^{\prime\prime}(a)=\sum_{a^{\prime}+a^{\prime\prime}=a}f^{\prime}(a^{\prime})f^{\prime\prime}(a^{\prime\prime}).

(7.5)

which is commutative in
f^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime\prime}
. We claim that this is a finite sum. If not, then there is a sequence
(a_{i}^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{\Pi}\times\mathbb{\Pi}
such that
a_{i}^{\prime}+a_{i}^{\prime\prime}=a
and
f^{\prime}(a_{i}^{\prime}),f^{\prime\prime}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\neq 0
. Then

\lambda_{\alpha}(a_{i}^{\prime})+\lambda_{\alpha}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\lambda_{\alpha}(a)

which is independent of
i
. Then

\sup_{i}\lambda_{\alpha}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\lambda_{\alpha}(a)-\inf_{i}\lambda_{\alpha}(a_{i}^{\prime})<\infty.

The finiteness condition in the definition of
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
shows that there are only finitely many distinct
a_{i}^{\prime\prime}
in this sequence. As
a_{i}^{\prime}=a-a_{i}^{\prime\prime}
, there are also only finitely many distinct
a_{i}^{\prime}
in this sequence. Hence the sum (
7.5
) is indeed finite. Therefore,
f^{\prime}f^{\prime\prime}
is defined. It is also straightforward to check that
f^{\prime}f^{\prime\prime}
satisfies the definition of
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
. Using this definition, one can easily see that the unit is given by the map which assigns the identity element of
{\mathbb{\Pi}}
to the multiplicative unit in
R
and
0
otherwise.
∎

We also define the subring

\Lambda_{R,0}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}:=\left\{f\in\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\ |\ f(a)\neq 0\Longrightarrow\lambda_{\alpha}(a_{i})\geq 0,\ \alpha=1,\ldots,k\right\}.

Now we consider the tensor product of Novikov rings. If
\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}
,
\mathbb{\Pi}_{2}
are two Novikov groups, then the Novikov ring

\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}_{2}}

is a certain completion of the tensor product
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}}\otimes_{R}\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{2}}
. We denote the completed tensor product by
\widehat{\otimes}
. More generally, if
\mathbb{\Pi}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}
are Novikov groups, then there is a natural identification

\Lambda^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}}_{R}\widehat{\underset{R}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{R}{\otimes}}\Lambda^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}}_{R}\cong\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}}.

Lastly, suppose one has a morphism of Novikov groups
\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
. Then there is a natural ring map

\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\to\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}}

making the latter an algebra over
\Lambda_{R}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
.

7.3.3.
Symmetries on flow categories

We introduce the concepts related to symmetries on flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies which appear everywhere in the Floer-theoretic setting.

Definition 7.24
.

Let
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
be a twisted Novikov group of rank
k
. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a (weak) flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
equipped with a filtration
{\mathcal{A}}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
. A
(free)
{\mathbb{\Pi}}\rtimes G
-action
on
{\mathbb{F}}
consists of a (free)
{\mathbb{\Pi}}\rtimes G
-action on
{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
together with
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-isomorphisms

T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}:M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to M_{ap\ aq}^{\mathbb{F}},\ \forall a\in{\mathbb{\Pi}}\rtimes G

such that

(1)

T_{0}^{\mathbb{F}}
is the identity morphism and for all
a,b\in\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
,

T_{a+b}^{\mathbb{F}}=T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}\circ T_{b}^{\mathbb{F}}.

(2)

For
a\in\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
and
p,q,r\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, denote
p^{\prime}=ap
,
r^{\prime}=ar
,
q^{\prime}=aq
. Then the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{prq}}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}\times T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}}
\textstyle{M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}}
\textstyle{M_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{p^{\prime}r^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{M_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{F}}}

(3)

For
a\in\mathbb{\Pi}\subseteq\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
and
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}(ap)={\mathcal{A}}(p)-\lambda(a)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k},\

\displaystyle\ {\rm deg}(ap)={\rm deg}(p)-\mu(a).

For
\theta\in G\subseteq\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
,

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}(\theta p)=\theta({\mathcal{A}}(p)),\

\displaystyle\ {\rm deg}(\theta p)={\rm deg}(\theta p).

(4)

Given
c>0
, the set of pairs

\Big\{(p,q)\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\ |\ p<q,{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\alpha}(q)<c\ \forall\alpha=1,\ldots,k\Big\}

consists of finitely many
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-orbits.

Remark 7.25
.

(1)

Notice that the last condition of Definition
7.24
implies that
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
is a locally finite flow category.

(2)

If
\mathbb{\Pi}_{i}
are Novikov groups and
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i})
are
\mathbb{\Pi}_{i}
-equivariant Novikov flow categories, then the direct product

({\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{A}}_{m})

is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}
-equivariant weak Novikov flow category. When all factors are equal to a
\mathbb{\Pi}
-equivariant Novikov flow category
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
, the power
({\mathbb{F}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{A}})
is
\mathbb{\Pi}^{m}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{m}
-equivariant where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{\Pi}^{m}
by permuting the factors.

7.3.4.
Symmetries on multimodules and homotopies

Definition 7.26
.

Let
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
and
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes G
be twisted Novikov groups. Let
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
resp.
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be a
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-equivariant resp.
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes G
-equivariant (weak) Novikov flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let

\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}

be a
G
-equivariant morphism of Novikov groups, which induces a group morphism

\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes G.

A
\rho
-equivariant (weak) Novikov bimodule
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is a (weak) bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
together with
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-isomorphisms

T_{a}^{\mathbb{B}}:M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\to M_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\ \forall a\in\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G

satisfying

(1)

T_{0}^{\mathbb{B}}
is the identity and for
a,b\in\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
,

T_{(a+b)}^{\mathbb{B}}=T_{a}^{\mathbb{B}}\circ T_{b}^{\mathbb{B}},

(2)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{q;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}\times T_{a}^{\mathbb{B}}}
\textstyle{M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{B}}}
\textstyle{M_{p;q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{B}}\times T_{\rho(a)}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{M_{ap;aq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}\times M_{aq;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}}
\textstyle{M_{ap;\rho(a)q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times M_{\rho(a)q^{\prime}\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(3)

For every constant
C>0
, the set

\left\{(p,p^{\prime})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}\neq\emptyset,\\
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(p)+\cdots+{\mathcal{A}}_{k}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})-\cdots-{\mathcal{A}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})<C\end{array}\right.\right\}

consists of finitely many
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-orbits.

The case of multimodules can be obtained from the case of weak bimodules.

Definition 7.27
.

Let
\mathbb{\Pi}_{i}
,
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
be Novikov groups. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{i}
-equivariant resp.
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
-equivariant flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let

\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}

be a morphism of Novikov groups. A
\rho
-equivariant multimodule
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
is a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
, which can also be viewed as a weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, together with
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-isomorphisms

T_{a}^{\mathbb{X}}:M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\to M_{a_{1}p_{1}\cdots a_{m}p_{m};\rho(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

for all
a=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})\in\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}
satisfying the conditions for an equivariant weak Novikov bimodules given in Definition
7.26
.

Remark 7.28
.

The last condition of Definition
7.26
implies that
{\mathbb{B}}
is locally finite (see Definition
7.5
).

Definition 7.29
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, and
\rho
be as in Definition
7.26
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
be two
\rho
-equivariant bimodules over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
.

(1)

A
\rho
-equivariant homotopy from
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
is a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
together with
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
-isomorphisms

T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}:M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\to M_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ \forall a\in\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G

satisfying 1)
T_{0}^{\mathbb{H}}
is the identity, 2)

T_{a+b}^{\mathbb{H}}=T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}\circ T_{b}^{\mathbb{H}},

and 3) the following diagrams commute.

\textstyle{{\mathbb{M}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{0}}\sqcup{\mathbb{M}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{0}}\sqcup T_{a}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{1}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{M}}_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{M}}_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{0}}\sqcup{\mathbb{M}}_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}}

\textstyle{M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{q;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{F}}\times T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}}
\textstyle{M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}}
\textstyle{M_{p;q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{a}^{\mathbb{H}}\times T_{\rho(a)}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{M_{ap\ aq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{aq;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{ap;\rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}}
\textstyle{M_{ap;\rho(a)q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\times M_{\rho(a)q^{\prime}\ \rho(a)p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

When
\mathbb{\Pi}=\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
and
\rho
is the identity, we simply say that
{\mathbb{H}}
is
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-equivariant.

(2)

Suppose in addition that
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes
and
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes
are twisted Novikov groups and
\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
is an equivariant morphism of Novikov groups. Suppose
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
are
\rho
-equivariant Novikov bimodules. Then a
\rho
-equivariant Novikov homotopy is a
\rho
-equivariant homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
which satisfies, in addition, that for all constants
C>0
, the set

\left\{(p,p^{\prime})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}M_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}\neq\emptyset,\\
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(p)+\cdots+{\mathcal{A}}_{k}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})-\cdots-{\mathcal{A}}_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})<C\end{array}\right.\right\}

consists of finitely many
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-orbits.

Note that incorporating
G
-symmetry for flow multimodules and bimodule homotopies is straightforward. We do not spell out the details because they are not needed for this paper.

7.4.
Algebraic structures with symmetry

In the previous two subsections, we provide separate treatments of convergence of structural maps of chain complexes and Novikov rings with geometric origin. As usual, in the concrete setting of Floer theory, the two structures interact with each other in the form that recapping of periodic orbits corresponds to multiplication by Novikov variables. Abstractly, equivariance under Novikov groups of flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies leads to linearity of differentials and chain maps under Novikov rings, and local finiteness of orbits under the Novikov group action is compatible with the convergence assumption. We record these classical observations here formally.

7.4.1.
Flow categories and bimodules

Suppose
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded, oriented, unobstructed, Novikov flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
, then we have obtained previously the complex
C({\mathbb{F}})
as a free abelian group.

Proposition 7.30
.

Let
(\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G,\lambda,\mu)
be a twisted Novikov group. If
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
is
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes
-equivariant, then
C({\mathbb{F}})
has the structure of a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-module with a
G
-action and the differential is
G
-equivariant and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-linear.

Proof.

The
\mathbb{\Pi}
-equivariance condition on
{\mathcal{A}}
implies that
C({\mathbb{F}})
has a natural
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-module structure. Moreover, the
\mathbb{\Pi}
-equivariance on the flow category implies that
d
is a
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-module map. In the presence of the finite group
G
, it follows from definition that the chain complex
C({\mathbb{F}})
is
G
-equivariant.
∎

The following proposition is straightforward.

Proposition 7.31
.

Let
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
and
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes G
be twisted Novikov groups with a
G
-equivariant morphism
\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
. Let
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathcal{A}})
resp.
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime})
be a
\mathbb{\Pi}\rtimes G
-equivariant resp.
\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}\rtimes G
-equivariant
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded, oriented, unobstructed flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}
be a
\rho
-equivariant graded, oriented, and unobstructed bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Then the chain map

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{B}}:C({\mathbb{F}})\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})

extends to a
\Lambda^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}^{\prime}}
-linear chain map

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{B}}:C({\mathbb{F}})\underset{\Lambda^{\mathbb{\Pi}}}{\otimes}\Lambda^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}^{\prime}}\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})

which is equivariant with respect to the
G
-actions.

7.4.2.
Multimodules and homotopies

Recall that by Proposition
7.18
, given a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded, oriented, unobstructed multimodule, and locally finite
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
, one has obtained a chain map

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}:C({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})

To obtain a chain map as modules over the Novikov ring, one needs to make use of the equivariance condition.

Proposition 7.32
.

Suppose
({\mathbb{F}}_{i},{\mathcal{A}}_{i}),i=1,\ldots,m
is equivariant with respect to the action of an (untwisted) Novikov group
(\mathbb{\Pi}_{i},\lambda_{i},\mu_{i})
. Then the tensor product

C({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C({\mathbb{F}}_{m})

is a differential graded module over

\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}}\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}}\cong\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}}.

Suppose
{\mathbb{X}}
is equivariant with respect to a Novikov group morphism
\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}\to\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}
, then the chain map
\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}
induces a
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}}
-linear chain map

\tilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{X}}:\left(C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}_{1})\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}}C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}_{m})\right)\underset{\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathbb{\Pi}_{m}}}{\otimes}\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}^{\prime}}\to C_{*}({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}).

In addition, if
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
are two such multimodules and there exists a
\rho
-equivariant unobstructed and oriented homotopy from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
, then the corresponding chain maps
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}
and
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
are homotopic as
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}^{\prime}}
-linear chain maps.

Proof.

It follows from the same reasoning as in Lemma
7.30
.
∎

Remark 7.33
.

There is a specific case corresponding to the equivariant pair-of-pants product which we would like to address in Section
24
and Section
26
in a more concrete setting rather than the current abstract setting.

7.5.
Quantitative theories

7.5.1.
Nonarchimedean linear algebra

We recall some basic notions about nonarchimedean linear algebra systematically used in
[
69
]
. Let
(\Pi,\lambda)
be an ungraded Novikov group; the relevant discussion is insensitive to
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading so we only consider a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded theory. Let
{\bf K}
be a field. Then on the Novikov field
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi}
there is the standard valuation

{\mathfrak{v}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}c_{i}T^{a_{i}}\right):=\inf\Big\{\lambda(a_{i})\ |\ c_{i}\neq 0\Big\}\in{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{+\infty\}.

Then
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi}
is a nonarchimedean field. Notice that in the setting of
[
69
]
one requires
\Pi\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
. We slightly relax this setting to better fit our discussion. To translate to the setting of
[
69
]
, denote

|\Pi|:=\lambda(\Pi)\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.6)

Then
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{|\Pi|}
is an algebra over
\Lambda_{{\bf K}}^{\Pi}
.

A
nonarchimedean normed vector space
of
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi}
(cf.
[
69
, Definition 2.2]
) is a pair
(C,\ell)
where
C
is a
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi}
-vector space and
\ell:C\to\{-\infty\}\cup{\mathbb{R}}
be a function satisfying

(1)

\ell(x)=-\infty\Longrightarrow x=0
.

(2)

\ell(\lambda x)=\ell(x)-{\mathfrak{v}}(\lambda)
.

(3)

\ell(x+y)\leq\max\{\ell(x),\ \ell(y)\}
.

There is a notion called
orthogonalizability
on nonarchimedean normed vector spaces (
[
69
, Definition 2.10]
) over
\Lambda_{{\bf K}}^{|\Pi|}
, which is satisfied by all concrete cases considered in Floer theory. Finally, Usher–Zhang defined the notion called
Floer-type complex
which is crucial in their abstract discussion on barcodes. As the consideration does not necessarily involve grading, we slightly modify the original definition.

Definition 7.34
.

(cf.
[
69
, Definition 4.1]
) Let
(\Pi,\lambda)
