. When the cylinder breaks off, we only quotient by the translation symmetry but not the rotation symmetry. This provides a compact moduli space

M_{pp^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},1}

and gives a homotopy from
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
to an intermediate bimodule, denoted by
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}\bullet\mathbb{F}}
, denoted by
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},1}
(see Figure
6
).

Figure 6.
The homotopy between
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
.

Next, we introduce a homotopy from
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}\bullet\mathbb{F}}
to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}\circ\mathbb{PF}}
.

First, given a nonnegative real number
\ell\geq 0
, we can consider the notion of
telephone
(see Figure
6
) of length
\ell
, which is a pearly-type configuration with two ends being solutions to a Floer equation while the surfaces in between are holomorphic cylinders connected by Morse flow segments of total length
\ell
. By compactifying the moduli space while allowing
\ell\to\infty
(meaning the Morse trajectories will break one or more times), one obtains moduli spaces

M_{pp^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},2}

providing a homotopy from
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}\bullet\mathbb{F}}
to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
, denoted by
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},2}
. Then define

\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},1}\cup\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},2}

which is a homotopy from
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
. We call it the
telephone homotopy
.

Remark 11.2
.

The homotopy
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},2}
is used to relate two different constructions of the Morse–Bott theory for the constant Hamiltonian
H=0
. The pearl flow category includes choosing a Morse function over the critical submanifolds and corresponds to the “cascades model” (see, e.g.,
[
16
]
) of Morse–Bott theory. In contrast, the natural output of the bimodule
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF},1}
corresponds to the “fiber product model” (see
[
8
]
for an early incarnation). The point of our construction is to compare the “Morse version” of Floer theory, coming from the flow category
\mathbb{F}
that is associated with a nondegenerate Hamiltonian, with the Morse–Bott version, which uses pearly trajectories, using the fiber product model as an intermediate gadget.

11.3.2.
Other concatenations along Pearly flow categories

We can also construct a homotopy from
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
to the concatenation
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathbb{MP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PF}}
and a homotopy from
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
to the concatenation
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PM}}
, denoted by
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{FP}\tilde{\circ}\mathbb{PM}}
. Since the two cases are dual to each other, we only explain the definition of the first homotopy.

Consider the bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
given by the collection of moduli spaces

M_{xp}^{\mathbb{MF}}

which is the natural compactification of the space of solutions to the Floer equation on the infinite cylinder with a Hamiltonian connection
\sigma
which is zero near
-\infty
and which is
Hdt
near
+\infty
. We deform
\sigma
to a 1-parameter family
\sigma_{\tau}
,
\tau\in[0,1]
such that when
\tau\to 1
, the support of
\sigma_{\tau}
is pushed to
+\infty
. Then as
\tau=1
, one obtains a bimodule consisting of moduli spaces whose configurations are similar to the third picture of Figure
6
. Then one can continue the homotopy by inserting gradient segments of lengths which vary from
0
to
+\infty
. The very end of this homotopy is the concatenation
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
.

11.3.3.
Remarks on concatenation at the Morse flow category

One can also form the concatenation at the Morse flow category, for example,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
. In the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
, it is homotopic to the bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
, firstly by gluing broken Morse trajectories and secondly by gluing holomorphic curves. However, it is hard to regularize such a homotopy. In particular, when gluing holomorphic curves at a node, unlike the Pearly case, the gluing has a complex gluing parameter. This does not fit very well with our framework. This is the reason why we need to go through the Pearly flow category in order to prove the invertibility of the PSS and SSP maps.

12.
Main Theorems on AMS Lifts

In this section, we state the main results regarding regularizations of moduli spaces introduced in the previous section. The proof will be presented in the next part.

12.1.
Main statements for Floer flow categories

We state the main theorem about global charts of Floer flow categories and other types of flow categories. The constructions depend on many choices. Most of the dependence of the choices can be absorbed via the global chart construction of bimodules; however, the dependence of the outercollaring width and the choice of the so-called integral action (Definition
15.1
) cannot be absorbed easily. Hence in the statement, we emphasize the dependence of these two choices.

Theorem O
.

In Situation
Flow(i)
, given any outercollaring width
\epsilon>0
and a choice of integral action on
{\mathbb{F}}
(Definition
15.1
) on
{\mathbb{F}}
, the
\epsilon
-outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}
admits a set of lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
(Definition
3.12
) as a
\Pi
-equivariant Novikov flow category. Each individual lift in this set is called an
AMS lift
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral action. The AMS lifts admit coherent orientations in the sense of Definition
7.12
. Moreover, for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, the
\epsilon^{\prime}
-outercollaring of
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
is an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to the outercollaring width
\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}
and the same integral action.

Proof.

See Section
18
except for the last statement, which is a straightforward consequence of the constructions.
∎

Remark 12.1
.

In fact what we mean by an AMS lift does not only mean the resulting flow category
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
, but also all the choices made in the process of construction. When an follow-up construction depends on a given AMS lift, it means the construction
a priori
depends on the choices made for the AMS lift.

The (in)dependence on the choice of the integral action is characterized by the following theorem. Recall that for the Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
, there is a bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}})
associated to the continuation map from
(J,H)
to
(J,H)
with the trivial Floer data.

Proposition 12.2
.

In Situation
Flow(i)
, let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
be two AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to the same outercollaring width and two different integral actions, provided by Theorem
O
. Then there exists a class of lifts of the corresponding outercollaring of
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{2})
. Moreover, with respect to the coherent orientations on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
, the lift is coherently oriented.

Proof.

See Section
20
.
∎

The (in)dependence on other choices for the AMS lift is included in the theorem on multimodules (Theorem
P
).

12.2.
Multimodules and homotopies

Next, we state the main theorems concerning regularizing flow multimodules and homotopies.

First, we formulate the theorem on lifts for multimodules. Due to technical reasons, we must consider the dependence on a specific choice, namely, that of integral actions (Definition
15.1
and Definition
15.2
) in the construction.

Theorem P
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{X}}
is a multimodule associated a smooth Floer domain
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
with
m\geq 0
negative ends and
n\geq 0
positive ends satisfying

n\in\{0,1\},\ n=0\Longrightarrow m=2\ {\rm and}\ m=0\Longrightarrow n=1.

It is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
where
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
are Floer flow categories and
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
is a Floer flow category when
n=1
and the trivial flow category when
n=0
. When
m=0
and
n=1
,
{\mathbb{X}}
is a cigar bimodule over
({\mathbb{O}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
.

Choose a common outercollaring width and compatible integral actions on the involved Floer flow categories (see Definition
15.2
). Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be AMS lifts to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
of
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
subject to the fixed outercollaring width and these integral actions provided by Theorem
O
(when
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{O}}
, take
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{O}}
).

(1)

There exists a set of lifts of
{\mathbb{X}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
(called an AMS lift subject to the chosen integral actions), which is a multimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{k};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Moreover, with respect to the coherent orientations on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, the lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
is coherently oriented.

(2)

Any two AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{X}}
(subject to the same set of integral actions) are homotopic as multimodules enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
via a lift of the the trivial homotopy from the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}
to itself and the homotopy is coherently oriented.

Proof.

See Section
18
.
∎

Notice that Theorem
P
depends on choosing an integral action. For comparing different choices, we consider a particular homotopy. Recall that
\mathbb{\Delta}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}\mathbb{F}_{j}}
,
\mathbb{\Delta}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
are the diagonal bimodules. There is an obvious homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from the concatenation (as concatenations of weak bimodules)

\Big(\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}\mathbb{F}_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{m}\mathbb{F}_{m}}\Big)\circ\mathbb{X}\circ\mathbb{\Delta}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}

to
{\mathbb{X}}
by simultaneously gluing at both positive and negative ends. When
n=0
and
m=2
, we replace the above concatenation by

\Big(\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}\mathbb{F}_{1}}\times\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}\mathbb{F}_{2}}\Big)\circ\mathbb{X}.

Proposition 12.3
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
P
, fix an outercollaring width.

(1)

When
n=1
, let

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}_{j}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ s=0,1

be two compatible sets of integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(s)}
be an AMS lift subject to the fixed outercollaring width and one set of integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{j}\mathbb{F}_{j}}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(10)}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}
be the lift provided by Proposition
12.2
. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
as a homotopy from

\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}\mathbb{F}_{1}}\times\cdots\times\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{m}\mathbb{F}_{m}}\Big)\circ\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(10)}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}

to
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(0)}
.

(2)

When
n=0
,
m=2
, let

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{2}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ s=0,1

be two compatible pair of integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(s)}
be an AMS lift subject to the fixed outercollaring width and one set of integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{j}\mathbb{F}_{j}}
,
j=1,2
be the lift provided by Proposition
12.2
. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
as a homotopy from

\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}\mathbb{F}_{1}}\times\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{2}\mathbb{F}_{2}}\Big)\circ\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(1)}

to
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(0)}
.

Proof.

See Section
20
.
∎

Now we state the corresponding results for homotopies of multimodules from varying Floer data on smooth domains.

Theorem Q
.

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, fix an outercollaring width and a compatible set of integral actions on involved Floer flow categories
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be AMS lifts of these flow categories subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
be AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
subject to the same outercollaring width and the integral actions. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
, called an AMS lift subject to the outercollaring width and integral actions as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
.

Proof.

See Section
18
.
∎

To establish algebraic relation such as the associativity of pair-of-pants product, one needs to consider the homotopy of multimodules corresponding to gluing of Floer domains. Recall the following setup. Let
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
be a Floer domain with
k
negative ends and one positive end, defining a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
, and let
\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}
be a Floer domain with
n
negative ends and one positive ends defining a multimodule
{\mathbb{Y}}
over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
. Here the source and target flow categories are all Floer flow categories associated to the limiting Hamiltonians on the two Floer domains. Recall that the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{i-1},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{i+1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n};{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
.

Theorem 12.4
.

Fix a common outercollaring width. In Situation
Concatenation(i)
, choose integral actions on the involved flow categories which are compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{n},\tilde{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
be AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
respectively subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
be an AMS lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions provided by Theorem
P
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}
be their descendants to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
which, by the discussion in Subsection
10.4
, admit a natural concatenation
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}
. Then there exists a concatenation
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
(see Definition
6.13
) which is a lift of
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.
18
18

18

For the purpose of this paper, we do not need to prove that the concatenation
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
is well-defined up to homotopy.

Proof.

See Section
19
.
∎

On the other hand, let
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{S}{\mathbb{Y}}}
be the family of Floer domains obtained from gluing along positive end of
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
matched with the negative end of
\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}
, each of which defines a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{S}{\mathbb{Y}}
. Recall (see Subsection
11.2
) that there is a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
to
{\mathbb{W}}:={\mathbb{X}}\circ_{S_{0}}{\mathbb{Y}}
for
S_{0}\gg 0
.

Theorem R
.

Fix a common outercollaring width. In Situation
Glue(i)
, choose integral actions on involved flow categories which are compatible with respect to the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
. Suppose
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{l},\tilde{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
are AMS lifts of the involved Floer flow categories subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
be AMS lifts of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}
resp.
{\mathbb{Y}}
as multimodules over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{i})
resp. over
(\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{l};\tilde{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime})
subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
be a concatenation provided by Theorem
12.4
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{M}}
be an AMS lift of the multimodule associated to the smooth domain
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{S}{\mathbb{Y}}}
subject to the same outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions. Then there exists a class of lifts of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{M}}
. Each lift in this class is called an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{H}}
.

Proof.

See Section
19
.
∎

Remark 12.5
.

We do not discuss comparing non-homotopic choices in the constructions of lifts of homotopies. We only use homotopies to ensure well-defineness of operations induced by flow multimodules, so we stop our discussion on non-homotopic choices at multimodules.

12.3.
Bimodules and homotopies associated to PSS type maps

We state the main technical theorem towards the definition of the PSS and SSP maps (cf. Theorem
E
).

Theorem S
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be Floer flow category on
(X,\omega)
associated to a Hamiltonian
H
and a time-independent almost complex structure
J
. Let
{\mathbb{M}}
be a Morse flow category on
X
associated to a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
with the standard behavior near critical points. Let
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
be the Morse-to-Floer resp. Floer-to-Morse bimodule associated to certain Floer data on the infinite cylinder. Fix an outercollaring width.

(1)

Choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
which is compatible with respect to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp. to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
be an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral action. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
as a bimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}})
resp. over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}};{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}})
.

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{(s)}}
,
s=0,1
be two integral actions which are compatible with respect to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(s)}
be AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{(s)}}
and let
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(s)}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(s)}^{\mathbb{FM}}
be AMS lift of
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
resp.
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
subject to
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega_{(s)}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{FF}}
be the AMS lift provided by Proposition
12.2
. Then there exists a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(1)}^{\mathbb{MF}}
to the composition
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(0)}^{\mathbb{MF}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{FF}}
resp. there exists a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(0)}^{\mathbb{FM}}
to the composition
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{FF}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{(1)}^{\mathbb{FM}}
.

Proof.

See Subsection
21.5
.
∎

Theorem 12.6
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime},\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}
be another set of Floer flow category, Morse flow category, Morse-to-Floer and Floer-to-Morse as in Theorem
S
. Let
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FF}^{\prime}}
be a bimodule over
(\mathbb{F};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
associated to a Floer datum on the infinite cylinder. Let
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MM}^{\prime}}
be a bimodule over
(\mathbb{M};{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime})
associated to a one-parameter family of Morse data. Let
{\mathbb{H}}
be a homotopy from
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FF}^{\prime}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}}
to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MM^{\prime}}}
by gluing the Floer/Morse data and varying in a one-parameter family. Then there exist AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathbb{F}}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
and AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
(as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}};{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}})
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}{\mathbb{M}}^{\prime}}
(as a bimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{M}^{\prime};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FF}^{\prime}}
, and a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

Proof.

See Subsection
21.5
.
∎

12.3.1.
PSS maps and pearly flow category

Next, we consider the pearly flow category and its diagonal bimodule.

Theorem 12.7
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a compact symplectic manifold.

(1)

Let
{\mathbb{P}}
be the pearly flow category associated to a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
and a compatible almost complex structure
J
. Then upon choosing an integral symplectic form
\Omega
which is tamed by
J
, any outercollaring of
{\mathbb{P}}
admits a set of lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Each individual lift is called an
AMS lift
(subject to the outercollaring width and the integral symplectic form).

(2)

Let
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
be the diagonal bimodule over
({\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{P}})
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
be AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{P}}
subject to the same outercollaring width and an integral symplectic form. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0};\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1})
.

Proof.

See Section
21.1
.
∎

12.3.2.
The triangle of Morse, Floer and pearly flow categories

Recall that in Subsection
10.3
, besides the Morse-to-Floer and Floer-to-Morse bimodules, we also described a few other bimodules, namely the Pearly-to-Floer bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
, the Floer-to-Pearly bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}}
, the Pearly-to-Morse bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}}
, and the Morse-to-Pearly bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MP}}
. Moreover, one has the diagonal bimodules
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
(which is a special case for the continuation map bimodule),
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
, and
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{MM}}
(a deformation of the diagonal bimodule of the classical Morse flow category). These bimodules are related by a triangle which commutes up to homotopies of bimodules.

Theorem T
.

Fix a common outercollaring width and an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
compatible with all involved bimodules and homotopy. There exist the following objects.

(1)

An AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
of
{\mathbb{F}}
(subject to the outercollaring width and the integral action).

(2)

An AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
of
\mathbb{P}
(subject to the outercollaring width and the integral symplectic form).

(3)

Lifts of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MP}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PM}}
,
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
,
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
,
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{MM}}
, denoted by

\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MP}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{PP}},\ \tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{MM}}

which are bimodules over two of the three flow categories
\tilde{\mathbb{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}
.

Moreover, the following diagram commutes in the following sense.

\textstyle{{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{MM}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MP}}}
\textstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}}
\textstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{PP}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PF}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}}}

(12.1)

All triangles are commutative up to homotopy of bimodules enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
, except for triangles whose vertex of concatenation is
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}
.

Proof.

See Section
21.6
.
∎

Remark 12.8
.

As discussed in Section
11.3.3
, the regularization of homotopies related to gluing Morse trajectories does not fit well with the current framework.

13.
Proof of the Main Theorems

In this section we prove (most of) the main theorems using previously stated theorems on Kuranishi lifts of various flow categories, bimodules, homotopies and the theorem on FOP perturbations (Theorem
N
). The proofs are mostly formal consequences of the existence of the regularizations and coherent FOP perturbations thereon, following the general theme of Floer theory which turns bordifications of moduli spaces into algebraic structures.

13.1.
Proof of Theorem
A

Suppose we are in Situation
Flow(i)
. Theorem
O
provides, depending on a choice, an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{{\rm NC}}_{\rm rig}
together with a coherent orientation. As the Floer flow category is an unobstructed (Definition
7.1
) and
\Pi
-equivariant Novikov flow category (Definition
7.2
and Definition
7.24
), Theorem
N
provides a
\Pi
-equivariant lift, i.e., an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
, which is a flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm FOP}_{\rm rig}
. Then apply the forgetful functor (i.e., taking the closure of the isotropy-free locus of the Kuranishi section)

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm FOP}_{\rm rig}\to\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}},

one obtains a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. Using the coherent orientation and the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading by the Conley–Zehnder index, by Proposition
7.16
and Proposition
7.30
, one obtains a canonically associated chain complex

CF_{*}(H,J;\Xi):=C_{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}})

generated by objects of
\mathbb{F}
, which has the structure of a graded
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Pi}
-module. Here
\Xi
denotes all choices made in constructing the AMS lift and the FOP perturbation; in particular,
\Xi
includes the information of the outercollaring width and the integral action. We call this process the
basic construction for Floer complex
.

We prove that the Floer chain complex has a well-defined chain homotopy type. Let
\Xi_{(0)}
,
\Xi_{(1)}
be two different sets of choices, including outercollaring widths, integral actions, and choices made for the two AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(0)}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(1)}
and the FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{(0)}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{(1)}
.

Lemma 13.1
.

There exists a chain homotopy equivalence

\Psi_{(01)}:CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(0)})\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(1)})

whose chain homotopy type is well-defined.

Proof.

By the part of Theorem
O
about outercollaring width, one can reduce the comparison to the case that the outercollaring widths are equal. Then by Proposition
12.2
, there exists a lift of the outercollaring of the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(0)};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(1)})
. Then by Theorem
N
, the FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{(0)}
and
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{(1)}
can be extended to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}
. Then by Proposition
7.31
, one obtains the chain map

\Psi_{(01)}:CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(0)})\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(1)}).

Using the part of Proposition
12.2
stating that two lifts of
{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}
are homotopic and Theorem
N
, one can prove that the homotopy type of
\Psi_{(01)}
is well-defined.

It remains to prove that
\Psi_{(01)}
is a homotopy equivalence. Indeed, for each object
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, the bimodule moduli space
M_{p;p}^{\mathbb{FF}}
consists of a single point corresponding to the constant trajectory at
p
. This moduli space has no lower strata, hence coincides with its outercollaring. Moreover, it is transversely cut out, so the FOP perturbation can be chosen to be the same as the original Kuranishi section. We can then choose the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}
inductively such that we do not perturb for
M_{p;p}^{\mathbb{FF}}
. For such a choice of FOP perturbation, the count
n_{p;p}^{\mathbb{FF}}
needed to define the corresponding coefficient of the chain map is
1
. With respect to the natural identification of the chain groups
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(0)})\cong CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(1)})
,
\Psi_{(01)}
is a higher order perturbation of the identity in terms of
T
-valuation. Therefore,
\Psi_{(01)}
is an invertible chain map, hence a chain homotopy equivalence.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorem
A
.

13.2.
General construction of chain maps and homotopics

13.2.1.
Chain maps associated to smooth Floer domains

Suppose we are in Situation
Module(i)
. Choose an outercollaring width and a compatible set of integral actions

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{j}\to{\mathbb{Z}},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{Z}}.

Let
CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})
resp.
CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})
be Floer chain complexes associated to the Floer flow categories
{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, which depend on the choices
\Xi_{j}
resp.
\Xi^{\prime}
, including the information of the chosen outercollaring width, the integral actions, the construction of the AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
reps.
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, and FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
resp.
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then by Theorem
P
, there exists an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
of
{\mathbb{X}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral actions as a multimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. By Theorem
N
, the FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
and
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
can be extended to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
. Then by Proposition
7.31
, one obtains a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta):\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

where
\Theta
contains the information of all choices made in order to define such a chain map.

In practice, the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
could be either a bimodule needed for establishing the continuation map, the multimodule needed for defining the pair-of-pants product, or the multimodule for defining the Poincaré duality etc. We need to prove that it is well-defined up to homotopy. Again, one can reduce the comparison to the case when the outercollaring widths are the same.

Lemma 13.2
.

Fix the outercollaring width and the compatible set of integral actions on the involved flow categories. Let
CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})
resp.
CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})
be Floer chain complexes associated to these flow categories. Let

\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta_{0}),\ \Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{1}):\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

be two chain maps defined via two differet sets of choices made for two AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
and two FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. Then
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{0})
and
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{1})
are chain homotopic.

Proof.

Consider the trivial homotopy
\mathbb{H}
from
{\mathbb{X}}
to itself. The set of integral actions remain compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{H}}
. Therefore, by part (2) of Theorem
P
, there exists a homotopy
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
as a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
. By Theorem
N
, the FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
can be extended to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
. Then by Proposition
7.32
one obtains a chain homotopy between
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{0})
and
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{1})
.
∎

Next we compare different choices of integral actions.

Lemma 13.3
.

Fix an outercollaring width. Let

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega_{(s)}},\ s=0,1

be two compatible sets of integral actions. Let

CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j,(s)}),\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(s)}^{\prime})

be Floer complexes whose constructions are subject to one of the sets of compatible integral actions, including the construction of the AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{j,(s)}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{(s)}^{\prime}
and FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{j,(s)}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{(s)}^{\prime}
. Let

\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta_{(s)}):\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j,(s)})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(s)}^{\prime})

be the chain map defined via two different AMS constructions subject to the two sets of integral actions, including the AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(s)}
and the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{(s)}
.

Moreover, let

\Psi_{j,(01)}:CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j,(0)})\to CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j,(1)})

and

\Psi_{(10)}^{\prime}:CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(1)}^{\prime})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(0)}^{\prime})

be the chain maps induced from the continuation maps provided by Proposition
12.2
. Then there exists a chain homotopy between
\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta_{(0)})
and the composition

\Psi_{(10)}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta_{(1)})\circ\Big(\Psi_{1,(01)}\otimes\cdots\otimes\Psi_{m,(01)}\Big).

Proof.

This is essentially a consequence of Proposition
12.3
. Indeed, the assumption of this lemma can be plugged into the assumption of Proposition
12.3
. Then one obtains a homotopy
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(0)}
to the concatenation

\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}\mathbb{F}_{1}}\times\cdots\times\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}_{m}\mathbb{F}_{m}}\Big)\circ\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{(1)}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}_{(10)}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}.

Then by Theorem
N
, the existing FOP perturbations can be extended to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
. The conclusion then follows.
∎

13.2.2.
Homotopy

We prove the following basic chain-level consequence of homotopies of multimodules from varying Floer data.

Lemma 13.4
.

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, fix an outercollaring width and a compatible set of integral actions on involved Floer flow categories. Let

\displaystyle\ CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j}),\

\displaystyle\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

be Floer chain complexes provided by Theorem
A
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral actions. Let

\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}(\Theta_{0}),\ \Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}(\Theta_{1}):\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

be chain maps provided by the basic construction of chain maps. Then these two chain maps are homotopic.

Proof.

The construction of the Floer chain complexes involves choosing AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
and FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then the chain maps are constructed by choosing AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}

as multimodules over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, and FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
as multimodules over
(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Then by Theorem
Q
, there exists an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. Then by Theorem
N
, the existing FOP perturbations can be extended to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
. Then Proposition
7.32
implies that the chain maps are homotopic via a concrete homotopy from
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
.
∎

13.2.3.
Concatenation and gluing

Now we consider the situation of concatenation.

Lemma 13.5
.

In Situation
Glue(i)
, fix an outercollaring width and a 1-parameter family of lateral lines in the domains. Suppose

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\Omega},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}^{\Omega},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{n}}^{\Omega},{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}

are integral actions on the involved flow categories which are compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{X}}
,
{\mathbb{Y}}
,
{\mathbb{M}}
, and the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
. Subject to these choices, let

CF(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1}),\ldots,CF(H_{m},J_{m},\Xi_{m})

and

CF(G_{1},I_{1},\Lambda_{1}),\ldots,CF(G_{n},I_{n},\Lambda_{n}),CF(G^{\prime},I^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime})

be Floer chain complexes provided by Theorem
A
. Let

\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}:\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF_{*}(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})\to CF_{*}(G_{i},I_{i},\Lambda_{i}),

\Phi^{\mathbb{Y}}:\bigotimes_{1\leq l\leq n}CF(G_{l},I_{l},\Lambda_{l})\to CF(G^{\prime},I^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}),

and

\Phi^{{\mathbb{M}}}:\bigotimes_{l<i}CF(G_{l},I_{l},\Lambda_{l})\otimes\bigotimes_{1\leq j\leq m}CF(H_{j},J_{j},\Xi_{j})\otimes\bigotimes_{l>i}CF(G_{l},I_{l},\Lambda_{l})\to CF(G^{\prime},I^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime})

be chain maps provided by the general construction of Floer chain maps. Then the last chain map is homotopic to the composition

(y_{1},\cdots,y_{i-1},x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},y_{i+1},\ldots,y_{n})\mapsto\Phi^{{\mathbb{Y}}}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{i-1},\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}),y_{i+1},\ldots,y_{n}).

Proof.

Subject to the integral actions and outercollaring widths, the construction of the Floer chain complexes depends on the AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{G}}_{n},\tilde{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
and FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
and
\mathring{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{\mathbb{G}}_{n},\mathring{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
. The chain maps
\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}
,
\Phi^{\mathbb{Y}}
, and
\Phi^{\mathbb{M}}
also depend on corresponding AMS lifts and FOP perturbations. One can also choose concatenations
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
and
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\mathring{\mathbb{Y}}
(provided by Theorem
12.4
and Proposition
8.6
).
Then by Theorem
R
, there exists a lift
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{M}}
. By Theorem
N
, one can find an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{M}}
. Then one obtains the desired chain homotopy.
∎

13.3.
Proof of Theorem
B

Assume we are in Situation
Flow(i)
. Above, we have defined the Floer chain complex

CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)

which appears to depend on the outercollaring width, the integral action, choices made for constructing the AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
, and the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
. We have already proved (see Lemma
13.1
) that the chain homotopy type only depends on
(H,J)
, but not on the system of choices
\Xi
. It remains to construct the continuation map.

13.3.1.
Definition of the continuation map

Let
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be another Floer flow category associated to another pair
(H^{\prime},J^{\prime})
. Choose a 1-parameter family of Floer data interpolating between
(H,J)
and
(H^{\prime},J^{\prime})
, resulting in a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
from
{\mathbb{F}}
to
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then we are in a special case of Situation
Module(i)
. Then, using the above construction, upon choosing an outercollaring width and a pair of integral actions on
{\mathbb{F}}
and
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{B}}
, after AMS constructions of
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}
, and choosing FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
,
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, and
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}
, one obtains a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{B}}(\Theta):CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

whose homotopy class only depends on the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
and the choices of the integral actions (see Lemma
13.2
).

13.3.2.
Independence of the integral actions

We first would like to reduce the dependence to
{\mathbb{B}}
but not the integral actions. Indeed, if
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega_{(s)}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega_{(s)}}
,
s=0,1
are two compatible pairs of integral actions, then by Lemma
13.3
, the diagram

\textstyle{CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(0)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}}(\Theta_{(0)})}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{(01)}^{\mathbb{F}}}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(0)}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi_{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}}(\Theta_{(1)})}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi_{(1)}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{(10)}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}}

commutes up to homotopy. Therefore, the homotopy class only depends on the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
.

13.3.3.
Independence of the interpolation of Floer data

Next we would like to prove the homotopy independence of the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}
. Suppose
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
are two such bimodules over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Since
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
are both defined via varying Floer data, one can construct a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{B}}_{1}
. Then by Proposition
15.4
, one can find a pair of integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}
compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{H}}
. This leads to the setup of Lemma
13.4
. Let

\displaystyle\ CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi),\

\displaystyle\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

be Floer chain complexes constructed subject to these integral actions and let

\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{0}}(\Theta_{0}),\ \Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{1}}(\Theta_{1}):CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

be continuation maps associated to these two bimodules constructed as above. Then by Lemma
13.4
, the two continuation maps are homotopic. This proves that the continuation map does not depend on the bimodule either.

13.3.4.
Composition of continuation maps

We prove that the composition of continuation maps is homotopic to another continuation map.

Proposition 13.6
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{F}}_{0},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
are Floer flow categories associated to three pairs
(H_{i},J_{i})
for
i=0,1,2
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}_{01}
resp.
{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
be a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{0};{\mathbb{F}}_{1})
resp. over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1};{\mathbb{F}}_{2})
associated to Floer data
\sigma_{01}
resp.
\sigma_{12}
on the infinite cylinder. Let
{\mathbb{H}}
be a homotopy associated to gluing
\sigma_{01}
and
\sigma_{12}
, as a homotopy from the composition
{\mathbb{B}}_{01}\circ{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
to a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}_{02}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{0};{\mathbb{F}}_{2})
associated to a Floer datum
\sigma_{02}
. Then there exist continuation maps

\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{01}}:CF_{*}(H_{0},J_{0},\Xi_{0})\to CF_{*}(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1})

\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{12}}:CF_{*}(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1})\to CF_{*}(H_{2},J_{2},\Xi_{2})

and

\Phi^{\mathbb{B}_{02}}:CF_{*}(H_{0},J_{0},\Xi_{0})\to CF_{*}(H_{2},J_{2},\Xi_{2})

such that the last one is homotopic to the composition
\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{12}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{B}_{01}}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
15.4
, we can choose integral actions on the three Floer categories which are compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{B}}_{01}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{02}
, and the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
. Then we are in the situation of Lemma
13.5
, which implies the desired homotopy.
∎

13.3.5.
Continuation maps are homotopy equivalences

Corollary 13.7
.

Any chain-level continuation map is a homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

Using the notations in Proposition
13.6
, take
{\mathbb{F}}_{2}={\mathbb{F}}_{0}
and denote
{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
by
{\mathbb{B}}_{10}
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}_{02}
be the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{0}\mathbb{F}_{0}}
associated to the trivial Floer datum on the cylinder. Choose a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
\mathbb{B}_{01}\circ\mathbb{B}_{10}
to
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{0}\mathbb{F}_{0}}
by gluing Floer data and varying in a one-parameter family, Proposition
13.6
provides a chain homotopy between the composition
\Phi_{01}\circ\Phi_{10}
and the chain map associated to an AMS lift of
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}_{0}\mathbb{F}_{0}}
. Notice that the latter has the form

\Phi^{{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{FF}}}={\rm Id}+{\rm higher\ order\ in\ }T

hence is an invertible chain map. Therefore, by a similar argument switching the roles of
\Phi_{01}
and
\Phi_{10}
, the continuation maps
\Phi_{01}
and
\Phi_{10}
are homotopy inverses to each other.
∎

Lastly, we prove

Corollary 13.8
.

The chain map from
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)
induced from an AMS lift of the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
is chain homotopic to the identity.

Proof.

The same argument of the proof of Lemma
13.1
also applies here, resulting in a chain map associated to an AMS lift of the bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
of the form

\Phi={\rm Id}+A.

Notice that
{\rm Id}
is also a chain map in this case. Now since
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}\circ\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
is homotopic to
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
, Proposition
13.6
implies that

({\rm Id}+A)^{2}-({\rm Id}+A)=A({\rm Id}+A)=d\rho+\rho d

for some linear map
\rho
. As
{\rm Id}+A
is an invertible chain map, one has

A=(d\rho+\rho d)({\rm Id}+A)^{-1}=d(\rho({\rm Id}+A)^{-1})+(\rho({\rm Id}+A)^{-1})d

meaning that
{\rm Id}+A
is chain homotopic to
{\rm Id}
.
∎

13.4.
Proof of Theorem
C

13.4.1.
Definition of the pair-of-pants products

The setting of Theorem
C
falls into Situation
Module(i)
. Let
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
be a smooth pair-of-pants with two negative ends and one positive end equipped with a Floer datum which are asymptotic to
H_{1}dt
and
H_{2}dt
on the negative ends and to
H^{\prime}dt
on the positive end. To simplify the argument, use a fixed almost complex structure
J
; more general cases can be treated with the help of continuation maps. Hence we omit the reference to almost complex structures. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be the Floer flow categories associated to
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
, and
H^{\prime}
respectively.

We fix an outercollaring width and will not mention it anymore in this subsection. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be the multimodule associated to
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
. Then by Proposition
15.4
, one can choose a triple of integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega},{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{2}}^{\Omega}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}
. Then one obtains Floer chain complexes

CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1}),CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2}),CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

which depend on the AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{2},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
subject to the integral actions and their FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{2},\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Upon choosing a pair of lateral lines in
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
, the basic construction provides a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta):CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2})\to CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})

which depends on an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
and an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}
.

13.4.2.
Well-defined homotopy class

Now we prove Part (1) of Theorem
C
. Similar to the case of continuation maps, by Lemma
13.2
, the homotopy class of
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta)
does not depend on choices made for constructing the AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}
and FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}
, so
a priori
only depends on the integral actions and the choices of lateral lines. These dependence is removed (up to homotopy) by Lemma
13.3
. Hence the homotopy class of
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}}(\Theta)

a priori
only depends on the Floer data defining
{\mathbb{X}}
. Lastly, if
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
are multimodules defined by two different Floer data on the pair-of-pants (including different cylindrical ends), then one can build a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
by varying the Floer data. Then Lemma
13.4
implies that the corresponding chain maps
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}(\Theta_{0})
and
\Phi^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}(\Theta_{1})
are also homotopic.

13.4.3.
Compatibility with the continuation maps

We only treat the case with composing continuation maps at the positive end, and the other cases follow from the same reasoning. Let
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime}
be a fourth Floer flow category associated to
(H^{\prime\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
. We assume that the almost complex structure is fixed and drop it from the notation; the general case when
J
is domain-dependent can be treated using continuation maps. Let
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}
be a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
provided by continuation map Floer data on the cylinder. Consider the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}
. Then by gluing the Floer domains, one obtains a homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{X}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}
to a multimodule
{\mathbb{Y}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
defined on the pair-of-pants with another Floer datum.

Then by Proposition
15.4
, one can choose integral actions on involved Floer flow categories which are compatible with respect to
\mathbb{X}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}
,
{\mathbb{Y}}
, and
{\mathbb{H}}
. Then we are in the situation of Lemma
13.5
, which provides specific chain complexes

CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1}),CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2}),CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime}),CF_{*}(H^{\prime\prime},\Xi^{\prime\prime})

chain maps

\begin{split}\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}:CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2})\to&\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime}),\\
\Phi^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}:CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})\to&\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime\prime},\Xi^{\prime\prime}),\\
\Phi^{\mathbb{Y}}:CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2})\to&\ CF_{*}(H^{\prime\prime},\Xi^{\prime\prime}).\end{split}

The Lemma
13.5
shows that
\Phi^{\mathbb{Y}}
is chain homotopic to the composition
\Phi^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{X}}
. This is Theorem
C
(2).

13.4.4.
Associativity

Associativity follows from Lemma
13.4
and
13.5
. In the notations of chain complexes, we drop the dependence on
J
.

Choose Hamiltonians
H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},H^{\prime}
,
H_{12}
,
H_{23}
independently. One can build a homotopy of two concatenated multimodules as shown in Figure
7
. One can choose integral actions on all involved Floer flow categories which are compatible with respect to this homotopy. Then apply Lemma
13.5
twice, one obtains the following diagram in which each triangle commutes up to homotopy.

\textstyle{CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{2},\Xi_{2})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{3},\Xi_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{23},\Xi_{23})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H_{12},\Xi_{12})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{3},\Xi_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{CF_{*}(H^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime})}

Here the diagonal arrow is provided by a multimodule with three source flow categories. As the homotopy types of continuation maps are well-defined, the associativity is proved. This is Theorem
C
(3).

Figure 7.
The homotopy between two concatenations.

13.4.5.
Identity element

There is a natural multiplicative identity of the pair-of-pants product coming from the cigar bimodule (see Subsection
10.3.6
). Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be the Floer flow category associated to a nondegenerate Hamiltonian
H
on the almost Kähler manifold
(X,\omega,J)
. On the other hand, let
{\mathbb{O}}
be the trivial flow category consisting of only one object. Then the cigar bimodule is a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{OF}}
associated to a Floer data on
{\mathbb{C}}
which is asymptotic to
Hdt
near
\infty
. Then this is a special case of Situation
Module(i)
.

By Proposition
15.4
, one can choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
on
{\mathbb{F}}
which is compatible with respect to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{OF}}
. Choose an outercollaring width. Let

CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)

be the associated Floer chain complex depending an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
subject to the outercollaring width, the integral action, and an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
. The basic construction for multimodules then provides a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{OF}}(\Theta):{\mathbb{Z}}\cong C_{*}({\mathbb{O}})\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)

which depends on an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{OF}}
and an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{OF}}
.

Following the same argument as before, one can prove that the homotopy class of the chain map
\Phi^{\mathbb{OF}}(\Theta)
is well-defined, independent of all choices as well as the Floer data responsible for the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{OF}}
. Moreover,
\Phi^{\mathbb{OF}}(\Theta)
is compatible with continuation maps up to homotopy. As the differential of
C_{*}(\mathbb{O})
is trivial, the image of
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
is closed and its homology class is well-defined. Therefore, one obtains a well-defined element

{\bf 1}\in HF_{*}(X,\omega;\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Pi}).

We prove that
{\bf 1}
is the multiplicative identity. This follows from a particular concatenation of multimodules and Lemma
13.5
. Consider the concatenation of the bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{OF}}
with a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
for pair-of-pants product. One can see that by gluing the Floer data, the concatenation is homotopic to a bimodule for continuation map
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}}
, hence a special case of Situation
Glue(i)
. Therefore, by following similar argument as previous situations where gluing is concerned, one can prove that
{\bf 1}
is a multiplicative identity. We omit the details. This is Theorem
C
(4) and we conclude the proof.

13.5.
Proof of Theorem
D

We establish the Floer-theoretic Poincaré duality. Consider the punctured Riemann sphere
\Sigma^{\rm PD}
which has two negative ends and no positive end. A Floer datum whose restrictions to the two negative ends are
(H_{i}dt,J_{i})
,
i=1,2
, leads to the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{PD}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2};\tilde{\mathbb{O}})
where
\tilde{\mathbb{O}}
is the
\Pi
-covering of the trivial flow category
{\mathbb{O}}
. In particular,
{\rm Ob}\tilde{\mathbb{O}}\cong\Pi
.

Now we define hte Poincaré pairing on the chain level. By Proposition
15.4
, one can choose integral actions on
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
,
{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm PD}
. Fix an outercollaring width. By Theorem
A
, one obtains chain complexes

CF_{*}(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1}),\ CF_{*}(H_{2},J_{2},\Xi_{2}).

Then the general construction of chain maps provided in Subsection
13.2
provides

\Phi^{\rm PD}:CF_{*}(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1})\otimes CF_{*}(H_{2},J_{2},\Xi_{2})\to C_{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{O}})\cong\Lambda^{\Pi}.

In the same way as proving independence of choices, one can see that
\Phi^{\rm PD}
has a well-defined homotopy type which does not depend on choices made for the AMS construction and FOP perturbations. It is also compatible with continuation maps, and compatible with the comparison between different choices of integral actions (here we do not need lateral lines). Hence the Poincaré pairing is well-defined.

We need to prove the compatibility between the Poincaré pairing and the pair-of-pants product. In fact this is similar to the proof of the associativity of the pair-of-pants product, using the concatenation of a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
(for the pair-of-pants products) and
{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm PD}
and a gluing construction, utilizing Lemma
13.4
and Lemma
13.5
. We omit the details.

Lastly, we prove the nondegeneracy of the Poincaré pairing with field coefficients. We only need to prove it on the homology level. We make use of the PSS map
\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}}
regarded as a map on homology. One can see that the composition

\Phi^{\rm PD}\circ(\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}}\otimes\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}})

is a bilinear pairing on
H_{*}(M;\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi})
. The pairing has a leading order term which can be identified with the classical Poincaré pairing on the integral homology of
X
. Hence the induced pairing is still nondegenerate on the Morse homology (tensoring with
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Pi}
). As the PSS map is an isomorphism on homology (Theorem
F
), it implies that the Poincaré pairing on the Floer homology is also nondegenerate. This finishes the proof of Theorem
D
.

13.6.
Proof of Theorem
E

The definition of the PSS and the SSP maps are simply special cases of general chain maps induced from bimodules. Fix a Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
on
(X,\omega)
associated to a pair
(H,J)
. To simplify the argument we assume
J
is time-independent; the general case can be treated with continuation maps. The same data provide the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
. Choose a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
on
M
. Then one has the Morse flow category
{\mathbb{M}}
associated to
(f,g)
. Choosing an interpolation between
H
and
0
, one obtains a Floer-to-Morse bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
; a backward interpolation provides a Morse-to-Floer bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
.

Fix a common outercollaring width. Choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
on
{\mathbb{F}}
which are compatible with all bimodules and homotopies involved in the discussion. Then Theorem
A
provides a Floer complex
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)
depending on choices, which involve an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral action, and an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
. Then by Theorem
S
, one can find AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
(which are enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
) of
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Notice that
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}
can be viewed as enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm FOP}
. Then the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
can be extended, by Theorem
N
, to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
and an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Then one obtains chain maps

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{MF}}:CM_{*}(f,g)\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi),\

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{FM}}:CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)\to CM_{*}(f,g).

We call them the
chain-level PSS map
and the
chain-level SSP map
.

Using similar argument as the case of continuation maps, based on part of Theorem
S
and Theorem
12.6
, one can show that the homotopy classes of the PSS and SSP maps are independent of the choices made for constructing the AMS lifts or the FOP lifts. Moreover, using homotopies realized by gluing Floer cylinders, one can show that the PSS and SSP maps are compatible with continuation maps. Namely, the composition of a chain-level PSS map resp. a chain-level SSP map with a chain-level continuation map (in the right way) is homotopic to a chain-level PSS resp. a chain-level SSP map. This finishes the proof of Theorem
E
.

13.7.
Proof of Theorem
F

To prove Theorem
F
, what remains to show is that the PSS and SSP maps are chain homotopy equivalences. We remind the reader again that the PSS and SSP maps are not known to be homotopy inverses to each other. To make the comparison, we need to go through the pearly flow category
\mathbb{P}
.

Keep the data fixed in the proof of Theorem
E
. Then the Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
and the almost complex structure
J
also determines the pearly flow category
\mathbb{P}
(see Subsection
9.3
). Meanwhile, one has the diagonal bimodules

\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}},\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{MM}},\ \mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}

and the Morse-to-pearly bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MP}}
, pearly-to-Morse bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PM}}
. Using the same Floer data on the infinite cylinder defining the bimodules
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
,
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
also provides the Floer-to-pearly bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}
and the pearly-to-Floer bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PF}}
. Moreover, one can build homotopies realizing the commutativity-up-to-homotopy of each triangle of the diagram (
11.1
).

Fix an outercollaring width. Choose an integral action on
{\mathbb{F}}
which is compatible with respect to all involved bimodules and homotopies. Then upon making choices, one obtains the Floer chain complex
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)
by Theorem
A
and the PSS and SSP maps as in the proof of Theorem
E
.

Moreover, by Theorem
12.7
, upon making choices, one can construct an AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{P}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
. By choosing an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{P}}
(granted by Theorem
N
), one obtains a chain complex

CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta)

where
\Theta
represents all the choices, including the outercollaring width, the integral symplectic form contained in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
, the choices made for constructing
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
, and the FOP perturbation. Then Theorem
12.7
also provides a lift
\tilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{PP}}
of the outercollaring of
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{P}};\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
. Then Theorem
N
allows us to extend the FOP perturbations
\mathring{\mathbb{P}}
to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{PP}}
. Then one obtains a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{PP}}:CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta)\to CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta).

Based on the above constructions and choices, Theorem
T
guarantees the existence of chain maps

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}:CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)\to CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta),\

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}:CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta)\to CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi).

and

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{PM}}:CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta)\to CM_{*}(f,g)\otimes\Lambda^{\Pi},\

\displaystyle\ \Phi^{\mathbb{MP}}:CM_{*}(f,g)\otimes\Lambda^{\Pi}\to CP_{*}(f,g,J,\Theta).

Lemma 13.9
.

\Phi^{\mathbb{FM}}
is chain homotopic to
\Phi^{\mathbb{PM}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}}
is chain homotopic to
\Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{MP}}
.

Proof.

One has fixed a specific homotopy
\mathbb{H}
from the concatenation
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{PM}}
to
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. By Theorem
12.4
, there exists a concatenation
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Then by Theorem
19.5
, there exists a lift
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a homotopy from
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}}
to
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Moreover, Proposition
8.6
allows us to have a concatenation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{PM}}
. Then Theorem
N
allows us to extend this concatenation and the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
. Then one obtains a chain homotopy between
\Phi^{\mathbb{FM}}
to
\Phi^{\mathbb{PM}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}
. The other chain homotopy can be established in the same way.
∎

Lemma 13.10
.

\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}
are invertible after passing to homology.

Proof.

Similar to the above lemma, by Theorem
T
, using FOP perturbations, one obtains a chain homotopy between
\Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}
and the chain-level continuation map associated to the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
, which is invertible after passing to homology. Via the reversed gluing along the Floer flow category, one obtains a chain homotopy between
\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}\circ\Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}
and the chain map
\Phi^{\mathbb{PP}}
associated to the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
, which is also invertible. Hence
\Phi^{\mathbb{PF}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{FP}}
are both invertible after passing to homology.
∎

Lemma 13.11
.

\Phi^{\mathbb{PM}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{MP}}
are invertible chain maps.

Proof.

Notice that the objects of
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{M}
are identical. Moreover, both
\Phi^{\mathbb{PM}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{MP}}
have leading order term being the identity map. Hence both these two chain maps are invertible.
∎

Now by the previous three lemmas,
\Phi^{\mathbb{FM}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}}
are chain homotopic to invertible chain maps. Hence they are chain homotopy equivalences. This finishes the proof of Theorem
F
.

13.8.
Proof of Theorem
G

By Hofer–Salamon
[
35
]
and Ono
[
50
]
(see also
[
23
]
and
[
46
]
), one can choose a time-independent
J
and suitable nondegenerate
H
satisfying

(1)

All simple nonconstant
J
-holomorphic sphere
u:S^{2}\to X
is transverse.

(2)

When
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)\leq 1
,
M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
is transverse and consists of stable Floer trajectories without sphere bubbles.

(3)

When
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)=0
,
M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
consists of maps defined over smooth cylinders.

To proceed, one needs to carefully make choices when constructing the AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
, especially the smooth structure. Choose an outercollaring width and an integral action on
{\mathbb{F}}
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
be a relatively NC AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Let
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq}/B_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})
be the corresponding relative Kuranishi space.

Notice that for pair
p<q
, there is an open subset

\mathring{M}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\subseteq M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

corresponding to smooth Floer trajectories. By the above assumption on
H
and
J
, we see that when
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)\leq 0
,
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}=\mathring{M}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. There is also the corresponding open subset

\mathring{V}_{pq}/\mathring{B}_{pq}.

We know that
\mathring{V}_{pq}
has a canonical smooth structure such that the map
\mathring{V}_{pq}\to\mathring{B}_{pq}
is a smooth submersion. Moreover,
\mathring{S}_{pq}:=S_{pq}|_{\mathring{V}_{pq}}
is smooth.

Recall that the stable smoothing of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
should be given by a
G_{pq}
-smoothing on stabilizations
\hat{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times W_{pq}
where
W_{pq}
is a linear representation of
G_{pq}
. The open set
\mathring{V}_{pq}\times W_{pq}
has a canonical smooth structure.

Lemma 13.12
.

There exists a stable smoothing
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}
of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
satisfying the following conditions. If
p<q
and
{\rm deg}(p)\leq{\rm deg}(q)
, then
\hat{V}_{pq}
is equivariantly diffeomorphic to
\mathring{V}_{pq}\times W_{pq}
near
S_{pq}^{-1}(0)
. In particular, the stabilization
\hat{S}_{pq}
is smooth near its zero locus and transverse in the classical sense.

Proof.

This is because the smoothing can be done locally and extend (cf.
[
14
, Theorem B.3]
).
∎

Now consider the AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}
. In order to define the Floer chain complex using FOP transverse perturbations, one needs to construct the perturbations inductively.

Lemma 13.13
.

There exists an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
of
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
satisfying the following conditions. Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
be the FOP transverse perturbation. If
p<q
and
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)\leq 0
, then
({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)=({\mathcal{S}}_{pq})^{-1}(0)
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
agrees with
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
near the zero locus.

Proof.

For the proof, except for the conditions listed in the statement, we further ask that for any
p<q
such that
{\rm deg}(p)<{\rm deg}(q)
, the FOP transverse perturbation satisfies
\|{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}-{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}
. We consider the inductive procedure provided in the proof of Theorem
N
. Consider a pair
p<q
. Let
{\mathcal{D}}_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq})
be the normally complex derived orbifold obtained from the AMS construction.

For the base case of the induction argument, we assume that
(p,q)
is minimal, i.e.,
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
does not contain broken trajectories. Then we can choose an FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
independent of other pairs. In this case, if
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)<0
, note that
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
is nowhere vanishing. Using the
C^{0}
-density of FOP sections (cf. Theorem
8.2
), via a partition-of-unity argument, we can choose a smooth FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}
such that
\|{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}-{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}
. Then, in particular,
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
is nowhere vanishing. If
{\rm deg}(p)={\rm deg}(q)
, then
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
vanishes at finitely many points which correspond to smooth solutions to the Floer equation. In this case, our stable smoothing for such Kuranishi spaces carry the original smooth structure (see Lemma
13.12
), the original section
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
is smooth near the zero locus and transverse in the classical sense. Moreover,
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is a manifold near its zero locus. Hence
(Classical Transversality)
condition of Theorem
8.2
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
is also FOP transverse near the zero loucs. Hence one can choose
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
to agree with
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
near its zero locus without creating more zeroes. If
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)\geq 1
, we choose an arbitrary FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
.

Inductively, suppose we have chosen FOP transverse perturbations
{\mathcal{S}}_{rs}^{\prime}
satisfying our requirement for all pairs
r,s
such that
{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(r)-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(s)<{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)
. To continue the induction, we analyze the induced perturbation near the boundary strata of
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
. We first consider the case when
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)<0
. For any triple
p<r<q
, one of the factors of
M_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times M_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}
has negative virtual dimension. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(r)<0
. By the inductive hypothesis, we know that
\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}-{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}\|_{C^{0}}
. By the compatibility requirement, the putative perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
is obtained from
{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{rq}^{\prime}
via a stabilization, which implies that the condition
\|{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}-{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}
is already met near the boundary stratum
\partial^{prq}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
. Because this induced section is nowhere vanishing, it is FOP transverse. Then we can apply the
(Extension Property)
of Theorem
8.2
together with the
C^{0}
-density of FOP transverse perturbations to construct
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}
which argees with the induced section near
\partial^{prq}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
with
\|{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}-{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}\|_{C^{0}}
. In particular, we see that
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}
is nowhere vanishing.

As for the case
{\rm deg}(p)={\rm deg}(q)
, if we have
p<r<q
, we can similarly analyze the induced section near
\partial^{prq}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
to see that it is nowhere vanishing near
\partial^{prq}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
. As for the interior part of
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
, similar to the above discussion, we know that the original section
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
vanishes at finitely many points, near which the Kuranishi spaces carry the original smooth structure with respect to which
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
is smooth, thereby being an FOP transverse section. So we can choose the perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}
to agree with
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
near
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}_{pq}(0)
and to satisfy the inductive compatibility near the boundary strata such that
({\mathcal{S}})^{-1}_{pq}(0)=({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}_{pq}(0)
.

Finally, if
{\rm deg}(p)-{\rm deg}(q)\geq 1
, we choose
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}_{pq}
such that it satisfies the compatibility condition near the boundary strata as in the proof of Theorem
N
. This finishes the inductive step and we conclude the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
G
.

By Lemma
13.13
, there exists an FOP transverse perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
such that the associated counts (with respect to the natural orientations) of zeros coincides with the counts of zero-dimensional moduli spaces using the classical method. Then the chain complexes defined using the FOP perturbation agrees with the classical one.
∎

13.9.
Proof of Theorem
L

We verify each item of Theorem
L
. In fact the verification is similar to the case of Hamiltonian Floer spectral invariants defined via the Floer chain complex over
{\mathbb{Q}}
(see for example
[
26
]
). The argument here is a rather straightforward adaptation to the current setting.

(1)

The
(Spectrality)
follows from Usher’s abstract result (Theorem
7.39
). Notice that the argument requires that
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)
is a Floer–Novikov complex, which is proved in Proposition
7.38
.

(2)

The
(Shifting property)
follows from the fact that the multiplication by
a\in\Lambda_{R}^{\Pi}
is an isomorphism of chain complexes from
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)^{\leq\tau}
to
CF_{*}(H,J,\Xi)^{\leq\tau-\mathfrak{v}(a)}
.

(3)

The
(Lipschitz Continuity)
is a straightforward consequence of Theorem
K
(2), where we recall that the Hofer distance between Hamiltonians
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
is

\int_{0}^{1}\max_{x\in M}(H_{1}(x,t)-H_{2}(x,t))dt-\int_{0}^{1}\min_{x\in M}(H_{1}(x,t)-H_{2}(x,t))dt,

which is an upper bound for the threshold (cf. Definition
7.41
) of the continuation maps.

(4)

The
(Isotopy invariance)
following from the continuation map construction. Let
H_{1},H_{2}
induce the same Hamiltonian isotopy
\tilde{\phi}\in{\rm H}\widetilde{\rm am}(X,\omega)
and
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
be two
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure. Then there exists a Floer data on the infinite cylinder connecting
H_{1}dt
and
H_{2}dt
and a family of compatible almost complex structures connecting
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
. Let
\mathbb{F}_{1},\mathbb{F}_{2}
be the associated Floer flow categories. Then the Floer data described here defines a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
from
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
to
{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
. Theorem
P
together with the FOP perturbation provides a chain map

\Phi^{\mathbb{B}_{01}}:CF_{*}(H_{1},J_{1},\Xi_{1})\to CF_{*}(H_{2},J_{2},\Xi_{2}).

The Hamiltonian connection can be chosen to be flat precisely because the Hamiltonian isotopies induced from
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are isotopic. Hence the threshold of the chain map
\Phi^{\mathbb{B}_{01}}
(Definition
7.41
) is zero. The same is true for a continuation map in the reversed direction. Hence we obtain the equality between the spectral numbers.

(5)

For the
(Symplectic invariance)
property, fix a symplectomorphism
\rho:X\to X
. Let
J_{\rho}
and
H_{\rho}
be the pullback almost complex structure and Hamiltonian. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{\rho}
be the Floer flow category associated to
(H,J)
resp.
(H_{\rho},J_{\rho})
. Then
\rho
induces an isomorphism of Floer flow categories
\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}_{\rho}
. The the AMS+FOP construction for
{\mathbb{F}}
can be pulled back to
\mathbb{F}_{\rho}
, providing an isomorphic chain complex
CF_{*}(H_{\rho},J_{\rho},\Xi_{\rho})
. This isomorphism also preserves the symplectic action and the filtration on the chain complexes.

(6)

The
(Triangle inequality)
follows from the chain-level pair-of-pants product construction provided by Theorem
C
. Let
H_{1},H_{2}
be nondegenerate Hamiltonians generating
\tilde{\phi}_{1},\tilde{\phi}_{2}\in{\rm H}\widetilde{\rm am}(X,\omega)
such that
\tilde{\phi}_{1}\circ\tilde{\phi}_{2}
is also nondegenerate. Then there is a composition
H_{1}\natural H_{2}
generating the composition
\tilde{\phi}_{1}\circ\tilde{\phi}_{2}
. Moreover, on the pair-of-pants
\Sigma^{\rm pants}
, there exists a flat Hamiltonian connection
\sigma
which converges to
H_{1}dt
,
H_{2}dt
