on the two negative ends and converges to
H_{3}dt:=H_{1}\natural H_{2}dt
on the positive ends. Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be the corresponding multimodule, which is an equivariant Novikov multimodule. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
be the Floer flow categories associated to
H_{i}
. Then it is well-known that the curvature of
\sigma
gives the threshold of the Novikov multimodule (Definition
7.41
), and the curvature can be taken to be zero. Therefore, by Proposition
7.42
, one has

c_{{\mathbb{F}}_{3}}({\mathfrak{x}}_{1}\star{\mathfrak{x}}_{2})\leq c_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{x}}_{1})+c_{\mathbb{F}_{2}}({\mathfrak{x}}_{2}).

(7)

The
(Poincaré duality)
property follows from the chain-level construction of the Poincaré duality and the spectral invariant continuatity result (Proposition
7.42
). The argument also follows that of
[
51
, Lemma 5.1]
.

Fix an almost complex structure
J
and omit it from the argument from now on. Let
H
be a nondegenerate Hamiltonian on
X
and
{\mathbb{F}}
be the associated Floer flow category. Let
\overline{H}
be the reversal of
H
, which has an induced Floer flow category
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
. Then on the domain
\Sigma^{\mathbb{PD}}
which has two negative ends and no positive end, there exists a flat Hamiltonian connection
\sigma^{\mathbb{PD}}
which is
Hdt
on one end and
\overline{H}dt
on the other. Consider this specific Poincaré duality multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{PD}}
(over
({\mathbb{F}},\overline{{\mathbb{F}}};{\mathbb{O}})
). Notice that the involved moduli spaces are identical to moduli spaces for the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{F}{\mathbb{F}}}
.

Notice that
{\mathbb{O}}
contains a special object, or rather a subcategory
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{O}}}
corresponding to the trivial element of
\Pi
. Let
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\rm PD}
be the restriction to
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{O}}}
, which is a multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}},\overline{\mathbb{F}};\underline{\smash{\mathbb{O}}})
. Let

\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}:CF(H)\otimes CF(\overline{H})\to{\bf K}

be the chain-level pairing. Notice that
\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}
only comes from trivial Floer cylinders, hence is obviously a nondegenerate pairing (in both variables).

Now we start to prove Poincaré duality property of spectral invariants. Fix
x\in HF(X)
.

(a)

We first prove that

c_{H}(x)\geq-\inf\Big\{c_{\overline{H}}(y)\ |\ \Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0\Big\}

Indeed, the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\mathbb{PD}}
has a threshold
0
(see Definition
7.41
). So for any
y\in HF(X)
, if
\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0
, then there exist cycles
{\mathfrak{x}}
and
{\mathfrak{y}}
representing them such that the chain-level pairing between
{\mathfrak{x}}
and
{\mathfrak{y}}
is nonzero. Then by Proposition
7.42
, one has

{\mathcal{A}}_{H}({\mathfrak{x}})+{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{H}}({\mathfrak{y}})\geq 0.

As
{\mathcal{A}}_{H}({\mathfrak{x}})
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{H}}({\mathfrak{y}})
can approximate
c_{H}(x)
and
c_{\overline{H}}(y)
respectively, one has

\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0\Longrightarrow c_{H}(x)+c_{\overline{H}}(y)\geq 0.

Then one has

c_{H}(x)\geq\sup\Big\{-c_{\overline{H}}(y)\ |\ \Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)=0\Big\}.

(b)

We prove that for all
\epsilon>0
,

c_{H}(x)<\epsilon-\inf\Big\{c_{\overline{H}}(y)\ |\ \Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0\Big\}.

Choose
\epsilon
such that
\alpha:=\epsilon-c_{H}(x)\notin{\rm Spec}(\overline{H})
. By energy consideration, the restriction of the chain-level pairing
\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}
to

CF(H)^{<\beta}\otimes CF(\overline{H})^{<\gamma}\to{\bf K}

vanishes when
\beta+\gamma<0
. Hence one obtains a well-defined nondegenerate pairing

\Phi_{0,\alpha}^{\rm PD}:HF(H)^{>-\alpha}\otimes HF(\overline{H})^{<\alpha}\to{\bf K}.

As
-\alpha=c_{H}(x)-\epsilon<c_{H}(x)
,

x\notin{\rm Im}\Big(HF(H)^{<-\alpha}\to HF(X)\Big).

Therefore, the image of
x
under

HF(X)\to HF(H)^{>-\alpha}

denoted by
x_{\alpha}
, is nonzero. By the nondegeneracy of the pairing
\Phi_{0,\alpha}^{\rm PD}
, there exists
y_{\alpha}\in HF(\overline{H})^{<\alpha}
such that
\Phi_{0,\alpha}^{\rm PD}(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha})\neq 0
. Choose a cycle
{\mathfrak{x}}\in CF(H)^{\leq c_{H}(x)}
representing
x
and a cycle
{\mathfrak{y}}\in CF(\overline{H})^{<\alpha}
representing
y_{\alpha}
. Then the chain level pairing
\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}({\mathfrak{x}},{\mathfrak{y}})
is nonzero. As
{\mathfrak{x}}
is closed,
{\mathfrak{y}}
is not a boundary. Hence the map
HF(\overline{H})^{<\alpha}\to HF(X)
does not annihilate
y_{\alpha}
. Denote the image by
y
. Then
\Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0
. Then one has

c_{\overline{H}}(y)\leq\alpha=\epsilon-c_{H}(x)\Longrightarrow c_{H}(x)\leq\epsilon-c_{\overline{H}}(y)\leq\epsilon-\inf\Big\{c_{\overline{H}}(y)\ |\ \Phi_{0}^{\rm PD}(x,y)\neq 0\Big\}.

Combining (a) and (b), we conclude that Poincaré duality holds.

Part III
THE AMS CONSTRUCTION

Outline of Part 3

We go through the AMS construction of lifts of various geometric flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies to the category of (collared rigidified) smooth normally complex Kuranishi spaces.

14.
The Flow Categories, Multimodules, and Homotopies of Domains

The Abouzaid–McLean–Smith (AMS) construction of global charts for genus zero Gromov–Witten theory
[
3
]
uses certain auxiliary moduli spaces of rational curves in projective spaces. To extend such construction to the case of Floer moduli spaces, we consider an analogy, which is an auxiliary flow category. The final goal of this section is to describe a flow category enriched in an intermediate category. The discussion in this section and subsequent ones has certain overlap with
[
14
, Section 5]
, but we include these expositions to make the paper self-contained and various technical aspects more streamlined.

14.1.
Monotone flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies

14.1.1.
Monotone flow category

We introduce the technical notion of monotone flow categories to simplify the discussions.

We introduce the following normal poset used frequently in this paper. For each
d\geq 0
, define

A_{d}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big\{(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})\ |\ d_{i}>0,\ d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}=d\Big\},&\ d\neq 0,\\
\{(0)\},&\ d=0.\end{array}\right.

The depth function is the number
l
. There are also homogeneous inclusions of degree
1

A_{d}\times A_{d^{\prime}}\to A_{d+d^{\prime}}

(14.1)

defined by the natural concatenation.

Definition 14.1
.

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
be a regular stratification category. A
monotone flow category
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{f}}
, consists of for each
d\geq 0
an object
M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
together with morphisms

\iota_{dd^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}:M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\to M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

For
d=0
,
M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}
is a singleton in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
.

(2)

The associativity: for
d,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{d^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{d^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d+d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}}}

Remark 14.2
.

Notice that given a monotone flow category
\mathbb{f}
, one obtains an ordinary flow category, denoted by
{\mathbb{F}}
, whose object set is
{\mathbb{Z}}
. The partial order is the opposite of the natural one on
{\mathbb{Z}}
. The space of morphisms from
a
to
b
(with
a\geq b
) is

M_{ab}^{\mathbb{F}}=M_{a-b}^{\mathbb{f}}.

Moreover, the flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
has a free action by
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Remark 14.3
.

One can generalize the notion of monotone flow categories by replacing
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
with certain other abelian monoids. One option is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{k}
, where the
k=2
case will be used to prove Proposition
12.2
. The corresponding flow category has object set being
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
. The other is the effective cone

\Pi^{\rm eff}=\{a\in\Pi\ |\ \omega(a)>0\}\cup\{0\}

of a symplectic manifold. The corresponding flow category has object set being
\Pi
.

14.1.2.
Monotone flow multimodules and homotopies

Now we discuss the case of multimodules.

Definition 14.4
.

Let
\mathbb{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{f}_{m}
resp.
{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}
be monotone flow categories enriched in a regular stratification category
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. A
monotone flow multimodule
over
(\mathbb{f}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{f}_{m};\mathbb{f}^{\prime})
, denoted by
{\mathbb{x}}
, consists of a collection of objects
M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}
of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
indexed by
d\geq 0
(the underlying poset may not be
A_{d}
), and for
d_{j},d,d^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
morphisms in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

\iota_{d_{j};d}^{\mathbb{x}}:M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\to M_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{x}}\ \ \ \ {\rm resp.}\ \ \ \ \iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}:M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\to M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}.

They need to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

When
d_{j}=0
resp.
d^{\prime}=0
, the morphism
\iota_{d_{j};d}^{\mathbb{x}}
resp.
\iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}
are the canonical isomorphism.

(2)

When
d_{j}>0
resp.
d^{\prime}>0
, the morphism
\iota_{d_{j};d}^{\mathbb{x}}
resp.
\iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}
has underlying poset map being homogeneous of degree
1
.

(3)

For each
d_{j},d,d^{\prime}\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{d_{j};d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{d_{j}+d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}}

Hence we may combine the morphisms
\iota_{d_{j};d}^{\mathbb{x}}
and
\iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}
as one

M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{\xi}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\to M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}.

(4)

For
d_{j},e_{j},d,d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{e_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}+d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}+e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}+d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}+e^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}}

(5)

For
i\neq j
,
d_{i},d_{j}\geq 0
and
d\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{i}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{i}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{i}+d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{i}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{i}}\times M_{d_{j}+d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{i}+d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{x}}}

Notice that the monotone multimodule
{\mathbb{x}}
induces an ordinary multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
with

M_{a_{1}\cdots a_{m};a^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}M_{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{m}-a^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}},&\ a_{1}+\cdots+a_{m}\geq a^{\prime},\\
\emptyset,&\ {\rm otherwise}\end{array}\right.

and structural maps induced from structural maps of
{\mathbb{x}}
. Moreover, it is equivariant with respect to the natural group morphism

\underbrace{{\mathbb{Z}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{Z}}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}.

The case of homotopies is similar and stated as follows.

Definition 14.5
.

Let
{\mathbb{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{f}}_{m},{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}
be as in Definition
14.4
. Let
{\mathbb{x}}_{0}
,
{\mathbb{x}}_{1}
be two monotone multimodules over
({\mathbb{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{f}}_{m};{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime})
A
monotone homotopy
from
{\mathbb{x}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{x}}_{1}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{h}}
, consists of, for each
d\geq 0
, an object
M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}
of
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, a morphism

\iota_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{0}\to{\mathbb{h}}}\sqcup\iota_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{h}}}:M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{0}}\sqcup M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{1}}\to M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}

of codimension 1, and for each
d_{j}\geq 0
resp.
d^{\prime}\geq 0
, a morphism

\iota_{d_{j};d}^{\mathbb{h}}:M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}\to X_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{h}}\ \ \ \ {\rm resp.}\ \ \ \ \iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}:M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{f}^{\prime}}\to M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}

which satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

When
d_{j}=0
resp.
d^{\prime}=0
, the morphisms
\iota_{d_{j};d}^{{\mathbb{h}}}
resp.
\iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}
are the canonical isomorphisms.

(2)

When
d_{j}>0
resp.
d^{\prime}>0
, the morphisms
\iota_{d_{j};d}^{{\mathbb{h}}}
resp.
\iota_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}
have codimension 1.

(3)

For
d_{j},d,d^{\prime}\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{h}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{h}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{h}}}}

Hence there is a well-defined composition

M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\to M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}.

(4)

For
d_{j},d,d^{\prime}\geq 0
,
\alpha=0,1
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{\alpha}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{h}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{x}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}}

(5)

For
d_{j},e_{j},d,d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{e_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{h}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{h}}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}+d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{h}}}\times M_{d^{\prime}+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{f}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{e_{j}+d_{j}+d+d^{\prime}+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}}}

(6)

For
d_{i},d_{j}\geq 0
,
i\neq j
and
d\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{i}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{i}}\times M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{\mathbb{h}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{i}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{i}}\times M_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{h}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d_{i}+d}^{\mathbb{h}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{i}+d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{h}}}

Again, a monotone homotopy induces an ordinary homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
with

M_{a_{1}\cdots a_{m};a^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}M_{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{m}-a^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{h}},&\ a_{1}+\cdots+a_{m}\geq a^{\prime},\\
\emptyset,&\ {\rm otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

14.2.
The category of curves

The following definition may not be the most canonical one. However, as we will consider only very concrete objects, the
ad hoc
description suffices our purpose. We should remind the reader that the following definition goes beyond the algebro-geometric situation as the base
B
is typically only a stratified smooth manifold.

Definition 14.6
.

An
equivariant families of curves
is a triple
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
where
{\mathcal{G}}
is a (not necessarily reductive) complex Lie group containing a maximal compact subgroup
G\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}
,
B
and
C
are stratified smooth
{\mathcal{G}}
-manifolds, with a
{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant map
C\to B
, and the structure of a prestable genus zero complex curve on each fiber
C_{\phi}\subseteq C
. This triple needs to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For any
g\in{\mathcal{G}}
and
\phi\in B
, the map
g:C_{\phi}\to C_{g\phi}
is an isomorphism of complex curves.

(2)

Let
\mathring{C}\subseteq C
be the complement of fiberwise nodes. Then
\mathring{C}
is a stratified
{\mathcal{G}}
-manifold and the map
\mathring{C}\to B
is a submersion.

For each object
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
, we introduce

\displaystyle\ P_{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{G}}/G,\

\displaystyle\ Q_{\mathcal{C}}={\rm Lie}({\mathcal{G}})/{\rm Lie}G.

We can also add additional structures to an equivariant family of curves, such as a collection of marked points, which are precisely
{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant sections of
C\to B
avoiding nodes and each other. Another structure we will use all the time is the cylindrical ends around markings and nodes, which are choices of germs of cylindrical coordinates.

We define morphisms of the category of equivariant families of curves.

Definition 14.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
be an equivariant family of curves.

(1)

A
group enlargement
of
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is an equivariant family of curves
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{G}}^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime})
where
{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}
is a complex Lie group embedding which sends the compact group
G
into
G^{\prime}
with
B^{\prime}={\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{G}}B
and
C^{\prime}={\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{G}}C
. We denote the group enlargement as such by

{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{G}}{\mathcal{C}}.

(2)

A
strict morphism
from
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}=({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})
to
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}=({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})
, denoted by
\zeta_{21}:{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
, consists of a complex Lie group embedding
\zeta_{21}^{{\mathcal{G}}}:{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
which restricts to a group map
\zeta_{21}^{G}:G_{1}\to G_{2}
and an equivariant commutative diagram

\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{C}}
\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{B}}
\textstyle{B_{2}}

satisfying 1) the map
\zeta_{21}^{B}:B_{1}\to B_{2}
is a stratified smooth map; 2) the map
\zeta_{21}^{C}:C_{1}\to C_{2}
is continuous such that the restricted map
\mathring{C}_{1}\to\mathring{C}_{2}
is smooth and such that for each
\phi_{1}\in B_{1}
sent to
\phi_{2}\in B_{2}
, the induced map
\mathring{C}_{\phi_{1}}\to\mathring{C}_{\phi_{2}}
is an isomorphism (of complex curves together with extra structures such as germs of cylindrical ends and marked points). Notice that a strict morphism
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
induces a strict morphism from
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
into
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
.

(3)

A strict morphism is called a
strict embedding
if the induced map
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
is an isomorphism.

(4)

A
unitary conjugation
of
({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is a strict morphism induced from an element
g\in G
which lifts the conjugation action of
g
on
{\mathcal{G}}
.

(5)

An
embedding
of equivariant families of curves is a unitary conjugacy class of strict embeddings, where we declare that two strict embeddings
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
are equivalent if one can be taken to another via a unitary conjugation of
g\in G_{2}
.

(6)

The category of equivariant families of curves, denoted by
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
, is the category whose objects are equivariant families of curves and whose morphisms are embeddings.

Notice that the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
is monoidal: given two object
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}=({\mathcal{G}}_{i},B_{i},C_{i})
, the product
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
is
({\mathcal{G}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{1}\times B_{2},C_{1}\sqcup C_{2})
where the fiber of
C_{1}\sqcup C_{2}
over
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in B_{1}\times B_{2}
is the disjoint union
C_{\phi_{1}}\sqcup C_{\phi_{2}}
. The initial object, which is
\emptyset
-stratified (see Definition
2.10
), is the object with
{\mathcal{G}}
being the trivial group,
B
being the singleton, and
C
being empty.

Notice that the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
also allows outercollaring.

14.2.1.
Lateral lines

We introduce the notation of lateral lines, which are used for gluing domains of solutions to Floer equations in various flow-category constructions.

Definition 14.8
.

(1)

Let
\Sigma
be a smooth genus zero curve with cylindrical ends. A
lateral line
in
\Sigma
is a real curve
{\mathbb{R}}\cong\ell\subseteq\Sigma
satisfying: near
\pm\infty
,
\ell
is contained in a cylindrical end of
\Sigma
and in cylindrical coordinates, is parametrized by
(s,0)
. In particular, for domains of Floer trajectories, this amounts to an
{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit of the translation action on the cylinder.

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
be an equivariant family of curves equipped with marked points and cylindrical ends. A family of lateral lines is a collection of lateral lines
\ell_{\phi}\subseteq C_{\phi}
such that
\ell_{g\phi}=g(\ell_{\phi})
for all
\phi\in B
and
g\in{\mathcal{G}}
.

By abuse of notations, we use
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
to denote the category of equivariant family of curves equipped with cylindrical ends and lateral lines. It is easy to see that lateral lines can be concatenated (as we always require the angular coordinate to be
0\in S^{1}
), which also guarantees their existence by induction.

14.2.2.
Rigidifications

To fit into the construction of Kuranishi charts and FOP perturbations, we need to introduce the more refined category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
. Comparing to other “rigidified” morphisms in categories such as
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
or
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
, the difference between the domain and target of an embedding in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
is caused typically by the enlargement of the complex Lie group. So the rigidification becomes more specific.

Definition 14.9
.

Let
\zeta_{21}:({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})
be a strict embedding in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. A
rigidification
of
\zeta_{21}
consists of an orthogonal representation
Q_{21}
of
G_{1}
and
a germ of
\zeta_{21}^{G}
-equivariant maps

\theta_{21}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon}\to B_{2}

(where
G_{1}
acts diagonally on the domain) whose restriction to
B_{1}\times\{0\}
coincides with
\zeta_{21}^{B}
, such that the map

\begin{split}G_{2}\times_{G_{1}}(B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon})\to&\ B_{2},\\
[g_{2},(b_{1},\eta_{21})]\mapsto&\ g_{2}\theta_{21}(b_{1},\eta_{21})\end{split}

is a diffeomorphism onto an open neighborhood of the image of
\zeta_{21}^{B}
. A
strict rigidified embedding
is a strict embedding together with a rigidification.

Notice that the group embedding
\iota_{21}^{\mathcal{G}}:{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
induces a linear injection

Q_{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}\to Q_{{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}

and
Q_{21}
is necessarily isomorphic to its cokernel.

Each group element
g_{2}\in G_{2}
induces a strict rigidified embedding from
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
to itself.

Lemma 14.10
.

Strict rigidified embeddings can be composed.

Proof.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}=({\mathcal{G}}_{i},B_{i},C_{i})
,
i=1,2,3
are three equivariant families. Let
\zeta_{21}:{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
and
\zeta_{32}:{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{3}
be strict embeddings with rigidifications

\displaystyle\ \theta_{21}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2},\

\displaystyle\ \theta_{32}:B_{2}\times Q_{32}^{\epsilon}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{3}.

The composed rigidification consists of the representation
Q_{21}\oplus Q_{32}
, the neighborhood
Q_{21}^{\epsilon}\oplus Q_{32}^{\epsilon}
, and the map

\begin{split}\theta_{31}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon}\times Q_{32}^{\epsilon}\to&\ {\mathcal{G}}_{3},\\
(b_{1},\eta_{21},\eta_{32})\mapsto&\ \theta_{32}\Big(\theta_{21}(b_{1},\eta_{21}),\eta_{32}\Big).\end{split}

The property that the induced map is a diffeomorphism onto a neighborhood of
\zeta_{31}^{B}(B_{1})
is an obvious consequence of the corresponding properties for the two rigidified embeddings.
∎

Definition 14.11
.

\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
has morphisms being conjugacy classes of strict rigidified embeddings.

Notice that we can naturally define the collared version of
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
, denoted by

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}.

14.2.3.
Stable complex structure

We would like to define a category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}
of equivariant families of curves whose bases have equivariant stable complex structures.

Definition 14.12
.

A
stable complex structure
on an equivariant family of curves
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is a
G
-equivariant stable complex structure of the tangent bundle
TB
(see Definition
3.17
). Recall that it consists of a
{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant complex vector bundle
I\to B
and a
{\mathcal{G}}
-equivariant stable isomorphism

\tau:TB\overset{s}{\to}I.

To define morphisms of stably complex objects, note the following fact. Let
\zeta_{21}:({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})
be a strict embedding of equivariant families of curves. Notice that there are
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
-equivariant vector bundles

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}TB_{1},\

\displaystyle\ \pi^{*}T({\mathcal{G}}_{2}/{\mathcal{G}}_{1})

where
\pi:{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}B_{1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}/{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
temporarily denotes the natural projection.
Via
\zeta_{21}
, one can see that there is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
-equivariant bundle isomorphism

TB_{2}\cong\pi^{*}T({\mathcal{G}}_{2}/{\mathcal{G}}_{1})\oplus({\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}TB_{1})

(14.2)

where the first summand is complex. Hence a stable complex structure on
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
naturally induces a stable complex structure on
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
via the strict embedding
\zeta_{21}
. One can see that the induced one is invariant under unitary conjugations of strict embeddings. Hence one can make the following definition.

Definition 14.13
.

A strict embedding of equivariant families of curves with stably complex structures from
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1},I_{1},\tau_{1})
to
({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2},I_{2},\tau_{2})
is a strict embedding
\zeta_{21}
from
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})
to
({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})
such that the bundle isomorphism (
14.2
) respects the
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
-equivariant stable complex structures. The category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}
consists of objects being equivariant families of curves with stable complex structures whose morphisms are unitary conjugacy classes of strict embeddings.

One can also define rigidifications for strict embeddings in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Indeed, suppose

({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1},I_{1},\tau_{1}),\ ({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2},I_{2},\tau_{2})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}

and

\zeta_{21}:({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})

is a strict embedding. Suppose

\theta_{21}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}

is a rigidification of
\zeta_{21}
as a strict embedding in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. Consider the image

B_{2}^{\epsilon}:={\rm Im}(G_{2}\times_{G_{1}}(B_{1}\times Q_{21}^{\epsilon})\to B_{2})

which is a
G_{2}
-invariant open subset of
B_{2}
. There is a projection

B_{2}^{\epsilon}\to G_{2}\times_{G_{1}}B_{1}.

We say that the rigidification
\theta_{21}
respects the stable complex structure if over
B_{2}^{\epsilon}
, the stable complex structure on
B_{2}
is pulled back from its restriction to
G_{2}\times_{G_{1}}B_{1}
via the above projection. This provides a category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. One also has the corresponding collared version
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

14.3.
Construction of the domain flow categories

The purpose of the rest of this section is to construct monotone flow categories (Definition
14.1
) enriched in the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, as well as monotone flow bimodules and homotopies.

14.3.1.
Some Lie groups and homogeneous spaces

We will consider variants of
U(d)
and
GL(d)
. Inside
PGL(d+1)
, there are two complex subgroups

{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{-}:=PGL^{-}(d+1)=\left\{\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}1&*\\
0&*\end{array}\right]\in PGL(d+1)\right\}

and

{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{+}:=PGL^{+}(d+1)=\left\{\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}*&0\\
&1\end{array}\right]\in PGL(d+1)\right\}.

In this paper we will use exclusively the positive version
19
19

19

This preference is due to the fact that the domains of multimodules always have a unique output. However, for Floer flow categories, using the negative version is also possible.
, hence we simply denote
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}={\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{+}
. The unitary group
G_{d}:=U(d)
embeds into
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
by

U(d)\ni g\mapsto\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}g&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right]\in{\mathcal{G}}_{d}.

One needs to consider certain specific group embeddings for different
d
’s. The embedding

G_{d_{1}}\times G_{d_{2}}=U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\to G_{d_{1}+d_{2}}=U(d_{1}+d_{2})

is obvious. For the corresponding complex Lie group
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
, there are corresponding embeddings. For example, for
d_{1}=d_{2}=2
, the embedding
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
reads

\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{11}&a_{12}&0\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&0\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&1\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}b_{11}&b_{12}&0\\
b_{21}&b_{22}&0\\
b_{31}&b_{32}&1\end{array}\right]\right)\mapsto\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}a_{11}&a_{12}&0&0&0\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&0&0&0\\
0&0&b_{11}&b_{12}&0\\
0&0&b_{21}&b_{22}&0\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&b_{31}&b_{32}&1\end{array}\right].

We set up the following notations for later purposes. Denote

P_{d}:={\mathcal{G}}_{d}/G_{d}.

The tangent space at the identity coset is the quotient of Lie algebras

Q_{d}:={\mathfrak{g}}_{d}^{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{d}.

There are canonical
G_{d}
-invariant metrics on
Q_{d}
making the linear embeddings

Q_{d_{1}}\oplus Q_{d_{2}}\to Q_{d_{1}+d_{2}}

isometric. Denote

Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}:=Q_{d_{1}+d_{2}}/(Q_{d_{1}}\oplus Q_{d_{2}}).

(14.3)

14.3.2.
Moduli spaces of cylinders in projective spaces

For
d\geq 1
, we will describe an object
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d},C_{d})
in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. Consider the moduli space of holomorphic maps

u:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{d}

of degree
d
, modulo the translation in
{\mathbb{R}}
-direction (but not the
S^{1}
-rotation). Let

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)

be the natural compactification of this moduli space (cf.
[
14
, Definition 5.14]
for an explanation of this notation). Then the group
PU(d+1)
acts on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)
. Let

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{{\mathbb{R}},+}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)

be the subset satisfying the constraint:

u(z_{+})=[0,\ldots,0,1]

(14.4)

which is invariant under
G_{d}=U(d)
, where
z_{+}=\{\infty\}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\supseteq{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}
. Let

B_{d}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{{\mathbb{R}},+}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)

be the open subset of curves
u
whose image is not contained in any hyperplane of
\mathbb{P}^{d}
. It is easy to calculate the dimension of
B_{d}
, which is

{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}B_{d}=2d(d+1)-1={\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{G}}_{d}-1.

Further, let
C_{d}\to B_{d}
be the universal curve; notice that fibers also carry canonical germs of cylindrical ends.

The moduli space
B_{d}
is stratified by the poset
A_{d}
(cf. the notations introduced in Section
14.1.1
): for any
\tau=(d_{0}\cdots d_{r})\in A_{d}
, the stratum
\partial^{\tau}B_{d}
is the subspace of curves in
\mathbb{P}^{d}
which breaks into
r+1
stable cylinders such that the
i
-th one has degree
d_{i}
. Therefore, one obtains an object

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d},C_{d})

in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

Remark 14.14
.

There is a refinement of the above stratification, namely, the stratification by combinatorial types, and each stratum is preserved by the
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-action.

14.3.3.
Orientations

The smooth part of the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is a complex manifold, which is canonically oriented. The interior of
B_{d}
has a natural
S^{1}
-action by reparametrizing the cylinders so that the quotient
{\rm Int}B_{d}/S^{1}
is identified with an open subset of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. Hence we can orient
B_{d}
by regarding the infinitesimal
S^{1}
-direction as positively oriented.

14.3.4.
Concatenation of cylinders

We would like to define the structural maps, i.e., morphisms

\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{C}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}},\ d,d^{\prime}>0

in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
such that they satisfy associativity. In this way, one obtains a monotone flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

We describe the definition as follows. Given two points
\phi\in B_{d}
,
\phi^{\prime}\in B_{d^{\prime}}
represented by maps
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d}
,
u^{\prime}:\Sigma^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}}
, let
\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}
be the prestable cylinder obtained by identifying the output of
\Sigma
with the input
\Sigma^{\prime}
. The map
u
can be represented by sections

h_{0},\ldots,h_{d}\in H^{0}(\Sigma,u^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)).

We abbreviate
L_{u}:=u^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)
and extend it by trivial bundle to
\Sigma^{\prime}
. Then
h_{0},\ldots,h_{d}
are sections over
\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}
. Similarly, the map
u^{\prime}
can be represented by sections
h_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,h_{d^{\prime}}^{\prime}
of the pullback bundle
L_{u^{\prime}}\to\Sigma^{\prime}
which can be extended to the whole of
\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}
.

For each
\phi^{\prime}\in B_{d^{\prime}}
, we choose the lateral line, a (broken) real curve

\ell_{\phi^{\prime}}\subseteq C_{\phi^{\prime}}

(14.5)

which is parametrized by
(s,0)
in the cylindrical coordinates in all cylindrical components. Along
\ell_{\phi^{\prime}}
, the parallel transport with respect to the Chern connection on
L_{u^{\prime}}
sends the (nonzero) vector
h_{d^{\prime}}(z_{+})\in L_{u^{\prime}}|_{z_{+}}
to a nonzero vector
\tau_{u^{\prime}}\in L_{u^{\prime}}|_{z_{-}}
. Then consider
1+d+d^{\prime}
sections of
L_{u}\otimes L_{u^{\prime}}\to\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}
given by

h_{0}\otimes\tau_{u^{\prime}},\ldots,h_{d-1}\otimes\tau_{u^{\prime}},h_{d}\otimes h_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,h_{d}\otimes h_{d^{\prime}}.

It is easy to check that it is base-point-free, hence defines a map

(\phi\#\phi^{\prime}):\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{d+d^{\prime}}

which represents a point in
B_{d+d^{\prime}}
. Moreover, it is straightforward to see that the equivalence class of
u\#u^{\prime}
is independent of the choice of the representatives
u
and
u^{\prime}
. Therefore, one obtains a well-defined map

\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}^{B}:B_{d}\times B_{d^{\prime}}\to B_{d+d^{\prime}}.

Lemma 14.15
.

The map
\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}^{B}:B_{d}\times B_{d^{\prime}}\to B_{d+d^{\prime}}
is smooth and equivariant with respect to the group embedding

\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{G}}:{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{d+d^{\prime}},

and satisfying the associativity, i.e., the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}\times B_{d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{d_{1}+d_{2}}\times B_{d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}+d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{d_{1}+d_{2}+d_{3}}}

Furthermore, the maps
\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}^{B}
can be lifted canonically to a strict morphism

\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{C}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}

in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
such that the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}+d_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}+d_{3}}}

Proof.

All elements in the definition depend smoothly on the variables
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
. From the explicit definition of
u\#u^{\prime}
given above, the equivariance follows immediately. The associativity can also be checked directly. See
[
14
, §5.2.4]
for more details.
∎

We need to prove that the morphisms are strict embeddings into the corresponding stratum.

Proposition 14.16
.

The morphism
\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}
is a strict embedding into the stratum
\partial^{d,d^{\prime}}{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

What we need to show is that the induced strict morphism

{\mathcal{G}}_{d+d^{\prime}}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{\prime}}}\left({\mathcal{C}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\right)\to\partial^{d,d^{\prime}}{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}

is an isomorphism. Indeed, given any curve in
\partial^{d,d^{\prime}}B_{d+d^{\prime}}
, it is represented by a holomorphic map
u:\Sigma\#\Sigma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{d+d^{\prime}}
such that the restriction of
u
to
\Sigma
resp.
\Sigma^{\prime}
has degree
d
resp.
d^{\prime}
. After applying a transformation in
{\mathcal{G}}_{d+d^{\prime}}
, one can make
u(\Sigma)
be contained in the special
{\mathbb{P}}^{d}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}^{d+d^{\prime}}
with last
d^{\prime}
coordinates vanish, and make
u(\Sigma^{\prime})
be contained in a
{\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}^{d+d^{\prime}}
which is spanned by the last
d^{\prime}
coordinates as well as a line contained in the special
{\mathbb{P}}^{d}
. Such curves are exactly in the image of
\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}^{B}
.
∎

14.3.5.
Rigidification

Lastly we construct the rigidifications (see Definition
14.9
).

Proposition 14.17
.

The monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
by taking the outer-collaring of the monotone flow category which has objects
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
and morphisms
\zeta_{d,d^{\prime}}
admits a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
.

Proof.

We first explain why a single embedding
\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}:{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}}\to\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}
can be rigidified (before outer-collaring). (See Definition
14.9
) the representation of
G_{1}=G_{d_{1}}\times G_{d_{2}}
is
Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}
given by (
14.3
). Roughly,
Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}
is the normal direction to
G_{d_{1}+d_{2}}({\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{2}})
inside
{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}
. To be more specific, one can choose a
G_{d}
-invariant germ of maps

\theta_{d_{1},d_{2}}:Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}

sending
0
to the identity (such as the restriction of an exponential map) which is orthogonal to
G_{d_{1}+d_{2}}({\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{2}})
.

By the definition of embeddings in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
, there is an isomorphism

{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{2}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}})\to\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}.

Then the map

\begin{split}Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}\times(B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}})&\ \to\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}B_{d_{1}+d_{2}}\\
(v,x_{1},x_{2})&\ \mapsto\theta_{d_{1},d_{2}}(v)(\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}(x_{1},x_{2}))\end{split}

is a local diffeomorphism after equivariantize by
G_{d_{1}+d_{2}}
.

We need to choose rigidifications for all such embeddings so that they satisfy the requirement for flow categories. For this one can allow
\theta_{d_{1},d_{2}}
to depend on
(x_{1},x_{2})\in B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}
so
\theta_{d_{1},d_{2}}
is a germ of equivariant bundle maps

Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}\times B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}}\times B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}

over
B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}
. All such rigidifications of the embedding
\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}
are equivariantly homotopic. The collaring region allows us to connect different choices.
∎

We choose such a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
and still denote it by
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
. The effect of such a lift to later constructions needs to be addressed in the following way. Roughly, the resulting Kuranishi spaces of the AMS construction have symmetry groups being compact Lie groups
G
while the domains we constructed here have symmetry groups being corresponding complex Lie groups
{\mathcal{G}}
. This redundance will be removed by using a notion called
group reduction
(see Definition
15.7
), which later becomes part of the Kuranishi section.

Consider a strict embedding

\zeta_{21}:{\mathcal{C}}_{1}=({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}=({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{1})

of equivariant families of curves. Denote by

\zeta_{21}^{P}:P_{1}={\mathcal{G}}_{1}/G_{1}\to P_{2}={\mathcal{G}}_{2}/G_{2}

the induced embedding of homogeneous spaces and

\zeta_{21}^{Q}:Q_{1}={\mathfrak{g}}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}\to Q_{2}={\mathfrak{g}}_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}

the linearization of
\zeta_{21}^{P}
.

Lemma 14.18
.

Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
be the monotone flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
we constructed above. There exist a collection of germs of
G_{d}\times G_{d}
-equivariant maps

\lambda_{d}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times Q_{d}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times P_{d}

as bundle maps over
{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

For each
\phi\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}
, the restriction of
\lambda_{d}
to
\{\phi\}\times Q_{d}
is a local diffeomorphism onto an open neighborhood of
[1]\in P_{d}
.

(2)

\lambda_{d}
is collared, i.e.,
\lambda_{d}
is determined over the collar neighborhood by its restriction to the boundary strata via the projections.

(3)

For each decomposition
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\big({}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{1}}\times Q_{d_{1}}\big)\times\big({}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{2}}\times Q_{d_{2}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{d_{1}}\times\lambda_{d_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}\times\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{Q}}
\textstyle{\big({}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{1}}\times P_{d_{1}}\big)\times\big({}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{2}}\times P_{d_{2}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}\times\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{P}}
\textstyle{\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times Q_{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{d_{1}+d_{2}}}
\textstyle{\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times P_{d}}

(4)

For each decomposition
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
,
x_{1}\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{1}}
,
x_{2}\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{d_{2}}
,
q_{1}\in Q_{d_{1}}
,
q_{2}\in Q_{d_{2}}
, and
v\in Q_{d_{1},d_{2}}
with
|v|
sufficiently small, one has

\lambda_{d}(\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}(x_{1},x_{2}),(q_{1},q_{2},v))=(1,\theta_{d_{1},d_{2}}(x_{1},x_{2},v))\cdot\Big(\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}(x_{1},x_{2}),\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{P}(\lambda_{d_{1}}(x_{1},q_{1}),\lambda_{d_{2}}(x_{2},q_{2}))\Big)

(14.6)

where
\cdot
denots the
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-action on
B_{d}\times P_{d}
.

Proof.

The proof, which is similar to that of Proposition
14.17
, is based on induction and homotopies over the collar region. For
d=1
, choose an arbitrary
\lambda_{1}
which realizes the local diffeomorphism
Q_{1}
with
P_{1}
. The formula (
14.6
) allows us to define
\lambda_{d}
on boundary. The condition that
\lambda_{d}
is collared implies that the boundary values of
\lambda_{d}
determine its values near the boundary. One can then extend to the interior of
{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}
.
∎

14.3.6.
Stable complex structure

As the first step towards constructing the stable complex structure for the AMS construction, we have the following “natural” stable complex structure on the monotone flow category of curves.

Proposition 14.19
.

The monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{Dom}}
(enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
) admits a natural lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof.

Notice that the smooth part
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
has a
PGL(d+1)
-invariant complex structure. The constraint at the negative or the positive marking does not affect this complex structure. Notice that the interior of
B_{d}
has a projection map
B_{d}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
whose fibers are the
S^{1}
-rotations (which has no fixed point); moreover, the
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-action sends
S^{1}
-orbits to
S^{1}
-orbits. Hence we can define
I_{B_{d}}\to B_{d}
as the pullback of the tangent bundle of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
so that there is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-equivariant vector bundle isomorphism

TB_{d}\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}\cong I_{B_{d}}

where the trivial bundle
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}
is the subbundle coming from the
S^{1}
-orbits.

Now we consider the structural maps. Given
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 1
, recall one has the map

\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}:B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}\to\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}B_{d_{1}+d_{2}}.

The differential reads

TB_{d_{1}}\oplus TB_{d_{2}}\to T\partial^{d_{1},d_{2}}B_{d_{1}+d_{2}}.

On the other hand, one can see from the construction of
\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}
that it induces a complex analytic embedding

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{1}},d_{1})\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{2}},d_{2})\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{*}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{1}+d_{2}},d_{1}+d_{2}).

Therefore, the map
\zeta_{d_{1},d_{2}}^{B}
respects the corresponding stable complex structures.
∎

14.3.7.
Orientation and concatenation

Recall that each
B_{d}
is oriented in a canonical way. Hence for
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
, there is a product orientation on
B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}}
. As
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
is a complex Lie group,
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{2}}}(B_{d_{1}}\times B_{d_{2}})
has the induced product orientation. On the other hand, it is a codimension one boundary of
B_{d}
, hence has a corresponding boundary orientation.

Lemma 14.20
.

The product orientation and the boundary orientation coincide.

Proof.

We only consider the interior of the moduli spaces
B_{d_{1}}
and
B_{d_{2}}
. Recall that
B_{d_{1}}/S^{1}
and
B_{d_{2}}/S^{1}
are open subsets of complex manifolds. There are also orientation-preserving gluing maps

B_{d_{1}}/S^{1}\times\Delta\times B_{d_{2}}/S^{1}\to B_{d}/S^{1}

where
\Delta
is a small disk of gluing parameters. By explicitly writing down the gluing map in terms of representative and the effect of reparametrizations, one can see that the induced map

B_{d_{1}}\times[0,\epsilon)\times B_{d_{2}}\to B_{d}

is orientation preserving. Because all
B_{d}
are odd-dimensional, our convention on induced boundary orientation leads to the conclusion.
∎

14.4.
Multimodules and homotopies for smooth domains

In this subsection, we describe the multimodules of domain curves which will be used for the AMS construction for moduli spaces for pair-of-pants products.

We would like to specify a class of constructions as follows. Fix a common outercollaring width. Choose (independently) lifts of the outercollared domain flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{m},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.
20
20

20

When we treat the cigar bimodule and the Poincaré duality multimodule, one does not need
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\mathbb{Dom}}}^{\prime}
. However the method can be easily modified to suit those cases.

(1)

For any smooth genus zero curve
\Sigma
with
m
negative cylindrical ends and one positive cylindrical end, we construct a class of monotone multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{m};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

(2)

Let
\Sigma_{t}
,
t\in[0,1]
be a smooth family of smooth genus zero curves with
m
negative cylindrical ends and one positive cylindrical end. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
be two monotone multimodules as above. We construct a class of homotopies
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{I}}
from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

We first describe the underlying posets of the monotone multimodule. For each
d
, define

A_{d}^{\Sigma}:=\left\{(r_{1},\ldots,r_{m};d_{0};r^{\prime})\ \left|\begin{array}[]{c}r_{j}\in A_{d_{j}},\ j=1,\ldots,m,\ r^{\prime}\in A_{d^{\prime}},\ d_{j},d^{\prime}>0,\ d_{0}\geq 0,\\
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{m}+d_{0}+d^{\prime}=d\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Then there are the natural poset maps

\prod_{j=1}^{m}A_{d_{j}}\times A_{d}^{\Sigma}\times A_{d^{\prime}}\to A_{d_{1}+\cdots+d_{m}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}.

These are the poset maps underlying the structural maps of the monotone multimodule we will define.

Now we describe, for each
d\geq 0
, an object
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma},B_{d}^{\Sigma},C_{d}^{\Sigma})\in\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. We set
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma}=PGL^{+}(d+1)
and
G_{d}^{\Sigma}=U^{+}(d)
, the same as before. For the space
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
, consider the moduli space of holomorphic maps
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
of degree
d
. Let

{\mathcal{M}}^{\Sigma}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)

be the subspace satisfying the positive constraint

u(z_{+})=[0,\ldots,0,1].

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\Sigma}({\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
be the natural compactification (where cylindrical bubbles are taken modulo only translation but not rotation). Let

B^{\Sigma}_{d}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)

be the subset of maps whose images are not entirely contained in any hyperplane. Notice that
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
is naturally stratified by the poset
A_{d}^{\Sigma}
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma}
acts on
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
. Let
C_{d}^{\Sigma}\to B_{d}^{\Sigma}
be the universal curve, which is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma}
-equivariant family. Define

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma},B_{d}^{\Sigma},C_{d}^{\Sigma})

which is an object in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

14.4.1.
Structural maps

To describe the structural maps, we need to choose the corresponding lateral lines on
\Sigma
.
21
21

21

Notice that there are homotopically non-equivalent choices of lateral lines. The comparison between two such choices of lateral lines on
\Sigma
will be given in Subsection
20.4
.
We also need to restrict to the case that there is only one output (in the positive version). The morphisms we need to describe are

\prod_{j=1}^{m}{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{j}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+\cdots+d_{m}+d}^{\Sigma}.

and

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}.

There is not much difficulty in defining the second map, which is similar to the previous case. On the other hand, there is a subtlety in defining the first structural map. In fact, the underlying group map is not well-defined; it depends on the ordering of the cylindrical end. However, different choices are related by conjugations.

Assume for simplicity
l=2
, which is the case of the pair-of-pants product. For any
d_{1},d_{2}
, we define

{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{2}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d}^{\Sigma}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{1}+d_{2}+d}^{\Sigma}

to be the same morphism described before. More explicity, when
d^{1}=d^{2}=d=2
, the map is

\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{11}&a_{12}&0\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&0\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&1\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}b_{11}&b_{12}&0\\
b_{21}&b_{22}&0\\
b_{31}&b_{32}&1\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}c_{11}&c_{12}&0\\
c_{21}&c_{22}&0\\
c_{31}&c_{32}&1\end{array}\right]\right)\\
\mapsto\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}a_{11}&a_{12}&0&0&0&0&0\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&0&0&0&0&0\\
0&0&b_{11}&b_{12}&0&0&0\\
0&0&b_{21}&b_{22}&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&0&c_{11}&c_{12}&0\\
0&0&0&0&c_{21}&c_{22}&0\\
a_{31}&a_{32}&b_{31}&b_{32}&c_{31}&c_{32}&1\end{array}\right].

Similarly, we also define the concatenation map. Consider the concatenation at the two negative ends of
\Sigma
. Let
\phi^{j}\in B_{d^{j}}
,
j=1,2
, be represented by maps
u^{j}:\Sigma^{j}\to\mathbb{P}^{d^{j}}
. Then they pullback line bundles
L_{u^{1}},L_{u^{2}}\to\Sigma
with frames

(h_{0}^{1},\ldots,h_{d^{1}}^{1}),\ (h_{0}^{2},\ldots,h_{d^{2}}^{2}).

The positive constraint is that

\displaystyle\ h_{0}^{1}(z_{+})=\cdots=h_{d^{1}-1}^{1}(z_{+})=0,\

\displaystyle\ h_{0}^{2}(z_{+})=\cdots=h_{d^{2}-1}^{2}(z_{+})=0.

On the other hand, suppose the point
\phi\in B_{d}^{\Sigma}
is represented by a map
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d}
, which pulls back a bundle
L_{u}\to\Sigma
having a frame

h_{0},\ldots,h_{d}

satisfying

h_{0}(z_{+})=\cdots=h_{d-1}(z_{+})=0.

The parallel transport along the two lateral lines induces nonzero vectors
\tau^{1}\in L_{u}(z_{-}^{1})
,
\tau^{2}\in L_{u}(z_{-}^{2})
. Then consider the prestable curve

\tilde{\Sigma}:=(\Sigma^{1}\sqcup\Sigma^{2})\#\Sigma.

We extend the bundles
L_{u^{1}}\to\Sigma^{1}
,
L_{u^{2}}\to\Sigma^{2}
, and
L_{u}\to\Sigma
to other components by trivial bundle extension. Then all the sections extend by constants. Then define
L=L_{u^{1}}\otimes L_{u^{2}}\otimes L_{u}
and consider the collection of sections (written in three groups)

\Big(h_{0}^{1}\otimes h_{d^{2}}^{2}\otimes\tau^{1},\ldots,h_{d^{1}-1}^{1}\otimes h_{d^{2}}^{2}\otimes\tau^{1};h_{d^{1}}^{1}\otimes h_{0}^{2}\otimes\tau^{2},\ldots,h_{d^{1}}^{1}\otimes h_{d^{2}-1}^{2}\otimes\tau^{2};h_{d^{1}}^{1}\otimes h_{d^{2}}^{2}\otimes h_{0},\ldots,h_{d^{1}}^{1}\otimes h_{d^{2}}^{2}\otimes h_{d}\Big).

Then it is easy to see that this collection forms a basis of
H^{0}(L)
. In particular, it is base-point-free. Hence it defines a holomorphic map

\tilde{u}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{P}^{d^{1}+d^{2}+d}.

One can also see that this is independent of the choices of the representatives. Hence one obtains a well-defined map

\zeta_{d^{1},d^{2};d}^{B}:B_{d^{1}}\times B_{d^{2}}\times B_{d}^{\Sigma}\to B_{d^{1}+d^{2}+d}^{\Sigma}.

It is easy to see that the map is equivariant with respect to the group embedding.

The associativity is more subtle than the previous case. Choose
d^{1}_{a},d^{1}_{b},d_{a}^{2},d_{b}^{2},d
. Denote
\tilde{d}=d_{b}^{1}+d_{b}^{2}+d
. Let’s compare the following two elements

\zeta_{d^{1}_{a}+d_{b}^{1},d^{2}_{a}+d_{b}^{2};d}^{B}\left(\zeta_{d_{a}^{1},d_{b}^{1}}^{B}(\phi^{1}_{a},\phi^{1}_{b}),\zeta_{d_{a}^{2},d_{b}^{2}}^{B}(\phi^{2}_{a},\phi^{2}_{b});\phi\right)\ {\rm vs.}\ \zeta_{d_{a}^{1},d_{a}^{2};\tilde{d}}^{B}\left(\phi_{a}^{1},\phi_{a}^{2};\zeta_{\phi_{b}^{1},\phi_{b}^{2};d}^{B}(\phi_{b}^{1},\phi_{b}^{2};\phi)\right).

They indeed differ by a conjugation. In fact, the diagram

\textstyle{U(d_{a}^{1})\times U(d_{b}^{1})\times U(d_{a}^{2})\times U(d_{b}^{2})\times U(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U(d_{a}^{1}+d_{b}^{1})\times U(d_{a}^{2}+d_{b}^{2})\times U(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U(d_{a}^{1})\times U(d_{a}^{2})\times U(\tilde{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U(d_{a}^{1}+d_{a}^{2}+d_{b}^{1}+d_{b}^{2}+d)}

commutes up to an element in
U(d_{a}^{1}+d_{a}^{2}+d_{b}^{1}+d_{b}^{2}+d)
which is the matrix permuting corresponding coordinates. Therefore, the above diagram commutes up to a conjugation.

One can extend the above construction to the general case when
\Sigma
is smooth with multiple inputs.

Proposition 14.21
.

The collections of equivariant family of curves
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}
together with the structural maps

{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{m}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\Sigma}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+\cdots+d_{m}+d+d^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}

define a monotone multimodule over
(\mathbb{Dom},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom};\mathbb{Dom})
.

14.4.2.
Rigidification and stable complex structure

We can construct a rigidification of any outercollaring of the multimodule
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}
in the same way as constructing the rigidification of the domain flow category (see Proposition
14.17
).

Proposition 14.22
.

Given lifts of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{m},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, there exists a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
as a monotone multimodule over the given lifts of the monotone flow categories. Moreover, for all
d
there exist
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-invariant orientations on
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
satisfying

(1)

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
d_{i}>0
, the map

{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{i}+d}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{i}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d}}(B_{d_{i}}\times B_{d}^{\Sigma})\to\partial^{d_{i},d}B_{d+d_{i}}^{\Sigma}

is orientation preserving.

(2)

For each
d^{\prime}>0
, the map

{\mathcal{G}}_{d+d^{\prime}}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{\prime}}}(B_{d}^{\Sigma}\times B_{d^{\prime}})\to\partial^{d,d^{\prime}}B_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}

is orientation reversing.

Proof.

The construction of the rigidification can be carried out as done in the proof of Proposition
14.17
. The construction of stable complex structure can be carried out similarly as in the proof of Proposition
14.19
. As for the orientation, the argument is similar to Lemma
14.20
. However, notice that
B_{d}
has an odd dimension and
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
has an even dimension, which make the difference in the two cases as one has to interchange the
[0,\epsilon)
factor with either
B_{d_{i}}
or
B_{d}^{\Sigma}
.
∎

14.4.3.
Homotopies

Flow homotopies associated to a 1-parameter family of variations of domain Floer data is indeed quite trivial. Indeed, let
\Sigma_{t}
,
t\in[0,1]
be a smooth family of Floer domains with
m
negative ends and
1
positive end. Then there are
m+1
copies of the monotone flow categories
\mathbb{Dom}
corresponding to the
m
inputs and 1 output. Suppose
\Sigma_{t}
is equipped with a smooth family of lateral lines. Then
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
correspond to monotone multimodules
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
and
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
over
(\mathbb{Dom},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom};\mathbb{Dom})
. Moreover, by allowing
t
to vary, one naturally obtains a monotone homotopy
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{H}}
from
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
. Upon choosing any outercollaring width, these items become corresponding collared versions. The following proposition demonstrates that the choices of rigidifications and stable complex structures can also be carried inductively.

Proposition 14.23
.

Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{m};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime}
be monotone flow categories enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
obtained from the monotone flow category
\mathbb{Dom}
by outercollaring and possibly different rigidifications. Let

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}},\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}

be monotone multimodules over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{m};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
constructed above. Then the outercollaring
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{H}}
admits a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
as a monotone homotopy from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
.

Proof.

Routine verification of the inductive constructions.
∎

Figure 8.
Lateral lines in a pair-of-pants.

14.4.4.
The case of cigar bimodule

Let
\Sigma^{\rm cigar}
be the complex plane
{\mathbb{C}}
equipped with a cylindrical end. It is used to construct the cigar bimodule and eventually lead to the multiplicative identity of the pair-of-pants product. The construction of the corresponding monotone bimodule, denoted by
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm cigar}
, is slightly different from the general case.

We shall regard
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm cigar}
not as a bimodule, but only a
right module
over
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
. This means it consists of objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm cigar}\in{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}

together with structural maps

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm cigar}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}^{\rm cigar}

which satisfy the obvious associativity relation. The construction of the object
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm cigar}
is the same as before. Indeed, consider the moduli space of degree
d
maps

\phi:\Sigma^{\rm cigar}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}

satisfying the constraint

\phi(\infty)=[0,\ldots,0,1]\in\mathbb{P}^{d}

and such that the image of
\phi
is not contained in any hyperplane. After compactification in the standard way, one obtains a moduli space
B_{d}^{\rm cigar}
equipped with a
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-action and a universal curve
C_{d}^{\rm cigar}\to B_{d}^{\rm cigar}
, hence provides an object

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm cigar}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d}^{\rm cigar},C_{d}^{\rm cigar})

stratified by the poset

A_{d}^{\rm cigar}=\{(d_{0},d_{1},\ldots,d_{l})\ |\ d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}=d,\ d_{i}\geq 1\}.

The difference from the previous case is the definition of the structural maps. In the current situation, we do not need a lateral line in
\Sigma^{\rm cigar}
. Other constructions are the same. This provides a monotone right module
\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm cigar}
over
\mathbb{Dom}
. Then after outercollaring and choosing a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, one can find a rigidification of the outercollaring
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm cigar}
.

14.4.5.
The case of Poincaré duality multimodule

Now let
\Sigma^{\rm PD}
be the smooth Floer domain with two negative ends and no positive ends, which is responsible for defining the Poincaré duality for Floer theory. One can similar define a monotone multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm PD}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
as above, whose construction slightly differ from previous cases.

In this case, the structure we build is better called a double left module, meaning a collection of objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm PD}\in{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}

together with two structural maps

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm PD}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{1}+d}^{\rm PD},\ {\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm PD}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d_{2}+d}^{\rm PD}

corresponding to breakings at the two negative ends, which satisfy similar associativity conditions as a general monotone multimodule.

We first consider the moduli space of degree
d
stable maps

\phi:\Sigma^{\rm PD}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}

whose images are not contained in any hyperplane. As the cylindrical ends are on the negative side, in the current convention we do not impose any evaluation constraint. After the standard compactification, one obtains such a moduli space

B_{d}^{\rm PD}

which has a
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-action and a universal curve
C_{d}^{\rm PD}\to B_{d}^{\rm PD}
, providing an object

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\rm PD}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d}^{\rm PD},C_{d}^{\rm PD}).

The structural maps are also defined similarly, but without using lateral lines. We omit the details. Hence one obtains a monotone double left module
\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm PD}
over
(\mathbb{Dom},\mathbb{Dom})
. Then after outercollaring, upon choosing two independent lifts
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{2}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, one can construct a lift
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\rm PD}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. We omit the details.

15.
Relatively Smooth AMS lifts

In this section we carry out perhaps the most complex part of the AMS construction. We demonstrate how to lift an outercollaring of a Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
, the category of (collared and rigidified) relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces. In fact, we will construct a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
, the category of collared and rigidified topological Kuranishi spaces. The lift to relatively smooth version is automatic. The collared-and-rigidified feature is always preserved in the construction as it is a basic prerequisite for inductively construct perturbations.

15.1.
Integral modification of topological energy

We generalize the method of Rezchikov
[
57
]
of modifying the topological energy. First, there is a way to modify the symplectic form.

Definition 15.1
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be the Floer flow category associated to a nondegenerate Hamiltonian
H
on
(X,\omega,J)
. An
integral action
on
{\mathbb{F}}
is a function

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

There exists an integral symplectic form
\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(X)
tamed by
J
such that
{\rm ker}\Omega={\rm ker}\omega\subseteq\pi_{2}(X)
and for each
A\in\Pi
and
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(A\cdot p)={\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{\mathbb{F}}(p)-\Omega(A).

(2)

There exists
C>0
such that whenever
p\neq q
and
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\neq\emptyset
, one has

0<C\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)\right)\leq{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{\mathbb{F}}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{\mathbb{F}}(q).

We need similar notions for multimodules and homotopies.

Definition 15.2
.

Consider a smooth genus zero Riemann surface with cylindrical ends
\Sigma
together with a family of Floer data
\sigma_{w}
parametrized continuously by
w
in a compact topological space such that for all
w
, the Floer data is asymptotic to
(H_{i}dt,J_{i})
on the
i
-th negative end (
i=1,\ldots,m
) and asymptotic to
(H_{j}^{\prime}dt,J_{j}^{\prime})
on the
j
-th positive end (
j=1,\ldots,n
). A compatible collection of integral actions consists of integral actions

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}\to{\mathbb{Z}}\ (i=1,\ldots,m),\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{j}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{Z}}

on the Floer flow categories
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{j}^{\prime}
associated to
(H_{i},J_{i})
resp.
(H_{j}^{\prime},J_{j}^{\prime})
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

The integral actions induce the same integral symplectic form
\Omega
.

(2)

For each parameter
w
and each point
z\in\Sigma
,
\Omega
is tamed by the almost complex structure
J_{w,z}
.

(3)

For objects
p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
and
p_{j}^{\prime}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{j}^{\prime}
, let
M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}
denote temporarily the moduli space of stable solutions of Floer equation for all parameter
s
asymptotic to the labeling objects. Then

M_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p_{1}^{\prime}\cdots p_{n}^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})>\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}(p_{j}^{\prime})

One can then specialize to multimodules and homotopies.

Definition 15.3
.

(1)

In Situation
Module(i)
, integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\Omega};{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}
on the involved flow categories are called
compatible
with respect to
{\mathbb{X}}
if it is compatible with respect to the fixed Floer data
\sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
on
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
.

(2)

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\Omega},{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}
are called
compatible
with respect to
{\mathbb{H}}
if it is compatible with respect to the 1-parameter family of Floer data.

Proposition 15.4
.

In the setting of Definition
15.2
, there exist compatible integral actions on involved Floer flow categories.

Proof.

It was proved in
[
57
, Lemma 16]
that given any collection
H_{1},\ldots,H_{m}
of nondegenerate 1-periodic Hamiltonians on
(X,\omega)
, there exists an open subset
U\subseteq X
containing the images of all 1-periodic orbits of all
H_{i}
and for any
\epsilon>0
, a rational symplectic form
\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(M)
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

\Omega|_{U}=\omega|_{U}
.

(2)

\|\Omega-\omega\|_{C^{0}(X)}\leq\epsilon
.

(3)

{\rm ker}\Omega={\rm ker}\omega\subseteq\pi_{2}(X)
.

Now consider a Floer domain
\Sigma
with the Hamiltonian connection
\sigma
. Let
\Sigma_{0}\subseteq\Sigma
be a compact region such that
\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{0}
is a union of half cylinders. Let
(s,t)
be the cylindrical coordinates and let
H_{i}dt
be the restriction of
\sigma
to the
i
-th cylindrical end
U_{i}
, where
H_{i}
is nondegenerate.

Let
U\subseteq X
be the open subset provided by Rezchikov’s lemma. Let
\Omega
be a symplectic form which agrees with
\omega
within
U
and which is
C^{0}
-close to
\omega
so
\Omega
tames
J_{w,z}
for all almost complex structures appearing. Then there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
satisfying

\forall i\ \ \forall\rho:S^{1}\to X,\ \int_{S^{1}}|\rho^{\prime}(t)-X_{H_{i,t}}(\rho(t))|^{2}dt\leq\epsilon_{0}\Longrightarrow{\rm Im}(\rho)\subseteq U.

(15.1)

For each capped 1-periodic orbit
p
of
H_{i}
where the capping is a map
u:{\mathbb{D}}^{2}\to X
, define

{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}(\gamma,u)=-\int_{{\mathbb{D}}^{2}}u^{*}\Omega-\int_{S^{1}}H_{i}(\gamma(t))dt.

Let
|\cdot|_{\Omega}
be the norm on the tangent bundle
TX
determined by
\Omega
and
J_{w,z}
. For any map
u:\Sigma\to X
, define

\nabla^{\sigma,\Omega}(u)=\nabla u+X_{\sigma}^{\Omega}(u)

where
X_{\sigma}^{\Omega}
is the 1-form on
\Sigma
taking values in Hamiltonian vector fields associated to
\Omega
. In any local coordinate
z=s+{\bf i}t
one can write
\sigma=\sigma_{s}ds+\sigma_{t}dt
and

\nabla^{\sigma,\Omega}u=\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}uds+\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}udt.

The difference of the covariant derivatives between
\Omega
and
\omega
is

\nabla^{\sigma,\Omega}u-\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}u=(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\Omega}(u)-X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega}(u))ds+(X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\Omega}(u)-X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega}(u))dt=:X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\Omega-\omega}(u)ds+X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\Omega-\omega}(u)dt

which is linear in the difference
\Omega-\omega
.

Define the energy density of the map
u
as

\frac{1}{2}|\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}(u)|_{\Omega}^{2}{\rm dvol}_{\Sigma}.

Then one has the calculation (see
[
46
, Lemma 2.2.1]
)

\frac{1}{2}|\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}(u)|_{\Omega}^{2}{\rm dvol}_{\Sigma}=|(\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}(u))^{0,1}|^{2}{\rm dvol}_{\Sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)+\Omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)\Big)dsdt.

We observe that

\begin{split}&\ |\Omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)|\\
=&\ |\Omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)+\omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)|\\
=&\ |\Omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\omega(J\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,J\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)+\omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)|\\
\leq&\ \|\omega-\Omega\|_{C^{0}(X)}\cdot|\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}(u)|_{\Omega}^{2}\end{split}

(15.2)

Now suppose
u
solves
(\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}u)^{0,1}=0
. Then one has

\begin{split}&\ \Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)\\
=&\ \Omega\Big(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u+X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u+X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u)\Big)\\
=&\ \Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u)+\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u)+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))\\
=&\ \Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u)+\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}(u))+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\Omega-\omega}(u))\end{split}

Notice that if
u
converges to capped 1-periodic orbits
p_{i}
at the
i
-th cylindrical end, then

\int_{\Sigma}\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u)dsdt=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\pm{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})+\int_{\Sigma}F_{\sigma}(u)

where the first term only depends on the homotopy class of
u
and
F_{\sigma}
does not depend on the symplectic form. Then one has

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\pm{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})=\int_{\Sigma}\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\Omega}u,\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\Omega}u)dsdt-\int_{\Sigma}F_{\sigma}(u)\\
-\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u)+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))\Big)dsdt.

We would like to estimate the last term. First, on the compact region
\Sigma_{0}
, one has

\left|\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big(\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u)+\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),\nabla_{t}^{\sigma,\omega}u)-\Omega(X_{\sigma_{s}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u),X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))\Big)dsdt\right|\\
\leq C(\epsilon)\|\omega-\Omega\|_{C^{0}(X)}^{2}+\epsilon\int_{\Sigma_{0}}|\nabla^{\sigma,\omega}(u)|_{\Omega}^{2}{\rm dvol}_{\Sigma}

for any
\epsilon>0
and a suitable
C(\epsilon)>0
. On the other hand, on each half cylinder
U_{i}\cong I_{i}\times S^{1}
where
I_{i}={\mathbb{R}}_{\pm}
, contained in
\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{0}
, we may take
(s,t)
to be the cylindrical coordinate. Then we know that
\sigma_{s}=0
. Hence we are left with

\int_{U_{i}}\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{H_{i}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))dsdt.

(15.3)

Recall the pre-chosen constant
\epsilon_{0}
(see (
15.1
)). Consider

I_{i}(\epsilon_{0}):=\Big\{s\in I_{i}\ |\ \int_{S^{1}}|\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}(u)(s,t)|_{\omega}^{2}dt\geq\epsilon_{0}\Big\}.

Then
|I_{i}(\epsilon)|\leq E^{\omega}(u)/\epsilon_{0}
. Since
\Omega=\omega
near 1-periodic orbits, by our assumption, the integrand of (
15.3
) vanishes outside
I_{i}(\epsilon_{0})\times S^{1}
. Then by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, one has

\left|\int_{U_{i}}\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))dsdt\right|=\left|\int_{I_{i}(\epsilon_{0})\times S^{1}}\Omega(\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u,X_{\sigma_{t}}^{\omega-\Omega}(u))dsdt\right|\\
\leq C\|\omega-\Omega\|_{C^{0}(X)}\sqrt{|I_{i}(\epsilon_{0})|}\sqrt{\int_{I_{i}(\epsilon_{0})\times S^{1}}|\nabla_{s}^{\sigma,\omega}u|_{\omega}^{2}dsdt}\\
\leq C\|\omega-\Omega\|_{C^{0}(X)}\frac{E^{\omega}(u)}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}.

On the other hand, when
\Omega
is close to
\omega
, the norms
|\cdot|_{\omega}
and
|\cdot|_{\Omega}
are compatible. Therefore, when
\Omega
is
C^{0}
-close to
\omega
, one has the estimate

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\pm{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}E^{\Omega}(u)-C

for some constant
C>0
by combining the above computations with (
15.2
) to rearrange the inequalities, provided that
\epsilon
is sufficiently small.

Further, to make the symplectic action rational, we define

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i}):={\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})+\delta_{p_{i}}

for a small real number
\delta_{p_{i}}
making the above rational. Then one still has

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\pm{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}_{i}}^{\Omega}(p_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}E^{\omega}(u)-C

after changing
C
a bit. Then we argue in two cases.

(1)

When
\Sigma
is an infinite cylinder,
\sigma=Hdt
and
J
only depends on
t\in S^{1}
, one can take
C=0
and obtain instead

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega}(q)\geq\frac{1}{2}E^{\omega}(u)=\frac{1}{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{H}^{\omega}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}^{\omega}(q)).

This provides an integral action on the associated Floer flow category.

(2)

In other cases, one can add rational constants to
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}
to make the sum of
\pm{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{i}}^{\Omega}
always positive.
