Finally, we choose a common large positive integral multiple of the
{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{\mathbb{F}}
constructed above so that the action becomes integral valued and the symplectic form
\Omega
becomes integral.
∎

15.1.1.
Pullback the domain flow category, multimodule, and homotopy

Now choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
with associated symplectic form
\Omega
. For each pair of objects
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, abbreviate
d=d_{pq}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(q)
. Inside the poset
A_{d}
(of positive partitions of
d
), there is an Alexandrov open subset

A_{pq}^{\Omega}\subseteq A_{d}

whose elements are decompositions

d=d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}

such that there exist objects
r_{1},\ldots,r_{l}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
such that

d_{0}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}({r_{1}}),\ d_{1}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(r_{1})-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(r_{2}),\ \ldots,d_{l}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(r_{l})-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(q).

Now we use these data to “pull back” the monotone flow categories
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
constructed before. Define a flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
whose objects are the same as
{\mathbb{F}}
, and whose morphism from
p
to
q
is the restriction of
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
to the corresponding open subset

{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}^{\Omega}\subseteq{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}.

Denote this object by

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{\Omega}=({\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{\Omega},B_{pq}^{\Omega},C_{pq}^{\Omega}).

When
\Omega
is understood from the context, we often drop it from the notation. Then the restriction of the structural maps of the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
gives a flow category

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}

enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
whose objects are the same as objects of
{\mathbb{F}}
and whose morphisms are the families
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{\Omega}
.

Now we summarize the construction as an update of Situation
Flow(i)
.

Situation Flow(ii)
.

In Siutation
Flow(i)
, one fixes an outercollaring width.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
is the monotone flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Furthermore, let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
be the flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
subject to a chosen integral action on
{\mathbb{F}}
.

Similarly, let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a multimodule associated to a Floer domain
\Sigma
. Choose an outercollaring width and a compatible set of integral actions on involved flow categories

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{j}\to{\mathbb{Z}},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{Z}}.

Then one can choose an integer
n^{\mathbb{X}}
such that for all tuples of objects
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
, one has

d:=d_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}(p_{i})-{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})+n^{\mathbb{X}}>0.

(15.4)

Let

A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}}\subseteq A_{d}

be the Alexandrov open subset of partitions of
d
corresponding to possible degenerations of
\Sigma
and let

{\mathcal{C}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

be the restriction to the open subset corresponding to
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\Omega,\mathbb{X}}
. Then this provides a multimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, denoted by

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}}.

Remark 15.5
.

The constant
n^{\mathbb{X}}
should be thought as shifting the integral action
{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}
by
-n^{\mathbb{X}}
such that for all
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
, we have

d:=d_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}(p_{i})-({\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})-n^{\mathbb{X}})>0.

The positive integer
d
will be the degree of an auxiliary line bundle over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.

Situation Module(ii)
.

In Situation
Module(i)
, one fixes an outercollaring width and a compatible list of integral actions on the involved Floer categories.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
are flow categories enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
subject to the chosen integral actions as described in Situation
Flow(ii)
.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}}
is the multimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
.

The case of Situation
Homotopy(i)
is similar and we only describe the construction below without details.

Situation Homotopy(ii)
.

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, one fixes an outercollaring width and a compatible list of integral actions on involved Floer flow categories.

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}

are flow categories enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
subject to the chosen integral actions as described in Situation
Flow(ii)
;

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}_{0}},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}

are multimodules over them associated to
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
respectively as described in Situation
Module(ii)
.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{H}}}
is a homotopy from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the integral actions.

15.2.
Framed maps

In this subsection, we describe a suitable package describing flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies of infinite-dimensional spaces of maps into the target symplectic manifold.

15.2.1.
Category of families of framed maps

The following definitions are rather artificial. However, they help us organize various constructions and package required properties. We use the following notations a lot: given any group
G
, let
\hat{G}=G\times G
. We often denote the first factor by
G_{L}
and the second factor by
G_{R}
.

Definition 15.6
.

(1)

The category
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
has objects

\hat{\mathcal{C}}:=({\mathcal{G}},B,C,Y)

where
({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
,
Y
is a
{\mathcal{G}}\times{\mathcal{G}}
-space together with a map
\pi:Y\to B
which is equivariant with respect to the projection
{\mathcal{G}}_{L}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{R}\to{\mathcal{G}}
onto the first factor and the action by
{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
on
Y
is free.

(2)

Let
\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{i}=({\mathcal{G}}_{i},B_{i},C_{i},Y_{i})
,
i=1,2
be two objects of
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
. A strict embedding from
\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
to
\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
, denoted by
\hat{\zeta}_{21}
, consists of a strict embedding

\zeta_{21}:{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}

in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
and a map
\zeta_{21}^{Y}:Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
equivariant with respect to
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\to\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
such that

(a)

The induced map

\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{2}\times_{\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{1}}Y_{1}\to Y_{2}

is an homeomorphism onto the corresponding union of strata.

(b)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{B}}
\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{Y}}
\textstyle{B_{2}}
\textstyle{Y_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(3)

A
rigidification
of a strict embedding
\hat{\zeta}_{21}
is a rigidification of the underlying strict embedding
\zeta_{21}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

(4)

The notion of unitary conjugations and embeddings are defined similar to many previous cases. We omit the details. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
be the category of framed maps with morphisms being embeddings and
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
be the rigidified version.

15.2.2.
Group reductions

By definition, Kuranishi spaces have symmetry group being a compact Lie group
G
, while the corresponding AMS domain has the symmetry group being a complex Lie group
{\mathcal{G}}
. The redundancy is reduced by choosing certain group reductions.

Definition 15.7

(The category of families of reduced framed maps)
.

(1)

Let
\hat{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C,Y)
be an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
. A
group reduction
of
\hat{\mathcal{C}}
is a map

\Lambda:Y\to{\mathcal{G}}/G

which is
{\mathcal{G}}_{L}
-invariant and
{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
-equivariant.

(2)

The category of
equivariant families of reduced framed maps
, denoted by
\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}
consists of objects
({\mathcal{G}},B,C,Y,\Lambda)
where
({\mathcal{G}},B,C,Y)
is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
and
\Lambda
is a group reduction. A
strict embedding

\hat{\zeta}_{21}:\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}
consists of a strict embedding of
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
, which induces a map

\zeta_{21}^{P}:{\mathcal{G}}_{1}/G_{1}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}/G_{2}

such that following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{Y}}
\textstyle{({\mathcal{G}}_{1}/G_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{P}}
\textstyle{Y_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{2}}
\textstyle{({\mathcal{G}}_{2}/G_{2})}

The notions of unitary conjugations and embeddings are defined in a way similar to previously considered situations. Morphisms in
\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}
are embeddings, i.e., strict embeddings modulo unitary conjugations.

(3)

A rigidification of a strict embedding
\hat{\zeta}_{21}
as considered above is a rigidification of the underlying strict embedding in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
, and we denote the resulting category by
\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
.

As usual, we indicate the outercollaring using the notation
⌊
.

15.2.3.
Modification of energy density

Given an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
on
{\mathbb{F}}
, there are also the induced modification of the energy density. Given any smooth map
u:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to X
which converges to a pair of objects
p
and
q
with
{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)>{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)
, define

\Omega_{u}:=\Omega(\partial_{s}u,\partial_{t}u-X^{\Omega}_{H_{t}}(u))ds\wedge dt\in\Omega^{2}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}).

(15.5)

Then its integral is the integer
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}(q)
. We may regard
\Omega_{u}
as a modified energy density, which is everywhere nonnegative when
u
is a Floer cylinder. Similarly, given a smooth map
u:S^{2}\to X
representing a sphere class
A\in\pi_{2}(X)
, define

\Omega_{u}:=u^{*}\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(S^{2}).

Its integral is
\Omega(A)
; when
u
is a
J
-holomorphic sphere,
\Omega_{u}
is everywhere nonnegative.

In general, given smooth map
u:\Sigma\to X
over a Floer domain with associated flow multimodudle
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
, recall that we have chosen an integer
n^{\mathbb{X}}>0
. In this setting, we consider the
2
-form

\Omega_{u}:=u^{*}\Omega+F^{\Omega}_{\alpha}(u)+n^{\mathbb{X}}d{\rm vol}_{\Sigma},

where
\alpha
is the Hamiltonian connection from the Floer datum, and
F^{\Omega}_{\alpha}(u)
denotes its curvature with respect to the symplectic form
\Omega
. If
u
solves the Floer equation,
\Omega_{u}
is everywhere nonnegative, and
\int_{\Sigma}\Omega_{u}
recovers
d_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
from (
15.4
). The same construction applies to maps from multimodule homotopies.

15.2.4.
Floer admissible maps
and framed maps

Now we provide the concrete flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
.

We first consider the flow category case. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a Floer flow category associated to
(X,\omega,J)
and the Hamiltonian
H
. Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
be an integral action with associated integral symplectic form
\Omega
. Recall that one has obtained a flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
with the same set of objects as
{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
. For each
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, one has the object

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}=({\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},C_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}.

We define a
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
-space
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
as follows. For each
\phi\in B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
, consider continuous maps
u:C_{\phi}\to X
which are smooth away from nodes and which converge exponentially fast towards 1-periodic orbits of
H
. We require that
u
converges to the orbits
p
resp.
q
at the negative resp. positive end of
C_{\phi}
, and that the cappings satisfy
p\#u=q
. In particular, the homotopy type of the map
u
is constrained by the underlying curve; for example, if
C_{\phi}
has a sphere component of degree
d^{\prime}
, then the restriction of
u
to that component has the same degree
d^{\prime}
measured by the modified integral symplectic form
\Omega
. For
\phi
and
u
as above, there is a Hermitian holomorphic line bundle

L_{u}\to C_{\phi}

determined by the 2-form
\Omega_{u}\in\Omega^{2}(C_{\phi})
(see (
15.5
)) which decays exponentially along cylindrical end. The degree of
L_{u}
is
d_{pq}
. Then consider frames

F=(f_{0},\cdots,f_{d_{pq}})

of the space
H^{0}(L_{u})
modulo
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
such that 1) at the negative end
z_{-}
one has

[f_{0}(z_{-}),\ldots,f_{d_{pq}}(z_{-})]=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}

and 2) the Hermitian matrix

\left(\int_{C_{\phi}}\langle f_{\mu},f_{\nu}\rangle\Omega_{u}\right)_{\mu\nu}

is positive definite, where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the fiberwise Hermitian metric on the bundle
L_{u}
. Notice that the set of such frames
F
is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-torsor. Then let
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
be the set of all tuples
(\phi,u,F)
described as above.

The set
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
has a natural topology and admits a natural forgetful map
\pi_{0}:Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. There is another map

\pi_{1}:Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},\ (\phi,u,F)\mapsto\phi_{F}

where
\phi_{F}:C_{\phi}\to\mathbb{CP}^{{\bf d}_{pq}}
is the Kodaira embedding induced by the frame. Moreover,
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
has an action by
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}\cong{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
, where the first factor acts by reparametrizing the domain and the second factor acts by linearly transforming the frame
F
. One can see that the tuple

({\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},C_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}})

together with the maps
\pi_{0},\pi_{1}
is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
.

The cases of multimodules and homotopies are similar, using the modified energy from Section
15.2.3
. One obtains on the level of spaces

\displaystyle\ Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}},\

\displaystyle\ Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

as well as the corresponding objects in
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
.

15.2.5.
The structural maps

We would like to make the spaces of framed maps into flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
. We only describe the flow category case. Given
(\phi,u,F)\in Y_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
and
(\phi^{\prime},u^{\prime},F^{\prime})\in Y_{qr}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
, one has defined the composition

\phi\#\phi^{\prime}:=\zeta_{prq}^{B}(\phi,\phi^{\prime})\in B_{d_{pq}+d_{qr}}=B_{pr}

using the structural maps of the monotone flow category
\mathbb{Dom}^{k}
with the corresponding curve
C_{\phi\#\phi^{\prime}}
. The maps
u
and
u^{\prime}
obviously define a Floer admissible map

u\#u^{\prime}:C_{\phi\#\phi^{\prime}}\to X.

Then for the two frames
F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d})
and
F^{\prime}=(f_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,f_{d^{\prime}}^{\prime})
, we will define
F\#F^{\prime}
consisting of holomorphic sections of
L_{u\#u^{\prime},H}\to C_{\phi\#\phi^{\prime}}
. We can extend
L_{u,H}
resp.
L_{u^{\prime},H}
to the other component of
C_{\phi\#\phi^{\prime}}
by trivial bundle and denote the extension still by the same notation. Then one has the natural isomorphism

L_{u\#u^{\prime},H}=L_{u,H}\otimes L_{u^{\prime},H}.

Let
\tau_{u}\in L_{u}|_{z_{+}}
be the parallel transport of
f_{0}(z_{-})
along the lateral line with respect to the connection on
L_{u}\to C_{\phi}
. Then in the same way as we define the concatenating
\phi\#\phi^{\prime}
, consider the frame

F\#F^{\prime}=\Big(f_{0}\otimes f_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,f_{d}\otimes f_{0}^{\prime},\tau_{u}\otimes f_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\tau_{u}\otimes f_{d^{\prime}}^{\prime}\Big).

Hence we have obtained a map

\hat{\zeta}_{prq}:Y_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times Y_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to\partial^{prq}Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}.

We can also prove the associativity of the composition maps as the case of the monotone flow category
\mathbb{Dom}
. Hence we obtained a flow category.

Proposition 15.8
.

(1)

The spaces
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
together with the composition maps form a flow category
\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
which lifts the flow category
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
.

(2)

The spaces
Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}},{\rm fr}}
together with the structural maps form a multimodule
\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
over
(\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}};\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}})
.

(3)

The spaces
Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{H}},{\rm fr}}
together with the structural maps form a homotopy
\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
from
\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{X}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
.

Proof.

We just need to point out that the difference between the image of
{\rm Map}_{pr}^{\rm fr}\times{\rm Map}_{rq}^{\rm fr}
and the stratum
\partial^{prq}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}
lies in the difference of the domains. The concatenations of frame maps into
X
do not affect the rigidification.
∎

15.2.6.
Group reductions

We have the following proposition showing the existence of compatible systems of group reductions.

Proposition 15.9
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
,
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},{\mathbb{X}},{\mathbb{X}}_{0},{\mathbb{X}}_{1},{\mathbb{H}}
be as above. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{H}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
be the corresponding flow categories, multimodules, and homotopy enriched in enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
.

(1)

There exists a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
, denoted by
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{map}{}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
.

(2)

Given lifts
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{F}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
respectively, there exists a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{Map}}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
as a multimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{F}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}};{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}})
, which is denoted by
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
.

(3)

Given lifts
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{F}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
and lifts
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
respectively, as multimodules over the tuple
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots,{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{F}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}};{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}})
, there exists a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}{}_{{\mathbb{H}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
as a homotopy from
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction. The construction of the base situation was provided in
[
3
]
, whose construction is recalled as follows. Given
p,q
and a framed map
(\phi,u,F)\in{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F},{\rm fr}}
, consider the
(d_{pq}+1)\times(d_{pq}+1)
positive-definite Hermitian matrix

(H_{F})_{ab}=\int_{C_{\phi}}\langle f_{a},f_{b}\rangle\Omega_{u}.

(15.6)

As frames are well-defined up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-rescaling, this Hermitian matrix is well-defined up to positive rescaling, hence defines a map

\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}:Y_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}/G_{pq}=P_{pq}.

This map is obviously equivariant with respect to the
{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
-action; moreover, as
{\mathcal{G}}_{L}
acts by reparametrizing the map and pulling back the framings,
\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
is
{\mathcal{G}}_{L}
-invariant. Therefore, one obtains a group reduction on
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
.

If
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
has no lower stratum, then its outercollaring is itself and hence one obtains the base case of the induction. Suppose inductively, one has constructed compatible collared group reductions

\Lambda_{rs}^{\mathbb{F}}:{}_{\lfloor}Y_{rs}^{\mathbb{F}}\to P_{rs}

for all pairs
r,s
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}(r)-{\mathcal{A}}(s)<{\mathcal{A}}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}(q)
. Notice that for each triple
p<r<q
, one has a strict embedding

{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times{}_{\lfloor}Y_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to\partial^{prq}{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

which is equivariant with respect to the complex group embedding
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pr}\times\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{rq}\to\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
. Hence one obtains an extension

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.42732pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.30872pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 28.92961pt\raise 7.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\zeta}_{prq}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.30872pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.42732pt\raise-16.06139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2222pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\times\Lambda_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.78947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.30872pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 74.95215pt\raise-16.06139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2222pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.95215pt\raise-24.78947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-24.2566pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{pr}\times P_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.59668pt\raise-39.32613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.23114pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\zeta_{prq}^{P}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.77829pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.77829pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

The compatibility assumption and the
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-equivariance imply that one obtains a group reduction on the boundary of
{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
which is collared. We also have a group reduction in
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
(viewed as a subset away from boundary) given by (
15.6
). Then using the product structure on the collar region (where the actions by the complex Lie groups fix the collar coordinates) and choosing a homotopy, one can interpolate over the collar region to get a continuous group reduction on the whole of
{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
.

The constructions for the cases of multimodules and homotopies are completely the same and we omit the details.
∎

15.2.7.
Linearizing the group reduction

We would like to regard the group reduction as a section of a vector bundle rather than a nonlinear fiber bundle. This requires linearizing the homogeneous space
{\mathcal{G}}/G
, which has a contractible set of choices.

Recall that we have constructed a collection of germs of maps

\lambda_{d}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times Q_{d}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{d}\times P_{d}

By abuse of notation, let
Q_{d}^{\epsilon}\subseteq Q_{d}
be a
G_{d}
-invariant disk bundle over which
\lambda_{d}
is defined and
P_{d}^{\epsilon}\subseteq P_{d}
the image of
Q_{d}^{\epsilon}
. Denote

\lambda_{pq}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times Q_{pq}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times P_{pq}

the pullback of
\lambda_{d_{pq}}
. Then define

{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}},\epsilon}:=(\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}})^{-1}(P_{pq}^{\epsilon})

and define the composition

\lambda_{pq}:=\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.11443pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.11443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\lfloor}Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F},\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.54204pt\raise 7.19441pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.2222pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.11443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.11443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{pq}^{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.27847pt\raise 7.10612pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda_{pq}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.46214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.46214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q_{pq}^{\epsilon}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.90636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 131.90636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right).

Viewing this as a section of a certain trivial vector bundle, this map will become part of the Kuranishi sections. We call such a section
\lambda_{pq}
a
linearized group reduction
.

15.3.
The topological Kuranishi lifts

15.3.1.
Thickening data

Given an equivariant family
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
and an almost complex manifold
(X,J)
. Recall
\mathring{C}\subseteq C
is the complement of nodes and markings. Consider the vector bundle

\Lambda^{0,1}_{\mathring{C}/B}\otimes TX\to\mathring{C}\times X.

Definition 15.10
.

A
thickening datum
on an equivariant family of curves
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
consists of a pair
(W,\nu)
where
W
is a finite-dimensional complex unitary representation
22
22

22

We restrict to complex representation in order to simplify the discussion of normal complex structures.
of the maximal compact subgroup
G\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}
and

\nu:W\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}\times X,\Lambda^{0,1}_{\mathring{C}/B}\otimes TX)

is a
G
-equivariant linear map. Here
\Gamma_{c}
stands for smooth sections with compact support.

Notice that given a thickening datum
(W,\nu)
on
{\mathcal{C}}
, for each
\phi\in B
and a smooth map
u:C_{\phi}\to X
, there is an induced linear map

\nu_{\phi,u}:W\to\Omega^{0,1}_{c}(\mathring{C}_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

given by restricting
\nu(e)
to the graph of
u
in
\mathring{C}_{\phi}\times X
. These maps will be used to thicken up the moduli space of Floer equation.

Definition 15.11
.

The
category of curves-with-thickening data
, denoted by
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}
is defined as follows. The objects are tuples

({\mathcal{G}},B,C,W,\nu)

where
({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is an equivariant family of curves and
(W,\nu)
is a thickening datum on
{\mathcal{C}}
.

A
strict embedding of perturbations
consists of a strict embedding

\zeta_{21}=(\zeta_{21}^{\mathcal{G}},\zeta_{21}^{B},\zeta_{21}^{C}):({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})

(see Definition
14.7
) and a
\zeta_{21}^{G}
-equivariant isometric linear embedding
\zeta_{21}^{W}:W_{1}\to W_{2}
such that 1) the following diagram commutes;

\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{21}^{W}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{1}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{1}/B_{1}}^{0,1}\otimes TX)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{2}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{2}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{2}/B_{2}}^{0,1}\otimes TX)}

(here the right vertical arrow is induced from the morphism
\zeta_{21}
) and 2) the restriction of
\nu_{2}
to the orthogonal complement
W_{1}^{\bot}\subseteq W_{2}
vanishes.

The conjugation of
({\mathcal{G}},B,C,W,\nu)
is a strict embedding induced by
g\in G
. An
embedding
of curvs-with-thickening-datum is a conjugacy class of strict embeddings.

The category
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}
is a regular stratification category with a forgetful functor
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf Curv}}
. One can easily formulate the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}
of collared curves-with-thickening-datum, together with forgetful functor

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}\to{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}.

We need to consider the rigidified version. Recall that for a strict embedding

\zeta_{21}:({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1})\to({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2})

a rigidification is a germ of
G_{1}
-equivariant map
\theta_{21}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
satisfying an appropriate condition.

Definition 15.12
.

Let
(\zeta_{21},\zeta_{21}^{W})
be a strict embedding in
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}
from
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},B_{1},C_{1},W_{1},\nu_{1})
to
({\mathcal{G}}_{2},B_{2},C_{2},W_{2},\nu_{2})
. A
rigidification
of
(\zeta_{21},\zeta_{21}^{W})
consists of a rigidification
\theta_{21}:B_{1}\times Q_{21}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
of
\zeta_{21}
satisfying the following condition. Fix
b_{1}\in B_{1}
. The rigidification induces identifications

C_{\zeta_{21}^{B}(b_{1})}\cong C_{\theta_{21}(b_{1},q)(\zeta_{21}^{B}(b_{1}))}

for all
q\in Q_{21}
sufficient close to the origin. Then we can regard
\nu_{2}
as a family of linear maps

\nu_{2}(b_{1},q):W_{2}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{\zeta_{21}(b_{1})}\times X,\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes TX).

We require that these family only depends on
b_{1}
. In particular, for
q
close to the origin,
\nu_{2}
vanishes on the orthogonal complement of
W_{1}
.

Definition 15.13
.

A
thickening datum
on a flow category/multimodule/homotopy enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
is a lift in
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}
. If the flow category/multimodule/homotopy is enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
, then a perturbation is called
collared and rigidified
if it is a lift in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}_{\rm rig}
.

In the following steps, one needs to choose perturbations satisfying additional properties. The construction of such perturbations will be provided in due course.

Now we can describe the AMS lift of the Floer flow categories, bimodules, and homotopies to the category of topological Kuranishi spaces.

15.3.2.
The thickenings

We describe the thickened moduli spaces as candidates of the Kuranishi regularizations of the moduli spaces. We first consider the flow category case. Return to the domain flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
whose morphism spaces are equivariant families of curves
{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. A thickening datum of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
is denoted by
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}}
which consists of thickening data

\nu_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}:W_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}/B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}}^{0,1}\otimes TX).

Definition 15.14
.

Given any collared and rigidified thickening datum
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}}
on the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
and the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
of reduced framed maps, for each pair
p,q
of objects in the Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
, define the following items.

(1)

The
thickened moduli space
is

V_{pq}:=V_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}:=\left\{(\phi,u,F,e)\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}(\phi,u,F)\in Y_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}},\epsilon},\ e\in W_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},\\
\overline{\partial}_{H}u+\nu_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(e)=0,\ \phi=\phi_{F}\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}.\end{array}\right.\right\}.

(2)

The
G_{pq}
-action on
V_{pq}
is the product of the diagonal
G_{pq}
-action on
Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F},\epsilon}
space of framed maps and the action on
W_{pq}
.

(3)

The
obstruction bundle
is the trivial bundle

E_{pq}:=E_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}:=\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to V_{pq}.

(4)

The
Kuranishi section
is (given by) the equivariant map

S_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(\phi,u,F,e)=(\lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(\phi,u,F),e).

Theorem 15.15
.

The natural “footprint” map

(S_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}})^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\to{}_{\lfloor}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

which sends the orbit of
(\phi,u,F,0)
to the equivalence class of the stable Floer trajectory
u:C_{\phi}\to X
is an isomorphism of orbispaces.

Proof.

We drop
{\mathbb{F}}
from the notations for simplification. We also assume
k=1
. We prove that the footprint map is surjective. Given any point of
{}_{\lfloor}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
represented by a stable Floer trajectory
u:\Sigma\to X
(assume that this point is not in the outer collar; the case that it is in the outer collar can be deduced similarly). Then
u
determines a unitary line bundle
L_{u}\to\Sigma
whose Chern connection has curvature
\Omega_{u}
. Let
F
be a frame of
L_{u}
satisfying the negative (or positive) constraint, which determines a holomorphic map
\phi_{F}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d_{pq}}
and hence can be identified with a point
\phi_{F}\in B_{pq}
. The domain
\Sigma
can be then identified with
C_{\phi_{F}}
. Let
u_{F}:\mathring{C}_{\phi_{F}}\to X
be the pullback map. As two different frames
F
and
F^{\prime}
differ by an element of the group
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq,R}
, one obtains a
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-orbit (where we regard
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
as the diagonal of
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq,L}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{pq,R}
) of framed maps

(\phi_{F},u_{F},F)\in Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}.

By the definition of a structural group reduction scheme, there is a unique
G_{pq}
-orbit (for the diagonal action) of framed maps such that

\Lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(\phi_{F},u_{F},F)=[1]\in P_{pq}

Then
(\phi_{F},u_{F},F)\in Y_{pq}^{\mathbb{F},\epsilon}
. Hence
\lambda_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(\phi_{F},u_{F},F)=0
. This
G_{pq}
-orbit is then sent by the footprint map to the given point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
. Therefore the footprint map is surjective.

We prove that the footprint map is injective. Suppose
(\phi,u,F,0)
and
(\phi^{\prime},u^{\prime},F^{\prime},0)
are sent to the same point of
{}_{\lfloor}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
. By definition, there is a domain isomorphism
C_{\phi}\cong C_{\phi^{\prime}}
, which must be given by an element
g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
. As
\phi=\phi_{F}
,
\phi^{\prime}=\phi_{F^{\prime}}
, it follows that

g(\phi,u,F,0)=(\phi^{\prime},u^{\prime},F^{\prime},0).

As in the same
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-orbit there can only be one normalized
G_{pq}
-oribt, it follows that
g\in G_{pq}
. This implies that the footprint map is injective.
∎

Now we turn to the case of multimodules
{\mathbb{X}}:=\mathbb{X}^{\Sigma}
associated to a connected Floer domain
\Sigma
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Previously one has constructed a multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{Dom}}^{\mathbb{X}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
, together with the group reductions

\lambda_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:{}_{\lfloor}Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}/G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

which extends the group reductions on the involved flow categories of framed maps.

Definition 15.16
.

Given a thickening datum
\mathbb{W}^{\Sigma}
on the multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}}
(as a multimodule over the flow categories equipped with thickening data, enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}_{\rm rig}
), for objects
p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime}
, define the following objects.

(1)

The
thickened moduli space
is

V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:=V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:=\left\{(\phi,u,F,e)\ \left|\begin{array}[]{l}(\phi,u,F)\in{}_{\lfloor}Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}},\epsilon},\ e\in W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},\\
\overline{\partial}_{\sigma}u+\nu_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}(e)=0,\ \phi=\phi_{F}\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.\end{array}\right.\right\}

(2)

The
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
-action on
V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
is the restriction of the diagonal action on
{}_{\lfloor}Y_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X},\epsilon}\times W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
.

(3)

The
obstruction bundle
is the trivial bundle

E_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:=E_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:=\underline{\smash{Q}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\to V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}.

(4)

The
Kuranishi section
is (equivalent to) the map

S_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}(\phi,u,F,e)\mapsto(\lambda_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}(\phi,u,F),e)

where
\lambda_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
is the linearized group reduction.

The case of homotopies is the same. We omit the detailed discussions. We will use
{\mathbb{H}}
to indicate the relationship with a multimodule homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
coming from the Floer theory background.

15.3.3.
Transversality

Next we deal with transversality. For this we need to set up a certain Fredholm problem. For each pair of objects
p,q
, define

V_{pq}^{\prime}:=\Big\{(\phi,u,e)|\ \phi\in{}_{\lfloor}B^{\mathbb{F}}_{pq},e\in W_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}},u:C_{\phi}\to X\text{ satisfies }\overline{\partial}_{H}u+\nu_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}(e)=0\Big\}.

Lemma 15.17
.

There is a canonical
G_{pq}
-equivariant isomorphism
V_{pq}^{\prime}\cong V_{pq}
.

Proof.

We show the natural map
V_{pq}\to V_{pq}^{\prime}
is bijective. Indeed, given any
(\phi,u,e)\in V_{pq}^{\prime}
, the set of all frames
F
on it are related by an element of
{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
which linearly transforms the frame. Each frame
F
induces a map
\phi_{F}:C_{\phi}\to\mathbb{P}^{\bf d}
. The condition that
\phi_{F}=\phi
specifies a unique frame. Hence the map
V_{pq}\to V_{pq}^{\prime}
is surjective. As it is obviously injective and
G_{pq}
-equivariant, the lemma follows.
∎

For a fixed
\phi
, let
V_{pq}^{\prime}(\phi)\subseteq V_{pq}^{\prime}
be the preimage of
\phi
. Then
V_{pq}^{\prime}(\phi)
is the zero locus of a smooth Fredholm section defined on a certain Banach manifold of maps from
C_{\phi}
to
X
; the concrete choice of the Banach manifold depends on certain Sobolev parameters.

Definition 15.18
.

We say that
(\phi,u,e)\in V_{pq}^{\prime}(\phi)
is
transverse
if the linearization of the corresponding Fredholm section is surjective (which is independent of the Sobolev parameters). We say that the perturbation
\mathbb{W}
is
transverse
if all points of
V_{pq}^{\prime}(\phi)
for all
p,q
are transverse.

We can choose the thicikening data appropriately so that transversality holds.

Proposition 15.19
.

We omit the dependence on
\Omega
from the notations.

(1)

Given any integral modification of the topological energy, there exists a transverse thickening datum
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}}
on the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
, i.e., a transverse lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}_{\rm rig}
.

(2)

Consider the multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{X}}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\cdots{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
. Suppose
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}
,
\cdots
,
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
are transverse thickening data on these flow categories. Then there exists a transverse thickening datum
{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathbb{X}}
which is a multimodule over
(\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\cdots\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
.

(3)

Suppose
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}
resp.
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
are two transverse thickening data on
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\mathbb{X}_{1}
as multimodules over
(\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\cdots,\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
. Then there exists a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}_{\rm rig}
, denoted by
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
, as a homotopy from
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}
to
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
.

Proof.

See Subsubsection
15.3.6
.
∎

Transversality implies the regularity of the thickened moduli spaces.

Proposition 15.20
.

Suppose we choose transverse thickening data as claimed in Proposition
15.19
.

(1)

For each
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, the thickened moduli space
V_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
is an
A_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
-stratified topological
G_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
-manifold near
(S_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}})^{-1}(0)
.

(2)

Each thickened moduli space
V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
is an
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
-stratified topological
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
-manifold near
(S_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}})^{-1}(0)
.

(3)

Each thickened moduli space
V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}
is an
A_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}
-stratified topological
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}}
-manifold near
(S_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{H}})^{-1}(0)
.

Proof.

We only discuss the flow category case. By definition, transversality implies that each
V_{pq}^{\prime}(\phi)\cong V_{pq}
is a topological manifold near where
e=0
for all
\phi\in B_{pq}
. The gluing argument implies that
V_{pq}^{\prime}
is a stratified manifold. Since the thickening datum is collared,
V_{pq}^{\prime}
is also collared. Finally the canonical identification
V_{pq}^{\prime}\cong V_{pq}
shows
V_{pq}
is a collared manifold.
∎

15.3.4.
The topological AMS lifts

Now assuming transversality, by abuse of notations, the “regular locus” of
V_{pq}
is still denoted by
V_{pq}
(similar treatment for thickenings for multimodules and homotopies). Then one obtains topological Kuranishi spaces

K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq}),\ p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}.

Together with the structural maps, one obtains a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{F}}
to the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
, denoted by
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
.

Lemma 15.21
.

The topological AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
can be canonically lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Proof.

We only need to construct rigidifications of the embeddings

\iota_{prq}:K_{pr}\times K_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}K_{pq}.

By definition, one needs to find an orthogonal representation
N_{prq}
of
G_{pr}\times G_{rq}
and a germ of open embedding

G_{pq}\times_{G_{pr}\times G_{rq}}(K_{pr}\times K_{rq}\times N_{prq})\to\partial^{prq}K_{pq}

which extends
\iota_{prq}
. Let

W_{prq}\subseteq W_{pq}

be the orthogonal complement of
W_{pr}\oplus W_{rq}\subseteq W_{pq}
. Moreover, the underlying strict embedding in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
is also rigidified via a representation
Q_{prq}\subseteq Q_{pq}
. Set

N_{prq}:=W_{prq}\oplus Q_{prq}.

Then one can check that there is a natural strict open embedding

G_{pq}\times_{G_{prq}}\left({\rm Stab}_{N_{prq}^{\epsilon}}(K_{pr}\times K_{rq})\right)\to\partial^{prq}K_{pq}

(for a sufficiently small ball
N_{prq}^{\epsilon}\subseteq N_{prq}
) which extends
\iota_{prq}^{\mathbb{F}}
.
∎

15.3.5.
Multimodules and homotopies

Similarly, for the flow multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
induced from a Floer domain
\Sigma
, choose integral modifications

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{i}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{k},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{k}

(and the constant
n_{\mathbb{X}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, see Definition
15.2
). Make choices for

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}}^{\mathbb{fr}},

namely group reductions, by Lemma
15.9
, one can find a multimodule

{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{X}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}

over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{F}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}},\cdots{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\mathbb{fr}};{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{fr}})
enriched in the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf map}}{}_{\rm rig}^{\rm fr}
.

In parallel, choose collared and rigidified thickening data
\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}
resp.
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}
over
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}
resp.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\bm{\Omega}},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
, by Proposition
15.19
, one can find a thickening datum
{\mathbb{W}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}
as a multimodule over
(\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbb{W}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}})
. Then the data
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{map}}{}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
and
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{X}}
induce the collection of topological Kuranishi spaces

K_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=(G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},E_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},S_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}).

This provides a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{X}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
, denoted by
\widehat{\mathbb{X}}
and called a
topological AMS lift
of the multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{X}}
. It is a multimodule over
(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Similar to Lemma
15.21
, this lift can be further lifted (in a natural way) to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}{}_{\rm rig}
.

The case of homotopies is similar and omited.

15.3.6.
Proof of Proposition
15.19

The existence of a transverse thickening datum for a single moduli space was (essentially) given in
[
4
, Section 4.1]
. To construct a transverse thickening datum on a flow category, one needs a more systematic treatment.

All the constructions towards the proof is
ad hoc
.

Definition 15.22
.

Let
G
be a Lie group.

(1)

A
\mu
-representation
of a Lie group
G
is a direct system of complex representations
(W_{\mu})_{\mu\geq 1}
of
G
where
W_{\mu}\to W_{\mu+1}
is an embedding. A
\mu
-representation is said to be
exhaustive
if any finite-dimensional representation
R
of
G
can be embedded into
W_{\mu}
for
\mu
sufficiently large.

(2)

A single representation
W
is regarded as a
\mu
-representation with
W_{\mu}\equiv W
.

(3)

A
\mu
-representation is called
finite-dimensional
if all
W_{\mu}
are finite-dimensional; is called
unitary
if all
W_{\mu}
are equipped with
G
-invariant Hermitian inner products and the embeddings
W_{\mu}\to W_{\mu+1}
are isometric.

(4)

A
strict embedding
of
\mu
-representations from
(W_{\mu})
to
(W_{\mu}^{\prime})
is a sequence of
G
-equivariant linear isometric embeddings
W_{\mu}\to W_{\mu}^{\prime}
such that

\textstyle{W_{\mu}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{\mu}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{\mu+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{\mu+1}^{\prime}}

Recall the definition of the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. One can define another category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}
whose objects are
({\mathcal{G}},B,C,(W_{\mu}))
where
({\mathcal{G}},B,C)
is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
and
(W_{\mu})
is a finite-dimensional, exhaustive, and unitary
\mu
-representation of
G\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}
. One can similarly define strict embeddings; the morphisms are conjugacy classes of strict embeddings. Then there is an obvious forgetful functor

\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}\to\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}.

Proposition 15.23
.

(1)

There exist a lift of the monotone flow category
\mathbb{Dom}
to
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}
, denoted by
\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu}
.

(2)

For each smooth Floer domain
\Sigma
with
m
negative cylindrical ends and one positive cylindrical end
23
23

23

The case with zero positive cylindrical ends, e.g., the case for Poincaré pairing, can be proved as well.
, a lift of
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}
to
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}
, denoted by
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}_{\mu}
, which is a monotone multimodule over
(\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu};\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu})
.

(3)

For any 1-parameter family of Floer domains
\Sigma^{I}
and the associated homotopy
\mathbb{Dom}^{I}
from
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma_{1}}
, a lift to
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}
, denoted by
\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu}^{I}
, which is a monotone homotopy from
\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
\mathbb{Dom}_{\mu}^{\Sigma_{1}}
.

Proof.

Consider the standard unitary representation
W_{d}={\mathbb{C}}^{d}
of
G_{d}\cong U(d)
. The induced representation of the exterior algebra
\Lambda W_{d}
is also unitary. Then define the
\mu
-representation with

W_{d,\mu}:=\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\mu}(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}\right)^{\oplus 2^{\mu}}

whose Hermitian structure is the direct sum of the Hermitian structures of summands. The inclusion of
W_{d,\mu}
into
W_{d,\mu+1}
is the composition

W_{d,\mu}=\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\mu}(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}\right)^{\oplus 2^{\mu}}\to\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\mu+1}(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}\right)^{\oplus 2^{\mu}}\oplus\{0\}\\
\subseteq\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\mu+1}(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}\right)^{\oplus 2^{\mu}}\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\mu+1}(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}\right)^{\oplus 2^{\mu}}=W_{d,\mu+1}

which is isometric. As any finite-dimensional representation of
U(d)
is contained in a sufficiently higher tensor power
(\Lambda W_{d})^{\otimes i}
, one obtains a
\mu
-representation.

Then for any decomposition
d=d_{0}+d_{1}
, one has the canonical isometry

W_{d_{0}}\oplus W_{d_{1}}\cong W_{d}

which is equivariant with respect to
U(d_{0})\times U(d_{1})\to U(d)
. This map induces an equivariant isometric embeddings of the exterior algebras

\Lambda W_{d_{0}}\oplus\Lambda W_{d_{1}}\to\Lambda W_{d}

and hence isometric embeddings

W_{d_{0},\mu}\oplus W_{d_{1},\mu}\to W_{d,\mu}.

There is an obvious associativity property of such embeddings, which induces well-defined maps for each partition
d=d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}

W_{d_{0},\mu}\oplus\cdots\oplus W_{d_{l},\mu}\to W_{d,\mu}.

Then for each
d
, the quadruple
({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d},C_{d},(W_{d,\mu}))
is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\mu}
and they form a monotone flow category enriched in this category. The cases of multimodules and homotopies are the same; notice that in these cases, there are no well-defined strict embeddings but up to conjugacy, the embeddings are well-defined.
∎

We explain what we will do next. We explain for the flow category case. We will choose sufficiently large
\mu_{pq}
for each pair
p,q
inductively, such that
W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
can cover all possible cokernels of the linearized operator of the Floer equation.

First we recall the following definition and lemma from
[
4
]
with slight modification.

Definition 15.24
.

Let
G
be a compact Lie group acting smoothly on a manifold
B
and
\pi:V\to B
be a smooth complex
G
-vector bundle. A
finite-dimensional approximation scheme
is a finite-dimensional
\mu
-representation
(V_{\mu})
and a morphism of
\mu
-representations
\lambda_{\mu}:V_{\mu}\to C_{c}^{\infty}(B,V)
such that the union of
\lambda_{\mu}(V_{\mu})
over
\mu
is dense with respect to the
C^{\infty}_{\rm loc}
-topology.

Lemma 15.25
.

[
4
, Lemma 4.2]

There exists a finite-dimensional approximation scheme for given
G,B,V
.

Corollary 15.26
.

When
G=U(d)
, the finite-dimensional approximation scheme can be taken such that
V_{\mu}=W_{d,\mu}
where
(W_{d,\mu})
is the
\mu
-representation specified in the proof of Proposition
15.23
.

Proof.

This is because any
V_{\mu}
is contained in some
W_{\mu^{\prime}}
for
\mu^{\prime}
sufficiently large.
∎

Now we can prove Proposition
15.19
.

Proof of Proposition
15.19
.

We can choose a collection of collared thickening data

\nu_{pq,\mu}:W_{pq,\mu}\to C_{c}^{\infty}({}_{\lfloor}\mathring{C}_{pq}\times X,\Lambda^{0,1}_{{}_{\lfloor}\mathring{C}_{pq}/{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}}\otimes TX)

such that they are compatible with the structural maps of the flow category. Then using the compactness of each
M_{pq}
, there exists
\mu_{pq}
such that the image of
\nu_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
, when restricted to the domain of each solution, covers the cokernel of the linearization. Then we can choose
\mu_{pq}
inductively. Then define
W_{pq}:=W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
and
\nu_{pq}:=\nu_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
. This provides a transverse thickening datum on the flow category. The inductive construction on multimodules and homotopies are exactly the same.
∎

15.4.
Relatively smooth AMS lifts

The topological AMS lifts actually admit refinement in the so-called relatively smooth category (see
[
34
]
). The discussion in this category is important for eventually obtaining smooth Kuranishi spaces. We introduce the category of Kuranishi spaces in this version.

15.4.1.
Relative spaces and relative smooth structures

Definition 15.27

(Relative spaces)
.

(1)

A (stratified)
relative space
consists of a pair of
A
-stratified topological spaces
Y
,
S
and a stratified continuous map
\pi:Y\to S
. When
\pi
is understood from the context, we denote a relative space by
Y/S
.

(2)

A
relative map
from
Y/S
to
Z/T
is a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.75293pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.49197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.49197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.2628pt\raise-23.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.75293pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.75293pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.75293pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi(Z)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

When
S=T
, without further declaration, we will assume that the underlying map from
\pi(Y)
to
\pi(Z)
is the restriction of the identity map of
S
.

(3)

A
relative homeomorphism
is a relative map
\varphi:Y/S\to Z/T
consisting of a homeomorphism from
Y
to
Z
and a homeomorphism from
S
to
T
.

(4)

If
G
is a topological group, a
G
-action on relative space is a continuous action via relative homeomorphisms. In particular, the map
\pi:Y\to S
is equivariant.

(5)

Let
D
be another topological space. The
horizontal stabilization
of a relative space
Y/S
by
D
is the relative space

{\rm Stab}_{D}^{\rm hor}(Y/S)=(Y\times D)/(S\times D).

The
vertical stabilization
of
Y/S
is the relative space

{\rm Stab}_{D}^{\rm vert}(Y/S)=(Y\times D)/S.

Notice that both horizontal and vertical stabilizations are functors on the categories of relative spaces. Moreover, given
D_{1},D_{2}
, there is a natural transformation

{\rm Stab}_{D_{1}}^{\rm hor}\circ{\rm Stab}_{D_{2}}^{\rm vert}\to{\rm Stab}_{D_{2}}^{\rm vert}\circ{\rm Stab}_{D_{1}}^{\rm hor}.

Remark 15.28
.

A fiber bundle is a canonically a relative space. In particular, we denote by
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}/S
the relative space associated to the trivial
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
bundle over
S
.

Definition 15.29
.

Let
U/S\subseteq\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{m}/S
and
V/T\subseteq\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}/T
be open subsets. A continuous map
\varphi:U/S\to V/T
is called rel-
C^{\infty,k}
if the map
F:U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, determined by

\varphi(v,x)=(F(v,x),\underline{\smash{\varphi}}(x))

has all partial derivatives in
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
and those up order
k
varies continuously in
x\in S
uniformly on compact subsets of
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. A rel-
C^{\infty,k}
-map is called a rel-
C^{\infty,k}
-diffeomorphism if it has a rel-
C^{\infty,k}
-inverse.
\varphi
is called
relatively smooth
, or rel-
C^{\infty}
, if it is rel-
C^{\infty,k}
for all
k
.

Definition 15.30
.

A
relative smooth structure
on
Y/S
consists of an atlas
\varphi_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}/S\to\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{n}/S
which are rel-
C^{\infty}
-compatible. More precisely 1)
Y=\cup U_{\alpha}
and 2) for any
\alpha,\beta
, the map

\varphi_{\alpha}\circ\varphi_{\beta}^{-1}:\varphi_{\beta}((U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta})/S)\to\varphi_{\alpha}((U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta})/S)

is a rel-
C^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism.

Notice that if
Y/S
has a relative smooth structure, then each fiber of
Y
has a well-defined smooth structure. Then there is a well-defined rel-
C^{\infty}
vector bundle

T^{\rm vert}Y/S\to Y,

called the
vertical tangent bundle
.

15.4.2.
The category of relative smooth Kuranishi spaces

Definition 15.31

(Category of relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces)
.

(1)

A
relative smooth Kuranishi space
is a quadruple
K/B=(G,V/B,E,S)
where
(G,V,E,S)
is a topological Kuranishi space,
B
is a smooth
G
-manifold, together with a
G
-invariant rel-
C^{\infty}
structure on
V/B
.

(2)

A
stabilization
of a relative smooth Kuranishi space
K/B
by a
G
-equivariant disk bundle

D=D^{\rm hor}\oplus D^{\rm vert}\subseteq N^{\rm hor}\oplus N^{\rm vert}

is the relative Kuranishi space

{\rm Stab}_{D}(K/B)=(G,\tilde{V}/\tilde{B},\tilde{E},\tilde{S})

where
\tilde{V}/\tilde{B}
is the stabilization of the relative space
V/B
by
D
(which carries a canonically induced relative smooth structure),
\tilde{E}
and
\tilde{S}
are the natural stabilization.

(3)

A
strict embedding
of relative smooth Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}/B_{1}=(G_{1},V_{1}/B_{1},E_{1},S_{1})
to
K_{2}/B_{2}=(G_{2},V_{2}/B_{2},E_{2},S_{2})
consists of a strict embedding

\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}

(which includes a group embedding
\iota_{21}^{G}:G_{1}\to G_{2}
) and an
\iota_{21}^{G}
-equivariant smooth embedding
B_{1}\to B_{2}
.

(4)

An
embedding
of relative Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}/B_{1}
to
K_{2}/B_{2}
is a conjugacy class of strict embeddings.

(5)

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}
be the category of relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces whose morphisms are embeddings. One can also consider the rigidified and collared versions of this category.

Notice that there is an obvious functor

\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}\to\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}

by sending

(G,V/B,E,S)\mapsto(G,V,E,S).

Proposition 15.32

(Relative smooth structures on AMS lifts)
.

(1)

In Situation
Flow(i)
, any topological AMS lift of the Floer flow category is canonically lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

(2)

In Situation
Module(i)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
be the AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
as a multimodule over the topological AMS lifts
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. Then
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
is canonically lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

(3)

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
be a topological AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. Then
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
is canonically enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Proof.

We only discuss the case about the flow category. The other cases can be proved in the same manner. For each pair
p<q
of objects in
{\mathbb{F}}
, the fiberwise smooth structure of
{}_{\lfloor}V_{pq}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}
follows from the implicit function theorem. The fact that these fiberwise structures vary in the rel-
C^{\infty}
sense can be proved using the method of
[
68
]
(which treats the case of Gromov–Witten theory). Alternatively, it follows from the proof of
[
14
, Proposition 6.2]
.
∎

We summarize the construction so far in the following situations.

Situation Flow(iii)
.

In Situation
Flow(ii)
, the domain flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
induces an associated flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
. Choose a transverse thickening datum
{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathbb{F}}
and a group reduction, which gives a relatively smooth AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Situation Module(iii)
.

In Situation
Module(ii)
,
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
is a relatively smooth AMS lift subject to an outercollaring width and a compatible list of integral actions on the involved flow categories.
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
is a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
where each member of this tuple is a relative smooth AMS lift of an involved Floer flow category subject to the outercollaring width and the chosen integral action.

Situation Homotopy(iii)
.

In Situation
Homotopy(ii)
,
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
is a relatively smooth AMS lift of the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
subject to an outercollaring width and a compatible list of integral actions on the involved Floer flow categories based on the homotopy
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{H}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. The two boundaries of
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
are relatively smooth AMS lifts of multimodules
\mathbb{X}_{0}
resp.
\mathbb{X}_{1}
.

16.
Vertical Normal Complex Structures and orientations

16.1.
Main statements about normal complex structures

Recall that one has defined the category of relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}
. Given any object
K/B=(G,V/B,E,S)
, one has a well-defined vertical tangent bundle

T^{\rm vt}V\to V

which is a
G
-equivariant relatively smooth vector bundle. Recall the definition of equivariant normal complex structures (Definition
3.23
). Given a relatively smooth Kuranishi space
K/B=(G,V/B,E,S)
, a
relative NC structure
on
K/B
is by definition a
G
-equivariant NC structure on
T^{\rm vt}V\to V
. One can then define the category of relative NC Kuranishi spaces, denoted by

\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}^{\rm NC}.

We can also consider the rigidified and collared versions. We omit the detailed definitions.

Now we state our main theorems about the existence of fiberwise stable complex structures of Floer flow categories, multimodules, and homotopies.

Theorem 16.1
.

In Situation
Flow(iii)
, the relatively smooth AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
admits a set of lifts, denoted temporarily by
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}
, to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. We call such a lift a
relatively NC AMS lift
.

Proof.

See Subsection
16.3
.
∎

Remark 16.2
.

In the following theorem, what we call a relative NC AMS lifts of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
actually involves more data than the lifts to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
; they also include certain choices made for the construction. The construction of the relative NC AMS lifts for multimodules actually depends on these choices, not only on the resulting lifts of the flow categories.

Theorem 16.3
.

In Situation
Module(iii)
, suppose
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m};\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime}
are relatively NC AMS lifts of them provided by Theorem
16.1
. Then
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
admits a set of lifts, denoted temporarily by
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}
, to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a multimodule over
(\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m};\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime})
. Each of such
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}
is called a
relatively NC AMS lifts
(of
{\mathbb{X}}
or
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
).

Proof.

See Subsection
16.4
.
∎

Theorem 16.4
.

In Situation
Homotopy(iii)
, suppose
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m},\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime}
are relatively NC AMS lifts of the involved Floer flow categories,
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{0}
and
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{1}
are relatively NC AMS lifts of the two multimodules subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the fixed list of compatible integral actions. Then the relatively smooth AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
admits a lift, denoted by
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{H}}}}
temporarily, to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a homotopy from
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{0}
to
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{1}
. We call
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{H}}}}
a relatively NC AMS lift (of
{\mathbb{H}}
or
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
).

Proof.

A tedious reformulation of the proofs of Theorem
16.1
and Theorem
16.3
. There are no essential new ingredients.
∎

16.2.
Abouzaid–Blumberg’s construction for a single moduli space

We first review the construction of
[
1
, Section 11]
in the case of a single moduli space from the Floer flow category. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be the Floer flow category associated to the Hamiltonian
H
. Consider a pair of objects
p<q
such that there is no intermediate object
r
with
p<r<q
. Let
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})
be an AMS global chart of
M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
, viewed as an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}
. We can ignore
E_{pq}
and
S_{pq}
for the discussion but should remember the reletive structure
V_{pq}/B_{pq}
. We will also drop the outercollaring for a moment.

16.2.1.
A complex-linear Cauchy–Riemann operator and an interpolation

We first specify a family of complex linear Cauchy–Riemann operator for points in
V_{pq}
. Fix an almost complex structure
J_{0}
and a
J_{0}
-linear connection
\nabla^{TX}
such that its holonomy along each 1-periodic orbit of
H_{t}
is trivial. Choose
\tau>2
.

We first look at an individual smooth map
u:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to X
which converges exponentially to 1-periodic orbits of
H
. Define the space

\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)\subseteq W^{1,\tau}_{\rm loc}(u^{*}TX)

to be the space of sections which differ from
\nabla^{TX}
-parallel sections along the limiting periodic orbits by
W^{1,\tau}
-small terms near infinity. Then the
J_{0}
-linear connection
\nabla^{TX}
induces a complex-linear Cauchy–Riemann operator

D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}:\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u^{*}TX).

As
u
converges exponentially to periodic orbits, it is easy to see that
D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}
is Fredholm.

The operator
D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}
and the linearization of the Floer equation can be connected by a particular 1-parameter family of Fredholm operators modulo certain finite-dimensional corrections. Choose a cut-off function

\chi:{\rm Dom}(u)\cong{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to[0,1]

which only depends on the
{\mathbb{R}}
-variable such that
\chi(s)=0
for
s\ll 0
and
\chi(s)=1
for
s\gg 0
. For each object
p
, let
u_{p}:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to X
be the corresponding constant solution to the Floer equation (which does not depend on the capping). Then there is an associated linearized operator

D_{u_{p}}(\xi)=\partial_{s}\xi+J_{t}\left(\nabla_{t}\xi-\nabla_{\xi}X_{H_{t}}(p)\right).

Let

\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)\subseteq W^{1,\tau}_{\rm loc}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)

be the subspace of
W^{1,\tau}_{\rm loc}
-sections which satisfies the same asymptotic condition near
-\infty
as
\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)
and which is
W^{1,\tau}
-small near
+\infty
. Then consider the interpolation

\begin{split}D_{p}:\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)\to&\ L^{\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u_{p}^{*}TX)\\
\xi\mapsto&\ (1-\chi)(\nabla^{TX}\xi)_{J_{0}}^{0,1}+\chi D_{u_{p}}(\xi).\end{split}

(16.1)

Then
D_{p}
is a real-linear Fredholm operator. Notice that from the definition,
D_{p}
only depends on the underlying 1-periodic orbit but not the capping.

We now choose, for each object
p
(actually, as discussed above, only depending on the underlying orbit), a finite-dimensional complex vector space
R_{p}^{+}
, a linear map

\nu_{p}:R_{p}^{+}\to C_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u_{p}^{*}TX)

(16.2)

such that the map

\nu_{p}\oplus D_{p}:R_{p}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u_{p}^{*}TX)

is surjective. Denote

R_{p}^{-}:={\rm Ker}(\theta_{p}\oplus D_{p})\subseteq R_{p}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u^{*}TX).

Notice that the pair
(R_{p}^{-},R_{p}^{+})
does not depend on the Sobolev exponent
\tau
.

Now we would like to construct interpolations between
D_{u}
and
D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}
, up to factors coming from
D_{p}
and
D_{q}
, in a 1-parameter family. The basic idea has been described in
[
1
, Section 11]
and
[
2
, Appendix B]
. However, we need to be more careful when generalizing to the case of multimodules.

More precisely, we would like to construct an interpolation between

D_{p}\oplus D_{u}:\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u_{p}^{*}TX)\oplus W^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u_{p}^{*}TX)\oplus L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u^{*}TX)

and

D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}\widetilde{\oplus}D_{q}:\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)\widetilde{\oplus}\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u_{q}^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u_{q}^{*}TX).

Here
\widetilde{\oplus}
means the subset of the direct sum satisfying the matching condition.

To build the interpolation, consider the family of cut-off functions

\chi_{\rho}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1],\ \chi_{\rho}(s)=\chi(s-\rho).

Then for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, define

\begin{split}D_{u,\rho}:\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(u^{*}TX)\to&\ L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes u^{*}TX),\\
\xi\mapsto&\ (1-\chi_{\rho})(\nabla^{TX}\xi)_{J_{0}}^{0,1}+\chi_{\rho}D_{u}(\xi).\end{split}

(16.3)

Notice that even if
u
has bubble components, these operators are still defined. Then intuitively, we have

\displaystyle\ \lim_{\rho\to-\infty}D_{u,\rho}\approx D_{p}\oplus D_{u},\

\displaystyle\ \lim_{\rho\to+\infty}D_{u,\rho}\approx D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}\widetilde{\oplus}D_{q}.

This is the underlying idea of interpolating the linearized operator
D_{u}
and a complex linear operator
D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}
, up to the two operators
D_{p}
and
D_{q}
which are independent of
u
.

To better formulate this construction over the whole Kuranishi chart
V_{pq}
, we need to introduce an auxiliary space. Denote

\widetilde{B}_{pq}:=B_{pq}\times[-\infty,+\infty],

viewed as a product of stratified spaces where
[-\infty,+\infty]
has one open stratum and two boundary strata. We define an auxiliary universal curve
\widetilde{C}_{pq}\to\widetilde{B}_{pq}
whose fiber
\widetilde{C}_{\phi,\rho}
at
(\phi,\rho)
with
\phi\in B_{pq}
and
\rho\in(-\infty,+\infty)
is still
C_{\phi}
, while for
\rho=-\infty
resp.
\rho=+\infty
,
\widetilde{C}_{\phi,\rho}
has the additional component of the domain of the map
u_{p}
resp. the domain of
u_{q}
. One can then define the additional space
\widetilde{V}_{pq}
which is identical to
V_{pq}\times[-\infty,+\infty]
. However, for each point
(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, we regard
u
as a map
\widetilde{u}:\widetilde{C}_{\phi,\rho}\to X
where if
\rho=\pm\infty
,
\widetilde{u}
maps the extra cylindrical component to the constant loop at the negative or the positive end. Then
\widetilde{V}_{pq}\to\widetilde{B}_{pq}
is still relatively smooth. The operator
D_{u,\rho}
can be viewed as a Cauchy–Riemann operator
D_{\widetilde{u}}
acting on the bundle
\widetilde{u}^{*}TX
(and extended to suitable Sobolev completions). We remark that, this setup facilitates the gluing construction: when
\rho\in\pm\infty
, if the operator
D_{u,\rho}
is surjective, then each vector in the kernel can be glued to an element in the kernel at a nearby point.

With the above viewpoint understood, using basic gluing construction, one obtains the following technical lemma. Let

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq}

be the infinite-dimensional vector bundle whose fiber over
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)
is the space

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}=\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(\widetilde{C}_{\phi,\rho},\widetilde{u}^{*}TX).

Notice that when
\rho=\pm\infty
, one has the splitting

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}=\Omega_{c}^{0,1}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)\oplus\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

(16.4)

or

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}=\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)\oplus\Omega_{c}^{0,1}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{q}^{*}TX).

(16.5)

Lemma 16.5
.

There exist a unitary representation
W_{pq}
of
G_{pq}
and a continuous
G_{pq}
-equivariant bundle map over
\widetilde{V}_{pq}\times X
