\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}:\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}\to\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}

satisfying the following conditions. Let
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
where
\widetilde{\phi}=(\phi,\rho)\in\widetilde{B}_{pq}
.

(1)

When
\rho=-\infty
, with respect to the splitting (
16.4
),
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
has the block form

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\nu_{p}&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right]

where
\nu_{p}
is specified in (
16.2
).

(2)

When
\rho=+\infty
, with respect the splitting (
16.5
),
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
has the block form

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&\nu_{\widetilde{x}}&0\\
0&0&\nu_{q}\end{array}\right]

where

\nu_{\widetilde{x}}:W_{pq}\to\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

is a complex-linear linear map.

(3)

For each
\widetilde{x}\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, the image of
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
at
\widetilde{x}
is transverse to the image of the operator
D_{\widetilde{u}}
.

Proof.

Basic Fredholm theory and a portion of gluing argument.
∎

16.2.2.
An extended index bundle

Now assume
W_{pq}
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
is given as in Lemma
16.5
, consider the family of linear maps parametrized by
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
:

\nu_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{\widetilde{u}}:\big(R_{p}^{+}\oplus W_{pq}\oplus R_{q}^{+}\big)\oplus\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(\widetilde{u}^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}(\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\widetilde{u}^{*}TX).

Proposition 16.6
.

The union of kernels of
\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{\widetilde{u}}
form a continuous
24
24

24

In fact it is a relative smooth vector bundle.
vector bundle

\widetilde{I}_{pq}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq}.

Proof.

The proof is based on the typical gluing construction. However, one needs to maintain the linear structure in the gluing procedure. The pregluing is constructed using parallel transport on the target manifold
X
and cut-paste on the domain, which are linear in the input. The use of the implicit function theorem can also respect the linear feature.
∎

Hence the vector bundles on the two boundary pieces,
\partial^{\pm}\widetilde{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times\{\pm\infty\}\cong V_{pq}
are (non-canonically) isomorphic. The family of kernels selects a homotopy class of isomorphisms. By the specific characterization of
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
given in Lemma
16.5
, one can see that

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\cong\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{-}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+};

and

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\cong\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{-}.

Here
I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
is formed by the kernels of the complex-linear operator

\nu_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}:W_{pq}\oplus\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)\to L^{\tau}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

hence is a complex vector bundle.

We can then choose a continuous bundle isomorphism

\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{-}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}\cong\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{-}.

Notice that
G_{pq}
acts trivially on the vector spaces
R_{p}^{\pm}
and
R_{q}^{\pm}
. As
W_{pq}
is complex, one can see that this is a stable isomorphism from
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
to
I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
and hence induces an NC structure on
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
.

16.3.
Proof of Theorem
16.1

16.3.1.
An extended Kuranishi flow category

We extend the previous construction to all moduli spaces involved in a single Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
. To construct the interpolation in a compatible way for all moduli spaces, we utilize the lateral lines (see Definition
14.5
). Recall that for each pair of objects
p\leq q
, in the AMS construction there is a
G_{pq}
-manifold
B_{pq}
parametrizing certain curves in
\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
. Consider the moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2,+}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},d_{pq})

of 1-marked stable cylinders with the interior marked points lying on the lateral lines of domains. There is the forgetful map with
{\mathbb{R}}
as generic fibers

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2,+}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},d_{pq})\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},d_{pq}).

Let
\widetilde{B}_{pq}
be the preimage of
B_{pq}
; let
\widetilde{\phi}
denote a typical element of
\widetilde{B}_{pq}
with the underlying point
\phi\in B_{pq}
. We use the convention that this interior marked point does not go to sphere bubbles but only stay in cylindrical components; namely, it can coincide with a node. Let

\widetilde{C}_{pq}\to\widetilde{B}_{pq}

be the pullback of
C_{pq}\to B_{pq}
, which still gives an equivariant family of curves

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}:=({\mathcal{G}}_{pq},\widetilde{B}_{pq},\widetilde{C}_{pq}).

Let
C_{\widetilde{\phi}}\subseteq\widetilde{C}_{pq}
be the fiber over
\widetilde{\phi}\in\widetilde{B}_{pq}
. Then there is a natural map

C_{\widetilde{\phi}}\to C_{\phi}

which collapses at most one constant cylindrical component. Notice that for each
\widetilde{\phi}\in\widetilde{B}_{pq}
, there is a special cylindrical component

C_{\widetilde{\phi}}^{\star}\subseteq C_{\widetilde{\phi}}

which contains the marked point.

Notice that there two disjoint closed subsets

\displaystyle\ \partial^{-}\widetilde{B}_{pq},\

\displaystyle\ \partial^{+}\widetilde{B}_{pq}

corresponding to configurations where the marked points is at the very left resp. very right of the curve. Notice that one has canonical identifications

\partial^{-}\widetilde{B}_{pq}\cong B_{pq}\cong\partial^{+}\widetilde{B}_{pq}.

Notice that
\widetilde{B}_{pq}
and
\widetilde{C}_{pq}
can also be organized as morphism spaces of a flow category. However, as they are objects in a special category which does not have an obvious symmetric monoidal structure, therefore, we will describe the flow category structure in a more
ad hoc
way, without specifying an underlying category of stratified objects. Define a “product”
\widetilde{B}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{rq}
via the diagram

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.53438pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.89206pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{B}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.8pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.19446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.8pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{B}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.84712pt\raise-24.19446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-24.53438pt\raise-31.52777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{pr}\times B_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.1136pt\raise-38.73111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.23114pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\zeta_{prq}^{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.53438pt\raise-31.52777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.53438pt\raise-31.52777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}},

which we require to be a pullback. The “product” universal curves also be defined as a pullback, which admits a domain map

\widetilde{C}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{C}_{rq}\to\widetilde{B}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{rq}.

Now suppose we are in Situation
Flow(iii)
where
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
is a relativley smooth AMS lift of the Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. For each pair of
p\leq q
, let

\widetilde{V}_{pq}\to\widetilde{B}_{pq}

be the pullback of
V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
. Then one can define similarly
\widetilde{V}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{V}_{rq}
and corresponding maps

\widetilde{V}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{V}_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}\widetilde{V}_{pq}.

Denote

\widetilde{V}_{pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q}:=\widetilde{V}_{pr_{1}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\widetilde{V}_{r_{l}q},

which is well-defined due to the associativity assumption of compositions of the flow category. Notice that there is also a corresponding stratum

\partial^{pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q}\widetilde{V}_{pq}\subseteq\widetilde{V}_{pq}

which contains
\widetilde{V}_{pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q}
.

16.3.2.
The construction of the normal complex structure

—
The family of Cauchy–Riemann operators
—Then we would like to build a family of Fredholm operators indexed by
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{V}_{pq}
. Fix
\tau>2
. The construction appears to depend on
\tau
but the resulting normal complex structure does not. Notice that for each
\widetilde{x}\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, there are a pair of Banach spaces

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{1}:=\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}(C_{\widetilde{\phi}},\widetilde{u}^{*}TX),\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}:=L^{\tau}(C_{\widetilde{\phi}},\Lambda^{0,1}\otimes\widetilde{u}^{*}TX).

We regard them as “vector bundles” over
\widetilde{V}_{pq}
only in a set-theoretic sense, denoted by

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{1}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq}.

However, we keep in mind that the induced index-theoretic constructions over the finite-dimensional moduli spaces is nevertheless well-defined as topological objects, and we can perform gluing constructions over the nodal locus to make sense of them.

We give an
ad hoc
construction of a family of Cauchy–Riemann operators between these spaces. First we construct a family of cut-off functions.

Lemma 16.7
.

There exist a collection of
G_{pq}
-invariant cut-off functions

\chi_{pq}:{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{C}_{pq}\to[0,1]

for all pairs of objects
p\leq q
of
{\mathbb{F}}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

\chi_{pq}\equiv 1
on components to the right of
C_{\widetilde{\phi}}^{\star}
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{pq}\equiv 0
on components to the left of
C_{\widetilde{\phi}}^{\star}
.

(2)

On
C_{\widetilde{\phi}}^{\star}
,
\chi_{pq}=0
for
s\ll 0
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{pq}=1
for
s\gg 0
where
s
is a cylindrical coordinate.

(3)

\chi_{pq}
is collared.

(4)

\chi_{pq}
respects the flow category structure. Namely, whenever
p<r<q
and
\widetilde{\phi}\in{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{C}_{pr}\boxtimes{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{C}_{rq}
, if the marked point is on the
pr
-section then on this part
\chi_{pq}
coincides with
\chi_{pr}
; if the marked point is on the
rq
-section then on this section
\chi_{pq}
coincides with
\chi_{rq}
.

(5)

\chi_{pq}
respect the rigidification.
\chi_{pq}
does not vary with the normal direction near
{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{C}_{pr}\boxtimes{}_{\lfloor}\widetilde{C}_{rq}
inside
\partial^{prq}\widetilde{C}_{pq}
.

Proof.

An inductive construction similar to the proof of Theorem
N
, which is standard at this point.
∎

Now for each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, consider the Cauchy–Riemann operator

D_{\widetilde{x}}:{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}

given by the same formula as (
16.3
). We would like to consider the index bundles. Let

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}

be the subspace of compactly supported smooth sections and let

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq}

denote the corresponding “subbundle.”

Definition 16.8
.

An
index bundle interpolator
over the Kuranishi flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
consists of the following items.

(1)

For each object
p\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, a finite-dimensional complex vector space
R_{p}^{+}
and a complex-linear map

\nu_{p}:R_{p}^{+}\to\Omega_{c}^{0,1}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)

which is transverse to the image of
D_{p}
. We require that
R_{p}
and
\nu_{p}
only depends on the
\Pi
-orbit, i.e., the underlying uncapped orbit
\underline{\smash{p}}
. Denote

R_{p}^{-}={\rm ker}(\nu_{p}\oplus D_{p})\subseteq R_{p}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{W}^{1,\tau}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX),

which is only a real vector space.

(2)

For each
p\leq q
, a finite-dimensional Hermitian representations
W_{pq}
of
G_{pq}
and a
G_{pq}
-equivariant complex-linear bundle maps over
\widetilde{V}_{pq}

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}:\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}\to\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}.

(3)

For each triple
p<r<q
, a complex-linear map

\widetilde{\iota}_{prq}:W_{pr}\oplus R_{r}^{+}\oplus W_{rq}\to W_{pq}

which is equivariant with respect to the group homomorphism
G_{pr}\times G_{rq}\to G_{pq}
.

They are required to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

(The original index bundle)
For each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, with respect to the natural decomposition

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}\cong\Omega^{0,1}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p}^{*}TX)\oplus\Omega^{0,1}_{c}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

we have

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\nu_{p}&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right]

(2)

(The complex index bundle)
For each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\partial^{+}\widetilde{B}_{pq}
, with respect to the natural decomposition

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}\cong\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)\oplus\Omega_{c}^{0,1}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{q}^{*}TX)

we have

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&\nu_{\widetilde{x}}&0\\
0&0&\nu_{q}\end{array}\right].

Here
\nu_{\widetilde{x}}:W_{pq}\to\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)
is a complex-linear map.

(3)

(Flow category structure I)
The above conditions imply that
\widetilde{\nu}_{pr}
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{rq}
determine a bundle map

\widetilde{\nu}_{prq}:(\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pr}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{r}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{rq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+})|_{\widetilde{V}_{prq}}\to\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}^{0}\boxplus\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}^{0}.

We require that the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\big(\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pr}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{r}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{rq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}\big)|_{\widetilde{V}_{prq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\nu}_{prq}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{R_{p}^{+}}\oplus\widetilde{\iota}_{prq}\oplus{\rm Id}_{R_{q}^{+}}}
\textstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}^{0}\boxplus\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}|_{\partial^{prq}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}}
\textstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{0}|_{\partial^{prq}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}}

(4)

(Flow category structure II)
Whenever
p<r<s<q
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{W_{pr}\oplus R_{r}^{+}\oplus W_{rs}\oplus R_{s}^{+}\oplus W_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}_{prs}\oplus{\rm Id}_{R_{s}^{+}}\oplus{\rm Id}_{W_{sq}}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{W_{pr}\oplus{\rm Id}_{R_{r}^{+}}\oplus\widetilde{\iota}_{rsq}}}
\textstyle{W_{ps}\oplus R_{s}^{+}\oplus W_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}_{psq}}
\textstyle{W_{pr}\oplus R_{r}^{+}\oplus W_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}_{prq}}
\textstyle{W_{pq}}

(5)

(Transversality)
For each
\widetilde{x}\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, the image
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
contained in
\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}
is transverse to the image of
D_{\widetilde{x}}
(which is a condition true for all
\tau>2
).

(6)

(Collar)
The maps
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
satisfy the natural collaring condition. Namely, near each stratum
\partial^{\alpha}\widetilde{V}_{pq}
,
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
is independent of the collar coordinates.

(7)

(Rigidification)

\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
does not vary in the normal direction to
\widetilde{V}_{prq}
specified by the rigidfication.

(8)

(Novikov equivariance)
These objects are equivariant with respect to the natural
\Pi
-action.

Lemma 16.9
.

There exists an index bundle interpolator on
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
.

Proof.

This is an inductive construction. The spaces
R_{p}^{+}
and the maps
\nu_{p}
can be chosen independently for all objects. The construction of Abouzaid–Blumberg recalled previously provides the required space
W_{pq}
and the bundle map
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
for
p<q
which does not have intermediate objects. Suppose for a pair of objects
p<q
, one has constructed such structures for all
r<s
with
d_{r}-d_{s}<d_{p}-d_{q}
, then for
W_{pq}
, one can choose initially
W_{pq}
to be the direct sum of all
W_{pr}\oplus R_{p}^{+}\oplus W_{rq}
for intermediate objects
r
. The value of
\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}
on the boundary is then determined by first three conditions of Definition
16.8
as well as the rigidification condition. The collar condition allows us to extend slightly into the interior of
\widetilde{V}_{pq}
. The transversality achieved on the boundary strata is carried over to such an extension. To achieve global transversality, one may need to enlarge
W_{pq}
by taking direct sum with another complex vector space.
∎

Once the index bundle interpolator is chosen, one automatically obtains a system of vector bundles over the spaces
\widetilde{V}_{pq}
. Indeed, for each
\widetilde{x}\in\widetilde{V}_{pq}
, denote

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\widetilde{x}}:={\rm ker}(\widetilde{\nu}_{pq}|_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{\widetilde{x}})\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{1}|_{\widetilde{x}}.

They form a topological vector bundle
\widetilde{I}_{pq}\to\widetilde{V}_{pq}
. We consider its restriction to
\partial^{\pm}\widetilde{V}_{pq}
. By the first two conditions of Definition
16.8
, one has
G_{pq}
-equivariant identifications

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\cong\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{-}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}

and

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\cong\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{-}.

Here
I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
has fiber over
\widetilde{x}
being

{\rm ker}(\nu_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}})

hence a
G_{pq}
-equivariant complex vector bundle. Notice that
\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pq}\cong V_{pq}\cong\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pq}
canonically and the inclusions of
V_{pq}
into
\widetilde{V}_{pq}
from both sides are
G_{pq}
-equivariant homotopy equivalences. Hence the two restriction bundles are equivariantly isomorphic.

To construct a normal complex structure on the flow category, one must choose such bundle isomorphisms in a way compatible with the flow category structure. Notice that the product
\widetilde{V}_{prq}\cong\widetilde{V}_{pr}\boxtimes\widetilde{V}_{rq}
, it is the union

\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{rq}\underset{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{rq}}{\cup}\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\widetilde{V}_{rq}.

Hence
\widetilde{I}_{pr}
and
\widetilde{I}_{rq}
determine a vector bundle

\widetilde{I}_{prq}\to\widetilde{V}_{prq}

which is
\widetilde{I}_{pr}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{r}^{-}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{rq}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q}^{+}
on the first piece and which is
\underline{\smash{R}}_{p}^{+}\oplus I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{rq}
on the second piece.

Lemma 16.10
.

There exists a system of
G_{pq}
-equivariant real vector bundle isomorphisms

\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}\cong\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pq}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Whenever
p<r<q
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{prq}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{rq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{prq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{rq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{prq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{pr}\times\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{rq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{prq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{pq}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{prq}}}

(2)

The isomorphisms are collared and respect the rigidification.

Proof.

Induction and homotopy triviality of the interpolating interval
[0,1]
.
∎

Now as
I_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
is complex and
R_{p}^{\pm}
,
R_{q}^{\pm}
are trivial representations of
G_{pq}
, the isomorphism induces an NC structure on
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
. The compatibility condition given in Lemma
16.10
implies that the structural maps of the flow category respect the vertical NC structures. Therefore, one obtains a lift of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
16.1
.

16.4.
Proof of Theorem
16.3

Let
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
be the smooth Floer domain in Situation
Module(iii)
. One still needs to relate the linearization of the Floer equation with a complex-linear operator on
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
via a certain interpolation. However, in order to be compatible with chosen interpolations for the involved flow categories, the interpolation here needs to depend on generally
m
parameters instead of just one, where
m
is the number of negative cylindrical ends, or more generally, the number of lateral lines in
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
.

Let
\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m}\subseteq\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
be the lateral lines. Choose diffeomorphisms

\rho_{j}:\ell_{j}\cong{\mathbb{R}}

such that on cylindrical ends,
\rho_{j}
coincides with the radial coordinates up to a shift. Let

\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

be the version of the moduil
B_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
whose elements are
(\phi,z_{1},\ldots,z_{m})
where
z_{j}
is contained in the
j
-th lateral line
\ell_{j}\subseteq C_{\phi}
and

{\rm either}\ \rho_{j}(z_{j})\leq 0\ \forall j\ \ {\rm or}\ \rho_{1}(z_{1})=\cdots=\rho_{m}(z_{m})\geq 0.

Compactify the spaces to allow the marked points to escape from cylindrical ends: notice that they can either escape from negative ends independently, or escape from the positive end simultaneously. Then the foregetful map

\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to B_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

has generic fibers being homeomorphic to a
m
-dimensional complex. Notice that the functions
\rho_{j}
extend to a continuous and
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
-invariant map

\rho:\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to[-\infty,+\infty]^{m}

such that either all
\rho_{j}
are negative or all
\rho_{j}
are nonnegative and identical.

Moreover, one can pullback the universal curve to

\widetilde{C}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

Similar to before, one has “structural maps”

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{B}_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}},\

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

which satisfy the usual compatibility condition.

We choose compatible cut-off functions, whose existence follow again from an induction argument.

Lemma 16.11
.

Suppose
\chi_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{\mathbb{F}_{j}}
and
\chi_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
have been chosen independently as given by Lemma
16.7
. Then there exists a family of
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
-invariant continuous functions

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}:\widetilde{C}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\to[0,1]

satisfying the following conditions. We characterize the property by considering its restriction to the fiber at each point
\widetilde{\phi}\in\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
. Denote the restriction by
\chi_{\widetilde{\phi}}:C_{\widetilde{\phi}}\to[0,1]
.

(1)

When
\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{m}\leq 0
, the support of
\chi_{\widetilde{\phi}}
is the disjoint union of
m
connected cylindrical regions contained in the
m
negative ends of
C_{\widetilde{\phi}}
.

(2)

When
\rho_{1}=\ldots=\rho_{m}\gg 0
, the support of
\chi_{\widetilde{\phi}}
is contained in the positive cylindrical end.

(3)

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
respects the structural maps of the multimodule about the concatenation from the negative side. More precisely, when
p_{j}<q_{j}
and
\widetilde{\phi}\in\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
,
\chi_{\widetilde{\phi}}
is determined by
\chi_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}
and
\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
in the canonical way.

(4)

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
respects the structural map of the multimodule about concatenation from the positive side. More precisely, if
\widetilde{\phi}\in\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
, then
\chi_{\widetilde{\phi}}
is canonically determined by
\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
and
\chi_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}
.

(5)

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
is collared. Namely, near each stratum, it is constant in collar coordinates.

(6)

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
respects the rigidification.

(7)

The collection of functions are equivariant with respect to the action by
\Pi^{m}
. Namely, if
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in\Pi
and
q_{1}=a_{1}p_{1},\ldots,q_{m}=a_{m}p_{m}
,
q^{\prime}=(a_{1}+\cdots+a_{m})p^{\prime}
, with respect to the identification
\widetilde{C}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cong\widetilde{C}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
, one has

\chi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=\chi_{q_{1}\cdots q_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

Now similar to the flow category case, for each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
, there are a pair of Banach spaces

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{1},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}

which form equivariant “vector bundles”

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{1},\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}.

The cut-off functions also induce a family of Cauchy–Riemann operators

D_{\widetilde{x}}:{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}.

Before we state the definition of index bundle interpolators for the multimodule case, we specify certain boundary strata of
\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
. For
j=1,\ldots,m
, there is a corresponding codimension 1 stratum

\partial^{j}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

corresponding to configurations where the
j
-th marked point goes to the very left of the underlying curve. There is also a positive end

\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

corresponding to configurations where all the
m
marked points go to the very right of the underlying curve. Notice that we also have a canonical identification

\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:=\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\partial^{j}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}.

We consider the compactly supported version of
{\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}
, which is denoted by

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}.

We give the analogue of Definition
16.8
.

Definition 16.12
.

Suppose we have chosen index bundle interpolators over
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
independently. An
index bundle interpolator
over
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
(compatible with the existing index bundle interpolators) consists of the following items.

(1)

A collection of finite-dimensional unitary representations
W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
of
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
.

(2)

A collection of equivariant complex-linear bundle maps

\widetilde{\nu}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\to\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}

(3)

A collection of equivariant complex linear maps

\widetilde{\iota}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}(p_{i}q_{i})p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}:W_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{\mathbb{F}_{i}}\oplus R_{q_{i}}^{+}\oplus W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\to W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}.

(4)

A collection of equivariant complex linear maps

\widetilde{\iota}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}:W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus R_{q^{\prime}}^{+}\oplus W_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\to W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

They are required to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For each
j
and
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\partial^{j}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
,
\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{x}}
sends
R_{p_{j}}^{+}
via
\nu_{p_{j}}
into the component over the domain of
u_{p_{j}}
while sends the complement of
R_{p_{j}}^{+}
into other components.

(2)

(The original index bundle)
For each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
, with respect to the decomposition

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{\widetilde{x}}^{0}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\Omega_{c}^{0,1}({\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1},u_{p_{j}}^{*}TX)\oplus\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

we have

\widetilde{\nu}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{p_{j}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right].

(3)

(The complex index bundle)
For each
\widetilde{x}=(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{u},e,F)\in\partial^{+}\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
, with respect to the decomposition

\widetilde{\nu}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&\nu_{\widetilde{x}}&0\\
0&0&\nu_{p^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]

where

\nu_{\widetilde{x}}:W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to\Omega_{c}^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

is a complex-linear map.

(4)

(Flow multimodule structure I)
For each
j
and
p_{j}<q_{j}
, the above conditions induces the following bundle maps

\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q_{j}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\to\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}.

Then the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{i}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{q_{j}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}|_{\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{i}}^{+}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{E}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{0}}

(5)

(Flow multimodule structure II)
For each
i
and
p_{i}<r_{i}<q_{i}
, the following diagram of linear maps commutes.

\textstyle{W_{p_{j}q_{j}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}}\oplus R_{q_{j}}^{+}\oplus W_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Lemma 16.13
.

Given index bundle interpolators for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
, there exists an index bundle interpolator for
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
.

Proof.

Induction.
∎

Then we obtain a family of continuous real
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
-equivariant vector bundles

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

whose fiber over
\widetilde{x}
is the kernel of the operator

\widetilde{\nu}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus D_{\widetilde{x}}.

We examine its restrictions to specific lower strata. By one of the conditions of Definition
16.12
, one has

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{-}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

which is a stabilization of the original vertical tangent bundle. On the other hand,

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{+}\oplus I_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{-}

where
I_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{C}}
is fiberwise the kernel of the complex-linear operator

\nu_{\widetilde{x}}\oplus D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}.

Since both the negative and positive inclusions

V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{\pm}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

are equivariant homotopy equivalences, the above two restriction bundles are isomorphic. One needs to choose a compatible system of isomorphisms in order to obtain a relative normal complex structure on the Kuranishi multimodule.

We consider certain codimension 1 strata of the thickenings.

(1)

Suppose
p_{j}<q_{j}
in
{\mathbb{F}}_{j}
. Then there is a corresponding relative space
\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{B}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
. Notice that there are further lower strata of the form

\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

corresponding to where the
j
-th marked point is on the very left,

\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\times\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

where all the marked points are on the very right. Notice that here we have the usual direct product but not
\boxtimes
. There is also an intermediate strata

\partial^{0}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}

corresponding to configurations where the
j
-th marked point is at the breaking at
q_{j}
and all other marked points are on the very left. Notice that all these strata are isomorphic with
V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
. Notice that, the bundle
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
, when restricted to
\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
, contains a subbudnle
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
coming from the index bundle interpolators, whose restrictions to the above three strata are canonically identified with

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\\
\cong\widetilde{I}_{p_{j}q_{j}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}},

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{0}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\\
\cong\widetilde{I}_{p_{j}q_{j}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}},

and

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}(p_{j}q_{j})p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\\
\cong\widetilde{I}_{p_{j}q_{j}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{j-1}q_{j}p_{j+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}.

(2)

Suppose
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then there is a corresponding relative space
\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{B}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\boxtimes\widetilde{B}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}
. The three further lower strata are

\begin{split}\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\cong&\ \partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}},\\
\partial^{0}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\cong&\ \partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}},\\
\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\cong&\ \partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\times\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}.\end{split}

The extended index bundle
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
, when restricted to then, has the subbundle
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}
and is identified respectively as

\begin{split}\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\cong&\ \widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}},\\
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{0}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\cong&\ \widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}},\\
\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\cong&\ \widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}}\oplus\widetilde{I}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

Lemma 16.14
.

Given bundle isomorphisms

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{I}_{p_{j}q_{j}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}}\cong\widetilde{I}_{p_{j}q_{j}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{j}q_{j}}},\

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{I}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}\cong\widetilde{I}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}}

satisfying conditions listed in Lemma
16.10
, there exists a system of
G_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
-equivariant real vector bundle isomorphisms

\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}\cong\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

When
p_{j}<q_{j}
, the following diagram commutes

(2)

When
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\times\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}\times\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{-}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{I}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}|_{\partial^{+}\widetilde{V}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}}

(3)

The isomorphisms are collared.

(4)

The isomorphisms respect the rigidifications.

Proof.

Induction.
∎

Now using the identifications of the extended index bundles over the negative and positive strata, one obtains the equivariant bundle isomorphisms

\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{-}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{+}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\smash{R}}_{p_{j}}^{+}\oplus I_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{p^{\prime}}^{-}

In particular, this gives an equivariant normal complex structure (see Definition
3.23
) of the vertical tangent bundle
T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
. Hence the chart
K_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}
becomes an object of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. The conditions of Lemma
16.14
imply that the structural maps are also morphisms in this category. Hence one obtains a lift of the Kuranishi flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
16.3
.

16.5.
Orientations

We explain how to obtain coherent orientations on the AMS construction. We give orientations on the tangent bundles of the domain moduli spaces as well as the vertical tangent bundles. After the stable smoothing to be done in the next section, they induce coherent orientations on the AMS lifts.

We first look at the operator
D_{p}
given by (
16.1
). Its Fredholm index is congruent (mod
2
) to the cohomological degree of the periodic orbit
p
, denoted by
|p|
. Let
\det D_{p}
be its real determinant line.

Consider the flow category case. For each pair of objects
p,q
, one can use the 1-parameter family of operators (
16.3
) to obtain an interpolation; in particular an isomorphism between determinant lines

\det D_{p}\otimes\det D_{u}\cong\det(D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus D_{q})\cong\det D_{q}.

The last isomorphism comes from the fact that
D_{u}^{\mathbb{C}}
is complex. We may regard the orientation of the moduli space as the family of isomorphismss on the determinant lines

\det D_{u}:\det D_{p}\to\det D_{q}.

In terms of the AMS lift, this means one obtains a family of isomorphisms (well-defined up to homotopy)

\sigma_{pq}:\det D_{p}\to\det D_{q}

over the thickened moduli
V_{pq}
. The construction implies

\sigma_{rq}\circ\sigma_{pr}=\sigma_{pq}.

The case with multimodules and homotopies are essentially the same. For a Floer multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
, using the interpolation constructed before, one obtains a family of isomorphisms

\sigma_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\det D_{p_{i}}\to\det D_{p^{\prime}}.

By considering indices, one has that

\sigma_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=(-1)^{(\sum_{j<i}|p_{j}|)(|p_{i}|-|q_{i}|)}\sigma_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\circ\sigma_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}

and

\sigma_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}=\sigma_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\circ\sigma_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

The case of homotopy is similar and we omit it.

17.
Stable Smoothings

We proceed with the last step of constructing the AMS lifts. The original argument of such abstract smoothing of moduli spaces was contained in
[
3
]
. The inductive argument in the setting of Floer theories was first systematically discussed in
[
14
]
.

17.1.
Main results about stable smoothings

In the construction we will frequently stabilize thickened moduli spaces obtained in the AMS construction. To simplify the discussion, we always stabilize by trivial complex vector bundles. We make the following formal definition.

Definition 17.1
.

The category
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}^{+}
has objects being
(K,R)
where
K=(G,V,E,S)
is a Kuranishi space and
R
is a finite-dimensional unitary complex representation of
G
. A strict embedding from
(K_{1},R_{1})
to
(K_{2},R_{2})
consists of a strict embedding of Kuranishi spaces
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
and an
\iota_{21}^{G}
-equivariant isometric linear embedding
\iota_{21}^{R}:R_{1}\to R_{2}
. Morphisms of
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}^{+}
are unitary conjugacy classes of strict embeddings.

Similarly, one can consider the collared and rigidified versions. Then there are two natural functors from
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}^{+}
to
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
: the first is to forget the representation
R
; the other is to take the stabilization by
R
. What we would consider is the version

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{+}

for collared, rigidified, relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces.

Definition 17.2
.

A
stabilizer
of a flow category/multimodule/homotopy enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
is a lift to
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}^{+}
.

Remark 17.3
.

In the AMS construction, a stabilizer can be viewed as adding trivial pieces to the thickening data.

Notice that there are two natural functors from
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{+}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. The first is to forget the stabilizer. The second is to stabilize each Kuranishi space by the corresponding trivial vector bundle via the vertical stabilization (see Definition
15.27
). We often freely switch between the stabilizer, which is a system of representations, and the corresponding vertical stabilization, viewed as total space of the corresponding product equivariant vector bundles.

Definition 17.4
.

A
smoothing
of a flow category/multimodule/homotopy enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
is a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
. A
stable smoothing
of a flow category/multimodule/homotopy enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
consists of a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{+}
and a smoothing of the corresponding stabilization.

Theorem 17.5
.

(Stable Smoothing Theorem)

(1)

In Situation
Flow(i)
, suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
is a relatively smooth AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to a certain outercollaring width. Then there exists a stable smoothing of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
. In other words, there exists a vertical stabilization
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
and a smoothing of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
. We still denote the flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
by
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
and call it an
AMS stable smoothing
of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
.

(2)

In Situation
Module(i)
, suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
are relatively smooth AMS lifts and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
is a relatively smooth AMS lift as a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. Suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+}
are AMS stable smoothings of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
respectively. Then there exists a class of stable smoothings of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
as multimodules over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+})
. These stable smoothings are called
AMS stable smoothings
of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
.

(3)

In Situation
Homotopy(iii)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\cdots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+}
be AMS stable smoothings of the relatively smooth AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{+}
resp.
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{+}
be AMS stable smoothings of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
resp.
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
as multimodules over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+})
. Then the relatively smooth AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
has a class of stable smoothings as a homotopy from
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{+}
to
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{+}
. Such stable smoothings are called
AMS stable smoothings
of
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
.

17.2.
Preliminaries

17.2.1.
Microbundles

Recall that a
microbundle
of rank
k
over a topological space
M
, denoted by
{\mathscr{F}}
, is a topological space
{\mathscr{F}}
together with two structural maps
p:{\mathscr{F}}\to M
,
i:M\to{\mathscr{F}}
such that
p\circ i={\rm Id}_{M}
and such that near the image of
i
, locally
{\mathscr{F}}
is an
{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
-bundle. Any real vector bundle
F\to M
together with the bundle projection and the zero section induces a microbundle
F_{\mu}
. If
M
is a topological manifold, then its tangent microbundle
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
has the total space
M\times M
with
p
the projection onto the first factor and
i
the diagonal embedding of
M
.

A
morphism
of microbundles over
M
from
{\mathscr{F}}
to
{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime}
is a germ of continuous maps from a neighborhood of
i(M)\subseteq{\mathscr{F}}
to
{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime}
which commutes with the structural maps. A morphism is called an
isomorphism
if it can be represented by a homeomorphism. An
isotopy
of microbundle isomorphisms from
\phi_{0}:{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime}
to
\phi_{1}:{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime}
is a microbundle isomorphism from
\tilde{\mathscr{F}}\to\tilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime}
over
M\times[0,1]
whose restriction to
M\times\{i\}
coincides with
\phi_{i}
(as germs) for
i=0,1
.

In particular, a
vector bundle reduction
of a microbundle
{\mathscr{F}}\to M
consists of a vector bundle
F\to M
and a microbundle isomorphism
F_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{F}}
. When a topological manifold
M
is equipped with a smooth structure, denoted by
M_{\alpha}
, then there is a vector bundle reduction
(TM_{\alpha})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
: a concrete choice could be the exponential map associated to a Riemannian metric. The isotopy class of such a vector bundle reduction is canonical. Hence it is a necessary condition for a topological manifold to admit a smooth structure that its tangent microbundle admits a vector bundle reduction.

All the above notions admit equivariant extensions with respect to actions by compact Lie groups. In particular, when a topological
G
-manifold
M
is equipped with a smooth structure
M_{\alpha}
such that the
G
-action is smooth, then there is a well-defined isotopy class of
G
-equivariant vector bundle reduction
(TM_{\alpha})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
.

Remark 17.6
.

We need the notion of smooth microbundles over smooth manifolds. These are microbundles whose total spaces and structural maps are all in the
C^{\infty}
category. One can also define smooth microbundle maps and smooth vector bundle reductions. Equivariant versions can be formulated as well. For example, when
M
is already a smooth manifold, the tangent microbundle is smooth and any vector bundle reduction of
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
using a smooth Riemannian metric is smooth.

17.2.2.
Stable
G
-smoothings

Now we recall notions about equivariant smoothings. Let
M
be a topological manifold with an action by a compact Lie group
G
. A
G
-smoothing
of
M
is a smooth structure
\alpha
on
M
such that the
G
-action is smooth. Two
G
-smoothings
\alpha_{0}
and
\alpha_{1}
are called
equivalent
if the identity map
M_{\alpha_{0}}\to M_{\alpha_{1}}
is a diffeomorphism; they are called
isotopic
if the identity map is homotopic through
G
-equivariant homeomorphisms to a diffeomorphism. More generally, a
stable
G
-smoothing
of
M
consists of an orthogonal
G
-representation
R
and a
G
-smoothing on
M\times R
. Notice that given any stable
G
-smoothing
(M\times R)_{\alpha}
and any other orthogonal
G
-representation
R^{\prime}
, the product smooth structure
(M\times R)_{\alpha}\times R^{\prime}
gives another stable
G
-smoothing. Two stable
G
-smoothings
(M\times R_{1})_{\alpha_{1}}
and
(M\times R_{2})_{\alpha_{2}}
are
stably isotopic
if there exist orthogonal
G
-representations
R_{1}^{\prime},R_{2}^{\prime}
such that
R_{1}\oplus R_{1}^{\prime}\cong R_{2}\oplus R_{2}^{\prime}
as orthogonal
G
-representations and such that the two
G
-smoothings
(M\times R_{1})_{\alpha_{1}}\times R_{1}^{\prime}
and
(M\times R_{2})_{\alpha_{2}}\times R_{2}^{\prime}
on
M\times R_{1}\times R_{1}^{\prime}=M\times R_{2}\times R_{2}^{\prime}
are isotopic.

Notice that given a
G
-smoothing
M_{\alpha}
on
M
, there is an induced isotopy class of
G
-equivariant vector bundle reductions
(TM_{\alpha})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
. In view of stable smoothings, one also need to define the notion of stable isotopy of stable vector bundle reductions.

Definition 17.7
.

[
38
, Page 300]

Let
{\mathscr{F}}\to M
be a
G
-microbundle. A
stable
G
-vector bundle reduction
of
{\mathscr{F}}
consists of a
G
-vector bundle
F
and an orthogonal
G
-representation
R
together with a
G
-microbundle isomorphism

\phi:F_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{F}}\oplus\underline{\smash{R}}_{\mu}.

Two stable
G
-vector bundle reductions
\phi_{i}:(F_{i})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{F}}\oplus(\underline{\smash{R}}_{i})_{\mu}
,
i=0,1
, are called
stably isotopic
if there exist orthogonal
G
-representations
R_{0}^{\prime},R_{1}^{\prime}
such that
R_{0}\oplus R_{0}^{\prime}\cong R_{1}\oplus R_{1}^{\prime}
and the stabilizations
\phi_{0}\oplus{\rm Id}_{\underline{\smash{R}}_{0}^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{1}\oplus{\rm Id}_{\underline{\smash{R}}_{1}^{\prime}}
are isotopic vector bundle reductions of
{\mathscr{F}}\oplus(\underline{\smash{R}}_{0})_{\mu}\oplus(\underline{\smash{R}}_{0}^{\prime})_{\mu}\cong{\mathscr{F}}\oplus(\underline{\smash{R}}_{1})_{\mu}\oplus(\underline{\smash{R}}_{1}^{\prime})_{\mu}
.

Remark 17.8
.

In the above discussions, we can replace “orthogonal representations” by “unitary representations” to suit our purpose. Any orthogonal representation can be stabilized to a unitary representation.

Now given any stable
G
-smoothing
(M\times R)_{\alpha}
of
M
, there is an isotopy class of
G
-vector bundle reductions
T(M\times R)_{\alpha}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}(M\times R)
. The restriction of this reduction to
M\times\{0\}
gives a stable isotopy class of
G
-vector bundle reductions of
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
. Lashof’s theorem says that this association has an inverse.

Theorem 17.9
.

[
38
]
Let
M
be a topological
G
-manifold with finitely many orbit types. Then the above map which sends each stable isotopy class of stable
G
-smoothings to a stable isotopy class of
G
-vector bundle reductions of
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
is invertible. In particular, if
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M
admits a
G
-vector bundle reduction, then
M
admits a stable
G
-smoothing.

17.2.3.
Micro-connections

Let
\pi:V\to B
be a
G
-equivariant relatively smooth manifold such that
B
is a smooth
G
-manifold. Then there is a well-defined
G
-microbundle over
V
:

\pi^{*}{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}B\oplus{\mathscr{T}}^{\rm vt}_{\mu}V.

To facilitate the stable smoothing of
V
, we need to identify the above microbundle with the tangent microbundle of
V
. Notice that there are natural microbundle maps

{\mathscr{T}}^{\rm vt}_{\mu}V\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V\to\pi^{*}{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}B.

Hence such an identification can be regarded as a microbundle version of connections.

Definition 17.10
.

Let
V/B
be a
G
-equivariant topological submersion. A
micro-connection
is a
G
-equivariant microbundle map
\nabla_{\mu}:{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}V
satisfying

(1)

The restriction of
\nabla^{\mu}
to
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}V
is the identity map.

(2)

\nabla_{\mu}
is represented by a germ of the map
\phi:\Delta^{+}(V)\to V
such that for any
(b,b^{\prime})\in(\pi\times\pi)(\Delta^{+}(V))\subseteq B\times B
and
p\in\pi^{-1}(b)\subseteq V
, the restriction of
\phi
to
\Delta^{+}(V)\cap(\{p\}\times\pi^{-1}(b^{\prime}))
is a homeomorphism onto an open neighborhood of
(p,p)
in
\{p\}\times\pi^{-1}(b))
.

Notice that a micro-connection induces a microbundle isomorphism
{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}M\cong\pi^{*}{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}B\oplus{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M
.

Lemma 17.11
.

Let
C\subseteq V
be a
G
-invariant closed subset and
U\subseteq V
be a
G
-invariant open neighborhood of
C
. Let
D\subseteq V
be another
G
-invariant closed set. Suppose
\nabla_{\mu}:{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}U\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}U
is a
G
-invariant micro-connection. Then there exists a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
W
of
C\cup D
and a
G
-invariant micro-connection
\nabla_{\mu}^{\prime}:{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}W\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}W
which coincides with
\nabla_{\mu}
near
C
.

Proof.

This is a restatement of the extension lemma
[
3
, Lemma 4.24]
and a relative version of
[
3
, Proposition 4.25]
.
∎

17.3.
Pre-smoothed relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces

To describe the structures one needs to perform the stable smoothing, we enhance the category of relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces to include various specific add-ons.

Definition 17.12
.

Let
K=(G,V/B,E,S)
be an object of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

(1)

A
pre-smoothing
on
K
consists of

(a)

A
G
-invariant Riemannian metric
g^{TB}
on
B
and a
G
-invariant metric
h^{T^{\rm vt}V}
on
T^{\rm vt}V
.

(b)

A micro-connection
\nabla_{\mu}
on
V/B
.

(2)

A pre-smoothing is called
collared
if for each stratum
V_{\alpha}/B_{\alpha}
, the following holds.

(a)

Near
B_{\alpha}
,
g^{TB}
is the product of
g^{TB_{\alpha}}
and the standard metric on the collar.

(b)

Near
V_{\alpha}
,
h^{T^{\rm vt}V}
is constant in collar coordinates.

(c)

Near
V_{\alpha}
,
\nabla_{\mu}
factors as

\textstyle{{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}V_{\alpha}}

where the first arrow is induced from the collar structure and the second arrow is the restriction of the micro-connection on the stratum
V_{\alpha}/B_{\alpha}
.

(3)

A pre-smoothed Kuranishi space is a relatively smooth Kuranishi space equipped with a pre-smoothing.

(4)

A stabilization of a pre-smoothed Kuranishi space
K
by a trivial
G
-equivariant disk bundle
D=D^{\rm hor}\oplus D^{\rm vt}
is the stabilized Kuranishi space
{\rm Stab}_{D}K
together with the naturally induced pre-smoothing (where
D^{\rm hor}
and
D^{\rm vt}
are equipped with linear metrics).

(5)

A
rigidified strict embedding
of pre-smoothed Kuranishi spaces from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
consists of a rigidified strict embedding
\iota_{21}:K_{1}\to K_{2}
(with normal disk bundle
D_{21}=D_{21}^{\rm hor}\oplus D_{21}^{\rm vt}
equipped with the direct sum of linear metrics) such that the induced germ of strict open embedding preserves the pre-smoothings.

(6)

A
rigidified embedding
is a unitary conjugacy class of rigidified strict embeddings. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
be the regular stratification category whose objects are collared, pre-smoothed Kuranishi spaces and whose morphisms are rigidified embeddings. There is an obvious forgetful functor

{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}\to{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}.

(7)

A
pre-smoothing
of a flow category/multimodule/homotopy enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
is a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Lemma 17.13
.

(1)

In Situation
Flow(iii)
, the relatively smooth AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
admits a pre-smoothing.

(2)

In Situation
Module(iii)
, suppose the AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
of the involved Floer flow categories are equipped with pre-smoothings, then
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
admits a pre-smoothing as a multimodule enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

(3)

In Situation
Homotopy(iii)
, suppose the AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime},\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0},\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
are equipped with pre-smoothings so
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
are multimodules over the tuple of pre-smoothed flow categories, then
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
admits a pre-smoothing as a homotopy enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Proof.

The collared and rigidified structure of the relatively smooth AMS lifts allows us to choose invariant horizontal and vertical Riemannian metrics inductively. On the other hand, Lemma
17.11
allows us to inductively construct micro-connections.
∎

Once one obtains a pre-smoothed Kuranishi space
K=(G,V/B,E,S)
, one obtains a
G
-vector bundle reduction of the tangent microbundle. More precisely, the exponential map of
g^{TB}
induces a reduction

(TB)_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}B.

The vertical exponential map of
h^{T^{\rm vt}V}
induces a reduction

(T^{\rm vt}V)_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}^{\rm vt}V.

Together with the micro-connection, one obtains

(\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}V)_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V.

Notice that such
G
-vector bundle reductions are compatible with morphisms of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

17.3.1.
Post-smoothed Kuranishi spaces

Definition
17.12
ensures the existence of coherent vector bundle lifts. The next definition is designed to make the stabizers explicit in order to carry out the inductive smoothing construction.

Definition 17.14
.

Let
K=(G,V/B,E,S)
be an object of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm pre}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. A
post-smoothing
on
K
consists of a
G
-smoothing of
V
, a collared smooth
G
-invariant vector bundle reduction

\exp:(TV)_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{T}}_{\mu}V,

and a collared
G
-equivariant vector bundle isomorphism

TV\cong\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}V

such that
\exp
and the
G
-vector bundle reduction induced from the pre-smoothing are in the same stable
G
-isotopy class on the interior of each stratum.

Now we state the results about constructing stable smoothings. Since we need to constantly stabilize charts, we would like to use the convenient system of unitary representations of
G_{d}\cong U(d)
specified by Proposition
15.23
.

Lemma 17.15
.

(1)

In Situation
Flow(iii)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
be a pre-smoothed AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
provided by Lemma
17.13
. Then there exists a stabilization
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
and a post-smoothing of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
. Each such flow category is called a post-smoothed AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
.

(2)

In Situation
Module(iii)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be pre-smoothed AMS lifts of the involved Floer flow categories. Let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
be a pre-smoothed AMS lift of the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
provided by Lemma
17.13
. Suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+}
are post-smoothings of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then there exists a stabilization
\hat{\mathbb{X}}^{+}
of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
and a post-smoothing of it as a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+})
.

(3)

In Situation
Homotopy(iii)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{+},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{+};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{}^{+}
be post-smoothed AMS lifts of the involved flow categories and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{+}
,
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{+}
be post-smoothed AMS lifts of the two multimodules
{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
be a pre-smoothed AMS lift of the homotopy provided by Lemma
17.13
. Then there exists a stabilization
\hat{\mathbb{H}}^{+}
of
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
and a post-smoothing of it as a homotopy from
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{+}
to
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{+}
.

Proof.

Again, it suffices to demonstrate the argument in the case of flow categories. Recall the system of unitary representations
W_{d,\mu}
. For each pair
p<q
, denote
W_{pq,\mu}:=W_{d_{pq},\mu}
where
d_{pq}
is the integer specified via the choice of integral action. Notice that once we choose a system of positive integers
\mu_{pq}
satisfying

\mu_{pq}>\max\{\mu_{pr},\mu_{rq}\ |\ p<r<q\}

(17.1)

then one obtains a stabilization
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
with each chart
K_{pq}
vertically stabilized by
W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
. The main claim is that one can choose such a stabilizer together with a post-smoothing of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{+}
satisfying the condition on stable isotopy classes of
G
-vector bundle reductions.

We construct
\mu_{pq}
and the post-smoothing inductively. For the base case when
V_{pq}
has no boundary strata, the existence of
W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
and the post-smoothing is a consequence of Lashof’s theorem (Theorem
17.9
), since every finite-dimensional representation of
G_{pq}
is contained in a certain
W_{pq,\mu}
for
\mu
sufficiently large. Choose such
\mu_{pq}
and denote
V_{pq}^{+}=V_{pq}\times W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
. Then we can choose a smooth
G_{pq}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
V_{pq}^{+}
and obtain the exponential map
\exp_{pq}
as a smooth vector bundle reduction of the tangent microbundle.

Now we state the induction hypothesis. Given
p<q
, suppose we have chosen
\mu_{rs}
for all
r,s
satisfying
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
satisfying (
17.1
) and post-smoothings such that the induced embeddings of the smoothed Kuranishi spaces are morphisms of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
.
25
25

25

This claim is a purpose of using rigidified embeddings: the trivialized normal bundle induces a canonical smoothing in the tubular neighborhood of the embedding. If we do not require the normal bundle to be trivialized, then one needs to make the painful choices of smooth bundle structures.

Now we can construct the stable smoothing on
V_{pq}
. Choose
\mu_{pq}^{\prime}
sufficiently large such that the already stabilized boundary strata can all be included into
V_{pq}\times W_{pq,\mu_{pq}^{\prime}}
. On the other hand, the interior
{\rm Int}V_{pq}
can be stably smoothed by Theorem
17.9
, meaning that one can choose
\mu_{pq}^{\prime\prime}
sufficiently large and a smoothing of
{\rm Int}V_{pq}\times W_{pq,\mu_{pq}^{\prime\prime}}
. By choosing
\mu_{pq}
greater than both
\mu_{pq}^{\prime}
and
\mu_{pq}^{\prime\prime}
, one can assume
\mu_{pq}^{\prime}=\mu_{pq}^{\prime\prime}
. Notice that the induction hypothesis implies a canonically induced smoothing near the boundary. On overlaps, these two smoothings induce the same stable
G
-isotopy class of microbundle reductions. Hence by the relative smoothing result proved in
[
14
, Appendix B]
, one can interpolate the two smoothings in the collar region, possibly after a further enlargement of
\mu_{pq}
, so that near the boundary the smoothing is still the one canonically induced from other already-stabilized charts. Therefore, one obtains a stabilization by
W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
, a smoothing of this stabilization, so that all the chart embeddings are morphisms of
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
. Further, we choose the smooth vector bundle reduction on the stabilized chart
V_{pq}\times W_{pq,\mu_{pq}}
.
∎

17.4.
Normally complex AMS lifts

Finally we are at the last step of proving Theorem
O
and counterparts for Floer multimodules and homotopies.

Theorem 17.16
.

(1)

In Situation
Flow(iii)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
be a post-smoothed AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
. Then
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
, viewed as a lift in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
, admits a class of lifts to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Each of such lifts, denoted by
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}
temporarily, is called an
NC AMS lift
of (either
{\mathbb{F}}
or
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
).

(2)

In Situation
Module(i)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be post-smoothed AMS lifts of involved Floer flow categories and let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
be a post-smoothed AMS lift of the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
. Suppose
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m};\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime}
are NC AMS lifts of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
. Then there exists a class of lifts of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as multimodules over
(\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m};\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime})
. Each of such lifts, denoted by
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}
temporarily, is called an
NC AMS lift
of (either
{\mathbb{X}}
or
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
).

(3)

In Situation
Homotopy(i)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m},\hat{\mathbb{F}},\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0},\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{H}}
be post-smoothed AMS lifts of the involved flow categories, multimodules, and homotopy. Suppose
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}_{m},\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{F}}}}^{\prime},\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{0},\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{1}
be NC AMS lifts. Then there exists a lift of
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a homotopy from
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{0}
to
\hat{\vphantom{\rule{1.0pt}{5.71527pt}}\smash{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}}_{1}
.

Proof.

Recall that the post-smoothing of a relatively smooth Kuranishi space
K=(G,V/B,E,S)
still remembers the vector bundle
\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}V
although the relative structure is destroyed. As the post-smoothing also includes the identification between this vector bundle and the smooth tangent bundle, we may assume
TV
has the canonical splitting.

Now notice that
T^{\rm vt}V
is equipped with the
G
-equivariant NC structure while
TB
is equipped with the
G
-equivariant stable complex structure. This does not yet provide an NC structure on
TV
since
TV
contains the subbundle of the infinitesimal
G
-action. To obtain the NC structure, one needs to make the obstruction bundle complex first.

Recall that from the construction of the AMS lifts, the obstruction bundle is always trivial and decomposes as

E=\underline{\smash{W}}\oplus\underline{\smash{Q}}

where
W
is a complex representation of
G
and
Q
is a real representation of
G
. However, we observe that in all the Kuranishi spaces we obtained in the construction, the direct sum

{\mathfrak{g}}\oplus Q

is naturally isomorphic to the Lie algebra of the complex Lie group
{\mathcal{G}}
. Therefore, we can stabilize all charts by the Lie algebra. Denote by

\tilde{K}:={\rm Stab}_{\mathfrak{g}}(K)=(G,\tilde{V},\tilde{E},\tilde{S}).

In this way, the obstruction bundles of all involved Kuranishi spaces are equivariant complex vector bundles. Let

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tilde{V}}\subseteq T\tilde{V}

be the subbundle spanned by infinitesimal
G
-actions. Then by Definition
3.24
, it remains to construct
G
-equivariant NC structures on the bundles
T\tilde{V}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tilde{V}}
. Indeed, for each chart
\tilde{K}=(G,\tilde{V},\tilde{E},\tilde{S})
after the stabilization, let
\pi:\tilde{V}\to V
be the projection. Then one has

T\tilde{V}=\pi^{*}TV\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}

where the previous constructions provide
TV
with NC structures. Therefore, it surfaces to define compatible equivariant bundle isomorphisms

\pi^{*}TV\cong(\pi^{*}TV\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}})/{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tilde{V}}.

One chooses such isomorphisms in the following way. Choose a compatible system of
G
-invariant metrics on
V
inductively. Then one obtains an equivariant splitting

TV\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}^{\bot}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}.

Notice that
{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}
is isomorphic with the trivial bundle
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Then define

\pi^{*}TV\cong\pi^{*}({\mathfrak{g}}_{V}^{\bot})\oplus\pi^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}\to\pi^{*}(TV\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}})

by sending
\pi^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}
into
\pi^{*}TV
while sending
\pi^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}_{V}
onto
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}
. It is then easy to see that the composition

\pi^{*}TV\to\pi^{*}(TV\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}})\to\pi^{*}(TV\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}})/{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tilde{V}}

is an isomorphism. This equips the Kuranishi spaces
G
-equivariant NC structures.
∎

17.5.
Coherent orientations

For all involved flow categories and all objects
p\in{\mathbb{O}b}{\mathbb{F}}
, choose a trivialization of the determinant line
\det D_{p}
. In last section, the isomorphism
\sigma_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
hence determines an orientation on the vertical tangent bundle
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}
. Similarly, there are orientations on the vertical tangent bundle
T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
for a multimodule.

After stable smoothing, one has equivariant bundle isomorphisms

TV_{pq}\cong\pi_{pq}^{*}TB_{pq}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}.

Both summands have been given certain orientations. We then orient
V_{pq}
using the above decomposition.

One can then verify the coherence requirement of Definition
7.12
. Indeed, for
p<r<q
, the isomorphism

\Big(\pi_{pr}^{*}TB_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pr}^{\mathbb{F}}\Big)\oplus\Big(\pi_{rq}^{*}TB_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}\Big)\to\pi^{*}TB_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

is orientation preserving up to a sign

(-1)^{{\rm rank}T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}}=(-1)^{|p|-|r|}

because
B_{rq}^{\mathbb{F}}
is odd-dimensional.

Suppose the AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
are oriented as above. W orient the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
as follows. For each tuple
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m};p^{\prime})
, orient the tangent bundle of the thickening
V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}
via the direct sum decomposition

TV_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\cong\pi_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{*}TB_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{F}}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}.

Notice that the domain moduli is even-dimensional. Then one can see that with respect to breakings, the isomorphism

TV_{p_{i}q_{i}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}\oplus TV_{p_{1}\cdots p_{i-1}q_{i}p_{i+1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\to TV_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

has a sign
(-1)^{(\sum_{j<i}|p_{i}|)(|p_{i}|-|q_{i}|)}
while the isomorphism

TV_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}\oplus TV_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}\cong TV_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}

has a sign
(-1)^{{\rm rank}T^{\rm vt}V_{p_{1}\cdots p_{m};q^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{X}}}
.

18.
Proofs of the Main Theorems on AMS Lifts

In this section we summarize the previous constructions as formal proofs of the main theorems about the AMS lifts for Floer flow categories, multimodules and homotopies to the category of (rigidified and collared) normally complex derived orbifolds
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

18.1.
Proof of Theorem
O

Assume we are in Situation
Flow(i)
. The construction is illustrated and summarized in Figure
9
. Each step of the roadmap is completed by a concrete theorem or proposition in previous sections. Upon taking the quotient orbifold, we obtain the proof of Theorem
O
except the orientation part.

Outercollaring width (Section
5.2
)

Integral modification of energy
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{k}
(Proposition
15.4
)

Flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}
(Subsection
15.1.1
)

Rigidification: lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
(Proposition
14.17
)

Flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\bm{\Omega},{\mathbb{F}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}^{\Omega,\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}_{\rm rig}
(Proposition
15.8
)

Transverse thickening datum
\mathbb{W}^{\mathbb{F}}
over
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\bm{\Omega},{\mathbb{F}}}
(Proposition
15.19
)

Group reduction: lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{map}_{\rm rig}^{\rm fr}
(Proposition
15.9
)

Topological Kuranishi lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
(Section
15.3.4
and smoothing Lemma
17.15
)
