Stabilization and lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
(Theorem
16.1
and Theorem
17.16
)

Figure 9.
Roadmap of the AMS construction for Floer flow categories. Here the pink, rounded rectangles involve choices to be made and the orange rectangles are items canonically induced from previous steps.

Now we explain how to obtain a coherent orientation for the AMS lift. Given a pair of objects
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, one needs to specify an orientation on the virtual vector bundle

T(V_{pq}/G_{pq})-E_{pq}/G_{pq}.

Recall that
E_{pq}=W_{pq}\oplus Q_{pq}
where
W_{pq}
comes from the thickening data and
Q_{pq}
corresponds to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}
. As the thickening space
W_{pq}
is complex, it can be neglected for orientation considerations. Moreover, if we stabilize the virtual vector bundle by
{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}
, then as
Q_{pq}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}^{\mathbb{C}}
is complex and hence canonically oriented, it is equivalent to orient the vector bundle

T(V_{pq}/G_{pq})\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{pq}/G_{pq}

where
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
is the trivial bundle with fibres being
{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}
and
\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{pq}/G_{pq}
is the induced orbifold vector bundle.

On the other hand, the stable smoothing operation preserves the (
C^{0}
) isomorphism

TV_{pq}\cong\pi_{pq}^{*}TB_{pq}\oplus T^{\rm vert}V_{pq}

where both summands have been given
G_{pq}
-invariant orientations. Moreover, there is a summand
{\mathfrak{g}}_{pq}\subseteq TV_{pq}
corresponding to the infinitesimal
G_{pq}
-actions. Then the direct sum orientation descends to an orientation on the bundle

T(V_{pq}/G_{pq})\oplus\underline{\smash{\mathfrak{g}}}_{pq}/G_{pq}

which is what we need.

It remains to verify that the individual orientations on the derived orbifolds are coherent. Suppose
p<r<q
in
{\mathbb{F}}
. Recall that the domain moduli spaces
B_{pq}
are oriented in a way which respects breakings (see Lemma
14.20
). On the other hand, the orientations on the vertical tangent bundles
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
satisfy

\det T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}\cong\det T^{\rm vt}V_{pr}\otimes\det T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}

as oriented bundles. As
B_{pq}
are always odd-dimensional, there holds

\det\Big(\pi_{pq}^{*}TB_{pq}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\Big)|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}\cong\det\Big(\pi_{pr}^{*}TB_{pr}\oplus\pi_{rq}^{*}TB_{rq}\oplus Q_{prq}\Big)\otimes\det\Big(T^{\rm vt}V_{pr}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}\oplus W_{prq}\Big)\\
\cong(-1)^{{\rm rank}T^{\rm vt}V_{pr}}\det\Big(\pi_{pr}^{*}TB_{pr}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pr}\Big)\otimes\det\Big(\pi_{rq}^{*}TB_{rq}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{rq}\Big).

Notice that here
Q_{prq}
and
W_{prq}
are both complex. The rank of the vertical bundle here is congruent mod 2 to the difference
|p|-|r|
of cohomological gradings. This means the orientations on the derived orbifolds specified above provide a coherent orientation on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
.

18.2.
Proof of Theorem
P

We also summarize the construction of AMS lifts of multimodules associated to a smooth Floer domain.

Proposition 18.1
.

In Situation
Module(i)
, fix an outercollaring width and choose a list of integral actions on the involved flow categories which are compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{X}}
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
be AMS lifts to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
(subject to the fixed outercollaring width and the integral actions). Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

To save notations, we write the proof in the case of bimodules and write
{\mathbb{X}}={\mathbb{B}}
. The case of multimodules is the same. Abbreviate
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
by
{\mathbb{F}}
. The constructions of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
start with the monotone flow categories
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime}
. Then by Proposition
14.22
, there exists a monotone bimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\prime})
. Then using the compatible integral actions, one obtains the corresponding bimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{X}}}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
. The choices of transverse thickening data for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
can be extended, by Proposition
15.19
, to a transverse thickening datum
{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathbb{X}}
. The choices of the group reductions for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}
can be extended, by Proposition
15.9
, to a group reduction on
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{X}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
. Then by Definition
15.16
, one obtains the collections of thickenings
V_{p;p^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}}
which provides (in a canonical way) a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{B}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
as a bimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
. The remaining structures, including the relative NC structure, the stable smoothing, and the NC structure can all be constructed using the inductive procedure. We omit the details.
∎

The two exceptional cases, i.e., the multimodules for Poincaré pairing and the cigar bimodule can be established similarly. We only give the statement without detailed proofs.

Proposition 18.2
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
be Floer flow categories associated to
(H_{1},J_{1})
and
(H_{2},J_{2})
respectively. Let
{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm PD}
be the Poincaré multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2};{\mathbb{O}})
associated to a Floer data interpolating on the infinite cylinder. Fix an outercollaring width and choose integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{2}}^{\Omega}
which are compatible with respect to this multimodule. Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
resp.
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
be AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
subject to the outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm PD}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2};{\mathbb{O}})
, which we call an AMS lift subject to the outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions.

Proposition 18.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be a Floer flow category associated to
(H,J)
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}^{\rm cigar}
be the cigar bimodule over
({\mathbb{O}};{\mathbb{F}})
associated to a Floer data on the complex plane. Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
be an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to an outercollaring width and an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
which is compatible with respect to the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\rm cigar}
. Then there exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{B}}^{\rm cigar}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
which is a bimodule over
({\mathbb{O}};\hat{\mathbb{F}})
, which we call an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{B}}^{\rm cigar}
subject to the outercollaring width and the chosen integral action.

Propositions
18.1
–
18.3
lead to the first item of Theorem
P
(without the orientation claim). To establish the second item, consider the trivial homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
from
{\mathbb{X}}
to itself. Notice that the chosen integral actions are still compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{H}}
. Then for any two AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
and
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
subject to the same outercollaring width and the same set of integral actions, one can construct a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
by using the corresponding homotopy version of each step of the AMS construction. We omit the details. After passing to derived orbifolds, one obtains the second item of Theorem
P
(without the orientation claim).

To obtain coherent orientations, one can follow the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
O
. The signs come from the properties of the orientations on the domain moduli spaces (see Proposition
14.22
) as well as the orientations on vertical tangent bundles (see Subsection
16.5
).

18.3.
Proof of Theorem
Q

The proof is simply a different organization of the previous constructions for flow categories and multimodules. Notice that it is important to choose integral actions which are compatible throughout the consideration. Then each stage of the AMS construction, starting from the domain flow category, rigidifications, choosing thickening data and group reductions, constructing relative NC structures, stable smoothing, and smooth NC structures, can all be inductively accomplished. The statement of Theorem
Q
then follows without any new difficulties than flow category and multimodule cases.

19.
Concatenation and Gluing—Proof of Theorem
R

In this section we prove Theorem
12.4
and Theorem
R
. Note that, although the statement of Theorem
12.4
is on the level of derived orbifolds, the constructions of the rigidification and NC structure require us to go through the AMS construction on the level of Kuranishi spaces.

19.1.
Concatenation and gluing of domains

19.1.1.
Concatenations of monotone multimodules

We make the analgoue of Definition
6.13
for the case of monotone multimodules. Let
{\mathbb{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{f}}_{m},{\mathbb{g}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{g}}_{n},{\mathbb{g}}^{\prime}
be monotone flow categories enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
. Let
{\mathbb{x}}
resp.
{\mathbb{y}}
be monotone multimodules over
({\mathbb{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{f}}_{m};{\mathbb{g}}_{i})
resp. over
({\mathbb{g}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{g}}_{n};{\mathbb{g}}^{\prime})
. For each
d\geq 0
, let

\displaystyle\ A_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\

\displaystyle\ A_{d}^{\mathbb{y}}

be the underlying regular posets of the objects
M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}
and
M_{d}^{\mathbb{y}}
respectively. Define

A_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}:=\left(\bigsqcup_{d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}=d}A_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{x}}\times A_{d^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{y}}\right)/\sim

(19.1)

where the equivalence relation
\sim
is similar to the case discussed in Subsection
6.5
. More precisely, the equivalence relation is generated by

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.2166pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 53.2166pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{d_{1}}^{\mathbb{x}}\times A_{d}\times A_{d_{2}}^{\mathbb{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.68222pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 156.05238pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 173.72745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-29.2166pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{d_{1}+d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times A_{d_{2}}^{\mathbb{y}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 85.36372pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 147.51085pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{d_{1}}^{\mathbb{x}}\times A_{d+d_{2}}^{\mathbb{y}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Then for each pair
d,e\geq 0
, there is a codimension zero subset

A_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times A_{e}^{{\mathbb{y}}}\cong A_{d;e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}\subseteq A_{d+e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}.

Definition 19.1
.

A
concatenation
of
{\mathbb{x}}
and
{\mathbb{y}}
at
{\mathbb{g}}_{i}
consists of the following objects.

(1)

A monotone multimodule denoted by
{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}
over
({\mathbb{g}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{g}}_{i-1},{\mathbb{f}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{f}}_{m},{\mathbb{g}}_{i+1},\ldots,{\mathbb{g}}_{n};{\mathbb{g}}^{\prime})
consisting of objects

M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf C}}

together with structural maps.

(2)

For
d,e\geq 0
, codimension zero morphisms

M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{e}^{\mathbb{y}}\to M_{d+e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}

whose underlying poset map the inclusion
A_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times A_{e}^{{\mathbb{y}}}\to A_{d+e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}
.

They are required to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

The objects
M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}
is stratified by the poset
A_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}
defined by (
19.1
).

(2)

For any
d,d_{i},e\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{d_{i}}^{{\mathbb{g}}_{i}}\times M_{e}^{\mathbb{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d+d_{i}}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{e}^{\mathbb{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{d_{i}+e}^{\mathbb{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d+d_{i}+e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}}

(3)

The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d}^{{\mathbb{x}}}\times M_{e}^{\mathbb{y}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{g}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d}^{\mathbb{x}}\times M_{e+e^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}}^{{\mathbb{f}}_{j}}\times M_{d+e}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}\times M_{e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{g}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{d_{j}+d+e+e^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{x}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{y}}}}

19.1.2.
Concatenations of domains

Now we consider the concatenation of two monotone multimodules associated to smooth curves.

Proposition 19.2
.

In Situation
Concatenation(i)
, let

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}},\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{n}},\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}}

be the monotone flow categories enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
subject to the same outercollaring width. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}
resp.
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{{\mathbb{Y}}}}
be monotone multimodules over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{i}})
resp. over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{n}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}})
constructed in Subsection
14.4
. Then there exists a concatenation
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{{\mathbb{Y}}}}
(see Definition
19.1
).

Proof.

The underlying monotone multimodules enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
have a canonical concatenation. Indeed, let
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
be the singular curve with two components
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{X}}}
and
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{Y}}}
glued along the cylindrical end of the concatenation. Notice that the lateral lines in
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
and
\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}
glue together to lateral lines in the singular domain. Then the same construction of
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma}
can be applied to
\Sigma^{{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}}
, providing a monotone multimodule
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
. It is straightforward to check that the outercollaring of
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
is also a concatenation of the corresponding outercollarings of
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}
and
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
.

The remaining is to construct the rigidifications and the stable complex structures. For simplicity, we assume that
{\mathbb{X}}
is a bimodule
\mathbb{B}
over
({\mathbb{F}};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
is a bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}
over
({\mathbb{F}}^{\prime};{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime\prime})
; the general case is essentially the same with only additional notational complexities.

We first consider the stable complex structure on the concatenation. Recall the case of smooth domains is obtained in Proposition
14.19
where the stable complex structure is naturally induced. The same fact holds for the concatenated domain. Therefore, the natural concatenation
\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\circ\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
is in fact in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

Now we consider rigidifications. We first rigidify the embeddings

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\mathbb{B}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}.

Notice that this is a codimension zero embedding and there is a target top stratum

{\mathcal{C}}_{d;d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}

such that

{\mathcal{G}}_{d+d^{\prime}}\times_{{\mathcal{G}}_{d}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\mathbb{B}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}^{\prime}})\cong{\mathcal{C}}_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}.

Recall that a rigidification of the above codimension zero embedding (see Definition
14.9
) is a map

\theta_{d;d^{\prime}}:B_{d}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}\times Q_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}\to B_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}.

These maps have to incorporate the existing one defining rigidifications of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{B}}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}
. One can construct these maps inductively, from the deepest strata up.

First, if
B_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}
has no lower strata, then there is no constraint coming from rigidifications of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{B}}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}
and one can choose the rigidification arbitrarily.

In general one needs to construct
\theta_{d;d^{\prime}}
inductively. We only explain how to construct it when
B_{d;d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}
has only a codimension one stratum given by a decomposition
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
. This codimension one stratum is shared with another open stratum
B_{d_{1};d_{2}+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{B}\circ\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}
. The rigidifications for these two open strata (with different symmetry groups) must be compatible along the shared fake boundary. We first choose rigidification of the codimension 1 embedding

B_{d_{1}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\to\partial^{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}B_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}.

Notice that the embedding can be factorized in two ways

\textstyle{\partial^{d_{1}d_{2}}B_{d}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times B_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{d_{1}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}B_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{B_{d_{1}}^{{\mathbb{B}}}\times\partial^{d_{2}d^{\prime}}B_{d_{2}+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the two arrows on the left are already rigidified. Consider

Q_{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}=Q_{d_{1}+d_{2}+d^{\prime}}/Q_{d_{1}}\oplus Q_{d_{2}}\oplus Q_{d^{\prime}}.

The rigidification on this lower stratum should be a germ of maps

\theta_{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}:\Big(B_{d_{1}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}}\times B_{d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}\Big)\times Q_{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}\to\partial^{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}B_{d+d^{\prime}}^{{\mathbb{B}}\circ{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}}

Notice that there is a natural inclusion

Q_{d_{1}d_{2}}\oplus Q_{d_{2}d^{\prime}}\subseteq Q_{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}

where the value of the rigidification is already determined. One then only has to extend it to the complement. Choosing such an extension equivariantly, one hence obtains the rigidification
\theta_{d_{1};d_{2};d^{\prime}}
on this lower stratum.

Notice that this lower stratum is shared by two top strata. One uses the collar regions in both directions to extend to a rigidification for both separately.
∎

19.1.3.
Gluing of domains

Now suppose we are in Situation
Homotopy(i)
. Suppose on the family of domains
\Sigma_{t}
(
t\in[0,1]
) one chooses a smooth family of lateral lines. Here
t=0
corresponds to the broken domain. Then without making further choices, one has the monotone flow categories

\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}_{m}},\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{G}_{n}},\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}

monotone multimodules

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}},\

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}.

As demonstrated above, the concatenation
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
within the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
is canonically defined. Moreover, by varying
t
, one also obtains a monotone homotopy
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{H}}
from the concatenation to
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{M}}
which is the multimodule associated to
\Sigma_{1}
.

What we do below is the construction of the further choices such as rigidifications and stable complex structure.

Proposition 19.3
.

Fix an outercollaring width. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}_{m}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{G}_{n}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
,
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
be as in Proposition
19.2
. Let
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{M}}
be a monotone multimodule associated to the smooth domain
\Sigma_{1}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Then for the outercollaring
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{H}}
, there exists a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
as a monotone homotopy from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}\circ_{i}{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Sigma^{\mathbb{Y}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{M}}
.

Proof.

Again, it only remains to construct the rigidifications and stable complex structures. The construction of rigidification is still based on the same induction principle. Then stable complex structure can be chosen after choosing a rigidification.
∎

19.2.
Relatively smooth AMS lifts for concatenations and gluing

In this subsection we treat the topological AMS construction for concatenations of Floer multimodules associated to smooth Floer domains.

19.2.1.
Concatenation

Assume we are in Situation
Concatenation(i)
. We have constructed relatively smooth AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
respectively. The purpose of this subsection is to demonstrate how to extend the constructions of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
to a lift of the concatenation
{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}{\mathbb{Y}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Theorem 19.4
.

In Situation
Concatenation(i)
, let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
be relatively smooth AMS lifts of the outercollarings of
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
respectively, subject to the same outercollaring width and integral actions which induce the same integral symplectic form on
X
. Then there exists a set of concatenations
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Proof.

As in Subsection
19.1.1
, we only describe the construction in the case of concatenations of bimodules. The case of multimodules differs only in notation. Denote
{\mathbb{X}}={\mathbb{B}}_{01}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}={\mathbb{B}}_{12}
where
{\mathbb{B}}_{01}
is a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{0};{\mathbb{F}}_{1})
and
\mathbb{B}_{12}
is a bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
, where
{\mathbb{F}}_{i}
is the Floer flow category associated to Hamiltonian
H_{i}
. Let
\Sigma
be the broken domain with two cylinders equipped with Floer datum
\sigma
from the concatenation of the two Floer data for
\mathbb{B}_{01}
and
\mathbb{B}_{12}
.

First consider the concatenation of domains. The AMS constructions of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{0},\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
and
\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{01}
,
\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
start with choosing monotone flow categories
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{0},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{1},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{2}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
as considered in Section
14
, as well as monotone bimodules
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{01},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{12}
respectively. Then Proposition
19.2
allows us to choose a concatenation
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{01}\circ{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{12}
as a monotone bimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{0};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{2})
.

Now using the chosen integral actions, one can obtain pullbacks

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}},i=0,1,2;\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{01}},\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{12}}

as ordinary flow categories and bimodules. The corresponding bimodules of framed maps are also obtained

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}_{01}}^{\rm fr},\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}_{12}}^{\rm fr}

with a concatenation

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}_{01}}^{\rm fr}\circ{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}_{12}}^{\rm fr}

canonically determined by the concatenation
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{01}}\circ{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{B}_{12}}
.

We need to show that the two further systems of choices, i.e., group reductions and thickening data, can be extended to the concatenation.

We first explain why the group reduction can be naturally extended. Indeed, given a triple of objects
p_{0},p_{1},p_{2}
of the three involved flow categories, one has the morphism

{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{0};p_{1}}^{{\rm fr}}\times{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{1};p_{2}}^{{\rm fr}}\to{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{0};p_{2}}^{{\rm fr}}

(19.2)

in the category of framed maps. One can see that if we choose any strict embedding representing the conjugacy class, then the strict embedding induces an open embedding onto the closure of a top stratum

\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{0};p_{2}}\times_{\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{0};p_{1}}\times\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{1};p_{2}}}\Big({}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{0};p_{1}}^{{\rm fr}}\times{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{1};p_{2}}^{{\rm fr}}\Big)\to{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{0};p_{2}}^{{\rm fr}}

(recall
\hat{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}_{L}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
where
{\mathcal{G}}_{L}
acts by reparametrizing the maps and frames while
{\mathcal{G}}_{R}
acts by linearly rearranging the frames). As the group reduction is invariant under the first group action and equivariant under the second action, the open embedding naturally induces an extension of the group reduction. One can check that the extension is well-defined on fake boundaries of
{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{p_{0};p_{2}}^{\rm fr}
(which are adjacent to two such top strata).

The extension of the thickening datum is also canonical in a neighborhood of the morphism (
19.2
), because the embedding is rigidified. Using the rigidification of this embedding, we can extend the thickening datum in a way which is constant in each normal fiber specified by the rigidification. As one can always shrink the resulting charts, up to shrinking, the extension of the thickening datum is canonically extended.

Then one can write down the thickenings in the same way as for a multimodule with a smooth domain. We can verify easily that one obtains a topological Kuranishi lift
\hat{\mathbb{B}}
which is also a concatenation
\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{01}\circ\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{12}
within the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. The relative smooth structure is canonically given and one can also easily see that the concatenation is indeed in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.
∎

19.2.2.
Gluing

Theorem 19.5
.

In Situation
Glue(i)
, fix an outercollaring width and integral actions on the involved flow categories compatible with respect to
{\mathbb{H}}
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
be relatively smooth AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the integral actions. Let
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
be a concatenation provided by Theorem
19.4
. Let
\hat{\mathbb{M}}
be an AMS lift of the multimodule
{\mathbb{M}}
. Then there exists a set of lifts of the outercollaring of the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
as a homotopy from
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
to
\hat{\mathbb{M}}
.

Proof.

At this stage, it follows from the same strategy as the proof of Theorem
Q
. The choices of rigidifications of domains are provided by Proposition
19.3
. The choices of group reductions and thickening datum can also be interpolated over the homotopy. The relative smooth structures are canonically obtained.
∎

19.3.
Normal complex structure and stable smoothing

Finally, we sketch the discussion on normal complex structures and stable smoothing of the objects arising in concatenation and gluing, which will finish the proof of Theorem
12.4
and Theorem
R
.

Proof of Theorem
12.4
.

We continue after Theorem
19.4
. Suppose the relatively smooth AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{n}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
, and the multimodules
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
are lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
using the construction of Section
16
. We would like to see if the composition
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
provided by Theorem
19.4
can also be lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a composition. In fact, the vertical tangent bundles contained in the composition
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
are canonically determined by the corresponding vertical tangent bundles. Hence the vertical NC structures are canonically induced. Therefore, we see that
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
is lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

The stable smoothing of the concatenation is also automatically induced. We briefly explain for the bimodule case. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{0},{\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
and
\mathbb{B}_{01}
,
\mathbb{B}_{12}
be as in the proof of Theorem
19.4
. For each triple of objects
p_{0},p_{1},p_{2}
, one has the embedding

K_{p_{0};p_{1}}^{\mathbb{B}_{01}}\times K_{p_{1};p_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}_{12}}\to K_{p_{0};p_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}_{01}\circ\mathbb{B}_{12}}.

The difference essentially lies in the difference between the complex Lie groups
{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{0};p_{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{1};p_{2}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{p_{0};p_{2}}
. In terms of Kuranishi charts, the unitary part of the difference is realized by changing the symmetry groups while the non-unitary part of the difference is realized by having a further stabilization. Therefore, if both
K_{p_{0};p_{1}}^{\mathbb{B}_{01}}
and
K_{p_{1};p_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}_{12}}
is equipped with a (stable) smoothing, then the corresponding top stratum of
K_{p_{0};p_{2}}^{\mathbb{B}_{01}\circ\mathbb{B}_{12}}
has an induced stable smoothing (possibly after doing further stabilizations).

Lastly, we combine the stable complex structures of the domain moduli space discussed in the proof of Proposition
19.2
, the NC structures on the vertical tangent bundles to obtain an NC structure on the quotient derived orbifolds.
∎

Proof of Theorem
R
.

We continue after Theorem
19.5
. Suppose the relatively smooth AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{n}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{M}}
are lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Suppose we also have a concatenation
\hat{\mathbb{X}}\circ_{i}\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. The homotopy
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
is also lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. We first can lift
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. This can be done by using the same argument as using a family of Cauchy–Riemann operators interpolating between the linearization of the Floer equation and a complex-linear one. We omit the details, which would be a tedious repetition of the flow category and multimodule case already considered.

Next, suppose
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{n}
,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\mathbb{X}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{Y}}
,
\hat{\mathbb{M}}
are also lifted to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
after further stabilization and smoothing. The stable smoothing and NC structures of
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
can be constructed using the same inductive argument as we always did. The result is obtained by passing to the orbifold quotient.
∎

20.
Comparing Non-homotopic Choices

The comparison between different choices which are not homotopic requires more setup. Choices in this category include

(1)

The choice of integral actions (see Definition
15.1
) in construction the AMS lifts.

(2)

Different choices of lateral lines in Floer domains.

We remove the dependence on these two choices uniformly via a doubling construction (cf.
[
3
, Section 6.10]
). Here, different integral actions give rise to different moduli spaces of curves of projective spaces; as for different choices of lateral lines under the same choice of integral actions, they induce different composition laws. Despite the different effects, we can handle them in the same way.

20.1.
Curves in products of projective spaces

We can generalize the notion of monotone flow categories/multimodules/homotopies (see Definition
14.1
, Definition
14.4
, Definition
14.5
) in the following way. Fix
k\geq 2
and consider the monoid
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{k}
instead of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. Then for each
{\bf d}=(d^{1},\ldots,d^{k})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{k}
, there is a natural poset

A_{\bf d}=\Big\{({\bf d}_{0},\ldots,{\bf d}_{l})\ |\ {\bf d}_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{k}\setminus\{0\},\ {\bf d}={\bf d}_{0}+\cdots+{\bf d}_{l}\Big\}.

A
monotone flow category of rank
k
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{f}}
, consists of a collection of
A_{\bf d}
-stratified objects

M_{\bf d}^{\mathbb{f}},\ \forall{\bf d}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{k}

and a collection of morphisms

M_{{\bf d}_{1}}^{\mathbb{f}}\times M_{{\bf d}_{2}}^{\mathbb{f}}\to\partial^{{\bf d}_{1},{\bf d}_{2}}M_{{\bf d}_{1}+{\bf d}_{2}}^{\mathbb{f}}

which satisfy the natural associativity condition, similar to that imposed in Definition
14.1
. One can see any monotone flow category
{\mathbb{f}}
of rank
k
induces an ordinary flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
with
{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{k}
. Similarly, one can define monotone multimodules and homotopies of higher ranks as we did in Definition
14.4
and Definition
14.5
: we leave it to the interested reader to spell out the details.

Now we would like to build rank
k
monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{k}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Indeed, for
{\bf d}=(d^{1},\ldots,d^{k})
, define

{\mathbb{P}}^{\bf d}={\mathbb{P}}^{d^{1}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{P}}^{d^{k}}.

Each element is an equivalence class of maps with
k
components. Indeed, one can consider the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{P}^{\bf d},{\bf d})
of cylinders in
\mathbb{P}^{\bf d}
of multidegree
{\bf d}
and the open subset

B_{\bf d}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{P}^{\bf d},{\bf d})

of cylinders satisfying 1) the evaluations of the
i
-th component at
+\infty
is
[0,\cdots,0,1]\in\mathbb{P}^{d^{i}}
and 2) the image of the
i
-th component is not contained in any hyperplanes of
\mathbb{P}^{d^{i}}
. Then
B_{\bf d}
is an
A_{\bf d}
-stratified manifold with a smooth action by the complex Lie group

{\mathcal{G}}_{\bf d}:={\mathcal{G}}_{d^{1}}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{k}}

which contains a maximal compact subgroup

G_{\bf d}:=G_{d^{1}}\times\cdots\times G_{d^{k}}\cong U(d^{1})\times\cdots\times U(d^{k}).

Similar to the rank 1 case, one obtains a collection of objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf d}=({\mathcal{G}}_{\bf d},B_{\bf d},C_{\bf d})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}

where
C_{\bf d}\to B_{\bf d}
is the universal curve.

One can define similar structural maps to obtain a monotone flow category, denoted by

\mathbb{Dom}^{k}.

Similarly, for any smooth Floer domain
\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}
, one obtains a monotone multimodule
\mathbb{Dom}^{k,\Sigma^{\mathbb{X}}}
over
(\mathbb{Dom}^{k},\ldots,\mathbb{Dom}^{k};\mathbb{Dom}^{k})
.

A slight difference from the previous rank
1
case is that we choose not to construct rigidifications and stable complex structures at this stage (although we could do so). We would like to do the construction later after pulling back so that certain compatibility holds directly from our construction. At this point, we only take outercollarings and the monotone flow categories/multimodules are enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

20.2.
Double-framed thickenings

We may carry out the roadmap of the AMS construction for the higher-rank approach which facilitates the comparison of different integral actions. We only need to consider the
k=2
case and call such a construction a “double-framed” AMS construction.

We briefly go through the construction for Floer flow categories. Assume one is in Situation
Flow(i)
. Fix an outercollaring width. Let

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{1}},\ {\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{2}}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}

be two integral actions on
{\mathbb{F}}
. Denote their direct sum by

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2}}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}.

Then for each pair
p<q
of objects of
{\mathbb{F}}
, define

{\bf d}_{pq}=(d_{pq}^{1},d_{pq}^{2})={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2}}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2}}(q)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{2}.

Then for each pair
p<q
, let

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}=({\mathcal{G}}_{pq},B_{pq},C_{pq}),

be the restriction of
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\bf d}_{pq}}
to the strata whose associated degrees can be realized by integralized actions of broken Floer trajectories of
H
. This provides a pullback

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}

which is a flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
whose objects are those of
{\mathbb{F}}
.

One can write down the associated flow category of framed maps in the same way as the rank
1
case. More precisely, given objects
p<q
, the space of framed maps consists of tuples
(\phi,u,F)
where
\phi\in B_{pq}
,
u:C_{\phi}\to X
is a smooth map satisfying the exponential decay condition, and
F=(F_{1},F_{2})
consists of two frames where
F_{i}
, called an
\Omega_{i}
-frame along
u
, is a frame of the holomorphic line bundle
L_{u,\Omega_{i}}\to C_{\phi}
satisfying the positive constraint.

The following fact is important for the comparison. For each
p<q
, write

{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}={\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{1}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{2}={\mathcal{G}}_{d_{pq}^{1}}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{pq}^{2}}.

Lemma 20.1
.

B_{pq}
is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{1}
-equivariant principal
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{2}
-bundle over
B_{pq}^{1}
.

Proof.

Abbreviate

d^{i}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}(p)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}(q),\ i=1,2.

Each element of
B_{pq}
is represented by a holomorphic map
(u^{1},u^{2}):C_{\phi}\to\mathbb{P}^{d^{1}}\times\mathbb{P}^{d^{2}}
. Then there is an obvious free
{\mathcal{G}}_{d^{2}}
-action on
B_{pq}
by linearly transforming the second component. Moreover, fix
\phi^{1}\in B_{pq}^{1}
, all holomorphic maps into
{\mathbb{P}}^{d^{2}}
which have the prescribed degrees on each irreducible component are in the same free
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{2}
-orbit. They come from projective embeddings of the irreducible components into
{\mathbb{P}}^{d^{2}}
induced by a basis of global sections of the line bundle with prescribed degree, with matching condition on the nodes. This amounts to looking at all framings with positive evaluation constraint, thereby producing
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{2}
.
∎

We denote the natural projection

\pi_{i}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}^{i},\ i=1,2.

We will use these projections to pull back all choices made for the rank 1 construction to the rank 2 case and take direct sums.

Corollary 20.2
.

The lifts of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{i},{\mathbb{F}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
canonically induce a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}
.

Proof.

By Definition
14.9
, the rigidification for
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{i},{\mathbb{F}}}
is given by maps

\theta_{prq}^{i}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{pr}^{i}\times{}_{\lfloor}B_{rq}^{i}\times Q_{prq}^{i}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}^{i}

satisfying certain conditions. One can simply pullback
\theta_{prq}^{i}
to
{}_{\lfloor}B_{pr}\times{}_{\lfloor}B_{rq}
. More precisely, define

\begin{split}\theta_{prq}:{}_{\lfloor}B_{pr}\times{}_{\lfloor}B_{rq}\to&\ Q_{prq}\\
(\phi_{pr},\phi_{rq})\mapsto&\ \Big(\theta_{prq}^{1}(\pi_{1}(\phi_{pr}),\pi_{1}(\phi_{rq})),\theta_{prq}^{2}(\pi_{2}(\phi_{pr}),\pi_{2}(\phi_{rq}))\Big).\end{split}

These maps define a rigidification of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}
.

On the other hand, the stable complex structure can also be obtained by using such pullbacks, whose compatibility can be checked easily.
∎

Now we assume that
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}
is enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Of course, such a lift does not necessarily come from pullbacks, but for our purpose, it suffices to consider this special situation.

Similarly, for a flow multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{m};{\mathbb{F}})
, if each of the flow categories are equipped with integral actions for
i=1,2
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}^{\Omega_{i}},\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}^{\Omega_{i}},{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}^{\Omega_{i}}.

we obtain a multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{X}}}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}_{1}},\ldots,{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}_{m}};{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

20.3.
Flow category situation

We spell out the comparison between different integral actions on the same flow category in detail.

20.3.1.
Framed maps and group reductions

We can write down the flow category of framed maps

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}^{\mathbb{fr}}_{\mathbb{F}}

which assigns to each pair
p<q
a space
{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}
consisting of triples
(\phi,u,F)
where
\phi\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq}
,
u:C_{\phi}\to X
is a component-wise smooth map which extends continuously over nodes and which converges exponentially fast to periodic orbits, and
F=(F_{1},F_{2})
is a double-frame where
F_{i}
is a frame of the holomorphic line bundle
L_{u,\Omega_{i}}\to C_{\phi}
satisfying the pre-chosen positive or negative constraint.

Notice that there are still natural projection maps

\begin{split}\pi_{i}:{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}\to&\ {}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq,i}^{\rm fr}\\
(\phi,u,F)\mapsto&(\pi_{i}(\phi),u,F_{i}).\end{split}

(20.1)

Now denote

\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}={\mathcal{G}}_{L,pq}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{R,pq}\cong{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}\times{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}.

It acts on
{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}
as before, where
{\mathcal{G}}_{L,pq}
reparametrizes the maps and the frames, while
{\mathcal{G}}_{R,pq}
linearly transforms the frames. Recall that a group reduction (Definition
15.7
) consists of a compatible system of
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-equivariant maps

\Lambda_{pq}:{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}/G_{pq}.

If we have chosen independently group reductions for
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{i}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
, then using the projection map (
20.1
), we can pull them back to obtain a group reduction on the double-framed construction
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
.

20.3.2.
Thickening data

The last step of constructing the topological AMS lift is to choose transverse thickening data. By slightly generalizing Definition
15.10
, a thickening datum on the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}
consists of a collection of unitary
G_{pq}
-representations and
G_{pq}
-equivariant linear maps

\nu_{pq}:W_{pq}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{pq}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{pq}/B_{pq}}^{0,1}\otimes TX).

satisfying the natural compatibility condition with the structural maps. With or without transversality condition, one can write down the thickening (cf. Definition
15.14
)

V_{pq}=\Big\{(\phi,u,e,F)\ |\ (\phi,u,F)\in{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr},e\in W_{pq},\ \phi_{F}=\phi\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{pq},\ \overline{\partial}_{H}u+\nu_{pq}(e)=0\Big\}.

The obstruction bundle
E_{pq}
is still the direct sum of the trivial bundles
W_{pq}\oplus Q_{pq}
and the Kuranishi section
S_{pq}:V_{pq}\to E_{pq}
is the direct sum of the (linearized) group reduction
\lambda_{pq}
and the forgetful map
(\phi,u,e,F)\mapsto e
. There is the natural footprint map

S_{pq}^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to{}_{\lfloor}M_{pq}^{\mathbb{F}}

sending the
G_{pq}
-orbit of
(\phi,u,e,F)
to the equivalence class of
u:C_{\phi}\to X
.

Then suppose one has chosen independently thickening datum

\nu_{pq}^{i}:W_{pq}^{i}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{pq}^{i}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{pq}^{i}/B_{pq,i}}^{0,1}\otimes TX),\ i=1,2,

then one can pullback each of them

(\pi_{i})^{*}\nu_{pq}^{i}:W_{pq}^{i}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{pq}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{pq}/B_{pq}}^{0,1}\otimes TX).

Lemma 20.3
.

If
\nu_{pq}^{i}
is transverse, so is
\pi_{i}^{*}\nu_{pq}^{i}
.

Proof.

This follows from the observation that if
W_{pq}^{i}
is mapped surjectively onto the cokernel of the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator, so is the pullback.
∎

20.3.3.
Finalizing proof

Now we would like to compare the two lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
using a rank 1 construction and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
using a rank 2 construction where the thickening datum is pulled back from the choices made for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
.

Recall the notion of free quotient of Kuranishi spaces (Definition
3.11
).

Definition 20.4
.

Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
be a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}
with morphism space

K_{pq}^{\sim}=(G_{pq}^{\sim},V_{pq}^{\sim},E_{pq}^{\sim},S_{pq}^{\sim}).

A
free quotient
of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
consists of for each pair of objects
p,q\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
a decomposition

G_{pq}^{\sim}=G_{pq}\times G_{pq}^{\prime}

of compact Lie groups satisfying 1) the conjugacy class of group embeddings
G_{pr}^{\sim}\times G_{rq}^{\sim}\to G_{pq}^{\sim}
respect the decomposition and 2)
G_{pq}^{\prime}
acts freely on
V_{pq}^{\sim}
. Notice that one obtains a new flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
with morphism spaces
K_{pq}
being the free quotient of
K_{pq}^{\sim}
by
G_{pq}^{\prime}
(see Definition
3.11
). By abuse of notation, we also call the resulting flow category consisting of these free quotients a free quotient of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
.

Notice that in the above definition, if
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
is enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}
, then the resulting flow category
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}
obtained by taking the quotient by the full group is isomorphic to the one obtained from the free quotient obtained by taking the residual group action.

Proposition 20.5
.

Assume we are in Situation
Flow(i)
. Fix an outercollaring width. Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
,
i=1,2
be two integral actions. Let
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
be an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
(of rank
1
, subject to an outercollaring width and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}
). Then there exists a rank 2 AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
(subject to the same outercollaring width and the rank 2 integral action) and another rank
2
lift of the outercollaring
{\mathbb{F}}
, denoted by
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
such that 1)
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
is isomorphic to a free quotient of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and 2)
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
is isomorphic to a stabilization of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
. The same applies to an AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to an outercollaring width and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}
.

Proof.

We provide a construction that works equally well for both
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
: their relations with our
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
are completely symmetric. We will focus on explaining the situation for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
.

Recall the roadmap of constructing
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
. First, consider the flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1},{\mathbb{F}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. By Lemma
20.1
, there is another flow category
\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{F}}}
whose corresponding moduli spaces are principal bundles over those in
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1},{\mathbb{F}}}
. Then the flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
is naturally induced from it in the following way. The differences between
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
lie in: 1) the compact Lie group associated to objects
p<q
is
G^{1}_{pq}\times G^{2}_{pq}
; 2) the space
B^{1}_{pq}
is replaced by a principal
G^{2}_{pq}
-bundle over
B^{1}_{pq}
, which is a subspace of
B_{pq}
discussed in Lemma
20.3
where we replace each fiber of the principal
{\mathcal{G}}^{2}_{pq}
-bundle
B_{pq}\to B^{1}_{pq}
by the maximal compact subgroup, i.e., looking at the unitary framings on the second factor; 3) the obstruction bundle and Kuranishi section are pulled back to the principal
G^{2}_{pq}
-bundle constructed in 2). By construction, we see that
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
is isomorphic to the free quotient of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
.

On the other hand, we define
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
taking full advantage of the double framing. This time, along each map
u:C_{\phi}\to X
there are two sets of frames, one for the line bundle induced from
\Omega_{1}
and the other for the line bundle induced from
\Omega_{2}
. The group reduction chosen for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
can be extended to this “double-framed” version. This time, we consider the total space
B_{pq}
for each
p<q
, acted on by
G_{pq}=G^{1}_{pq}\times G^{2}_{pq}
. As for the obstruction bundle, we first pull back the thickening data
{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{W}}^{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
, which is still transverse by Lemma
20.3
, then we take the direct sum with
\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{1}
and
\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{2}
so that the obstruction bundle is

\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}\oplus\underline{\smash{W}}_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{2}}\oplus\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{1}\oplus\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{2}

where the
\underline{\smash{Q}}_{pq}^{2}
-component of the Kuranishi section is induced from the group reduction for the
\Omega_{2}
-frames. Therefore, in the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
, the statement of Proposition
20.5
holds, where we construct the rigidification by pullback.

To complete the construction of
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
, one needs to extend the statement to the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Notice that the pullback is induced from maps between domain moduli. Hence the construction of the relative NC structure for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
can be canonically lifted to
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
. To extend the smoothing, notice that for each
p,q
, the ambient space associated with
p<q
in
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
is a topological
G_{pq}^{2}
-principal bundle over the ambient space associated with
p<q
in
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
. One can always use the group actions to construct smooth bundle structures inductively, hence
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
is smoothed. Then using the stabilization structure between
\hat{\mathbb{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
, one can induce a canonical smooth structure on
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
. Hence the statement is also true within the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}
. Lastly, the NC structure construction is quite elementary and can also be respected.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12.2
.

Using the notations introduced in Proposition
20.5
, recall that the flow categories
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
are obtained as quotients of the flow categories
\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. We can use the intermediate flow category
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\sim}
to provide the desired construction. Indeed, recall that the Floer flow category requires passing to the quotient under the
{\mathbb{R}}
-translation action if the energy is strictly greater than
0
. To obtain the lift of the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{FF}}
, we take the normally complex Kuranishi spaces before taking the quotient. By passing to the orbifold quotient, we finish the proof.
∎

In fact we can also prove that given a pair of integral actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega_{i}}
,
i=1,2
, the double-framed construction together with FOP perturbation produces a chain complex
C({\mathbb{F}})
which is well-defined up to homotopy given by a continuation map also defined via a double-framed construction. The proof of Proposition
12.2
can be viewed as a special case of this fact.

20.4.
Multimodules

Compared with flow categories, for multimodules and homotopies, the lateral lines specified in Definition
14.8
may not be homotopically unique. Note that under the double-frame construction, we need to use the lateral lines from two different versions simultaneously to define concatenation maps of the framings. They exactly facilitate the construction of the doubly-thickened moduli spaces.

Proof of Proposition
12.3
.

We only present the proof in the first situation; the second situation (the Poincaré duality multimodule) only differs in notations. Fix an outercollaring width. For any involved flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
, the double-framed construction produces AMS lifts
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{\rm double}
(to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
), which depends on choices made in the construction. Two particular choices as described above are the “pullbacks” from each single-framed versions, denoted by

\displaystyle\ \hat{\mathbb{F}}_{(0)}^{{\rm double}},\

\displaystyle\ \hat{\mathbb{F}}_{(1)}^{{\rm double}}.

Now turn to the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}
. Starting from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega_{1}\oplus\Omega_{2},{\mathbb{X}}}
. For any collection of double-framed AMS lifts

\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{\rm double},\ \ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{\rm double},\ \hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm double}}

then one can construct a double-framed AMS lift

\hat{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm double}

as a multimodule over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{\rm double},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m}^{\rm double};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm double}})
. Moreover, the construction is compatible with continuation maps connecting different double-framed AMS lifts of each individual flow category.

Notice that one has two particular situations of
\hat{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm double}
coming from pulling back the single-framed construction
\hat{\mathbb{X}}^{\rm double}_{(s)}
for
s=0,1
, which are, respectively, multimodules over
(\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{1,(s)}^{{\rm double}},\ldots,\hat{\mathbb{F}}_{m,(s)}^{\rm double};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{}^{\prime}{\rm double}}_{(s)})
. Now let
\hat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{j}\mathbb{F}_{j}}_{(01)}
and
\hat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}_{(10)}
be the double-framed AMS lift of the diagonal bimodules. Then the double-framed concatenation/gluing produces a homotopy from

\left(\hat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{1}{\mathbb{F}}_{1}}_{(01)}\times\cdots\times\hat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{m}{\mathbb{F}}_{m}}_{(01)}\right)\circ\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{(1)}^{\rm double}\circ\hat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}{\mathbb{F}}^{\prime}}_{(10)}

to
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{(1)}^{\rm double}
. This requires extending the proof of Theorem
12.4
provided in the last section to the double-framed setting, which is nothing more complicated. Passing to the quotient via the functor
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
, one obtains a homotopy exactly expected in Proposition
12.3
.
∎

21.
AMS Construction for Other Flow categories, Multimodules, and Homotopies

In this section we discuss the AMS construction for flow categories etc. which are not from smooth Floer domains.

21.1.
Pearly flow category

Recall that the Pearly flow category
\mathbb{P}
is associated to an almost Kähler manifold
(X,\omega,J)
and a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
on
X
. The object set is

\displaystyle\ {\rm Ob}\mathbb{P}={\rm crit}f\times\Pi\

\displaystyle\ {\rm where}\ \Pi=\frac{\pi_{2}(X)}{{\rm ker}c_{1}\cap{\rm ker}\omega}.

An object is written as
x=(\underline{\smash{x}},a_{x})
where
\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\rm crit}f
and
a_{x}\in\Pi
. We will construct AMS lifts of outercollarings of
\mathbb{P}
as declared in Theorem
12.7
. The main difference from the case of the Floer flow category is that the boundary strata of moduli spaces are given by fiber products instead of direct products.

21.1.1.
Fibered topological spaces, Kuranishi spaces, and derived orbifolds

Definition 21.1
.

Let
Y
be a smooth manifold (without boundary and corners).

(1)

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}(Y)
be the regular stratification category whose objects are pairs
(N,f_{-},f_{+})
where
N\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
and
f_{\pm}:N\to Y
is a pair of continuous maps. A morphism in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}(Y)
from
(N_{1},f_{1,\pm})
to
(N_{2},f_{2,\pm})
is a morphism of stratified spaces
\rho:N_{1}\to N_{2}
such that the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{N_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1,\pm}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{Y}}
\textstyle{N_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2,\pm}}
\textstyle{Y}

The (non-symmetric) product of
(N_{1},f_{1,\pm})
and
(N_{2},f_{2,\pm})
is the fiber product

\left(N_{1}\underset{Y}{\times}N_{2},\ \pi_{1}^{*}f_{1,-},\ \pi_{2}^{*}f_{2,+}\right):=\left(N_{1}\ {}_{f_{1,+}}\underset{Y}{\times}{}_{f_{2,-}}N_{2},\ \pi_{1}^{*}f_{1,-},\ \pi_{2}^{*}f_{2,+}\right).

The monoidal unit is
(Y,{\rm Id}_{Y},{\rm Id}_{Y})
.

(2)

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}(Y)
be the regular stratification category whose objects are
(K,f_{-},f_{+})
where
K=(G,V,E,S)
is a Kuranishi space and
f_{\pm}:V\to Y
is a pair of
G
-invariant topological submersions. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}(Y)
be the corresponding category of smooth Kuranishi spaces together with smooth submersions to
Y
. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
be the subcategory whose objects are smooth normally complex Kuranishi spaces with
G
-invariant submersions. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}(Y)
be the category of relatively smooth Kuranishi spaces
K/B=(G,V/B,E,S)
together with
G
-invariant maps
f_{\pm}:V\to Y
such that they are fiberwise submersions. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
be the subcategory where the relative Kuranishi spaces are equipped with relative NC structures.

(3)

Let
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}(Y)
be the category whose objects are pairs
({\mathcal{D}},f_{-},f_{+})
where
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is a derived orbifold and
f_{\pm}:{\mathcal{U}}\to Y
are smooth submersions. Let
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
be the category whose objects are
({\mathcal{D}},f_{-},f_{+})
where
{\mathcal{D}}
is an NC derived orbifold and
f_{\pm}:{\mathcal{U}}\to Y
are smooth submersions.

Remark 21.2
.

Unlike the presumed stable complex versions, the monoidal structure given by fiber products in the NC versions (
\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
,
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
,
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}(Y)
) does not require the almost complex structure on
Y
because
Y
is assumed to be a manifold.

Notice that one can also talk about collared and rigidified versions of
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}(Y),\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}(Y)
, and
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}(Y)
.

One needs to generalize the discussion of stable smoothings of topological Kuranishi spaces to the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}(Y)
.

Definition 21.3
.

A
stable smoothing
of an object
(K,f_{\pm})\in\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}(Y)
where
K=(G,V,E,S)
consists of a unitary representation
W
of
G
, a
G
-smoothing on
V\times W
, a
G
-invariant extension
\tilde{f}_{\pm}:V\times W\to Y
of
f_{\pm}:V\cong V\times\{0\}\to Y
such that
\tilde{f}_{\pm}
are smooth submersions.

Lemma 21.4

(Stable smoothing with maps)
.

(cf.
[
3
, Lemma 4.5]
)
Let
V
be a smooth
G
-manifold and
f_{0}:V\to Y
be a continuous
G
-invariant map. Then there exists a unitary representation
W
of
G
, a
G
-smoothing on
V\times W
which is in the same stable isotopy class of the given smooth structure on
V
, and a smooth submersion
\tilde{f}:V\times W\to Y
whose restriction to
V\times\{0\}
coincides with
f_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially that of
[
3
, Lemma 4.5]
, which we reproduce using our language. Choose a Riemannian metric on
Y
. Then one can choose a smooth
G
-invariant approximation
f:V\to Y
and a smooth
G
-invariant function
\epsilon:V\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
such that for all
v\in V
, there is a unique shortest geodesic of length shorter than
\epsilon(v)
connecting
f_{0}(v)
and
f(v)
, and such that the exponential map
\exp_{f(v)}\xi
is well-defined for
\xi
in the
\epsilon(v)
-ball of
T_{f(v)}Y
. Then there is a smooth equivariant bundle map
\rho:f^{*}TY\to f^{*}TY
compressing
f^{*}TY
diffeomorphically into the
\epsilon
-disk bundle. Therefore the map

\tilde{f}:f^{*}TY\to Y,\ \tilde{f}(v,\xi)=\exp_{f(v)}(\rho(\xi)).

is a smooth submersion. Choose another smooth (real)
G
-vector bundle
E\to V
such that

E\oplus f^{*}TY\cong V\times W

for some unitary representation
W
of
G
.
\tilde{f}
clearly extends to a smooth
G
-equivariant map from
V\times W
to
Y
, which is still a smooth submersion.

We would like to define a self-homeomorphism of
V\times W
to pullback the smooth structure on
V\times W
. Indeed, we can write

f_{0}(v)=\exp_{f(v)}\xi_{0}(v),\ \xi_{0}(v)\in T_{f(v)}Y.

Define a nonlinear bundle map

F:f^{*}TY\to f^{*}TY,\ (v,\xi)\mapsto(v,\xi+\xi_{0}(v)).

Extend it to a smooth map
V\times W\to V\times W
which is the identity on the summand
E
. Then we can see the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{v\mapsto(v,0)}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{V\times W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{V\times W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}

Therefore,
f_{0}
is extended to a smooth submersion from
V\times W
to
Y
. Lastly, the smoothing on
V\times W
is in the same stable isotopy class because the choices made in the proof, especially the smooth approximation
f
of
f_{0}
, can all be made isotopic.
∎

21.1.2.
The monotone flow category of holomorphic cylinders

Let
(X,\omega,J)
be an almost Kähler manifold. Denote

\Pi^{\rm eff}:=\Big\{a\in\Pi\ |\ \omega(a)>0\Big\}\cup\{0\}

which is an abelian monoid whose Grothendieck construction is
\Pi
.

We would like to generalize the notion of monotone flow categories. Recall that a monotone flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}
contains objects
M_{d}^{\mathbb{f}}
indexed by
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. In the current case, one can define a generalization consisting of objects
M_{a}^{\mathbb{f}}
indexed by
a\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
, together with structural maps from the fiber product of
M_{a}^{\mathbb{f}}
and
M_{b}^{\mathbb{f}}
over a space to
M_{a+b}^{\mathbb{f}}
satisfying a version of associativity condition. We describe such a monotone flow category
\mathbb{cyl}(X)
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf C}}=\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}(X)
by defining

M_{a}^{\mathbb{cyl}}(X):=\left(\bigsqcup_{\omega(A)=a}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(X,J,A),\ {\rm ev}_{-},\ {\rm ev}_{+}\right)\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}(X),

where
{\rm ev}_{\pm}
are the evaluation maps at the marked points. Then for
a,b\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
, there is a natural map

M_{a}^{\mathbb{cyl}}(X)\underset{X}{\times}M_{b}^{\mathbb{cyl}}(X)\to M_{a+b}^{\mathbb{cyl}}(X).

These structures define the monotone flow category
\mathbb{cyl}(X)
. By taking the Grothendieck construction, it induces an ordinary flow category

\mathbb{Cyl}(X)

called the
cylinder flow category
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}(X)
whose object set is
\Pi
.

To carry out the AMS construction, we first choose an integral symplectic form
\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(X)
satisfying 1)
J
is tamed by
\Omega
and 2)
{\rm Ker}\Omega={\rm ker}\omega\subseteq\pi_{2}(X)
. Then for each
a\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
, there is an associated integer
d=d_{a}
. As
J
is tamed by
\Omega
,
d_{a}\geq 0
.

Proposition 21.5
.

There exists a lift
\widehat{\mathbb{cyl}}(X)
of the outercollaring
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{cyl}(X)
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}(X)
as a monotone flow category supported over
\Pi^{\rm eff}
.

Proof.

We first describe a monotone flow category of curves
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
, which is the restriction of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
(specified in Section
14
) to open sets induced from
\Omega
. For each
d
, let

A_{d}^{\Omega}\subseteq A_{d}

to be the Alexandrov open subset of partitions
d=d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}
such that for each
d_{l}
, there exists
a_{l}\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
with
d_{l}=\Omega(a_{l})
. Recall that the monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}
gives objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d},C_{d})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}.

For
a\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
, let
B_{a}^{\Omega}\subseteq B_{\Omega(a)}
be the union of strata prescribed by
A_{d}^{\Omega}
, which is open and
{\mathcal{G}}_{d}
-invariant. Define

{\mathcal{C}}_{a}^{\Omega}\in{\rm Ob}{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}

to be the restriction of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Omega(a)}
to
B_{a}^{\Omega}
. Then one obtains a monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\Omega,\mathbb{cyl}}
.

Then one has a corresponding monotone flow category of framed maps consisting of objects

{\rm Map}_{a}^{\rm fr}(X)\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{a}^{\Omega}

whose fiber of
\phi\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{a}^{\Omega}
is the space of pairs
(u,F)
where
u:\mathring{C}_{\phi}\to X
is a smooth map on each irreducible component which extends continuously to
C_{\phi}
and which converges exponentially at cylindrical nodes and markings and where
F
is a frame of the holomorphic line bundle
L_{u,\Omega}\to C_{\phi}
.

One can also choose a collection of transverse thickening data

\nu_{a}:W_{a}\to\Gamma_{c}({}_{\lfloor}\mathring{C}_{a}^{\Omega}\times X,\Lambda_{{}_{\lfloor}\mathring{C}_{a}^{\Omega}/{}_{\lfloor}B_{a}^{\Omega}}^{0,1}\otimes TX)

which are collared and respect the structural maps. Here
W_{a}
is a collection of unitary representations of
G_{\Omega(a)}
. On the other hand, one can choose a group reduction scheme, given by a collection of continuous maps

\lambda_{a}:{\rm Map}_{a}^{\rm fr}(X)\to Q_{a}\cong{\mathcal{G}}_{\Omega(a)}/G_{\Omega(a)}.

Then one obtains a corresponding thickening

K_{a}=(G_{a},V_{a},E_{a},S_{a})\in{\rm Ob}{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}

where

V_{a}=\Big\{(\phi,u,F,e)\ |\ (\phi,u,F)\in{\rm Map}_{a}^{\rm fr}(X),\ e\in W_{a},\ \phi=\phi_{F}\in{}_{\lfloor}B_{a}^{\Omega},\ \overline{\partial}_{J}u+\nu_{a}(e)=0\Big\},

with the natural
G_{a}
-action,
E_{a}
is the trivial bundle with fiber
W_{a}\oplus Q_{a}
, and
S_{a}
is the section given by

S_{a}(\phi,u,F,e)=(e,\lambda_{a}(\phi,u,F)).

Similar to Theorem
15.15
, the natural map

S_{a}^{-1}(0)/G_{a}\to{}_{\lfloor}M_{a}^{\mathbb{cyl}}(X)

is an isomorphism of orbispaces. By an abuse of notation, let
V_{a}
denote the regular locus for the operator
\overline{\partial}_{J}u+\nu_{a}(e)=0
, i.e., the subspace such that the linearization of this equation is surjective. Then, it contains the zero locus by the transversality assumption. Then one obtains a lift of the monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{cyl}(X)
to the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}(X)
. In the same way as argued in Subsection
15.4
, the lift is in fact enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}(X)
. We denote this lift by

\widehat{\mathbb{cyl}}(X).

The construct of normal complex structure is slightly easier than the case of Floer flow categories. Indeed, because we do not have Hamiltonian perturbations, the (vertical) linearized operators are the Cauchy–Riemann operators

D_{u,e}=\left[D_{u}\overline{\partial}_{J}+D_{u}\nu_{a}(u,e)+\nu_{a}(u)\right]:\Omega^{0}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)\oplus W_{a}\to\Omega^{0,1}(C_{\phi},u^{*}TX)

where
W_{a}
is a complex representation. Recall that the canonical linearization is calculated by the Levi–Civita connection on
X
induced from the symplectic form and the almost complex structure, which is not necessarily complex-linear. However, one can choose a 1-parameter family of linear operators connecting
D_{u,e}
to a complex linear one, without using the complicated interpolation argument as in the Floer case. This means for each individual moduli space, one has a canonical homotopy class of isomorphisms between the vertical tangent bundle and a complex vector bundle. One can choose concrete isomorphisms inductively. This provides a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}(X)
.

The smoothing procedure is slightly different from the previous case as we also need to make the evaluation maps smooth. However, by Lemma
21.4
, one can perform stable smoothing on
(K_{a},{\rm ev}_{\pm})
so that not only the Kuranishi spaces become smooth, but also the evaluation maps become smooth submersions. Then following the same inductive procedure, after stabilization, one obtains a lift to the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}(X)
. Together with the stable complex structures on the domain flow categories, this is a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}(X)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12.7
.

We first describe the lift of the pearly flow category
{\mathbb{P}}
. Choose a lift
\widehat{\mathbb{cyl}}(X)
provided by Proposition
21.5
. By taking quotient, one obtains a monotone flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}(X)
, which induces an ordinary flow category, denoted by
\widetilde{\mathbb{Cyl}}(X)
, whose objects are elements of
\Pi
.

Then one can obtain a lift of the corresponding outercollaring of
{\mathbb{P}}
by “inserting flow lines.” We do this inductively. In the lowest energy case, one only needs to add flow rays at the negative and positive ends to obtain Kuranishi charts of moduli spaces labelled by a pair of critical points. In higher energy case, there are configurations where in the interior there are gradient segments of positive length. An inductive scheme can be designed in a rather elementary way to produce inductively all charts for involved moduli spaces. Hence one obtains a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{P}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
(inserting flow lines does not affect the NC structure).

Next, we discuss the regularization of the bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
. In fact, one can take different Morse–Smale pairs and different almost complex structures to define
\mathbb{P}
and consider the bimodule as an interpolation between them. However, we choose the same one to simplify the notations.

The construction is very similar to the regularization of the Pearly flow category. First, there is a monotone bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{cyl}(X)}
given by holomorphic cylinders which have a parametrized principal component. This is a monotone bimodule over
(\mathbb{cyl}(X);\mathbb{cyl}(X))
.

Then after outercollaring, following the same procedure as the proof of Proposition
21.5
, one obtains an AMS lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}(X)
, denoted by
\widehat{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{cyl}(X)}
. After taking quotient, one obtains a bimodule
\widetilde{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{\mathbb{cyl}^{\mathbb{P}}(X)}
over
(\widetilde{\mathbb{cyl}}(X);\widetilde{\mathbb{cyl}}(X))
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}(X)
. Then using the fact that evaluation maps are smooth submersions, by inserting Morse flow lines, one obtains a lift of
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{PP}}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{P}};\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
. This proves the second item of Theorem
12.7
.
∎

21.2.
Deformed Morse-to-Morse bimodule

The regularization of the Morse-to-Morse bimodule was discussed in
[
14
]
using the original perturbation from
[
3
]
.

Proposition 21.6
.

Upon choosing an integral symplectic form
\Omega
, there exists a lift of
\Delta^{\mathbb{MM}}
of the deformed Morse-to-Morse bimodule to
\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
as a bimodule over
(\mathbb{M};\mathbb{M})
(without outercollaring).

Proof.

For each
d
, consider the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
of 2-pointed stable holomorphic spheres in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
. Choose the negative convention; the other choice works as well. Let
B_{d}=B_{d}^{-}
be the subset of curves whose images are not contained in any hyperplane and whose value at the negative end is
[1,0,\ldots,0]
. Then consider the curve
{\mathcal{C}}_{d}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d},C_{d})
. Notice that this is a family of curves without boundary or corners.

Now choose an integral symplectic form
\Omega
. For each
a\in\Pi^{\rm eff}
, denote
d=d_{a}
. Consider the space of framed maps

{\rm Map}_{d}^{\rm fr}=\Big\{(\phi,u,F)\ |\ \phi\in B_{d},u:C_{\phi}\to X,\ F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d})\ {\rm is\ a\ basis\ of\ }H^{0}(L_{u,\Omega})\Big\}.

Choose a group reduction scheme
\lambda_{d}:{\rm Map}_{d}^{\rm fr}\to Q_{d}
and a transverse perturbation

\iota:W\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{d}\times X,\Lambda^{0,1}_{\mathring{C}_{d}/B_{d}}\otimes TX).

This induces a topological Kuranishi space

K_{d}=(G_{d},V_{d},E_{d},S_{d})

with ambient space

V_{d}=\Big\{(\phi,u,F,e)\ |\ (\phi,u,F)\in{\rm Map}_{d},\ e\in W_{a},\ \phi=\phi_{F}\in B_{d},\ \overline{\partial}_{J}u+\iota(e)=0\Big\},

obstruction bundle
E_{d}=W\oplus Q_{d}
, and Kuranishi section

S_{d}(\phi,u,F,e)=(e,\lambda_{d}(\phi,u,F)).

It also comes with a ref-
C^{\infty,1}
-structure on
V_{d}/B_{d}
as well as continuous evaluation maps
{\rm ev}_{\pm}:V_{d}\to X
. Then using Lemma
21.4
, one can find a stable smoothing of
V_{d}
making the evaluation maps submersive.

Last, using the submersiveness of the evaluation maps, one obtains a lift of the bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{\mathbb{MM}}
by taking fiber products with stable/unstable manifolds of the Morse flow.
∎

Remark 21.7
.

In
[
14
]
one considered the Morse-to-Floer and Floer-to-Morse bimodules and their concatenation along the Floer flow category. However, their concatenation along the Morse flow category is difficult to regularize (the codimension two gluing is hard to fit into the framework of flow bimodules). In this paper, we do not consider these two bimodules and their concatenations. The Morse-to-Morse bimodule will arise from the concatenations of the Morse-to-Pearly and the Pearly-to-Morse bimodules, whose regularization will be addressed later.

21.3.
Pearly-to-Morse and Morse-to-Pearly bimodules

In this subsection we regularize the two bimodules shown in the title. To simplify the notations, we will only use a single integral symplectic form
\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(X)
which is a multiple of a rational approximation of
\omega
which also tames
J
. The case with different choices of
\Omega
can be discussed in a similar way as the case of pair-of-pants multimodules.

21.3.1.
The domains

The model for the Morse flow category can be viewed as a monotone flow category over the trivial monoid
\{0\}
. Let such a trivial monotone flow category be
\mathbb{o}
, which has only one object
M_{0}^{\mathbb{o}}
chosen to be a singleton in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. We would like to define a monotone flow bimodule over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
. For each
d
, we consider the family of curves

{\mathcal{C}}_{d}^{\mathbb{PM}}=({\mathcal{G}}_{d},B_{d}^{\mathbb{PM}},C_{d}^{\mathbb{PM}})

where the space of curves
B_{d}^{\mathbb{PM}}
in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
