Then the chain-level quantum Steenrod operation can be defined using the count of zero-dimensional moduli spaces. One can use the standard argument to show that up to chain homotopy the chain-level map is well-defined.

24.4.2.
The AMS lifts and FOP perturbations with an inhomogeneous term

Now we define a perturbation of the quantum Steenrod multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
by using the inhomogeneous term. Notice that each
\eta\in\widehat{S}^{\infty}
corresponds uniquely to a smooth parametrized Morse flow line
y_{w}(s)
in
S^{\infty}
. Then for objects
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sf p},w;(x^{\prime},w^{\prime})
, consider the moduli space

\mathring{M}_{x_{1}\cdots x_{\sf p}w;(x^{\prime},w^{\prime})}^{\mathbb{X}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}}=\left\{(u,\eta)\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}u:\Sigma^{\mathbb{QSt}}\to X,\ \eta\in W^{\rm us}_{w}\cap W^{\rm st}_{w^{\prime}}\subseteq\widehat{S}^{\infty},\\
\overline{\partial}_{J}u+\nu=0,\\
u(z_{i})\in W^{\rm us}_{\underline{\smash{x}}_{i}},\ u(z_{\infty})\in W^{\rm st}_{\underline{\smash{x}}^{\prime}}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

It can be compactified in a natural way, which is denoted by

M_{x_{1}\cdots x_{\sf p}w;(x^{\prime},w^{\prime})}^{\mathbb{X}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}}.

These moduli spaces form a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
over
({\mathbb{M}},\ldots,\mathbb{M},{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{M}}\#{{\mathbb{E}}})
, which is a variant of the
\nu=0
case considered previously, which is denoted by
\mathbb{X}_{0}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
. After outercollaring, using the same method as proving Theorem
24.2
, one can construct an AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. The inhomogeneous term
\nu
can be dealt with by considering the graph
\mathrm{graph}(u):\Sigma^{\mathbb{QSt}}\to\Sigma^{\mathbb{QSt}}\times X
, where the product
\Sigma^{\mathbb{QSt}}\times X
is equipped with the sheared almost complex structure (e.g.
[
3
, Lemma 6.18]
) so that we can go back to the standard situation. The resulting lift in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
is denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
.

Lemma 24.9
.

When the virtual dimension is zero, the original section is FOP transverse.

Proof.

By the monotonicity condition and the classical transversality condition, when the virtual dimension is zero, the moduli space
\mathring{M}_{x_{1}\cdots x_{\sf p}w;(x^{\prime},w^{\prime})}^{\mathbb{X}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}}
agrees with its compactification and there are no orbifold points in the moduli space. The Kuranishi section on the thickening is transverse to the zero section because the Cauchy–Riemann equation is transversely cut out. Then by the
(Classical Transversality)
property of FOP transverse perturbations, the original section is FOP transverse.
∎

Proposition 24.10
.

There exists an FOP transverse perturbation of
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
such that for moduli spaces of dimension at most
0
, the perturbation agrees with the original Kuranishi section.

Proof.

This follows from the same proof of Lemma
13.13
.
∎

Furthermore, there is a clear homotopy
\mathbb{H}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
from
\mathbb{X}_{0}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to
\mathbb{X}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
by adding an additional parameter
t\in[0,1]
and changing
\nu
to
t\nu
in the equation. One can further construct an AMS lift
\hat{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
as a homotopy from
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to
\hat{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
. Moreover, one can extend the FOP transverse perturbations
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to an FOP transverse perturbation on
\hat{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
, in an equivariant way. This leads to a chain homotopy between the chain maps defined with or without the inhomogeneous term. This concludes the proof of Theorem
24.6
.

25.
The Equivariant Floer theory for iterations—Proof of Theorem
I

In this section we construct the equivariant Floer theory, including equivariant Floer cohomology, equivariant continuation maps, and equivariant PSS/SSP maps, induced from a prime iteration of a Hamiltonian. Let
{\sf p}
be a prime.

25.1.
Equivariant Floer flow category as a coupling

25.1.1.
Coupling of Floer and Morse flow categories

We define a notion called
coupling
of flow categories. This notion cannot be defined on the abstract level but rely on the geometric setup.

Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be the Floer flow category on
X
associated to a pair
(H,J)
. Let
{\mathbb{M}}
be the Morse flow category on another manifold
Y
associated to a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
. Define
{\mathbb{F}}\#{\mathbb{M}}
be a flow category as follows. The set of objects is

{\rm Ob}({\mathbb{F}}\#{\mathbb{M}})={\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{M}}

whose elements are denoted by
p=(p^{\mathbb{F}},p^{\mathbb{M}})
. For the morphism space
M_{pq}^{{\mathbb{F}}\#{\mathbb{M}}}
, consider the moduli space of pairs
(u,v)
where
u:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to X
is a solution to the Floer equation connection
p^{\mathbb{F}}
and
q^{\mathbb{F}}
and
v:{\mathbb{R}}\to Y
is a Morse trajectory connection
p^{\mathbb{M}}
and
q^{\mathbb{M}}
; two pairs
(u,v)
and
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
are equivalent if there exists
s_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\ u(\cdot,\cdot)=u^{\prime}(\cdot+s_{0},\cdot),\

\displaystyle\ v(\cdot)=v^{\prime}(\cdot+s_{0}).

One then compactifies this moduli spaces by allowing “synchronized” breakings (as well as sphere bubblings).

Now we describe the equivariant Floer flow category as a special coupling.

Definition 25.1
.

Let
H
be a 1-periodic Hamiltonian on
(X,\omega)
such that the
{\sf p}
-th iteration
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
is nondegenerate. Let
J
be a fixed
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure. Define the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant Floer flow category
to be the coupling

{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:={\mathbb{F}}\#{\mathbb{E}}

where
{\mathbb{E}}
is the Morse flow category on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
.

On
{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
there is an action

{\mathcal{A}}=({\mathcal{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{A}}_{2}):{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

where

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{1}(p^{\mathbb{F}},p^{{\mathbb{E}}})={\mathcal{A}}_{H^{\natural p}}(p^{\mathbb{F}}),\

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}_{2}(p^{\mathbb{F}},p^{{\mathbb{E}}})=f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(p^{{\mathbb{E}}}).

The grading is given by

{\rm deg}(p^{\mathbb{F}},p^{{\mathbb{E}}}):={\rm deg}(p^{\mathbb{F}})+{\rm deg}(p^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Moreover, there is a free action on
{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
the twisted Novikov group

{\mathbb{\Pi}}_{\sf p}:=\Pi\times{\mathbb{Z}}\times{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}={\mathbb{\Pi}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}.

For different Hamiltonians one can also define an equivariant continuation bimodule. Let
H_{i}^{\natural{\sf p}}
be pairs as above for
i=0,1
. Choose a Floer data on the infinite cylinder connecting
H_{0}
and
H_{1}
, denoted by
\sigma
. Let
\sigma^{\natural{\sf p}}
be the
{\sf p}
-th iteration as a Floer daum on the infinite cylinder connecting
H_{0}^{\natural{\sf p}}
and
H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}}
. By coupling the bimodule induced from
\sigma^{\natural{\sf p}}
and the diagonal bimodule
{\mathbb{\Delta}}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}}
, one obtains a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant Novikov bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
, denoted by
{\mathbb{B}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}_{01}
.

Proposition 25.2
.

\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant Novikov flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
and
\mathbb{B}_{01}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant Novikov flow bimodule over
(\mathbb{F}_{0}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}};\mathbb{F}_{1}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
.

Now we state the technical theorem regarding equivariant AMS lifts of
{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
as well as
\mathbb{B}_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
.

Theorem 25.3

(Equivariant AMS lifts)
.

(1)

Let
{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
be the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant Floer flow category defined in Definition
25.1
. Fix the outercollaring width and an integral action on
{\mathbb{F}}
. There is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
, called the
equivariant AMS lift
of
\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
(subject to the outercollaring width and the integral action).

(2)

Choose integral actions on
{\mathbb{F}}_{0}
and
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}
(not necessarily compatible with respect to the interpolating Floer data). Given equivariant AMS lifts of
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
resp.
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
of
{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the chosen integral actions, there exists a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant lift of the outercollaring of
\mathbb{B}_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
as a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
.

Proof.

See next Subsection.
∎

Now assuming this theorem, one can define the equivariant Floer chain complex. Since the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-action is free, by Theorem
N
, one can find a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant FOP perturbation on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, denoted by
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. Therefore, by taking the isotropy free part of the perturbed zero locus, one obtains a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded Novikov flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
. Notice that there is still the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action which preserves all structures. Then applying the abstract construction in Section
7
, one obtains a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded cochain complex

CF^{*}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},J^{\natural{\sf p}},\Xi^{\natural{\sf p}}):=C^{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})

which is a graded
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-module with a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action. The
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed part, denoted by

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}),

is called the (Tate)
equivariant Floer complex
of
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
.

Furthermore, suppose one has two such equivariant complexes

\displaystyle\ CF^{*}(H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}}),\

\displaystyle\ CF^{*}(H_{2}^{\natural{\sf p}}),

one can extend, by Theorem
N
, the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant FOP perturbation over
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
, which induces a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant chain map

\Phi_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:CF^{*}(H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}})\to CF^{*}(H_{2}^{\natural{\sf p}}).

Then, upon taking
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed part, one has the continuation maps

\Phi_{01}:CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H_{2}^{\natural{\sf p}}).

One can proceed in the same way as proving Theorem
A
and Theorem
B
. Therefore, one obtains a well-defined chain homotopy class of cochain complexes of
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-modules, hence a well-defined symplectic invariant

HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(X)

which is a module over
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
.

25.2.
AMS construction for coupling of Floer and Morse flow categories

In this subsection we carry out the AMS construction for the coupled flow category.

25.2.1.
The domain flow category

We modify the previous construction of AMS lifts for non-equivariant Floer theory. We do not consider the monotone flow category of domains as we have to couple it with the Morse flow category. Instead, one describes a flow category
\mathbb{Dom}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
with objects the same as those of
{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
.

First we choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
for the Floer flow category associated to
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
. Notice that from the construction in the proof of Proposition
15.4
, the integral action can be chosen to be
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant. Then for each pair of objects
p=(p^{\mathbb{F}},p^{\mathbb{E}})
,
q=(q^{\mathbb{F}},q^{\mathbb{E}})
, denote

d_{pq}:={\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}(q^{{\mathbb{F}}})-{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}(p^{\mathbb{F}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

We describe an object

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}=({\mathcal{G}}_{pq},B_{pq},C_{pq})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}

as follows. Define
{\mathcal{G}}_{xy}={\mathcal{G}}_{d_{pq}}
as considered before. Consider the moduli space of pairs

\phi:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},\ v:{\mathbb{R}}\to\widehat{S}^{\infty}

where
\phi
is holomorphic of degree
d
and
v
is a flow line connection
p^{\mathbb{E}}
and
q^{\mathbb{E}}
. Two pairs
(u,v)
,
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
are equivalent if they differ by a common translation in
{\mathbb{R}}
. Then we compactify this space in the usual way, requiring the positive constraint

\phi(z_{+})=[0,\ldots,0,1]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}

and requiring the image of
\phi
is not contained in any hyperplane. The space we obtain is denoted by
B_{pq}
, whose dimension is

{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}B_{pq}=2d_{pq}(d_{pq}+1)+{\rm index}(q^{{\mathbb{E}}})-{\rm index}(p^{{\mathbb{E}}})-1.

Let
C_{pq}\to B_{pq}
be the universal curve whose fiber at
\phi\in B_{pq}
is the corresponding rational curve in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
. This is a
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-equivariant family, hence one obtains an object
{\mathcal{C}}_{xy}
in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
.

Notice that the space
B_{pq}
is stratified by the following poset

A_{pq}=\left\{\Big((d_{0},\ldots,d_{l}),p^{{\mathbb{E}}}r_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\cdots r_{l}^{{\mathbb{E}}}q^{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big)\ \left|\begin{array}[]{c}\ d_{i}\geq 0,\ \displaystyle\sum_{i}d_{i}=d,\\
p^{{\mathbb{E}}}=r_{0}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\leq r_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\leq\cdots\leq r_{l}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\leq r_{l+1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}=q^{{\mathbb{E}}},\\
d_{i}=0\Longrightarrow r_{i}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\neq r_{i+1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Notice that there is a free
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action on the object
{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}
, given by the domain rotation and the action on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
: for
\gamma\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
and
\phi\in B_{xy}
represented by
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
and
v:{\mathbb{R}}\to\widehat{S}^{\infty}
, define

\gamma\cdot\phi=[u\circ\gamma^{-1},\gamma\cdot v]

where
u\circ\gamma^{-1}
is the composition with the domain rotation and
\gamma\cdot v
is induced by the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
. This action commutes with the action of
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
for the following reason: the
{\mathcal{G}}_{pq}
-action comes from the geometry of the target space
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
while
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
acts by reparametrizing the maps. On the other hand, there is also an action by
{\mathbb{Z}}
by the corresponding action on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
.

Using the construction of Section
14
, one can define the following morphisms

\zeta_{prq}:{\mathcal{C}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{rq}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}

in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. From the explicit construction of the domain curves, this morphism is
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant. Hence one obtains a flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
, denoted by
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
.

Proposition 25.4
.

Any outercollaring of
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}
admits a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof.

The rigidification can be constructed in the same way as in Section
14
. The freeness of the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-action allows us to achieve the equivariance easily.
∎

25.2.2.
Thickenings and group reductions

We follow the construction of Section
15
. We can write down the flow category

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}^{\mathbb{fr}}

which gives each pair of objects
p,q
an object

{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}

of framed maps. This flow category is canonically induced from previous steps and still carries the same symmetry.

Lemma 25.5
.

There exist
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant group reduction schemes.

Proof.

This can be constructed in the same way as in Proposition
15.9
. The freeness of the action allows us to maintain the symmetry.
∎

Hence one obtains a compatible system of maps

\lambda_{pq}:{\rm Map}_{pq}^{\rm fr}\to Q_{pq}.

Next we choose thickening data.

Lemma 25.6
.

There exists a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant and transverse thickening datum on
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}
.

Proof.

The same argument as in Proposition
15.19
applies. The freeness of the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-action allows us to maintain the symmetry.
∎

Hence, we obtain a compatible system of maps

\nu_{pq}:W_{pq}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{pq}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{pq}/B_{pq}}^{0,1}\otimes TX).

Then similar to Definition
15.14
, one can write down the thickened moduli spaces

K_{pq}=(G_{xy},V_{xy},E_{xy},S_{xy})\in{\rm Ob}{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}

and obtain a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

25.2.3.
Stable smoothing and NC structure

Next, the stable smoothing procedure works in a similar way. Indeed, the projection map
V_{xy}\to{}_{\lfloor}B_{xy}
has a rel-
C^{\infty,1}
-structure, allowing us to inductively perform stable smoothing. We omit the details. After taking quotient, one obtains a lift to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}
. The construction of stable complex structure is also the same. Hence one obtains

\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}

enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
. This finishes the proof of (1) of Theorem
25.3
.

25.2.4.
Equivariant continuation maps

To prove (2) of Theorem
25.3
, i.e., the lift of the equivariant continuation bimodule, we also modify the previous non-equivariant case. Let
{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
and
\mathbb{F}_{1}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be the two equivariant flow categories associated to the (iterations of) Hamiltonian
H_{0},H_{1}
. Let
\mathbb{B}_{01}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be the equivariant continuation bimodule associated to the
{\sf p}
-th iteration of a flow data on the cylinder interpolating between
H_{0}^{\natural{\sf p}}
and
H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}}
.

We first define a collection of objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0};x_{1}}=({\mathcal{G}}_{x_{0};x_{1}},B_{x_{0};x_{1}},C_{x_{0};x_{1}})\in{\rm Ob}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}.

Using the same integral modification of the symplectic action
\Omega
, for
x_{0}=(x_{0}^{\mathbb{F}},x_{0}^{{\mathbb{E}}})
and
x_{1}=(x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}},x_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}})
, consider solutions
(u,v)
where
u:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
,
v:{\mathbb{R}}\to S^{\infty}
,
u
is holomorphic of degree
d=d_{x_{0}}-d_{x_{1}}
, and
v
is a flow line connecting
x_{0}^{{\mathbb{E}}}
to
x_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}
. We impose the negative constraint

u(z_{-})=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}.

We compactify this space by allowing sphere bubbles and breakings at both positive and negative infinity. Moreover, breakings on either side are treated in the “synchronized” fashion. In this compactification, we also remove those solutions whose images in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
lie in some hyperplane. Then one obtains the moduli space
B_{x_{0};x_{1}}
over which there is the universal curve
C_{x_{0};x_{1}}\to B_{x_{0};x_{1}}
. Notice that the space
B_{x_{0};x_{1}}
is stratified by the poset

A_{x_{0};x_{1}}:=\left\{\Big((d_{-r}\cdots d_{-1};d_{0};d_{1}\cdots d_{s}),w_{-r}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\cdots w_{0}^{{\mathbb{E}}};w_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\cdots w_{s+1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\Big)\left|\begin{array}[]{c}d_{i}\geq 0,\ \displaystyle\sum_{i=-r}^{s}d_{i}=d_{x_{0}}-d_{x_{1}}\\
x_{0}^{{\mathbb{E}}}=w_{-r}^{{\mathbb{E}}},\ x_{1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}=w_{s+1}^{{\mathbb{E}}},\\
d_{i}=0,\ i\neq 0,\Longrightarrow w_{i}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\neq w_{i+1}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Let
A_{x_{0};x_{1}}^{\Omega}\subseteq A_{x_{0};x_{1}}
be the open subset of tuples such that every
d_{i}
is equal to the
\Omega
-area of some sphere class. Redefine
{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0};x_{1}}
to be the restriction to this open subset.

We can also do the outercollaring and rigidification construction in the equivariant way. We have the following analogue of Proposition
25.4
.

Proposition 25.7
.

The collection of objects
{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0};x_{1}}
forms a bimodule
\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}
over
(\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}};\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}_{1}})
, whose outercollaring admits an enrichment in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
which is
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant.

Following Section
15
, we can then construct a bimodule

{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}_{01}}^{\mathbb{fr}}

over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}^{\mathbb{fr}},{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{F}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}_{1}}^{\mathbb{fr}})
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Map}}^{\rm fr}
. Using the freeness of the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-action, we learn that:

Lemma 25.8
.

There exist
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant group reduction schemes lifting
\mathbb{Map}_{\mathbb{B}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}_{01}}^{\mathbb{fr}}
to a flow bimodule enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{{\bf map}}}{}^{\rm fr}
.

Furthermore, following Proposition
15.19
, the freeness of the
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-action guarantees the following:

Lemma 25.9
.

There exists a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant and transverse thickening datum on
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{B}}_{01}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}
, i.e., a transverse lift to
\underline{\smash{\bf Thick}}_{\rm rig}
.

Therefore, the above statements ensure that
\mathbb{B}_{01}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
admits a lift over
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Kur}}{}_{\rm rig}
. The constructions of normal complex structures and coherent smoothings follow from the recurring discussions of this paper. Therefore, by taking the quotient, we get

\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{01}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}

enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
which is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
25.3
(2).

25.3.
Equivariant PSS and SSP maps

One uses spiked disks used to define the ordinary PSS and SSP maps. Here we would like to define equivariant analogues, which are maps

\displaystyle\ \widehat{\Phi}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{MF}}:H^{*}(X;\Lambda^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}\langle\uptheta\rangle)\to HF_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}^{*}(X),\

\displaystyle\ \Phi_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}:HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{*}(X)\to H^{*}(X;\Lambda^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}\langle\uptheta\rangle).

To define these maps, we need to describe certain bimodules, denoted by
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{FM},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
respectively. Consider the Morse-to-Floer map first. Choose a Morse flow category
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{M}}}
on the symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
associated to a Morse–Smale pair
(f^{\mathbb{M}},g^{\mathbb{M}})
and let
{\mathbb{M}}
be the trivial product of
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{M}}}
with
\Pi
. Consider the coupled flow category

{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}:=\mathbb{M}\#{{\mathbb{E}}}.

The equivariant Morse-to-Floer bimodule is a bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
over
(\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}};\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
.

We consider spiked disks equipped with a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant Floer data. Regard
\Sigma^{\mathbb{MF}}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
as a surface with cylindrical end which has a natural
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action which rotate the cylindrical coordinates by multiples of
\frac{2\pi}{{\sf p}}
. One can choose the Floer datum
\sigma^{\mathbb{MF}}
on
\Sigma^{\mathbb{MF}}
which is
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant, such that near
\infty
it is equal to
H^{\natural{\sf p}}dt
.

Now we describe the moduli spaces. Choose a pair of objects
x=(x^{\mathbb{M}},x^{{{\mathbb{E}}}})\in{\rm Ob}\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
and
y=(y^{\mathbb{F}},y^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. Consider the set of solutions
(\gamma,u,v)
where
\gamma:(-\infty,0]\to X
,
u:\Sigma^{\mathbb{MF}}\to X
, and
v:{\mathbb{R}}\to S^{\infty}
are smooth maps solving

\gamma^{\prime}(s)+\nabla f^{\mathbb{M}}(\gamma(s))=0,\ \overline{\partial}_{\sigma^{\mathbb{MF}}}u=0,\ v^{\prime}(s)+\nabla f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(v(s))=0,\ \gamma(0)=u(0)\in X

and subject to the following constraint

\lim_{s\to-\infty}\gamma(s)=x^{\mathbb{M}},\ \lim_{s\to-\infty}v(s)=x^{{\mathbb{E}}},\ \lim_{s\to+\infty}v(s)=y^{{\mathbb{E}}},\lim_{s\to+\infty}u(s,t)=y^{\mathbb{F}}.

We compactify the space of such solutions
(\gamma,u,v)
by allowing synchronized breakings of Morse trajectories in
X\times S^{\infty}
at the negative end and synchronized breakings of Floer trajectories in
X
coupled with Morse trajectories in
S^{\infty}
at the positive end. The compactified space is denoted by

M_{x;y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}.

This space is stratified by the poset

A_{x;y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:=\Big\{(x_{-r}\cdots x_{-1};y_{1}\cdots y_{s})\ |\ x_{-r}<\cdots<x_{-1},\ y_{1}<\cdots<y_{s}\Big\}.

There are also natural structural maps

\displaystyle\ \iota_{x_{1}x_{0};y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:M_{x_{1}x_{0}}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\times M_{x_{0};y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to M_{x_{1};y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}},\ \

\displaystyle\ \iota_{x;y_{0}y_{1}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:M_{x;y_{0}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\times M_{y_{0}y_{1}}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to M_{x;y_{1}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}

satisfying obvious associativity conditions. Hence one obtains a bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
over
(\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}};\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
.

Fix an outercollaring width and let the outercollaring of the involved flow categories be

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},\

\displaystyle\ {}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}.

The source flow category is already enriched in both
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. The target flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
admits an AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
coming from a lift
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
(see Theorem
25.3
). Here we will establish the lift of the bimodule
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

Proposition 25.10
.

Given an equivariant AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
of
\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, there exists a class of lifts of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
, called an equivariant AMS lift, which is a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant Novikov flow category over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}\#{\mathbb{E}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
. Moreover, two different equivariant AMS lifts are homotopic via a
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant Novikov homotopy.

The proof of this proposition is given momentarily.

Now using FOP perturbations one can define the equivariant PSS map on the chain level. Choose an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
of
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, one has the associated cochain complex

C^{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})

whose
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part is the equivariant Floer cochain complex

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{*}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}).

As
{\mathbb{M}}\#{\mathbb{E}}
can be viewed as its own FOP perturbation, by the multimodule case of Theorem
N
, one can extend the FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to an FOP perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
. Then one obtains a cochain map

\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:C^{*}(\mathbb{M}\#{\mathbb{E}})\to C^{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})

which is
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}
-linear and
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant.

Lemma 25.11
.

The
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant chain homotopy class of
\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
is independent of choices. Moreover, if
{\mathbb{M}}_{1},{\mathbb{M}}_{2}
are two Morse flow category associated to two Morse–Smale pair
(f_{1},g_{1})
and
(f_{2},g_{2})
on
X
and
{\mathbb{F}}_{1},{\mathbb{F}}_{2}
are Floer flow categories associated to
H_{1}^{\natural{\sf p}}
and
H_{2}^{\natural{\sf p}}
, then the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{C^{*}({\mathbb{M}}_{1}\#{\mathbb{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{*}({\mathbb{M}}_{2}\#{\mathbb{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{*}(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{2}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})}

up to
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant chain homotopy. Here the horizontal arrows are the chain-level PSS maps and the vertical arrows are induced from Morse or Floer continuation maps.

Therefore, after restricting to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part, one obtains a canonical
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}
-linear map

\Phi^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:H^{*}(X;\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}\langle\uptheta\rangle)\to HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{*}(X).

In a similar way, one can construct a chain-level Floer-to-Morse (SSP) map, which induces a well-defined
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-linear map

\Phi^{\mathbb{FM},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{*}(X)\to H^{*}(X;\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\langle\uptheta\rangle).

Just as in the nonequivariant case, we note that the maps
\Phi^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{FM},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
are isomorphisms.

Theorem 25.12
.

The maps
\Phi^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
and
\Phi^{\mathbb{FM},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
are isomorphisms on cohomology.

Proof.

On the chain level, we can turn on a exhaustive, complete, and decreasing filtration on both
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{*}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
and
C^{*}(\mathbb{M}\#{\mathbb{E}})^{\mathbb{Z}_{\sf p}}
by tracking the degree in
\mathtt{U}
and
\uptheta
. Then both the equivariant diffferentials and equivariant PSS/SSP maps respect the filtration. On the other hand, geometrically, the constructions in Section
25.2
can be performed such that the induced differentials and chain maps on the associated graded agrees with the ones coming from the ordinary Morse cohomology, the ordinary Hamiltonian Floer cohomology, and the ordinary PSS/SSP maps. Therefore, based on Theorem
F
, we conclude the theorem.
∎

25.4.
AMS construction for equivariant PSS and SSP maps

We provide detailed construction for the PSS map. The case for SSP map is very similar.

Similar to the non-equivariant case, we first choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}^{\Omega}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
which is compatible with respect to the bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
(or more precisely, compatible with the Floer data).

25.4.1.
The domain bimodule for the AMS construction

We first describe the analogue of the space of domains, i.e., a bimodule enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. Recall that the AMS construction for
\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
(see Subsection
25.2
) provides a flow category
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
whose objects are the same as those of
\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. However, the flow category
\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
does not yet have such a corresponding flow category enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. We define such a flow category as follows. For each pair
x_{0},x_{1}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, define

{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0}x_{1}}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}=(G,B_{x_{0}x_{1}},C)

where
G
is the trivial group,
B_{x_{0}x_{1}}=M_{x_{0}x_{1}}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
is the moduli space of flow lines between
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
, and
C=\emptyset
, regarded as a family of empty curves over
B_{x_{0}x_{1}}
. After outercollaring, we denote this rather trivial flow category enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
by

\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}.

As the concatenation of Morse flow lines does not involve stabilization and the Lie groups do not change, this flow category is automatically enriched in the rigidified version
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}
. However we do not consider stable complex structure.

Now we describe a bimodule over
(\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
, denoted by
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. Fix a pair of objects

\displaystyle\ x=(x^{\mathbb{M}},x^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},\

\displaystyle\ y=(y^{\mathbb{F}},y^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}.

Notice that
x
and
y
determines an integer
d_{x;y}
which is the difference of the symplectic area of
x^{\mathbb{M}}
and the integrally modified symplectic action of
y^{\mathbb{F}}
.

Consider the the space of triples
(\gamma,\phi,\eta)
where
\gamma:(-\infty,0]\to X
,
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{x;y}}
, and
\eta:{\mathbb{R}}\to\widehat{S}^{\infty}
are smooth maps, solving the following equation

\gamma^{\prime}(s)+\nabla f^{\mathbb{M}}(\gamma(s))=0,\ \overline{\partial}\phi=0,\ \eta^{\prime}(s)+\nabla f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(\eta(s))=0

satisfying the following asymptotic constraints

\lim_{s\to-\infty}\gamma(s)=x^{\mathbb{M}},\ \lim_{s\to-\infty}\eta(s)=x^{{\mathbb{E}}},\ \lim_{s\to+\infty}\eta(s)=y^{{\mathbb{E}}}

and the constraint

\phi(+\infty)=[0,\ldots,0,1]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{x;y}}.

Notice that there is no requirement for
\gamma(0)
and
u(-\infty)
. We compactify this space by allowing sychrnoized breakings in
X\times\widehat{S}^{\infty}
at the negative end and synchronized breakings of holomorphic cylinders in
\mathbb{CP}^{d_{x;y}}
and Morse trajectories in
S^{\infty}
. Next, we remove those elements whose images in
\mathbb{CP}^{d_{x;y}}
lie in any hyperplane. Denote this space by

B_{x;y}.

Then this is a smooth stratified manifold with a
{\mathcal{G}}_{d_{x;y}}
-action. On top of it there is the universal curve

C_{x;y}\to B_{x;y}.

Then the triple

{\mathcal{C}}_{x;y}:=({\mathcal{G}}_{x;y},B_{x;y},C_{x;y})\ {\rm where}\ {\mathcal{G}}_{x;y}={\mathcal{G}}_{d_{x;y}}

is an object of
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}
. We can also define structural maps

{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0}x_{1}}^{{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{1};y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{0};y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}},\ {\mathcal{C}}_{x;y_{0}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{y_{0}y_{1}}^{{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{x;y_{1}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}.

This provide a flow bimodule
\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
over
(\mathbb{Dom}^{{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
. One can check that this bimodule is
\mathbb{\Pi}_{\sf p}
-equivariant.

25.4.2.
Thickening

One can consider the space of framed maps
{\rm Map}_{x;y}^{\rm fr}(X)
whose elements are
(\phi,u,F)
where
\phi\in B_{x;y}
,
u:C_{\phi}\to X
is an admissible map, and
F
is a frame determined by
u
and the integral symplectic form
\Omega
. Recall that
\phi
carries the information of a flow ray in
X
and we do not require that the evaluation of
u
at
-\infty
coincides with the value of the flow ray at
0
. One chooses an equivariant group reduction scheme

\lambda_{x;y}:{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{x;y}^{\rm fr}(X)\to Q_{x;y}:=Q_{d_{x;y}}

which extends the group reduction scheme chosen for the AMS construction for
\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. On the other hand, we also choose thickening data

\nu_{x;y}:W_{x;y}\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathring{C}_{x;y}\times X,\Lambda_{\mathring{C}_{x,y}/B_{x;y}}^{0,1}\otimes TX)

extending the thickening data chosen for the AMS construction for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. Define the
thickening

\check{V}_{x;y}:=\left\{(\phi,u,F,e)\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}(\phi,u,F)\in{}_{\lfloor}{\rm Map}_{x;y}^{\rm fr}(X),\ e\in W_{x;y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\\
\overline{\partial}u+\nu_{x;y}(e)=0,\ \phi=\phi_{F}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

The obstruction bundle
E_{x;y}
is the trivial bundle with fiber
W_{x;y}\oplus Q_{x;y}
and the Kuranishi section is

S_{x;y}(\phi,u,F,e)=(e,\lambda_{x;y}(\phi,u,F)).

We summarize the construction so far in the following proposition.

Proposition 25.13
.

The collection of Kuranishi spaces

\check{K}_{x;y}:=(G_{x;y},\check{V}_{x;y},E_{x;y},S_{x;y})

together with the structural maps

{}_{\lfloor}M_{x_{0}x_{1}}^{\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\times K_{x_{1};y}\to K_{x_{0};y}

and

K_{x;y_{0}}\times K_{y_{0}y_{1}}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to K_{x;y_{1}}

define a bimodule
\check{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
over
({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{M}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
.

Notice that
\check{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
is not a lift of
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
as we did not impose the matching condition in
X
.

We then construct the vertical NC structure. Similar to the case for
\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
, the construction can be carried out in a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant fashion. Hence one obtains a bimodule enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

We still apply the same smoothing strategy to obtain a stable smoothing of
\check{\mathbb{M}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. To simplify notations, we still denote by the same symbol. Moreover, we may require that the natural map

{\rm ev}:\check{V}_{x;y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to X\times X

is a submersion, hence transverse to the diagonal. Define

V_{x;y}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:={\rm ev}^{-1}(\Delta_{X})

and define
K_{x;y}
be the restriction of
\check{K}_{x;y}
to this closed submanifold. Finally, incoporating the stable complex structure on the domain bimodule, one obtains an NC structure. We summarize the construction as follows.

Proposition 25.14
.

The collection of Kuranishi spaces
K_{x;y}
together with the restriction of the structural morphisms of Proposition
25.13
define a lift of
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
, denoted by
\hat{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
, which is a bimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}}\#{\mathbb{E}};\hat{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
.

Finally, by taking the quotient by the Lie group actions, we obtain
{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF},{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. One can further follow the machinery of this paper to address the invariance property. This concludes Proposition
25.10
.

26.
The Equivariant Pair-of-Pants—Proof of Theorem
J

The equivariant pair-of-pants product can be viewed as a Floer-theoretic analogue of the quantum Steenrod operation. It is defined via a multimodule associated to a Floer domain having a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-symmetry. The main results regarding such a construction is stated in Theorem
J
. Given the recurring nature of our arguments, we highlight the distinctive features.

26.1.
The equivariant pair-of-pants multimodule

Let
(X,\omega,J)
be a compact almost Kähler manifold. Again, we do not discuss the dependence on
J
, which could be done without new difficulty. Let
{\mathbb{F}}
be the Floer flow category of a Hamiltonian
H
and let
{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be the coupling of the Floer flow category of the
{\sf p}
-th iteration
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
with the Morse flow category
{{\mathbb{E}}}
on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
.
27
27

27

In general one could allow the
{\sf p}
-fold iteration of a different Hamiltonian.
We describe a multimodule
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
over
({\mathbb{F}},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}},{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
where
{\mathbb{F}}
appears
{\sf p}
times in the source.

26.1.1.
The Floer domain and the equation

Let
z_{1},\ldots,z_{{\sf p}}\in{\mathbb{C}}\subseteq\mathbb{CP}^{1}
be all the
{\sf p}
-th roots of unity. Let

\Sigma:=\mathbb{CP}^{1}\setminus\{z_{1},\ldots,z_{{\sf p}},z_{\infty}=\infty\}

be the punctured sphere, which has a holomorphic
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action permuting the finite punctures. Choose a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant Floer datum
\sigma^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
on
\Sigma
whose restriction on the
{\sf p}
negative ends are
Hdt
and whose restriction on the positive end is the
{\sf p}
-th iteration
H^{\natural{\sf p}}dt
. In fact, one can choose different
H
for negative and positive ends as in the setup of Theorem
J
, but we spell out the argument for
H_{-}=H_{+}=H
to simplify the notations. Solutions to the Floer equation

\overline{\partial}_{\sigma^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}u=0,\ u:\Sigma\to X

converge to periodic orbits of
H
at negative ends and periodic orbits of
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
at the positive end.

26.1.2.
The moduli spaces and the multimodule

We define the morphism spaces of the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. Choose a collection of objects

(x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}},\ldots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}},x^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in\underbrace{{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}\times\cdots\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}}_{{\sf p}}\times{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{E}}

and an object
y=(y^{\mathbb{F}^{\natural{\sf p}}},y^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, where
y^{\mathbb{F}^{\natural{\sf p}}}
is the flow category associated with the Hamiltonian
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
, we describe a moduli space as follows. Consider pairs

u:\Sigma\to X,\ v:{\mathbb{R}}\to\widehat{S}^{\infty}

where
u
is a solution to
\overline{\partial}_{\sigma^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}}u=0
converging to
x_{i}^{\mathbb{F}}
at the
i
-th negative cylindrical ends,
i=1,\ldots,{\sf p}
, to
y^{\mathbb{F}^{\natural{\sf p}}}
at the positive cylindrical end, and where
v
is a Morse flow line of
f^{{\mathbb{E}}}
connecting
x^{{\mathbb{E}}}
and
y^{{\mathbb{E}}}
at negative and positive ends respectively. We compactify the space of such solutions in the following way: while allowing sphere bubbling, the breakings at negative ends are treated as independent breaking while the breakings at positive ends are synchronized. Let the compactified space be
M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}
.

There are obvious inclusions given by breakings of the domains. For
i=1,\ldots,{\sf p}
and
x_{i}^{{\mathbb{F}}}\leq w_{i}^{{\mathbb{F}}}
in the
i
-th source flow category, there is a map

M_{x_{i}^{\mathbb{F}}w_{i}^{\mathbb{F}}}^{{\mathbb{F}}}\times M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{i-1}^{\mathbb{F}}w_{i}^{\mathbb{F}}x_{i+1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{{\mathbb{E}}}};y}^{\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}\to M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}};

for
x^{{\mathbb{E}}}\leq w^{{\mathbb{E}}}
in
{{\mathbb{E}}}
, a map

M_{x^{{\mathbb{E}}}w^{{\mathbb{E}}}}^{{\mathbb{E}}}\times M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}w^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}\to M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{\mathbb{X}^{\mathfrak{FSt}}};

for
w\leq y
in
\mathbb{F}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
, a map

M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};w}^{\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}\times M_{wy}^{\mathbb{F}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}.

These maps satisfy the conditions required for a multimodule. Hence one obtains a multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
over
(\mathbb{F},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}},{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
.

26.1.3.
The symmetry

Define the Novikov monoid

\mathbb{\Pi}^{({\sf p})}:=\underbrace{\Pi\times\cdots\times\Pi}_{{\sf p}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}.

There is a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action on
\mathbb{\Pi}^{({\sf p})}
by isomorphisms of Novikov monoids given by permuting the first
{\sf p}
factors in the above product. Then we can form the semi-direct product
\mathbb{\Pi}^{({\sf p})}\rtimes{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
.

We can view the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
as a weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}\times{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
. On the source weak flow category, it has the action

{\mathcal{A}}(x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}},\ldots,x^{\mathbb{F}}_{\sf p},x^{{{\mathbb{E}}}})=({\mathcal{A}}_{H}(x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}),\ldots,{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}),{\mathcal{A}}_{{{\mathbb{E}}}}(x^{{\mathbb{E}}}))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{{\sf p}+1}.

Proposition 26.1
.

The collection of moduli spaces
M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{F}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{F}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}}
define a Novikov multimodule over
({\mathbb{F}},\cdots,{\mathbb{F}},{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
which is equivariant with respect to the natural morphism

\rho:\mathbb{\Pi}^{({\sf p})}\to\mathbb{\Pi},\ (a_{1},\ldots,a_{\sf p},l)\mapsto(a_{1}+\cdots+a_{\sf p},l)

of Novikov monoids. Moreover, as a weak bimodule over
({\mathbb{F}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{F}}\times{{\mathbb{E}}};{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
, it is equivariant with respect to
\rho
.

Proof.

Straightforward.
∎

26.1.4.
Regularization and the equivariant pair-of-pants product

Fix an outercollaring width and a compatible choice of integral actions. Recall that the outercollarings of
{\mathbb{F}}
and
{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
both admit AMS lifts to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}{}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

Proposition 26.2
.

There exists a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
, denoted by
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
, to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}^{\rm NC}_{\rm rig}
, which is a
\rho
-equivariant Novikov multimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbb{F}},{}_{\lfloor}{{\mathbb{E}}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
.

Proof.

The construction is analogous to the AMS construction for a general multimodule induced from a smooth Floer domain, except that the moduli spaces curves now has the parametrization factor from the moduli spaces of Morse trajectories in
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
as in Section
25.4.1
. One can carry out the construction almost verbatim; the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariance condition can be achieved because the action is free.
∎

Now let
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}
be an FOP transverse perturbation on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
and
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant FOP transverse perturbation on
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. Let
CF(H)
resp.
C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
be the associated complex over
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
resp. over
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}}
; the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part of
C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})
is the equivariant complex
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
. By the multimodule case of Theorem
N
, one can extend these FOP transverse perturbations to a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant FOP transverse perturbation
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
on
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
. Then by Proposition
7.18
, it induces a
{\bf F}_{\sf p}
-linear chain map

\widetilde{P}:\overbrace{CF(H)\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}CF(H)}^{{\sf p}}\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}C({{\mathbb{E}}})\to C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}).

The
\rho
-equivariance condition on the multimodule
\mathring{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
implies that
\Phi^{\rm FSt}
induces a map

\widetilde{P}:\left(\overbrace{CF(H)\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}\cdots\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}CF(H)}^{{\sf p}}\widehat{\underset{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}{\otimes}}CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)\underset{\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{{\mathbb{\Pi}}^{({\sf p})}}}{\otimes}\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\to C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})

which is
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant and
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-linear.

Restricting to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part, one obtains the chain-level equivariant pair-of-pants product

P:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})

hence a map on the cohomology level (denoted by the same notation)

P:H_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}).

26.2.
Proof of Theorem
J

As above, we write down the proof for
H_{-}=H_{+}=H
to simplify the notations.

26.2.1.
Independence of choices

Lemma 26.3
.

\widetilde{P}
is well-defined up to
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant chain homotopies. Therefore, restricting to the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}
-invariant part,
P
is well-defined up to chain homotopies.

Proof.

Two concrete chain level constructions can be connected by a homotopy: we can carry out the construction of multimodule homotopies in the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}
-equivariant setting using families of curves with parametrization having the additional factor from the moduli spaces of Morse trajectories in
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
, and the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}
-equivariance can be achieved due to the freeness.
∎

Therefore, following the definition of
{\it FSt}_{{\sf p}}
in Theorem
J
, we see that the Floer-theoretic Steenrod operation does not depend on any choices after passing to cohomology. This finishes proving Theorem
J
(1) and (3).

26.2.2.
Isomorphism on cohomology

Theorem
J
(2) follows from a stronger assertion in the quantitative setting, Corollary
27.10
, which is proven in the next Section. The idea is that instead of directly constructing a chain-homotopy inverse of
P
by dualizing the equivariant pair-of-pants product, we turn on the action filtration on the (equivariant) Floer chain complexes, which the operation
P
respects, so that the induced operation on the associated graded reduces to the equivariant pants product on local Floer cohomology. Then, by appealing to the known isomorphism property in
[
61
,
62
]
in the aspherical setting, which, in particular, applies to local Floer theory, we conclude that
P
is an isomorphism on cohomology.

Remark 26.4
.

Using Proposition
23.3
, we further see that
{\it FSt}_{{\sf p}}
is an isomorphism. This is the generalization of the localization results in
[
61
,
62
]
for general compact symplectic manifolds in Hamiltonian Floer theory. We expect that the method in this paper can also be used for proving an analogous result for the fixed point Floer cohomology associated with a general symplectic automorphism, though one needs a slight different regularization procedure.

26.2.3.
The multimodule homotopy

Finally, we prove the commutativity of Diagram (
1.7
) in Theorem
J
(4). The core of the proof is the following chain-level assertion. As we only need the cohomological level conclusion, one fixes a Hamiltonian
H=H_{-}=H_{+}
such that
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
is nondegenerate. Moreover, fix a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
on
X
with associated Morse flow category
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{M}}}
(whose cohomology is isomorphic to
H(X;\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Pi})
).

Lemma 26.5
.

The following diagram commutes up to
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant homotopy.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overbrace{C({\mathbb{M}})\otimes\cdots\otimes C({\mathbb{M}})}^{{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\mathbb{M})\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underbrace{CF(H)\otimes\cdots\otimes CF(H)}_{{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathbb{QSt}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Phi^{\mathbb{MF}})^{\otimes{\sf p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}}

Assuming this lemma, the restriction of the commutative diagram to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part reads

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern The objects in this diagram are all modules over
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
. As the quasi-Frobenius map respects equivariant chain homotopies, after passing to cohomology, one obtains the commutative diagram

80.30344pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.30344pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.30344pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 170.05812pt\raise-38.07892pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 210.72702pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 178.58817pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 210.72702pt\raise-32.68726pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
