234.83813pt\raise-16.34363pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.8472pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 234.83813pt\raise-11.75pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

(26.1)

The outer layer of this diagram is just (
1.7
). This would finish the proof of Theorem
J
.

Now we prove Lemma
26.5
. The proof is based on a multimodule homotopy between the following two multimodules. The first one is the Steenrod multimodule
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
over
(\mathbb{M},\ldots,\mathbb{M},{{\mathbb{E}}};\mathbb{M}\#{{\mathbb{E}}})
, which will lead to the quantum Steenrod operation
{\it QSt}_{{\sf p}}
. The second one is the concatenation of three multimodules: first, there is the non-equivariant Morse-to-Floer bimodule
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
. Consider the product of
{\sf p}
copies of this bimodule with a copy of the diagonal bimodule
\mathbb{\Delta}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}}

(\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}})^{\sf p}\times\mathbb{\Delta}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}}:=\underbrace{\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}}_{{\sf p}}\times\mathbb{\Delta}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}{{\mathbb{E}}}},

which is a multimodule over
(\mathbb{M},\ldots,{\mathbb{M}},{{\mathbb{E}}};\mathbb{F},\ldots,\mathbb{F},{{\mathbb{E}}})
. It can be composed with the multimodule
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
, followed by the equivariant SSP bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
.

Before we move on, we specify the symmetries of the flow categories and multimodules. The flow categories
{\mathbb{M}}
and
{\mathbb{F}}
carry the action by the group
\Pi
and
{{\mathbb{E}}}
carries the action by
{\mathbb{Z}}
. The flow category
\mathbb{M}\#{{\mathbb{E}}}
carries the action by (the trivially twisted)
\Pi\times\mathbb{Z}\times{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. The multimodule
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
is equivariant with respect to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant morphism of Novikov monoids

\rho^{\mathbb{St}}:\Pi\times\cdots\times\Pi\times{\mathbb{Z}}\to\Pi\times{\mathbb{Z}}.

Moreover, as a weak bimodule (see Definition
6.17
),
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
is
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant. It is easy to see that the same is true for the triple concatenation.

Now fix a common outercollaring width. Also choose an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
on
{\mathbb{F}}
and an integral action
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}}^{\Omega}
which are compatible with respect to involved Floer data.

Suppose
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
is an AMS lift of
{\mathbb{F}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the action
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{F}}^{\Omega}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be an equivariant AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
subject to the outercollaring width and the action
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}}^{\Omega}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}}
is an AMS lift of the outercollaring of
\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}}
as a bimodule over
({}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{M}},\tilde{\mathbb{F}})
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
be an equivariant AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
extending
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
be an equivariant AMS lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{B}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
.

Lemma 26.6
.

There exists a
\rho^{\mathbb{St}}
-equivariant homotopy
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
from
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to

\Big((\tilde{\mathbb{B}}^{\mathbb{MF}})^{\sf p}\times{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{\Delta}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}{{\mathbb{E}}}}\Big)\circ\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}\circ\tilde{\mathbb{B}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}

enriched in the category
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
.

Assuming this lemma, we can conclude the assertion of Lemma
26.5
. Since all actions are free, one can choose equivariant FOP transverse perturbations, firstly on the flow categories
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, then on the multimodules, and then on the homotopy
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
, thanks to Theorem
N
. Then the corresponding homotopy
\mathring{\mathbb{H}}
enriched in
\underline{\smash{{\bf\Psi}{\bf Man}}}
induces a chain homotopy
\Psi^{\mathring{\mathbb{H}}}
between the two chain maps, the chain map corresponding to the quantum Steenrod operation and the composition of the three chain maps. This means the diagram (
26.1
) commutes up to homotopy.

26.2.4.
Proof of Lemma
26.6

We first describe the homotopy in the category
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
before the outercollaring, from
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to the concatenation

\mathbb{Y}:=\left((\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{MF}})^{\sf p}\times\mathbb{\Delta}^{{{\mathbb{E}}}{{\mathbb{E}}}}\right)\circ\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}\circ\mathbb{B}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}.

Consider the sphere
\Sigma_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{St}}\cong\mathbb{CP}^{1}
equipped with a 1-parameter family of Floer data
\sigma_{t}
parametrized by
t\in[0,1]
where, when
t=0
,
\sigma_{t}\equiv 0
while as
t\to 1
, the long necks around each negative markings have the Hamiltonian connection
Hdt
which is
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant while the long neck around the positive marking has the Hamiltonian
H^{\natural{\sf p}}dt
. Then for any
t
,
\sigma_{t}
is invariant under the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action on the domain.

Figure 10.
The concatenation of three multimodules. Notice that the gluing on the left have
{\sf p}+1
gluing parameters and the gluing on the right has only one gluing parameter.

Now we can describe the moduli spaces responsible for the homotopy. Choose

x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}},\ldots,x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{M}},\ x^{{\mathbb{E}}}\in{\rm Ob}{{\mathbb{E}}},\ y=(y^{\mathbb{M}},y^{{\mathbb{E}}})\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{M}}^{{\mathbb{E}}}.

Consider the moduli space of triples
(t,u,v)
where
t\in[0,1)
,
u:\Sigma_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{St}}\to X
and
v:{\mathbb{R}}\to\widehat{S}^{\infty}
are smooth maps solving

(\nabla^{\sigma_{t}})^{0,1}u=0,\ v^{\prime}(s)+\nabla f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(v(s))=0

and

u(z_{i})\in W^{\rm us}_{x_{i}^{\mathbb{M}}},\ i=1,\ldots,{\sf p},\ u(z_{\infty})\in W^{\rm st}_{y^{\mathbb{M}}},\lim_{s\to-\infty}v(s)=x^{{{\mathbb{E}}}},\ \lim_{s\to+\infty}v(s)=y^{{\mathbb{E}}}.

Let the space of solutions be
\mathring{M}_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
. We may regard the constraints of
u
at
z_{i}
as adding gradient rays of
f
in
X
. Then let

M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{H}}}

be its natural compactification which allows sphere bubbling, Morse trajectory breaking, and breakings along Floer trajectories as
t\to 1
. Moreover, when Morse trajectories break in the positive end, the trajectories broken off are regarded as flow lines in the product
X\times\widehat{S}^{\infty}
. The following lemma can be verified easily.

Lemma 26.7
.

The collection of spaces
M_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
forms a homotopy from
{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to the concatenation
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Moreover, this is a
\rho^{\mathbb{St}}
-equivariant Novikov homotopy and
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant as a weak homotopy.

26.2.5.
The AMS construction

Now we describe the AMS lift of the outercollaring of the homotopy
{\mathbb{H}}
, given AMS lifts of involved Floer flow categories and multimodules.

Recall the AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}
of the Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}
contains a domain monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}{\mathbb{D}om}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
. The AMS lift
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
also contains a domain monotone flow category
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
enriched in
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
which also has a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action corresponding to the domain rotation.

The lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
contains the domain monotone multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
. The lift
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
has a similar monotone multimodule
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
. There are also the monotone bimodules
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{MF}}
and
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\mathbb{FM}}
. Then similar to previous cases of obtaining monotone homotopies involving concatenations and gluing, one can define a monotone homotopy from
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{QSt}}
to the concatenation

({}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{MF}})^{{\sf p}}\circ{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{FSt}}\circ{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{FM}}.

This monotone homotopy, denoted by
{}_{\lfloor}\mathbb{Dom}^{\mathbb{H}}
, is enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}
and is
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant.

The remaining construction will be a tedious repetition of existing constructions. We only give a sketch. For a tuple of objects
x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}},\ldots,x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}},x^{\mathbb{E}};y
, by using the chosen integral actions, one can pull back the monotone homotopy to obtain objects

{\mathcal{C}}_{x_{1}^{\mathbb{M}}\cdots x_{\sf p}^{\mathbb{M}}x^{{\mathbb{E}}};y}^{\mathbb{H}}\in{\rm Ob}{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf Curve}}_{\rm rig}^{\mathbb{C}}

which describe configurations containing not only surfaces but also flow lines/rays. The choices we need for the AMS construction can all be extended from existing ones, including the group reduction and thickening data. All the extensions can be made in a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant way.

After these choices, one obtains a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf S^{\rm rel}Kur}}_{\rm rig}
. There is no essential difference from previous cases of constructing vertical NC structures in a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant way. The stable smoothings can also be made equivariantly, first without requiring the matching condition between flow rays and maps defined on surfaces. The submersive condition implies that the incidence condition cuts down smooth Kuranishi spaces. By incorporating the domain stable complex structures, one obtains the lift of
{\mathbb{H}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf SKur}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. By passing to the orbifold quotient, we obtain the final construction.

27.
Quantitative Results about Equivariant Floer Theory

In this section we discuss the quantitative features of the equivariant Floer theory and prove Theorem
M
.

27.1.
Filtration on the
{\sf p}
-fold tensor product

Let
H
be a nondegenerate Hamiltonian on
X
. Let
CF(H)
be a Floer complex associated to
H
, obtained by a particular AMS construction and FOP perturbation. The source of the equivariant pair-of-pants product is the complex

C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})

which is the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part of

CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}}).

By our convention, this tensor product is a module over the Novikov field

\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi\oplus\cdots\oplus\Pi\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}}

(where one has
{\sf p}
copies of
\Pi
in the exponent). Here within this section, without introducing further notations, we always change coefficients to the Novikov field
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
using the summation homomorphism
\Pi\oplus\cdots\oplus\Pi\to\Pi
. Then there is a filtration on

CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})

corresponding to the sum of the symplectic actions of the
{\sf p}
tensor factors such that the differential preserves this filtration. The filtration restricts to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part. Moreover, after changing coefficients to
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
, one obtains a Floer-type complex over this Novikov field

\left(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}

as well as its
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part.

In principle one should hope to establish a chain-level inverse of the equivariant pair-of-pants product via the coproduct construction (cf.
[
62
]
). However, the corresponding AMS construction is quite involved. Since our main purpose of the quantitative aspects is to compare barcodes, instead of this potential direct approach, we use more algebraic treatment to bypass the coproduct construction (although we will make use of the local coproduct construction in
[
62
]
, see the proof of Lemma
27.11
).

27.1.1.
Homological perturbation

In view of localization, the cohomology of
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
should only come from generators of the form
x\otimes\cdots\otimes x
for
x\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}
, i.e.,
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed points of
{\sf p}
-tuples of
H
-orbits. We use the homological perturbation argument to remove the effects of the other free
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbits. We would like to construct a complex

C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})

which is filtered chain homotopy equivalent to
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
and which is only generated by the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed points.

Let
{\bf x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sf p})
denote a
{\sf p}
-tuple of capped
H
-orbits. We write

CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})=\bigoplus_{{\bf x}\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)^{{\sf p}}}{\bf F}_{\sf p}\langle{\bf x}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}}).

Here the infinite direct sum indicates we take a completion. This is a decomposition of
{\bf K}_{\sf p}
-modules. Let
[{\bf x}]
denote the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbit of
{\bf x}
. Then there is a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant summand

\bigoplus_{{\bf x}\in[{\bf x}]}{\bf F}_{\sf p}\langle{\bf x}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}}).

Its
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed part is denoted by

C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},[{\bf x}])\subseteq C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}).

Lemma 27.1
.

The differential
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
on
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
admits a decomposition

d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}=\delta_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}+D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}

which satisfies the following conditions.

(1)

\delta_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
preserves the filtration and for each tuple
{\bf x}
,

\delta_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\Big(C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},[{\bf x}])\Big)\subseteq C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},[{\bf x}]).

(2)

The restriction of
\delta_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
to each
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},[{\bf x}])
, denoted by
\delta_{[{\bf x}]}
, is a differential.

(3)

If
{\bf x}=(x,\ldots,x)
is a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed point, then
\delta_{[{\bf x}]}
is trivial. If
{\bf x}
is not a fixed point, then the cohomology of
\delta_{[{\bf x}]}
is trivial.

(4)

There exists a constant
\epsilon_{H}>0
such that
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
increases the filtration by at least
\epsilon_{H}
.

Proof.

The differential on the tensor product
CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})
, denoted by
\tilde{d}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
, is the tensor product differential. Let
d_{H}:CF(H)\to CF(H)
be the Floer differential and
d_{{{\mathbb{E}}}}
be the Morse differential on
CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})
. Then the obvious
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant decomposition

\begin{split}\tilde{d}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}=&\ \tilde{\delta}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}+\tilde{D}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\\
:=&\ \ast{\rm Id}_{CF(H)}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\rm Id}_{CF(H)}\otimes d_{{{\mathbb{E}}}}+d_{H}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes{\rm Id}_{CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})}.\end{split}

Here
\ast=\pm 1
depends on the degrees of the variables. The restriction to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part provides the desired decomposition.
∎

Now we would like to apply the homological perturbation argument to construct a differential on the chain group

C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}):=\bigoplus_{x\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)}C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},x\otimes\cdots\otimes x),

(27.1)

which is the cohomology of
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
under the differential
\delta_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
defined above. Notice that there is a natural linear inclusion

\sigma:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})

and a projection

\pi:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}).

Lemma 27.2
.

There exists a differential
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}
on
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
, together with maps

\displaystyle\ \sigma^{\prime}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}),\

\displaystyle\ \pi^{\prime}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}
strictly increases the energy filtration.

(2)

\sigma^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime}
strictly increase the energy filtration.

(3)

\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma+\sigma^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\pi}=\pi+\pi^{\prime}
are filtered chain homotopy inverses to each other.

Proof.

For each
[{\bf x}]
which is not a fixed point, since the cohomology of
\delta_{[{\bf x}]}
is trivial, one can find a chain homotopy between identity and zero, denoted by
\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}
satisfying

{\rm Id}=\delta_{[{\bf x}]}\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}+\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}\delta_{[{\bf x}]}.

When
[{\bf x}]
is a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fxed point, define
\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}=0
. One can choose
\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}
in a
\Pi
-equivariant way. Then define

\Theta:=\bigoplus_{[{\bf x}]\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)^{{\sf p}}/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\Theta_{[{\bf x}]}.

Then
\Theta
preserves the filtration. Then following the explicit formula of
[
37
]
and
[
45
]
), define

\tilde{D}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}:=\pi\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\circ\Theta)^{l}\right)D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\sigma=\pi D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\otimes{\sf p}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}(\Theta D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})^{l}\right)\sigma.

Its restriction to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part is defined to be
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}
. The terms
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime}
are also defined by explicit formulas (see, e.g.
[
12
, Appendix B]
), and we omit the details.
∎

27.2.
Filtration on equivariant Floer cochain complex

Let
H
be a Hamiltonian on
X
so that an equivariant Floer cochain complex
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
can be defined (see Theorem
I
). Recall that to define this chain group, we first consider the chain group

C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}}):=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}c_{i}p_{i}\otimes w_{i}\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{l}c_{i}\in{\bf F}_{\sf p},\ p_{i}\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}),\ w_{i}\in{\rm crit}f^{{\mathbb{E}}},\\
\displaystyle\inf_{i}{\mathcal{A}}(p_{i})>-\infty,\ \inf_{i}f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(w_{i})>-\infty,\\
\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}{\mathcal{A}}(p_{i})+f^{{\mathbb{E}}}(w_{i})=+\infty\end{array}\right.\right\}

It is a module with a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action, and it is defined over the Novikov field
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
. The equivariant complex
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
is (roughly) its
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-fixed part.

Lemma 27.3
.

The
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part
C(F^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}})_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
is a free module over
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
. Its rank is
\#{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
when
{\sf p}=2
and
2\#{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
when
{\sf p}>2
.

Proof.

Choose for each contractible 1-periodic orbit a capping. Let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}
be these capped 1-periodic orbit. Let
\tau\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be the generator. When
{\sf p}=2
, define a map

F:\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{2}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\langle p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}\rangle\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}})_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}

by

F(p_{\alpha})=p_{\alpha}\otimes w_{0}^{+}+\tau(p_{\alpha})\otimes w_{0}^{-}

where
w_{0}^{\pm}
are the two critical points of
f^{{\mathbb{E}}}
of index
0
. One can see that this is an isomorphism of free
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-modules. On the other hand, when
{\sf p}>2
, define

F:\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}\langle p_{1},\ldots,p_{m},p_{1}\otimes\uptheta,\ldots,p_{m}\otimes\uptheta\rangle\to C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}})_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}

by sending

\displaystyle\ F(p_{\alpha})=\sum_{j=1}^{\sf p}\tau^{j}(p_{\alpha})\otimes w_{0}^{j},\

\displaystyle\ F(p_{\alpha}\otimes\uptheta)=\sum_{j=1}^{\sf p}\tau^{j}(p_{\alpha})\otimes w_{1}^{j},

where
w_{0}^{j}
,
w_{1}^{j}
are the critical points of
f^{{\mathbb{E}}}
of index
0
and
1
. One can also verify that
F
is a linear isomorphism.
∎

Note the difference between the Novikov fields
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
and
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
, where the latter does not require the pole order of the variable
\mathtt{U}
or
\uptheta
is bounded. Then we can write the chain group after tensoring with this larger Novikov field by

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi})=C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{{\mathbb{E}}})_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\\
\cong\Big\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\tilde{c}_{i}p_{i}\ |\ \tilde{c}_{i}\in\widetilde{\bf K}_{\sf p},\ p_{i}\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}),\ \lim_{i\to\infty}{\mathcal{A}}_{H^{\natural{\sf p}}}(p_{i})=+\infty\Big\}.

To directly use results from
[
69
]
, change coefficients to
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}
, which becomes only
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded. Recall from (
7.6
) the meaning of the notation
|\Pi|
.

Lemma 27.4
.

The complex

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}):=CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi})\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}

is a Floer-type complex (Definition
7.34
) over the Novikov field
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}
.

Proof.

To see this, we first need to prove that
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|})
is orthogonalizable. Indeed, it is the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant subspace of the cochain complex
C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}})\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}
, which is orthogonalizable. Then by
[
69
, Corollary 2.17]
, the subspace
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|})
is also orthogonalizable. On the other hand, the differential preserves the energy filtration, hence
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|})
is a Floer-type complex.
∎

As a corollary, by the basic construction of
[
69
]
, the complex
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|})
has well-defined verbose and concise barcodes.

Proposition 27.5
.

The verbose barcode only depends on
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.40
, one only needs to show that the filtered isomorphism type only depends on
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
. Indeed, given two constructions of
C({\mathbb{F}}^{\natural{\sf p}}\#{\mathbb{E}})
as a chain complex of
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\mathbb{\Pi}}
-modules with
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action corresponding to two AMS lifts

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}},

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}},

by Theorem
25.3
, one can construct a lift of the outercollaring of the diagonal bimodule to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
as a bimodule over
(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}};\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
. Then by choosing FOP perturbations, one obtains a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-equivariant chain map

\Phi:C(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{0}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}})\to C(\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{1}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}).

Similar to many other cases, this chain map has a leading order term being the identity map on the same chain group. Hence
\Phi
is invertible. Moreover,
\Phi
preserves the energy filtration because all the lifts cover the constant homotopy of
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
. Therefore, after changing coefficients to
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{|\Pi|}
, the chain map
\Phi
is a filtered isomorphism.
∎

27.3.
Local equivariant cohomology and homological perturbation

We would like to compare the barcodes between the ordinary Floer complex of a Hamiltonian and the equivariant Floer complexes of its
{\sf p}
-th iteration using the chain-level equivariant pair-of-pants product. In the presence of simple
{\sf p}
-periodic orbits, we do not expect to obtain a filtered isomorphism. On the other hand, simple
{\sf p}
-periodic orbits, which form free
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbits, are not expected to contribute in the Tate equivariant cohomology. We use local equivariant cohomology and the homological perturbation method to remove simple
{\sf p}
-periodic orbits from a reduced version of the equivariant Floer complex.

27.3.1.
Local equivariant Floer theory

Now consider the
{\sf p}
-th iteration
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
. Recall that the chain group
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
is the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part of the module

\bigoplus_{\overline{y}\in{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})}\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\langle\overline{y}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}}).

Notice that
\overline{y}
is either an iterated orbit (fixed point) or a simple
{\sf p}
-periodic orbit (free
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbit). Then we can write the above chain group as

\left(\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(H)}\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\langle\underline{\smash{x}}^{({\sf p})}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)\widehat{\oplus}\left(\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{y}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\rm simple}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})}\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\langle\underline{\smash{y}}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right).

Notice that
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
acts trivially on the set of iterated orbits and acts freely on the set of simple orbits. Let
[\underline{\smash{y}}]
be the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbit of
\underline{\smash{y}}
. Then one reorganizes the decomposition as

\begin{split}CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\cong&\ \bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(H)}\Lambda_{\widetilde{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\langle\underline{\smash{x}}^{({\sf p})}\rangle\widehat{\oplus}\left(\bigoplus_{\underline{\smash{y}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\rm simple}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})}\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\langle\underline{\smash{y}}\rangle\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\\
\cong&\ \bigoplus_{[\underline{\smash{y}}]\in{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[\underline{\smash{y}}]),\end{split}

where each
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[\underline{\smash{y}}])
is a submodule coming from each
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-orbit. Notice that for each
[y]\in{\mathcal{O}}(H)
, there is a
{\bf K}_{\sf p}
-summand

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural},[y])\subseteq CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[\underline{\smash{y}}]).

The following lemma basically says that one can decompose the equivariant differential into a local part and a positive-energy part, where the local part respects the above decomposition.

Lemma 27.6
.

The equivariant differential

d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}:CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}};\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi})

can be written as

d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}=d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}+D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}

which satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For each
[y]\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})/{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
,

d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}\big(CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[y])\big)\subseteq CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[y]).

Therefore
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}
preserves the filtration. Denote the restriction of
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}
to the summand
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[y])
by
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},[y]}
.

(2)

For each
[y]
,
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},[y]}^{2}=0
.

(3)

There exists
\delta_{H^{\natural{\sf p}}}>0
such that
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
increases the filtration by at least
\delta_{H^{\natural{\sf p}}}
.

(4)

If
y=x^{({\sf p})}
for
x\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)
,
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},[y]}
vanishes and denote the cohomology by

HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},x^{({\sf p})})\cong\widetilde{\bf K}_{\sf p}.

(5)

If
y
is a simple
{\sf p}
-periodic orbit, then the cohomology of
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},[y]}
is trivial.

Proof.

The decomposition
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}=d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}+D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
and the first three conditions are due to a basic compactness argument, usually termed under cross-energy lemma, see e.g.
[
12
, Lemma B.1]
. In our nondegenerate setting, we know that
d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}
comes from differentials in
CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})
and
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
comes from the contribution from nontrivial Floer cylinders in
X
.

If
y=x^{({\sf p})}
, the summand
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},x^{({\sf p})})\cong CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
and the differential is trivial on
CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
. Hence (4) is verified.

If
y
is simple, then we have the following identification. Let
\tau\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
be the generator and let
w_{l}^{\pm}
resp.
w_{l}^{0},\ldots,w_{l}^{{\sf p}-1}
be the
{\sf p}
critical points of
f^{{\mathbb{E}}}
in degree
l
in
{\sf p}=2
resp.
{\sf p}>2
case.

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},[y])=\bigoplus_{\tau^{k}\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\left({\bf F}_{\sf p}\langle\tau^{k}y\rangle\oplus CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\\
\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bf K}_{2}\Big\langle y\otimes w_{0}^{-}+\tau(y)\otimes w_{0}^{+},y\otimes w_{0}^{+}+\tau(y)\otimes w_{0}^{-}\Big\rangle,&\ {\sf p}=2,\\[5.69046pt]
{\bf K}_{\sf p}\Big\langle\left.\sum_{k=0}^{{\sf p}-1}\tau^{k}(y)\otimes w_{0}^{k+s},\ \sum_{k=0}^{{\sf p}-1}\tau^{k}(y)\otimes w_{1}^{k+s}\right|\ s=0,1,\ldots,{\sf p}-1\Big\rangle&\ {\sf p}>2.\end{array}\right.

One can see from the differential of
CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})
that the cohomology is trivial.
∎

Then we would like to apply the homological perturbation argument.

Lemma 27.7
.

There exist a differential
D_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}
on the “reduced” equivariant complex

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}):=\bigoplus_{x\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)}HF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},x^{({\sf p})})

making it a Floer-type complex, filtered chain homotopy equivalences

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\sigma}:CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}),\

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\pi}:CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}}),

and a filtered chain homotopy

\tilde{\Theta}:CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})

such that

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\pi}\circ\tilde{\sigma}={\rm Id},\

\displaystyle\ \tilde{\sigma}\circ\tilde{\pi}={\rm Id}+d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\circ\tilde{\Theta}+\tilde{\Theta}\circ d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}.

Proof.

This lemma can be proved in the same way as Lemma
27.2
. We omit the details.
∎

27.4.
Equivariant pair-of-pants product

We consider the quantitative property of the equivariant pair-of-pants product. Let
H
be as above. First, the complex
CF(H)
is a Floer-type complex over the Novikov field
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
-modules with a filtration
{\mathcal{A}}_{H}
. Then consider the completed tensor product

\left(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\right)\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}

which has the energy filtration
{\mathcal{A}}_{H}^{\otimes{\sf p}}
. It is a nonarchimedean normed vector space over
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
and is orthogonalizable. Then its
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant subspace

C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}):=\Big(\Big(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\Big)\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\Big)_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}

is orthogonalizable. Moreover, the differential clearly preserves the filtration. Hence one has a Floer-type complex with associated barcode.

Now consider the chain-level equivariant pair-of-pants product, which was originally defined as

P:\Big(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\otimes\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}\Big)_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})

as a
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
-linear chain map.

Proposition 27.8
.

There exists a chain-level construction of
P
which preserves the nonarchimedean norm, i.e.,

{\mathcal{A}}(P(x))\geq{\mathcal{A}}_{H}^{\otimes{\sf p}}(x).

Proof.

Consider the branched covering
\Sigma^{\mathbb{FSt}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}
, which pulls back the trivial Floer data
Hdt
on the cylinder to a Hamiltonian connection on
\sigma_{H}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
on
\Sigma^{\mathbb{FSt}}
. Consider the multimodule
\mathbb{X}^{\mathbb{FSt}}
. From the energy identity for Floer equation, one can see that the multimodule has a threshold zero (Definition
7.41
) with respect to the symplectic action. Then by Proposition
7.42
, the chain map preserves the filtration.
∎

Theorem 27.9
.

There exists a filtered chain isomorphism

P^{\rm red}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})

such that the following diagram commutes up to filtered homotopy, where the vertical arrows are provided by the homological perturbation lemma.

\textstyle{C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P^{\rm red}}
\textstyle{CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})}

The proof is provided momentarily. As the reduced versions of the involved complexes are filtered homotopy equivalent to the original versions, by Theorem
7.40
, one has the following immediate corollary.

Corollary 27.10
.

P
is a filtered homotopy equivalence. Therefore, the concise barcodes of
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
and
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
coincide.

To prove Theorem
27.9
, we proceed by building the local inverses.

Lemma 27.11
.

We can write the equivariant pair-of-pants product
P
as

P=P_{\rm loc}+P_{\rm big}

which satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For each
x\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(H)
, denote
x^{\otimes p}=x\otimes\cdots\otimes x
. Then one has

P_{\rm loc}(C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},x^{\otimes{\sf p}}))\subseteq CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},x^{({\sf p})}).

Denote the restriction by

P_{x}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}},x^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}},{\rm loc}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},x^{({\sf p})}).

(2)

There exists
\delta_{H}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that
P_{\rm big}
increases the filtration by at least
\delta_{H}^{\prime\prime}
.

(3)

P_{x}
is independent of choices and agrees with the local equivariant pair-of-pants product constructed by Seidel
[
61
]
(for
{\sf p}=2
) and Shelukhin–Zhao
[
62
]
(for
{\sf p}>2
).

(4)

P_{x}
is invertible.

Proof.

The decomposition and the first two items are based on the crossing energy argument (cf.
[
12
, Lemma B.1]
). To see that
P_{\rm loc}
is well-defined, suppose
P^{\prime}
is another construction which has a similar decomposition
P^{\prime}=P_{\rm loc}^{\prime}+P_{\rm big}^{\prime}
. Then
P
and
P^{\prime}
are homotopic via a filtered chain homotopy
K
. So

P-P^{\prime}=Kd_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}+d_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}K.

Since
K
also admits the decomposition into the local component and “big” component in view of the cross-energy lemma, by passing to the local piece, one has
P_{\rm loc}=P_{\rm loc}^{\prime}
on cohomology.

Furthermore, since the local argument does not involve sphere bubbling, the equivalence between our FOP counts and the counts defined using classical framework as used in
[
60
]
and
[
62
]
can be made using the same argument as proving Part (2) of Theorem
H
.

Lastly, the invertibility of
P_{x}
is proved in
[
60
]
by a local computation for
{\sf p}=2
, and in
[
62
]
by constructing the local equivariant coproduct and prove that the local coproduct is the inverse of
P_{x}
for
{\sf p}>2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
27.9
.

Now consider the composition

P^{\rm red}:=\pi\circ P\circ\sigma:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})

which is a filtered chain map. Notice that there is an obvious leading order term, denoted by
P_{\rm loc}^{\rm red}
, which is the direct sum of
P_{x}
, while the difference

P_{\rm big}^{\rm red}:=P^{\rm red}-P_{\rm loc}^{\rm red}

strictly increases the energy filtration. Hence
P^{\rm red}
is invertible. Therefore, after tensoring with the correct Novikov field,
P^{\rm red}
is a filtered isomorphism of Floer-type complexes.
∎

27.5.
Proof of Theorem
M

The first item, which says that the equivariant Floer cochain complex
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
is a Floer-type complex over
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Pi}
follows directly from the construction.

The second item is exactly Corollary
27.10
.

For the third item, by Corollary
27.10
, the barcodes of the two complexes
C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})
and
CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}})
are identical. On the other hand, there exists an algebraically defined quasi-Frobenius map (see (
23.3
))

qF:HF(H)\to H_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}).

Although
qF
is not induced from a cochain map, it still respects the filtration in the following way. For each
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}
, one has corresponding
{\bf F}_{\sf p}
-linear map

qF:HF^{\leq\tau}(H)\to H_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\leq{\sf p}\tau}(CF(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}).

In the current setting, it is hard to obtain the relationship between barcodes, but only the bar-length spectrum. Then, following the argument in
[
65
, Section 6.2]
, one can define a version of Floer complex over the universal Novikov ring

\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ}

(with base ring
{\bf F}_{\sf p}
), denoted by
CF(H;\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ})
. Then by the general structural results of modules over
\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ}
, the bar-lengths
\beta_{i}(H)
correspond to torsion parts of
HF(H;\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ})
of the forms
\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ}/T^{\beta_{i}(H)}\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ}
. Then one can prove that the quasi-Frobenius map is an isomorphism of
\Lambda_{0}^{\rm univ}
-modules after the rescaling by
{\sf p}
. The result in Theorem
M
(3) follows from Theorem
M
(2).

28.
Quantitative Theory for Noncontractible Hamiltonian Orbits

In this section we provide constructions for the Floer complex for noncontractible orbits (in the homological sense) and the corresponding quantitative results involving a version of equivariant pair-of-pants product. As mentioned before, the main motivation for developing this variant is to generalize the result of Sugimoto
[
67
]
to full generality, which will be done in a separate paper with Shelukhin and Wilkins
[
12
]
. Here in this section, we will prove Theorem
1.11
and Theorem
1.12
. The only notable technical difference is that the capping of a non-contractible orbit is defined relative to a reference homology class; the rest of the construction is exactly the same as the contractible case.

28.1.
The Floer flow category and bimodules

Let
\gamma
be a smooth loop in
X
which represents a nontrivial class in
H_{1}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})_{\rm free}
. Denote

\Gamma=H_{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})/{\rm ker}\omega.

For a nondegenerate Hamiltonian
H
, we describe a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded
\Gamma
-equivariant flow category
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
of orbits of
H
which are homologous to
\gamma
. Recall terms defined in Definition
1.10
. Define

{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}:=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\gamma}(H).

To define morphisms of
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
, choose a 1-periodic family of
\omega
-compatible almost complex structures
J
and considering moduli spaces of Floer equations and their compactifications. Then the morphism set between two
\gamma
-capped orbits is the moduli space of stable Floer trajectories connecting them, where the Floer cylinders naturally concatenate the
\gamma
-cappings. One then obtains the flow category
\mathbb{F}_{\gamma}
enriched in
\underline{\smash{\bf Top}}
where objects are naturally
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded. The action functional

{\mathcal{A}}_{H,\gamma}:{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}\to{\mathbb{R}}

makes
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
a locally finite flow category (Definition
7.2
). The
\Gamma
-action on objects makes
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
a
\Gamma
-equivariant flow category (Definition
7.24
).

Notice that if we choose a different loop
\gamma^{\prime}
homologous to
\gamma
, then one essentially get the same flow category except that the actions
{\mathcal{A}}_{H,\gamma}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{H,\gamma^{\prime}}
differ by a constant.

If we choose a different pair
(H^{\prime},J^{\prime})
and consider the same construction (for a fixed loop
\gamma
), then one obtains a similar Floer flow category
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{\prime}
. By choosing a Floer datum on the infinite cylinder interpolating between
(H,J)
and
(H^{\prime},J^{\prime})
and considering moduli spaces of Floer equations associated to the Floer datum, one obtains a bimodule
\mathbb{B}_{\gamma}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma};{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{\prime})
, which is naturally
\Gamma
-equivariant (Definition
7.26
). This is completely analogous to the usual contractible case.

28.2.
The complex and continuation map

Since the Floer homology of the noncontractible case is (expected to be) trivial, in fact, the Floer complex is null-homotopic, the only interesting theory is the quantitative theory. Consider

CF_{\gamma}(H):=\Big\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}p_{i}\ |\ a_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ p_{i}\in{\rm Ob}{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma},\ \lim_{i\to\infty}{\mathcal{A}}_{H,\gamma}(p_{i})=-\infty\Big\}

which is a module over
\Lambda_{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\Gamma}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

The AMS construction for the flow category
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
is almost identical to the contractible case. There are only two minor places which we would like to comment. The first one is the existence of integral actions. The proof of Proposition
15.4
works equally well for the noncontractible case. Then one can go through the AMS construction for the flow category. The change of Novikov group from
\Pi
to
\Gamma
does not affect our argument to make each step of the construction equivariant. Then upon making choices, one obtains a lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. Choosing FOP perturbations allows us to construct the differential, hence obtaining a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded chain complex

CF_{\gamma}(H,J,\Xi)

(linear over
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Gamma}
).

After changing coefficients to
\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Gamma}
, one obtains a Floer-type complex because the differential increases the filtration.

To see that the filtered isomorphism class only depends on
H
, we compare two different almost complex structures
J
,
J^{\prime}
and two different sets of choices
\Xi
,
\Xi^{\prime}
, resulting in AMS lifts
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{\prime}
. Choose an interpolation between
J
and
J^{\prime}
and consider the corresponding bimodule
{\mathbb{B}}_{\gamma}
over
({\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma};{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{\prime})
via continuation map moduli spaces. One apply the AMS construction for
{\mathbb{B}}_{\gamma}
, ending with a chain map

\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{\gamma}}:CF_{\gamma}(H,J,\Xi)\to CF_{\gamma}(H,J^{\prime},\Xi^{\prime}).

Notice that the two chain groups are canonically identified since the Hamiltonian
H
is fixed. The leading order term of
\Phi^{{\mathbb{B}}_{\gamma}}
is then the identity map on the chain group
CF_{\gamma}(H)
. Hence after tensoring with
\Lambda_{{\bf K}}^{\Gamma}
, the chain map is a filtered isomorphism.
∎

Therefore, if we switch to field coefficients, for each field
{\bf K}
, we obtain a well-defined verbose barcode
{\mathcal{B}}_{\gamma}(H)
. It still depends on the concrete choice of the loop
\gamma
. We shorten the notation for the chain complex by

CF_{\gamma}(H;\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Gamma}).

Corollary 28.1
.

For two homologous loops
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
, the bar-length spectrum of
CF_{\gamma}(H;\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Gamma})
coincides with that of
CF_{\gamma^{\prime}}(H;\Lambda_{\bf K}^{\Gamma})
.

Proof.

Let
\gamma^{\prime}
be another loop which is homologous to
\gamma
. Choosing a concrete cobordism between them. Then one obtains a
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism of flow categories
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}\cong{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
which shifts the action by a constant. An AMS construction and FOP perturbation for
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
can be transported to
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
. Hence the bar-length spectrum does not depend on the representating loop
\gamma
.
∎

Remark 28.2
.

It is standard to argue that the barcode
{\mathcal{B}}_{\gamma}(H)
varies Lipschitz continuously with
H
, where on barcodes we use the bottleneck distance. Hence the barcode
{\mathcal{B}}_{\gamma}(H)
also extends to degenerate Hamiltonians.

28.3.
Equivariant Floer complex

Now we can repeat the equivariant Floer construction to the noncontractible case. Let
{\sf p}
be a prime and suppose
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
is nondegenerate, which may have noncontractible orbits homologous to
\gamma
. By coupling with the Morse flow category
{{\mathbb{E}}}
on
\widehat{S}^{\infty}
, one obtains a flow category (see Definition
25.1
)

{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}

which has a free action by the twisted Novikov group
(\Gamma\times{\mathbb{Z}})\rtimes{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
. Notice that although the reference loop
\gamma
is not invariant under the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action via loop rotation, the symplectic action is still well-defined.

Using exactly the same construction as the contractible case, one can construct an equivariant lift of the outercollaring of
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}
to
{}_{\lfloor}\underline{\smash{\bf dOrb}}_{\rm rig}^{\rm NC}
. After choose an equivariant FOP perturbation, denoted by
\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
, one obtains a complex with a
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-action, denoted by

C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}).

Its
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part provides a complex

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma)

which is a module over
\Lambda_{{\bf F}_{\sf p}}^{\Gamma\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}}
. By tensoring with the larger Novikov field
\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Gamma}
one obtains a Floer-type complex

CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma;\Lambda_{{\bf K}_{\sf p}}^{\Gamma}).

By using the equivariant continuation map, one proves that the filtered isomorphism class is well-defined. Hence one obtains a barcode

{\mathcal{B}}_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma)

which only depends on
H
. Again, choosing a homologous loop
\gamma^{\prime}
will shift the barcodes but does not change the bar-length spectrum.

28.4.
Equivariant pair-of-pants product

One can construct the noncontractible version of the equivariant pair-of-pants product. Let
H
be a Hamiltonian such that
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
is nondegenerate. Let
\gamma^{({\sf p})}
be the
{\sf p}
-th iteration of
\gamma
. Basically, the same setup as the contractible case provides a multimodule

{\mathbb{X}}_{\gamma}^{\mathbb{FSt}}

over
({\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma},\ldots,{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma},{\mathbb{E}};{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma^{({\sf p})}}^{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}})
. More precisely, let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sf p}
be objects of
{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}
whose underlying loops
\underline{\smash{x}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\smash{x}}_{\sf p}
are homologous to
\gamma
. Let
\underline{\smash{x}}^{\prime}
be a Hamiltonian loop of
H^{\natural{\sf p}}
homologous to
\gamma^{({\sf p})}
. Consider
p
-legged pair-of-pants forming a cobordism (up to equality of symplectic area) between
\underline{\smash{x}}_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\underline{\smash{x}}_{\sf p}
represented by a map
u:\Sigma^{\mathbb{FSt}}\to X
. Then the
\gamma
-cappings
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\sf p}
together with the relative homology class of
u
induces a
\gamma^{({\sf p})}
-capping of
\underline{\smash{x}}^{\prime}
as illustrated in Figure
11
.

Figure 11.
Configurations of equivariant pair-of-pants product for noncontractible orbits

The AMS construction and FOP perturbation provide a chain map

\underbrace{CF_{\gamma}(H)\otimes\cdots\otimes\cdots CF_{\gamma}(H)}_{{\sf p}}\otimes CM(f^{{\mathbb{E}}})\to C(\mathring{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma^{({\sf p})}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}).

Restricting to the
{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}
-invariant part one obtains

P_{\gamma}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(CF_{\gamma}(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma^{({\sf p})}).

Proposition 28.3
.

P_{\gamma}
is a filtered chain homotopy equivalence of Floer-type complexes.

Proof.

The argument is similar to the contractible case. One can first define the reduced versions

\displaystyle\ C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF_{\gamma}(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}}),\

\displaystyle\ CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma^{({\sf p})})

(see (
27.1
) and Lemma
27.7
) which are filtered chain homotopy equivalent to the unreduced versions via the homological perturbation construction. Moreover, for each orbit
x\in\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\gamma}(H)
, one has a local equivariant pair-of-pants product
P_{x}
which can be proved to be invertible. Moreover, the direct sum of
P_{x}
is the leading order term of
P_{\gamma}
. Then
P_{\gamma}
is transferred to a filtered isomorphism

P_{\gamma}^{\rm red}:C_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}^{\rm red}(CF_{\gamma}(H)^{\otimes{\sf p}})\to CF_{{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\sf p}}}(H^{\natural{\sf p}},\gamma^{({\sf p})}).\qed

Proof of Theorem
1.12
.

The isomorphism class of the Floer-type complex is disucssed in Section
28.3
. Proposition
28.3
shows Theorem
1.12
(2) holds. Finally, Theorem
1.12
(3) follows from the same argument as in Section
27.5
with the above ingredients.
∎
